# Feature Specification: forge-plugin

**Created**: 2026-08-08
**Revised**: 2026-10-06
**Status**: Draft (Revision 22)
**Revision 22 status**: This specification-first authority retires policy-bearing run journals, passed/blocked run closure, journal-to-chain bindings, scope admission, run-open route freezing, committed run archive generation, and journal-derived learning; implementation code units follow separately, and retired text is removed rather than marked. A remaining `(Revision N authority; deferred to task X of <run>)` marker scopes only the sentence it follows and is removed when the named task lands.
**Intent**: Forge is a Claude Code plugin for fail-closed commit and merge gate chains, independent binding review, configurable Claude and Codex execution routes, disciplined worktrees and locks, operator control approval, evals, drift sensing, and a kill-switch. The Claude main session orchestrates and verifies; launched roles use per-developer routes. Chain evidence authorizes exact candidates, while append-only run journals record executions and decisions without governing work or landing.

Inputs: `docs/design/0001-founding-decisions.md`, `docs/design/0002-verification-expansion.md`, `docs/design/0003-forge-cli-plumbing.md`, the committed control contracts below, and the operator ruling of 2026-10-06 recorded in §5A.

---

## 2. Implementation Scope

**Capabilities**:

1. Ship a Claude Code plugin named `forge` with seven skills (`init`, `workflow`, `orchestrate`, `report`, `commit`, `worktree-merge`, `drift`), the `review-final` agent, provider launch profiles, governance hooks and scripts, and the review constitution.
2. Keep the vendored engine's layout under `scripts/codex_orch_tools.py`, `scripts/codex_orchestrator/`, `tests/`, and `docs/orchestration-contract.md`; record executions and decisions through §5A's plain journal and structural reader.
3. Provide exact-candidate, fail-closed commit and merge chains, retained per-chain evidence, operator approval for control changes, a non-narrowable review-final floor, and same-chain fixes with deterministic gate invalidation.
4. Render `forge-project.md` into CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md through idempotent `/forge:init`, preserving project-owned content and the existing migration and binding self-review safeguards.
5. Preserve the commit guard, kill-switch, STRICT and fresh-reviewer evals, invariants, risk tiers, decision telemetry, worktree discipline, and commit and rebase locks.
6. Preserve detached drift sensing and immutable drift and migration reports; generate a run report from available execution and chain evidence without a passed-close or archive prerequisite.
7. Preserve the CLI merge and standalone-push state machines and activated history-mutation guard; phase-5 marker deletion remains gated by FR-242 and phase-6 plan sealing remains deferred by FR-243.

**Guard rails**:

- No runtime dependencies beyond the Python 3.10+ standard library; no build system; engine invoked by path via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.
- Existing journal bytes remain readable and MUST NOT be migrated or rewritten in place; new records follow FR-250..FR-252.
- Vendored engine files keep upstream-identical relative paths; every deliberate in-file deviation carries an inline `# forge: modified from upstream — <reason>` marker.
- The string `opencode` MUST NOT appear anywhere in the plugin outside `UPSTREAM`, `docs/design/`, and `docs/specs/`, except that it MAY appear in `scripts/forge/migrate-upstream.py` and in tests that exercise that migration reader because FR-181 requires reading those legacy paths. This prohibition keeps the plugin free of legacy runtime support; reading a legacy path during migration is not legacy runtime support. Everywhere else the prohibition remains unchanged.
- Test framework is stdlib `unittest` (upstream convention); no pytest.
- No `commands/` directory: skills are the only invocation surface (preserves upstream doc-contract assertion).

**Threat model**: the enforcement layer (guard hook, markers, sentinels, execpolicy) defends against accident, negligence, and prompt-injection-driven attempts that traverse the registered model-tool paths it actually observes. It is not an OS-wide command broker: shell aliases, wrappers, existing Git hooks, direct same-user processes, and other paths outside those registrations are not claimed covered. Confinement is the committed per-provider profile value: Codex implementers use `workspace-write`, Codex review-cheap, review-final, and plan use `read-only`, Claude plan uses `read-only` without Bash, and Claude implementer, review-cheap, and review-final use `instruction-bounded` Bash; choosing Claude for implementer is therefore a developer-local weakening relative to the Codex default and remains legible in each execution's `sandbox` field. The Claude orchestrator remains the operator's same-user process. The design does NOT defend against a deliberately adversarial orchestrator: Claude runs as the operator's OS user, and no same-user file mechanism can bind it. The last pre-Git tree observation narrows arbitrary index-writer races, and the post-Git produced-identity gate contains rather than prevents them; a mismatch can leave an untouched divergent commit for explicit operator disposition. The kill-switch and audit log are operator-facing controls; anything stronger (an external broker or signing service) is out of scope for this release.

---

## 3. Existing Codebase Context

| Area | Existing files | Required change |
|------|----------------|-----------------|
| Engine CLI + journal | `.upstream/codex-orchestrator/scripts/codex_orchestrator/{cli,journal,events,monitor}.py`, `scripts/codex_orch_tools.py` | Keep the layout; provide the non-authorizing writer and tolerant structural reader |
| Engine skills | `.upstream/codex-orchestrator/skills/{orchestrate,workflow,report}/SKILL.md` + `orchestrate/references/*.md` | Vendor; rename skill frontmatter to `forge-*`; weave gate steps, hardening rules, and role routing into workflow/orchestrate |
| Engine tests | `.upstream/codex-orchestrator/tests/` (7 modules, fixtures, `replay/long-run-001/`) | Preserve applicable engine tests; test structural reads and non-authorizing appends |
| Orchestration contract | `.upstream/codex-orchestrator/docs/orchestration-contract.md` | Describe execution records and chain-owned evidence |
| Gate chains | `.upstream/forge/system/template/.opencode/rules/{commit-workflow,worktree-workflow}.md` | Rewrite as plugin skills `commit`, `worktree-merge`; regions relocate to `forge-project.md`; paths re-rooted |
| Review constitution | `.upstream/forge/system/template/.opencode/rules/review-constitution.md` | Ship as plugin `rules/review-constitution.md`; project regions relocate to `forge-project.md` |
| Governance rules | `.upstream/forge/system/engine/.opencode/rules/{operating-model,risk-authority-classification,control-integrity,untrusted-input,operator-halt,commit-locking,evaluation-harness}.md` | Ship condensed under plugin `rules/`; DVRR spine excerpted into the `forge-project.md` template |
| Scripts | `.upstream/forge/system/engine/.opencode/scripts/{check-halt,acquire-commit-lock,release-commit-lock,aggregate-telemetry}.sh`, `.opencode/evals/run-evals.sh` | Port to plugin `scripts/forge/`; repo-local state paths move to `.forge/tmp/` |
| Installer | `.upstream/forge/bin/forge-install.sh`, `commands/forge-init.md`, `skills/forge-init/SKILL.md` | Rewrite as plugin skill `init` + `scripts/forge/install.sh`; all paths plugin-root-relative |
| .codex layer | `.upstream/forge/system/template/.codex/{config.toml,hooks.json,rules/forge.rules,agents/*.toml}` | Reduce to `implementer` + `review-cheap` + `plan` TOMLs; ship as init-installed template under plugin `system/codex/` |
| Claude agents | `.upstream/forge/system/template/.claude/agents/review-final.md` | Ship as plugin `agents/review-final.md`; constitution path re-rooted |
| Seeds | `.upstream/forge/system/seeds/{eval-tasks/*.template.md,validation-snippets/stacks.md,brownfield-exploration.md}` | Ship under plugin `system/seeds/` |

`.upstream/` is an untracked local reference checkout whose authoritative commit SHAs are recorded in `UPSTREAM` per FR-004.

---

## 4. Terminology

| Term | Definition |
|------|------------|
| Run | One orchestration lifecycle under `.codex-orchestrator/runs/<run-id>/` with `journal.jsonl` as a non-authorizing execution and decision log |
| Gate 1 / Gate 2 / Gate 3 | Project tests / lint+types / adversarial review — the merge-blocking verification chain |
| Control-class change | A path in FR-253's built-in control set or a project extension; requires review-final and explicit operator approval |
| Review-final floor | FR-254's non-narrowable hard-tier path set; review-final is mandatory independently of whether operator approval is required |
| Region | A `<!-- FORGE:REGION <name> BEGIN/END -->` block in `forge-project.md`; unfilled while it contains a `<!-- forge-init: ... -->` comment |
| Iteration | One review round under the existing binding-review, fresh-eval and merge-attempt accounting rules; restaging does not reset the eight-round cap |
| Golden task | A committed eval fixture with `expected_verdict`; its committed `.result` is the baseline |
| Detached launch | A worker launched in its own process group (`set -m` job control, or `setsid` where available) via `nohup ... & disown`, unmanaged by the Claude Code task layer, with a `pid` sidecar file in its execution directory |
| Merge generation | One immutable merge candidate tuple binding the fixed target, fetched base, full candidate HEAD, exact three-dot diff digest, committed policy, worktree identity, and positive generation number |
| Lock epoch | One bounded acquisition of the common rebase lock that performs at most one integration/final cycle; external review and approval never occur inside it |
| History mutation mode | The committed DM-015 manifest value that leaves phase-1 dual-accept behavior in `legacy-v1` or atomically enables phase-4 raw-commit/raw-push denial in `forge-verbs-v1` |
| Push outcome unknown | A persisted `pushing` condition in which the remote cannot be observed authoritatively and the CLI therefore cannot safely retry or report failure/success |

**Commit candidate identity.** After staging, Forge writes the real worktree index to an immutable
Git tree object with `git write-tree` in a pinned repository/index context. The commit-authorization
identity is the lowercase SHA-256 of the exact ASCII domain-separated tuple
`forge-commit-candidate/2`, the repository object format, and the full resulting tree OID. It
identifies the complete tree Git is authorized to place in the produced commit, including modes,
symlinks, and gitlinks, and is independent of diff rendering, path-list rendering, Git
user/repository diff configuration, and review-package ordering. The exact deterministic
binary-capable tree-to-tree patch from the recorded base tree to that candidate tree remains
mandatory review evidence and has its own SHA-256 and byte count; that evidence digest is never
commit authorization. Finalize MUST re-observe the index tree immediately before intent and, after
Git returns success, MUST verify the produced commit's single parent, tree OID, and exact message
digest against intent before consuming authorization, recording a landing, or closing. A mismatch
leaves the commit untouched, records a failed identity verification, and freezes the chain in
`committing` under `frozen-chain`.

---

## 5. Surface / API Inventory

### New surfaces

- `/forge:init` — install/refresh the per-repo layer (region file, AGENTS.md splice, `.codex/`, gitignore block, `.forge/`, eval fixtures)
- `/forge:commit` — the 5-step fail-closed commit gate chain
- `/forge:worktree-merge` — the 4-gate merge chain with locked rebase reintegration
- `/forge:drift` — periodic semantic drift review over the FR-161 Drift summary schema v1 JSON
- Plugin PreToolUse hook (`scripts/forge/commit-guard.sh`) — inspects matched Git/Forge command segments that traverse the model's registered Bash-tool path and denies them on halt or missing authorization; it is not a repository-native Git hook and is not an exhaustive claim about every same-user execution path
- Plugin PostToolUse hook (`scripts/forge/invariant-guard.sh`) — advisory invariant feedback after Edit/Write
- Plugin Stop/SessionStart hooks — the Stop union of telemetry aggregation plus a drift-staleness nudge, and the SessionStart drift-staleness nudge, in forge-initialized repos
- `agents/review-final.md` — instruction-bounded, execution-capable Claude reviewer with a binding verdict; it has no Edit/Write tools but shares the orchestrator's writable worktree, so its no-write boundary is not an OS sandbox
- `system/codex/agents/plan.toml`, `system/codex/prompts/plan.md`, `system/claude/prompts/implementer.md`, `system/claude/prompts/plan.md`, and `system/claude/prompts/review-cheap.md` — the read-only Codex planner route and the four routing-chain-B role bodies
- `scripts/forge/{check-halt.sh,acquire-commit-lock.sh,release-commit-lock.sh,run-evals.sh,invariant-guard.sh,configure-dcg.sh,drift-check.sh,drift-staleness.sh,aggregate-telemetry.sh,commit-guard.sh,install.sh}` and `scripts/forge/{check-test-quality.py,archive-run.py,audit-commitments.py,emit-decision-event.py,route_config.py}` — executable governance script surfaces
  `archive-run.py` and `audit-commitments.py` are retired by Revision 22 and stay listed only until the implementing unit deletes them; they are not invoked by any kept requirement.
- `system/local/routes.toml.seed` — owner-only per-clone routing seed; `scripts/forge/route_config_probe.py` is the non-executable provider-probe helper, `scripts/forge/route_config_git.py` is the non-executable stdlib Git-boundary helper used by the route-config CLI, `scripts/forge/route_floor.py` is the non-executable, interpreter-invoked CLI version-floor helper, and `scripts/forge/route_evidence.py` is the non-executable execution-route evidence helper
- `scripts/forge/route_vocab.py` — non-executable, Python-interpreter-loaded shared route-vocabulary library (canonical `ROLE_IDS`, `REVIEW_ROLE_IDS`, `PROVIDER_IDS`, `EVENT_SOURCE_IDS`, `MODE_IDS`, and `SANDBOX_IDS`; `validate_new_write`; the legacy read map; and model-id members `MODEL_ID_RE`, `normalize_model_id`, and `model_family`); imported by `scripts/codex_orchestrator` and `scripts/forge` readers and writers; outside the executable-script inventory
- `scripts/forge/run-scoped-mutation.py` — Python-interpreter-loaded governance helper (the merge path invokes it as `python3 <path>` and the drift path imports it), so it is outside the executable-script inventory
- `scripts/forge/fr223_verify.py` — non-executable, Python-interpreter-invoked FR-223 qualification helper that preserves committed-HEAD authority for a plugin root that is its own Git work-tree top level and supplies the digest-chained qualification path only for an eligible non-Git installed root; it remains outside the executable-script inventory. (Revision 20 authority; deferred to task G40 of run-20261003-archive)
- `scripts/forge/codex_layer_merge.py` — non-executable, Python-interpreter-loaded standard-library helper for strict Codex-layer preflight, marker-owned `hooks.json` merging, and `config.toml` collision classification; it remains outside the executable-script inventory. (Revision 20 authority; deferred to task G42 of run-20261003-archive)
- `scripts/forge/forge_cli/` — the Python-interpreter-loaded package the Forge CLI is being split into (bead forge-plugin-95e): `scripts/forge/cli.py` remains the sole invoked entry point and shim that FR-221's matcher and the FR-223 corpora pin; the shim puts its own directory on `sys.path`, re-imports the envelope and policy names by explicit named lists, and forwards attribute reads of every `runtime`, `chain_core`, `engine`, and `app` `__all__` export through a module `__getattr__` (PEP 562), so every historical module attribute stays resolvable while tests reach the package through one shared loader (`tests/_cli_loader.py`); no file under it is executable, and it is outside the executable-script inventory exactly as `run-scoped-mutation.py` is. The package holds `envelope.py` (reason codes, `Refusal`, `FrozenError`, `Outcome`), `policy.py` (the committed-policy parser, whose fence helpers are the canonical patch seams), `runtime.py` (the one canonical module for the patchable controls — the clock, the bounded process runner, the merge lifecycle flag, the path roots and the fast-tier mechanical-skip predicate — which every module of the split reads by attribute so one in-memory patch on `forge_cli.runtime` disables a control everywhere), `candidate.py` (the shared bounded, pinned Git-context implementation for immutable tree snapshots, deterministic review evidence, marker identity, and raw produced-commit inspection), the `chain_core/` package (`scripts/forge/forge_cli/chain_core/`, whose root `__init__.py` holds the verbatim `__all__` and re-exports every symbol the historical module defined (its unused imports are not re-bound on the root), and whose modules — the root `__init__.py` and the submodules, together exactly the `scripts/forge/forge_cli/chain_core/` production subjects listed in `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json` — hold the fenced process runner, the FR-235 common-lock arbiter and chain leases, chain and merge-chain storage, merge state and transition validation), the `engine/` package (`scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/`, whose root `__init__.py` holds the verbatim `__all__` and re-exports every symbol the historical `engine.py` defined (names it merely imported are re-bound on the root only where an importer or test reads them there), and whose submodules — the root and the submodules together exactly the `scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/` production subjects listed in `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json` — hold the `Engine` commit-chain class (`_engine.py`), argument-parser construction, the finalize pipeline, review transport, the request-free shared review-lane primitives in `_review_lane_api.py`, approval, secret-scan and fresh-eval helpers, merge admission/claim/epoch/conflict/worktree helpers and the fenced bootstrap child protocol; the bootstrap child re-execs `scripts/forge/cli.py` resolved from `runtime.py`'s own location), and the `app/` package (`scripts/forge/forge_cli/app/`, whose root `__init__.py` holds the verbatim `__all__` and re-exports every symbol the historical `app.py` defined, and whose modules — the root and the submodules together exactly the `scripts/forge/forge_cli/app/` production subjects listed in `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json` — hold the `MergeEngine` merge-chain class (`_merge_engine.py`), merge-start admission (`_admission.py`), current-candidate observation (`_candidate_observation.py`), the shared chain-verb router, the dispatch entry point and `main` (`_dispatch.py`); it obtains the parser and other engine helpers through `forge_cli.engine`). The existing FR-230 phase-3/4 manifest retains its historical production subjects; every changed or added production subject, including `candidate.py`, requires a current applicable Recorded-baseline integrity (`strict-evals`) result binding, plus `fresh-reviewer-evals` when the authenticated-base trigger matches; an unverifiable trigger blocks through its required INVALID path. The package layout preserves immutable prior corpora and the sole CLI entry point.
- `scripts/forge/forge_cli/fresh_evals.py` — the non-executable, Python-interpreter-loaded internal collector and verifier for candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation; behind the chain-owned `engine/` package launcher adapter it implements authenticated trigger derivation, candidate-tree fixture inventory and oracle withholding, bounded native child execution, canonical request-bound artifacts, manifest verification, and the current-candidate PASS predicate without adding a second CLI entry point
- `scripts/forge/cli.py` — the Forge CLI commit-chain slice (FR-210..FR-224): a persisted chain state machine invoked as `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" <verb>`, owning commit-chain sequencing, staging, candidate identity, evidence capture, and finalize. No PATH binary named `forge` is installed (the Foundry `forge` collision); `forge <verb>` anywhere in this spec is shorthand for that invocation form. Phase-gated: ships only after the FR-223 precondition evals pass
- Phase-3 Forge CLI merge surfaces specified by FR-230..FR-237: `status`, `merge start|refresh|verify|gate run|approve|finalize|recover|cleanup|abort`, and the shared `review request|collect|cancel|attach|disposition` verbs. These are normative future surfaces, not implementation delivered by Revision 7; Revision 15 limits `attach` to FR-246's legacy request shape.
- Phase-4 `forge push`, DM-015 activation parsing, and the v2 PreToolUse matcher/raw-denial behavior specified by FR-238..FR-241. These are normative future surfaces, not implementation delivered by Revision 7.
- Additive phase-3/4 v2 reason-code and hook-vector artifacts in the exact DM-016 inventory. Every v1 artifact remains immutable.

### Modified surfaces

- `/forge:workflow`, `/forge:orchestrate`, `/forge:report` — vendored skills renamed into the `forge` namespace; workflow records executions and outcomes without close judgment; orchestrate retains role routing, detached launch mechanics, and hardening rules; report consumes available evidence without a close or archive prerequisite
- `hooks/hooks.json` — add PostToolUse invariant feedback and the plugin-level SessionStart drift-staleness registration; make Stop the union contract in FR-093
- `scripts/forge/install.sh`, `system/template/forge-project.md`, and `tests/test_commit_and_region_template.py` — migrate the same closed region inventory from nine through the admitted predecessor inventories to the exact DM-003 sixteen-region order
- `scripts/forge/acquire-commit-lock.sh [<lock-path>]` and `scripts/forge/release-commit-lock.sh [<lock-path>]` — generalize both helpers to accept one optional explicit repository-relative lock-path argument. When the argument is omitted, both helpers default to `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` and preserve FR-055's current behavior and output byte-for-byte; FR-157 reserves the explicit `.forge/tmp/events.lock` path for the prune read-and-replace and uses recovery-only lock-state revalidation for emitters waiting on a live pruner
- `scripts/forge/aggregate-telemetry.sh` — required code changes: consume the FR-157 decision-event stream before any no-Markdown/no-unit return; extend the standalone CSV schema to all thirteen counters and, in a Forge-initialized repository, emit the CSV including the `__decision_totals__` row whenever `events.jsonl` exists, independently of whether any per-unit `*.md` decision logs or fenced telemetry blocks exist; accept paired `--since <UTC-ISO-8601> --until <UTC-ISO-8601>` bounds plus append-only `--append-csv <path> --session <session-id>` Stop mode; and implement §9's exit-2 contract for a missing or malformed CLI argument, unpaired/malformed/reversed window arguments, or a read/write failure. The Stop surface serializes session-identified appends, while `drift-check.sh` constructs its JSON counters directly without a CSV artifact
- `system/seeds/validation-snippets/stacks.md` — add per-stack assertion, mutation-tool, and property-library seed metadata required by FR-139
- `tests/test_docs_contract.py` — assertions migrated to the merged prose (same contract-testing approach)
- Future phase-3/4 implementation surfaces governed by this revision: `scripts/forge/cli.py`, `scripts/forge/commit-guard.sh`, `hooks/hooks.json`, installed hook templates, `.forge-manifest` parsing and init/re-init rendering, `/forge:worktree-merge`, the installed Beads close-protocol block, CLI help/routing inventories, and their conformance tests. Revision 7 changes none of those files.

### Deferred From This Spec

- Upstream PRs to alexzh3/codex-orchestrator — post-implementation work per the contribution table in `0001-founding-decisions.md`
- Cost/token capture in the eval runner — upstream open decision; the >20% cost threshold stays advisory and manually recorded
- A `forge-sync` upstream-diff command — upstream assessment is manual against the `UPSTREAM` manifest until cherry-pick volume justifies tooling
- Claude agents beyond `review-final` (debugger, security-auditor, test-runner, simplifier, docs-writer, council-seat, taskify-agent, code-reviewer) — outside the D1/D2 role split; reassess after real usage
- GPU compute gating (`references/compute.md` nvidia-smi section) — retained verbatim in the vendored reference but not integrated with the gate chain
- Renumbering the constitution's missing SEC-10 — principle IDs are cited by other documents; renumbering breaks references for zero behavioral gain
- Phase-5 removal of DM-006 markers, their parser, or their tests — FR-242 defines admission only; Revision 7 does not authorize deletion, weakening, or dormant-code cleanup.
- Phase-6 implementation of `forge plan seal --file <plan>` or `forge proposal unseal` — FR-243 reserves the future names and honest best-effort claim only; no executable surface, parser branch, storage, or confidentiality claim is authorized.

### Non-goals

Reviewer quorum and FR-124 plan/proposal sealing remain deferred; FR-243 reserves future phase-6 names without authorizing an executable parser or storage surface.

Revision 22 does not add immutable task file lists, a green-main check before landing, per-landing token and wall-time records, or three-path ceremony scaling.

## 5A. Record-keeping simplification (Revision 22)

The operator ruling of 2026-10-06 follows `docs/analysis/process-reality-check-2026-10-06.local.md`, an untracked operator report covering about 120 runs, 220 chains and 148 sessions in this repository and its consumer: 71 of 73 blocked closes reflected binding rules rather than rejected work, and no product defect was found by a journal, close rule, scope registry or archive. The scope registry prevented no observed collision and caused three wedges (GH#1, #2 and #11), route freezing forced eight runs to close and re-home, and passed-close prerequisites starved archives. All 57 code defects among major/critical findings were caught by `review-final`, none by `review-cheap`, including seven fail-open commit-guard shapes under `scripts/forge/` and `hooks/`; 184 of 220 chains were control-class solely because of `scripts/**` and `tests/fixtures/**`, costing 217 hours of approval wait. Of 72 aborted chains, 54 followed a review BLOCK whose fix could not land in that chain, requiring a fresh complete gate set. The Revision-18 rule that a run-bound chain runs Gate 1 for a docs-class candidate (`run-bound-gate-one`) retires with run binding, so every chain now takes the docs-class Gate 1 skip, which still excludes control, floor and trigger matches. The same ruling keeps the merge fetch sidecar's existing `forge-run-scope-fetch-binding/2` shape with its scope members null (FR-236) rather than introducing a new schema. Beyond the ruling's text this revision adds three mechanisms, each part of this revision: FR-255's operator-only `commit skip review-cap` reset, so that the cross-chain cap has an operator exit; FR-032's refusal of a `review-final` route equal to the implementer route, which makes the ruling's different-model binding review enforceable; and `scripts/forge/route_config.py` as a built-in control path, because its committed defaults can weaken the review.

The following controls retain their force:

- Independent binding review by a different model, reviewer isolation and the eight-iteration cap: FR-031, FR-033, FR-053, FR-110..FR-112, FR-123..FR-125 and FR-216, with FR-032 amended only to name the fix-in-chain target and the review-final different-model rule.
- Exact-candidate, fail-closed gates, control approval and chain evidence: FR-050..FR-056, FR-060..FR-063, FR-210..FR-224 and FR-230..FR-243.
- Commit guard and kill-switch, STRICT and fresh-reviewer evals, invariants, risk tiers, worktrees and locks: FR-090..FR-095, FR-100..FR-103, FR-130..FR-132, FR-145..FR-157, FR-054..FR-055 and FR-062..FR-064.

- **FR-250** (MUST): Each run directory MUST retain one append-only `journal.jsonl`, one JSON object per line. New records carry `kind` and `run_id`, where `run_id` is a nonempty string that is not dot-prefixed and contains no path separator, NUL or control character (the existing journal run-id grammar), because it forms the run directory path: `run_started` records the repository, intent text and actor; `task` records a task id, title and free-text scope; `execution_started` and `execution_finished` record execution id, task id, role, provider, model, effort, worktree, applicable start/end times, exit status and output artefact path, with tokens when reported by the launcher; `decision` records free text, actor and optional references; `run_closed` records a free-text outcome and no judgment field. Fields not yet available at execution start MAY be omitted until its finish record. Execution records MUST describe the route actually used, with no run-open route snapshot. Any record MAY cite chain ids and verdict paths as references, which confer no authority and are never validated against a chain.
- **FR-251** (MUST): The writer MUST append and return without checking session ownership, scope, routes, task status, chain state, reference existence, prior close or close eligibility. It MUST retain a single-writer append lock whose only journal purpose is to prevent torn or interleaved lines; FR-034 additionally serializes execution and attempt id allocation under the same lock, and the lock grants no run ownership. Its only refusals are I/O failure or a malformed envelope: a record that is not a valid JSON object, lacks `kind` or `run_id`, or carries a `run_id` outside the FR-250 grammar; an invalid run id is refused before any directory or file is created and is never echoed. Content beyond that envelope MUST NOT be a policy refusal. A failed append MUST be reported without changing any gate, landing, merge, approval, launch or run-operation decision; recorded closure is descriptive and does not seal the file against later facts.
- **FR-252** (MUST): Readers MUST preserve unknown and every pre-Revision-22 kind as opaque data and MUST NOT fail a read because a kind, field or legacy lifecycle is unfamiliar. `validate` MUST check only parseable JSON objects whose envelope is either the current `kind` plus `run_id` or the legacy `type` key alone (legacy records carry `run_id` only on `run_started`), accept unknown kinds, and never derive a judgment; `--gates` is retired. No chain verb, approval, merge, landing or run operation may read the journal to determine permission. Optional verdict mining MAY read journal facts as non-authorizing data; drift MUST obey FR-160..FR-166's detached input contract.
- **FR-253** (MUST): The built-in `control` category MUST comprise `forge-project.md`, `.forge-manifest`, `rules/**`, `agents/**`, `system/**`, `hooks/**`, `skills/**`, `.claude-plugin/**`, `.codex/**`, `.claude/settings*.json`, `.github/workflows/**`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/specs/**`, `.forge/evals/tasks/**`, `.refactor/type-baseline.json`, and `scripts/forge/route_config.py` (the committed route defaults and provider sandbox table, which can weaken the review). Project `file-categories` MAY extend this set and MUST NOT remove or narrow any built-in entry. Every control change requires `review-final` and explicit operator approval bound to the reviewed candidate; no other path under `scripts/**`, and no path under `tests/fixtures/**`, is built-in control; they classify as `python`, `bash`, `config` or `docs` by suffix unless a project explicitly extends control to them.
- **FR-254** (MUST): The built-in `review-final-floor` MUST comprise `scripts/**`, `hooks/**`, `tests/fixtures/**` and every control path, including project control extensions. A project extends this floor's review selection and no-skip rule by adding patterns to `trigger-paths` or a `hard` row in `risk-tiers`, while Recorded-baseline integrity under FR-103 applies to the built-in control set and the built-in floor only; a project MUST NOT remove or narrow the floor through `file-categories`, `risk-tiers` or `trigger-paths`. Every candidate touching it is `hard` and MUST receive `review-final` at commit and merge; only its control subset requires approval, so floor membership alone adds no approval wait. The classifier MUST record the matched built-in and project floor rows in commit evidence exactly as FR-151 records matched tier rows, and MUST classify the full new candidate after every restage. `trigger-paths` retains its independent hard-tier effect. This repository's `forge-project.md` and the policy/category/risk/trigger tables shipped and rendered from `system/` MUST implement both sets, remove `scripts/**` and `tests/fixtures/**` from their control row, preserve existing eval and other gate triggers, and never fast-route a floor match; an existing consumer control row listing those paths remains an honored project extension which its operator MAY drop. The gate engine and commit guard remain under binding final review: removing approval wait from engine code MUST NOT route it to the cheap reviewer or weaken another gate.
- **FR-255** (MUST): Fix in chain. After a review BLOCK, `commit restage --paths <path>...` MUST accept the revised file set in the same live chain; the candidate MAY include any safe repository path, and no task, run, scope or earlier path set restricts it. A path is safe when it is repository-relative, lies inside the candidate worktree, is not under `.git`, and does not resolve through a symlink to a location outside the worktree; any other path does not exist for restage: `commit restage` refuses it with exit 1, the existing `path-missing` member and exactly `forge: commit restage refused — unsafe path: <path>`, where `<path>` is the offending path rendered under §9's precedence rule as an escaped single-line JSON string literal when it is a syntactically valid pathspec and the literal `(un-echoed)` otherwise, staging nothing. The engine MUST record in chain evidence the superseded and new candidate identities, the exact tree delta between them (additions, deletions, type or mode changes, and both ends of a rename) and the resulting gate plan. On the new candidate, Gate 1, every `stack:<category>` validation and every candidate-bound mechanical cell (classification, snapshot currentness, secret scan, policy and safety checks) MUST run again with any exemption freshly derived; binding review MUST run again on the complete candidate, told the prior candidate identity and the delta and never inheriting a verdict; a triggered fresh-reviewer eval suite MUST run again under FR-103 with a new request and no manifest reuse. Every other gate (evals, invariants, changelog, assertion sensing) MUST run again when a delta path matches its committed trigger under the old or new policy, when its command, policy, tool or input identity changed, when it has no prior PASS, or when its trigger coverage cannot be proven; a gate that declares no trigger matches every path. A prior PASS carries forward only through a chain event that names its original evidence, the trigger derivation and the delta proof; the original result stays bound to its original candidate and is never relabeled. Any uncertainty reruns or fails closed. A candidate change voids approval, authorization, review dispositions and every candidate-bound `commit skip` record, including a fresh-reviewer-evals skip, and returning to an earlier tree does not restore them; the `review-cap` skip is bound to the chain, not the candidate, and survives restages within it. The eight-round cap counts review rounds for one unresolved review across candidate generations and chains, using the existing review, fresh-eval and merge-attempt accounting. An unresolved review is identified by worktree: on `commit start`, when the most recently created commit chain among those for the same canonical worktree or the same checked-out branch has an effective review round count of at least one (its own completed rounds plus any `inherited_rounds`) and did not land a candidate that received a review PASS, the new chain inherits that chain's effective round count as `inherited_rounds`, records it and the predecessor chain id in its DM-012 `review` object and `chain_started` evidence, and continues the count from there; otherwise the count starts at zero; merge chains are never predecessors, and a candidate predecessor whose state cannot be read or classified counts as eight rounds, so the new chain starts at the cap until the operator resets it. A predecessor that landed a fast-tier candidate without review passes its effective count on like any other predecessor; only a landing whose candidate received a review PASS ends the inheritance. The budget lives in chain state as the existing per-chain iteration counter plus that inherited base, and every iteration-cap refusal uses the existing `iteration-cap` member and reports exactly `forge: review refused — iteration cap reached: <rounds> rounds (<inherited> inherited from <predecessor-chain-id>)`, with `0 inherited from none` when nothing was inherited. Only the operator may reset an inherited budget, through `commit skip review-cap --reason <text>`, an operator-bound verb under FR-217 that records a durable skip on the new chain before its first review request; the skip event names the operator and the reason, and from then on the chain's effective inherited base is zero for every cap decision, for fresh-eval `iteration` numbering and for any later chain's inheritance from this chain, while `inherited_rounds` and `predecessor_chain_id` stay recorded unchanged as history. No model-path verb, abort, or new chain resets a budget. A chain on a different branch in a different worktree is a different review; this limitation is deliberate, and the predecessor id in chain evidence keeps the inheritance visible. At the cap without PASS the workflow records residual risk and escalates without landing. Merge integration and in-lock re-verification keep their own generation rules and cannot use this rule to skip a mandatory in-lock gate.
- **FR-256** (MUST): Existing run directories, journals and activated bindings MUST remain untouched; legacy records are readable data, and obsolete owner, registry, binding, receipt and route-snapshot fields or files MUST neither be consulted for permission nor migrated in place. Chain event logs, verdict files and gate logs remain under `.forge/chains/`; an existing chain's legacy journal-binding fields MUST cease to gate its verbs without changing exact-candidate, recovery, review or approval requirements; legacy chain state MAY retain inert extra journal-binding/outbox keys for replay, while new chains omit those keys; the legacy commit-chain events `journal_receipted` and `abort_disposition_recorded` and legacy payload members such as `journal_batch` and `source_event_digest` replay on a pre-Revision-22 chain as inert, digest-verified history and are never emitted on a new chain. Existing `.forge/history/runs/` files remain readable ordinary history, never rerendered or required; no archive backfill, archive-only chain, audit or learn pipeline runs. A consumer installed at 0.7.1 retains its bytes until a separately reviewed upgrade; after upgrading, FR-253's built-in control set and FR-254's built-in floor apply to the consumer's own paths of those names, and its `scripts/**` and `tests/fixtures/**` become hard with Recorded-baseline integrity where FR-103 applies. Such a consumer preserves project extensions through init, and receives no automatic history rewrite; the next release implementing these contracts is breaking for callers of `validate --gates`, `journal close-preflight`, `run-readmit`, `run-retire`, `journal batch-recover`, `journal ingest-chain`, `chain outbox-drain`, `commit abort-disposition`, `journal verification-add`, `commit start --archive-run-id <run-id>` including its legacy/backfill flag pairs, `worktree-check`, `archive-run.py`, `audit-commitments.py`, `journal-patterns.py`, their interpreter-loaded helper modules `archive_closing.py`, `chain_evidence_codec.py`, `commitment_paths.py`, `learn-proposals.py`, `learn-proposals-locked.py` and `route_provenance.py` (FR-247 provenance; the reviewer-facing trigger rows that name it are updated by its implementing unit under the trigger-table discipline), and `/forge:learn`. Run open/close and report remain available with non-authorizing semantics. Retired options, including the former run-open and run-close judgment, scope, successor, route-snapshot and binding flags, leave the CLI grammar: `cli.py` refuses them through its argument parser with exit 1, `state-precondition` and its existing invalid-invocation diagnostic, `codex_orch_tools.py` refuses its retired run-open and run-close flags through its plain argparse usage error with exit 2 and no reason code, and `commit start` or `merge start` given the still-global `--run-id` or `--task` refuses exactly `forge: <verb> refused — --run-id and --task are not admitted` with `state-precondition`; no retired flag is accepted and ignored.

## 6. Data Model Changes

DM-001..DM-002 retired by Revision 22 (journal gate bindings and gate validation).

**DM-003**: `forge-project.md` (repo root, committed). Contains, in order: a header naming the plugin and install date; a compact DVRR spine (operating model, instruction priority, git policy, untrusted-input rule, risk/authority classes — static text, no regions); pointers to the plugin skills; then exactly sixteen regions in the order below. The region order is fixed so installer migration is deterministic:

| Region | Content | Required for gates |
|---|---|---|
| `project-overview` | 5–15 line project description, tech stack, CI, repository facts | no |
| `file-categories` | one `\| category \| file patterns \|` row per stack + generic `bash`/`docs`/`config`/`control` rows | yes (Gate 2 / commit Step 1) |
| `stack-validations` | per-category executable validation commands | yes (Gate 2 / commit Step 2) |
| `gate1-test-command` | targeted test command + always-run blast-radius suite | yes (Gate 1) |
| `changelog-policy` | changelog gate or the explicit text "No changelog gate is configured for this repository." | no |
| `review-prompt-project-focus` | 3–5 review-focus bullets | no |
| `project-triggers` | 3–8 prose `\| Pattern \| Required Checks \|` rows used only as review-prompt context; mechanically inert | no |
| `completeness-project-items` | 2–4 review-completeness checklist items | no |
| `agent-project-context` | 3–8 lines of per-repo context injected into every Codex agent prompt | no |
| `mutation-testing` | `\| category \| command \| changed-files form \| timeout \|` rows, or one explicit infeasible-stack declaration per stack | no |
| `invariants` | `\| invariant \| check command \| enforcement point \|` rows; enforcement point is `commit`, `merge`, or `hook` | no |
| `risk-tiers` | a `\| tier \| path patterns \|` table, a `\| formatting-only category \|` opt-in table, and the fixed dependency-manifest pathspec block below | no |
| `drift-config` | exactly `cadence: <positive-integer>d`, `retention: forever\|<positive-integer>d` for committed reports, and `event-retention: <positive-integer>d` of at least `366d` for `events.jsonl`; defaults are `14d`, `forever`, and `400d` respectively, with 400 days exceeding four UTC quarters | no |
| `trigger-paths` | zero or more positive repository-relative Git pathspec globs, one per `\| Path pattern \|` row, used only for tiering | no |
| `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` | the fixed ordered `\| control \| path patterns \|` table below, used only to trigger candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation | yes (Gate 2 / commit fresh evaluation) |
| `guard-denied-commands` | an operator-maintained `\| pattern \| reason \|` table of additional parsed direct-invocation prefixes, or the exact filled-empty statement `No additional denied commands configured.` | yes (PreToolUse guard) |

Region markers and the unfilled sentinel use upstream syntax: `<!-- FORGE:REGION <name> BEGIN -->` / `<!-- FORGE:REGION <name> END -->`, with an embedded `<!-- forge-init: ... -->` comment marking a region unfilled. The shipped `gate1-test-command` default body is `echo "forge: Gate 1 test command not configured — run /forge:init before merging" >&2; exit 1`. The `guard-denied-commands` template body carries its project-specific `forge-init:` sentinel followed by `No additional denied commands configured.`; this repository's filled root policy carries the same statement without the sentinel.

A filled `stack-validations` region contains one or more nonempty, NUL-free fenced `bash` or `sh`
cells. Numbered prose, prose lists, and inline-backtick commands remain inert context and never
count as executable cells; the complete contents of each fence are one unchanged `bash -c` argv
element under FR-149. When `/forge:init` fills this region, its stronger output grammar is exactly
one such cell per detected stack category, with every adjacent category label using that
category's exact key from the `file-categories` table; the shared runtime parser deliberately
enforces the executable-fence boundary rather than interpreting those prose labels.

The `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` region is the sole maintained reviewer-facing trigger path list and contains exactly these ordered rows:

| control | path patterns |
|---|---|
| constitution | rules/** |
| agent-prompt-template | agents/**, system/codex/prompts/**, system/claude/prompts/**, .claude/agents/** |
| reviewer-routing | system/codex/agents/**, system/codex/config.toml, .codex/agents/**, .codex/config.toml, skills/orchestrate/SKILL.md, scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/**, scripts/forge/forge_cli/app/**, scripts/forge/route_config.py, scripts/forge/route_config_git.py, scripts/forge/route_config_probe.py, scripts/forge/route_evidence.py, scripts/forge/route_floor.py, scripts/forge/route_provenance.py, scripts/forge/route_vocab.py, system/local/** |
| execpolicy | system/codex/rules/**, .codex/rules/** |
| model-provider-version | docs/specs/forge-plugin-spec.md, agents/**, system/codex/agents/**, .codex/agents/**, skills/orchestrate/SKILL.md, scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/**, scripts/forge/route_config.py, scripts/forge/route_config_git.py, scripts/forge/route_config_probe.py, scripts/forge/route_evidence.py, scripts/forge/route_floor.py, scripts/forge/route_provenance.py, scripts/forge/route_vocab.py |
| commit-review-prompt | skills/commit/SKILL.md |

Each path cell is a comma-separated list of positive repository-relative Git pathspec globs. The complete LF-terminated table above is the current canonical parser constant; authenticated base-policy parsing additionally admits only the released superseded bodies specified below, so every other well-formed substitution or narrowing is malformed. The template and root region carry those same bytes, and `/forge:init` retains an exact match but refreshes any noncanonical current-format body from the canonical template on reinstall. The table is therefore also malformed if it is missing or repeated, its header or row order differs, a control label is unknown or repeated, a path cell is empty, or a pattern is empty, repeated within its row, absolute, negative, magic-prefixed, contains `..` as a path component, or contains NUL, CR, or LF. Repetition of one pattern in different control rows is permitted and every matching `(control, pattern, path)` tuple is evidence. Matching uses only the bytewise-sorted exact path set derived from the DM-012 base-tree/candidate-tree comparison; it never uses caller-supplied targets, `git status`, or working-tree names. The region and its digest are loaded from the chain's authenticated base-policy bytes, so candidate edits cannot narrow their own trigger. For a control-class or review-final-floor candidate, a missing, repeated, malformed, unreadable, or unauthenticatable base region is mandatory fresh-evaluation applicability with an INVALID result, not an empty match set or an unconfigured gate; it takes FR-220's fresh-evaluation exit-2 transport. Only a successfully authenticated and strictly parsed region whose derived match array is empty makes `fresh-reviewer-evals` unconfigured. First-policy bootstrap instead takes FR-083's unconditional fixed-policy path.

The current complete LF-terminated table, SHA-256 `e347704d6b910b38c617b712c2eb14f0c4a512903c1a4cf3a72ecc30db629790`, remains canonical. An authenticated base-policy region is successfully and strictly parsed only when its complete body is byte-for-byte equal either to that current table or to exactly one of these released superseded table bodies: SHA-256 `3d1be7b789a8ee5cc7b5f65ac7f77a5ce3622fe0214a09650c85424c91147d93` for releases 0.6.11–0.6.12, `9ad0623e2eb7c9d56df44a0c57cb4c7c79e30e4322d9bbc0ea2e50b8817c3ce2` for release 0.6.13, or `45e2e69e0067f06f99cb0e3d42f185d3fdc8ee45f6961100eb51f3298c9500c3` for release 0.6.14. The unreleased 2026-09-27 body with SHA-256 `895486c4ef0392b704860dbfc3fe52fdf51583544e354d1375544c2e487bdea1` and every other body remain malformed. For an admitted body, the effective trigger rows are, per control and in current canonical control order, the current canonical row's patterns followed by every pattern present for that control only in the authenticated base body, without duplication; trigger matching and fresh-reviewer-evaluation applicability use those effective rows. The trigger evidence's `region_sha256` remains the SHA-256 of the authenticated base region's own exact bytes, not a digest of the current table or effective rows. No admitted superseded table can therefore narrow current coverage; currently only the 0.6.11–0.6.12 body contributes the base-only `scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine.py` pattern in the `reviewer-routing` and `model-provider-version` rows. A missing, repeated, malformed, unreadable, unauthenticatable, or non-admitted base region retains required fresh-evaluation applicability with FR-220's INVALID exit-2 result. The installed-project upgrade-interval skip remains legitimate only when the authenticated base region is absent or outside the current and admitted released set; an admitted superseded body does not by itself justify that skip. FR-072's reinstall refresh to current canonical bytes remains unchanged. Every future canonical-table change MUST append the exact superseded bytes and their SHA-256 digest to the closed released set in the same transition.

For the current implementation, the `scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/` package (`scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/**` in the fixed table; the `Engine` class in `_engine.py`, the fresh-eval request and evidence helpers in `_fresh_eval.py` and `_fresh_eval_evidence.py`) remains the chain-owned launcher adapter named by the fixed table: it owns gate routing, request-event persistence, launch admission, and terminal step publication. An interpreter-loaded helper may implement bounded collection mechanics but does not replace that adapter or become a second maintained trigger list.

The shipped and rendered `file-categories`, `risk-tiers`, and `trigger-paths` tables MUST implement §5A's narrowed control category and built-in `review-final-floor`. A project MAY extend either protected path set and MUST NOT remove or narrow it through any of those tables; trigger-path matches retain their independent hard-tier effect. An existing consumer row classifying `scripts/**` or `tests/fixtures/**` as `control` remains an honoured project extension until the consumer removes it. The fixed reviewer-facing evaluation table above remains in force, including its historical helper paths.

Inside the `risk-tiers` region, the template MUST ship this plugin-owned block verbatim; `/forge:init` MUST preserve it when filling or refreshing the surrounding region. It is part of committed `forge-project.md`, and therefore control-class. The classifier and guard consume this same committed block under FR-151/FR-154 rather than maintaining separate manifest lists:

```text
<!-- FORGE:DEPENDENCY-MANIFEST-PATHS BEGIN -->
package.json
package-lock.json
yarn.lock
pnpm-lock.yaml
requirements*.txt
pyproject.toml
poetry.lock
uv.lock
Cargo.toml
Cargo.lock
go.mod
go.sum
Gemfile
Gemfile.lock
pom.xml
build.gradle*
composer.json
composer.lock
<!-- FORGE:DEPENDENCY-MANIFEST-PATHS END -->
```

`changelog` is the sole policy-configurable mutating gate; phase 1 has no generic mutating-gate registry, mutability column, or `mutating: true` declaration. The whole stripped `changelog-policy` body means no gate only when it full-matches, case-insensitively and with dot matching newlines, the following grammar:

```regex
No changelog gate (?:is configured|applies)(?: for| to)?(?: this)? .*?repository\.
```

Every other body MUST contain exactly one nonempty, NUL-free fenced `bash` or `sh` cell and at least one declared output. Outputs come from two shipped parser channels. A case-insensitive multiline search contributes only the first matching `Output path:` or `Output paths:` prose line; later matching prose lines are ignored. Separately, every Markdown row whose normalized first logical cell is `output`, `output path`, or `output paths` contributes in document order. The prose-channel tokens are accumulated first, followed by the Markdown-row tokens; values are comma-split, trimmed, and first-occurrence deduplicated. Surrounding backticks are removed at the shipped parser boundary: from each comma token on the first prose line, and from the whole logical Markdown cell before that cell is comma-split. A successful changelog run adds those outputs to the chain path set, stages them explicitly, recomputes the candidate, invalidates downstream evidence, and reruns classification before recording the gate against the new candidate.

**DM-004**: AGENTS.md splice block: `/forge:init` inserts (or refreshes) the full rendered content of `forge-project.md` between `<!-- FORGE:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- FORGE:END -->` markers in the repo's `AGENTS.md`, creating the file when absent. Content outside the markers is never modified. CLAUDE.md receives the line `@forge-project.md` (appended when missing; file created when absent).

**DM-005**: `.forge-manifest` (repo root, committed) — line-oriented keys: `forge_version: 1`, `plugin_ref: <git describe or SHA of the plugin>`, `installed: <YYYY-MM-DD>`, `project_name: <name>`, `default_branch: <branch>`, `init_completed: true|false`, one `region: <name>` line per filled region.

**DM-006**: Content-addressed gate-pass marker
`.forge/tmp/authorized/<authorization-id>`. Every marker is strict ASCII, ends in exactly one LF,
and has exactly one of these three forms:

```text
format: forge-commit-candidate/2
candidate: <64-lowercase-hex authorization-id>
tree: <sha1|sha256>:<full matching tree OID>
authorized-at: <UTC ISO-8601>
```

```text
format: forge-commit-candidate/2
candidate: <64-lowercase-hex authorization-id>
tree: <sha1|sha256>:<full matching tree OID>
authorized-at: <UTC ISO-8601>
skip: user-directed
```

```text
format: forge-commit-candidate/2
candidate: <64-lowercase-hex authorization-id>
tree: <sha1|sha256>:<full matching tree OID>
authorized-at: <UTC ISO-8601>
tier: fast
policy: <full commit OID>
```

The guard requires the filename basename, line-2 ID, and recomputed authorization ID to be equal;
requires the `sha1`/40-hex or `sha256`/64-hex line-3 pairing to match the live index-tree
observation; applies the unchanged 30-minute clock rule to line 4 (with the existing future-skew
refusal); and treats the skip and fast suffixes as mutually exclusive. Missing terminal LF, CRLF,
an extra blank line, reordered or duplicate fields, and every other annotation or value are
malformed. The public denials `marker missing`, `marker malformed`, `marker stale`, `marker hash
mismatch`, `fast-path policy drift`, and `fast-path eligibility drift` remain byte-identical. Old
two-, three-, and four-line v1 markers may be parsed only to perform deterministic stale cleanup;
they never authorize after upgrade, even while unexpired. The operator remediation is to rerun
`/forge:commit`, never to infer which 64-hex representation an old marker intended.

A legacy-skill produced-identity mismatch atomically creates the exact sibling
`.forge/tmp/authorized/<authorization-id>.quarantine` while the commit lock remains held, before
releasing that lock. Its owner-only regular-file bytes are strict ASCII and exactly:

```text
format: forge-commit-candidate-quarantine/1
candidate: <64-lowercase-hex authorization-id>
produced: <full matching Git OID|none>
reason: produced-commit-mismatch
```

`produced: none` means Git returned zero without moving HEAD. Any filesystem entry at the exact
quarantine sibling makes the guard fail closed through the existing `marker malformed` denial,
before either marker or chain fallback can authorize. Thus the original exact v2 marker remains as
mismatch evidence until its timestamp is stale but is not a reusable capability. Stale cleanup
excludes suffix entries from marker enumeration, validates the original marker timestamp through
the shared cleanup parser, then removes the marker first and its sibling second; a missing marker,
fresh timestamp, unsafe entry, or partial cleanup remains fail-closed.

**DM-007**: Repo-local state layout: `.forge/evals/tasks/` contains committed promoted fixtures and `.result` baselines; existing `.forge/evals/candidates/` files remain committed advisory material never consumed by a gate; `.forge/history/` contains committed drift and migration reports under DM-008 and ordinary pre-existing history; and `.forge/tmp/` is gitignored working state for locks, content-addressed markers under `authorized/`, telemetry, the `decisions/` directory, halt audit log, transient drift JSON under `drift/`, and the operator-cleared CRITICAL state file `.forge/tmp/drift-block`. The init gitignore block, guarded by `# --- forge agent system --- #`, contains `/.forge/tmp/`, `/.forge/chains/`, `.worktrees/`, `/AGENT_HALT`, `/AGENT_HALT_*`, and `*.local.md`; re-init adds missing entries. The block MUST NOT ignore `.forge/history/` or `.forge/evals/candidates/`. The drift block persists until an operator clears it and does not alter a clean-tree predicate because it is inside the ignored `.forge/tmp/` directory.

**DM-008**: `.forge/history/` contains committed append-only skill output at `drift/<date>.md` for a drift report and `migrations/<date>.md` for a migration report. `<date>` is a collision-free UTC filename timestamp `YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSSZ`; if that name already exists, the writer appends `-02`, `-03`, and so on before `.md`. Forge automation MUST NOT overwrite, truncate, delete, or amend any existing history file. Already committed run archives under `.forge/history/runs/` and learned material remain ordinary readable history, are never rerendered or required by a control, and receive no new generated entries. Forge skills never prune this directory; `drift-config`'s report `retention` key is the minimum operator retention period and does not authorize automated report deletion.

**DM-009**: `.forge/history/migrations/<date>.md` is the mandatory committed migration report defined by FR-186. It is a DM-008 history file and therefore uses the same collision-free `<date>` rule and no-overwrite rule.

**DM-010**: Stable session identity for lock ownership. `FORGE_SESSION_PID` is an environment contract injected by a long-lived harness for the locks that require session ownership: it MUST name one live same-host owner in the same PID namespace and MUST be inherited unchanged by every fresh tool shell for that session. No `--session-pid` flag exists, and skills MUST NOT export or substitute shell `$$`, `$PPID`, or any transient tool-process PID as the identity. Missing or malformed identity MUST retain exactly `forge: FORGE_SESSION_PID must be exported as a positive base-10 integer`; a syntactically valid PID that is dead or whose same-host liveness cannot be verified MUST refuse exactly `forge: FORGE_SESSION_PID does not name a live same-host session owner` before a lock or marker mutation. The existing lock protocols retain their stale-owner recovery and foreign/live-owner protections. This is a same-user coordination primitive, not authentication; it is not a journal-writer, run-open, or run-close precondition, and no journal owner sidecar is required.

DM-011 retired by Revision 22 (run scope registry and successor reconciliation).

**DM-012**: Commit-chain state (FR-210..FR-224). One materialized-state JSON per chain at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.json` plus an append-only event log at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.events.jsonl`, with per-chain evidence transcripts and review artifacts under `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/`. `.forge/chains/` is gitignored working state under the DM-007 layout via the `/.forge/chains/` entry in the init gitignore block. `chain_id` has the form `c-<UTC compact timestamp>-<4 hex>`; the schema identifier is `forge-chain/1`; `kind` is `commit`. Top-level keys remain exactly `schema`, `chain_id`, `kind`, `state`, `created_at`, `last_event_at`, `inactive_after`, `repo_head`, `policy_source`, `paths`, `staging`, `candidate`, `tier`, `steps`, `review`, `approval`, `authorization`, `commit_result`. A chain created before Revision 22 MAY additionally carry the inert legacy keys `run_binding` and `journal_outbox`; readers MUST accept them on such chains without consulting them, and new chains omit them. From Revision 22 the `review` object additionally carries `inherited_rounds` (a nonnegative integer) and `predecessor_chain_id` (a chain id or null), set once at `commit start` and never changed afterwards; the chain's effective review round count is its own completed iterations plus its effective inherited base, which is `inherited_rounds` unless a durable FR-255 `review-cap` skip is recorded on the chain and zero afterwards; every iteration-cap decision and every successor's inheritance uses that sum, and the `chain_started` event payload carries the same two fields. Before staging, `candidate` is null. An accepted historical v1 candidate has exactly the two keys `sha256` and `computed_at`, no `schema` key, a 64-lowercase-hex stdout-diff digest, and a valid UTC ISO-8601 timestamp. A v2 `candidate` has exactly `schema`, `sha256`, `authorization_id`, `object_format`, `tree_oid`, `base_commit_oid`, `review_diff_sha256`, `review_diff_byte_count`, and `computed_at`. Its `schema` is exactly `forge-commit-candidate/2`; during the compatibility interval `sha256` equals `authorization_id`; `object_format` is `sha1` or `sha256`; `tree_oid` is the corresponding full OID produced by bounded `git write-tree`; and `authorization_id` is exactly `sha256(b"forge-commit-candidate/2\0" + object_format.encode("ascii") + b"\0" + tree_oid.encode("ascii") + b"\n")`. `base_commit_oid` is the full HEAD used to render review evidence (or null for an unborn HEAD); `review_diff_sha256` and its nonnegative byte count identify the exact bounded deterministic tree-to-tree patch and are review evidence, never authorization. The exact key set, plus the v2 `schema` literal, is the version discriminator: a mixed, partial, extra-key, unknown-schema, or otherwise malformed candidate is neither v1 nor v2 and freezes rather than falling back. Every authorization, approval, event binding, and marker uses `authorization_id`; a record about inspected patch bytes additionally uses the review-evidence digest deliberately. Commands write the event first, then the materialized state; on startup a divergence is replayed from events, and an irresolvable divergence is an exit-2 frozen chain. The sole exception to ordinary event-replay reconstruction is the finalize window: recovery in `committing` invokes FR-219's shared raw produced-commit verifier and reuses its durable `commit_identity_checked` result. The events log is append-only; automation never edits, truncates, or replaces it. `user_skip` records inside `steps` are written only by the operator-bound `forge commit skip` verb and carry `{directed_by, reason, argv_digest, journaled_at}`. The FR-255 `review-cap` skip is a `user_skip` record under the step key `review-cap` with the same members, written by the same verb only while the chain has no review request, and it emits the same `user_skip` decision event with `gate: review-cap`.

Candidate snapshot paths are derived only from the immutable base-tree/candidate-tree pair, sorted
bytewise after strict NUL-framed enumeration, and become the single source for `paths`,
`staging.staged_paths`, classification, drift targets, changelog expansion, secret-finding
paths, and review-profile selection. The matching deterministic binary-capable patch is streamed
once to an owner-controlled review artifact while hashing it; enumeration and patch output each
have an independent 16 MiB ceiling and a one-byte overflow probe. Git failure, malformed framing,
invalid OIDs, unmerged-index failure, non-UTF-8 candidate paths, or either overflow refuses
before partial candidate, path, classification, evidence, or authorization state can pass.
Computing `write-tree` may add immutable tree objects to the repository object database but MUST
NOT modify the real index or working tree.

Closed and aborted v1 chains remain readable with their historical stdout-digest meaning and are
never rewritten. For a nonterminal v1 chain outside
`committing`, only `status` and `commit restage` are admitted: restage creates a fresh v2 snapshot
generation and reruns all invalidated classification, gates, review, and approval. For a v1 chain
already in `committing`, recovery first performs one bounded HEAD observation. If HEAD still equals
the recorded pre-commit HEAD, recovery revokes rather than revives the ambiguous v1 authorization,
returns the chain only to the restage-required nonterminal posture, and the next mutating verb must
be `commit restage`. If HEAD changed, the chain cannot be auto-closed: recovery records the bounded
observable legacy HEAD/intent facts and freezes it for explicit operator disposition; the existing
non-Git `commit abort --reason <text>` may seal that historical migration case without calling the
old digest proof a landing or a PASS. No new evidence or finalize operation may advance either
branch before a successful restage. Restage never revives an old verdict or authorization merely
because it recreates an earlier tree OID.

The commit-family event `commit_identity_checked` occurs exactly once per produced commit and carries `result: passed|failed`, expected and observed full tree, parent, and message-digest values, the produced SHA, and a bounded transcript reference. Its PASS MUST be durable before authorization consumption, `commit_produced`, a landing, or chain close. Its FAIL leaves authorization unconsumed, latches the complete mismatch in `commit_result`, retains `state: committing`, and permits no landing or success event. Repeated status, finalize, or recovery reuses that latch and appends neither a duplicate check nor a contradictory result. All evidence remains in the chain; recording it requires no journal transaction or receipt.

The only ordinary `commit abort` admission from `committing` is a state carrying the durable failed `commit_identity_checked` mismatch latch; the preceding historical-v1 migration exception remains available. That abort performs no Git operation, preserves rather than overwrites the complete mismatch `commit_result`, revokes and clears the unconsumed authorization without recording it as consumed, and emits the existing `chain_aborted` event as a non-landing disposition. The failed identity check remains immutable chain evidence.

Candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation uses the existing `steps` object and per-chain artifact directory without adding a top-level chain key. One suite lives at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/fresh-reviewer-evals/iteration-<NN>/<request-id>/`, where `<request-id>` is a newly generated unpredictable 128-bit value rendered as exactly 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters. Before any reviewer child starts, a digest-chained prelaunch event MUST persist that request ID, the iteration, the current v2 authorization ID, object format/tree, base commit, review-diff digest/count, authenticated base-policy SHA and trigger-region digest, the complete staged path and trigger-match sets, and the complete applicable fixture inventory with each fixture-package digest. Replay MUST reject a launch or result not descended from that exact event and current candidate. Each fresh-review fixture retains owner-controlled `prompt.md`, `events.jsonl`, `verdict.txt`, and `completion.json`; the suite retains one canonical LF-terminated `manifest.json` whose schema is `forge-fresh-reviewer-evals/1` and whose exact top-level keys are `schema`, `chain_id`, `request_id`, `requested_at`, `completed_at`, `candidate`, `projection`, `trigger`, `suite`, `results`, and `outcome`. A post-request terminal `fresh-reviewer-evals` step binds the manifest path, byte count, digest, request ID, trigger evidence, and current candidate whenever a manifest was safely published; a post-request INVALID with no safely published manifest records no manifest citation. Every terminal step remains per-chain Gate-2 evidence; it neither projects into the journal nor creates a candidate-promotion path.

Each fresh fixture package digest binds the exact current candidate record, one-time request ID, fixture ID and byte/subject digests, and resolved candidate route. The fixed plugin-owned verdict grammar requires exactly one `VERDICT: PASS|BLOCK`, `authorization_id`, `request_id`, and `fixture_package` binding, followed only by zero or more severity-tagged findings; a missing, duplicate, foreign, or malformed binding is INVALID rather than a mismatch. The mechanical collector reopens every artifact without following symlinks, checks the exact schemas, key sets, UTF-8/JSON grammar, caps, digests, byte counts, timestamps, route, process completion, and verdict binding, re-derives the trigger and complete fixture inventory from the authenticated base and candidate objects, rejects missing or extra results, recomputes candidate authorization, and re-observes the production index before publishing the manifest. Inventory, match, and result arrays are deterministically bytewise sorted by their defined identity fields; duplicate JSON keys, unknown object keys, duplicate array identities, or order drift are INVALID. A published manifest's `outcome` is exactly `PASS` only for a complete matching suite, `BLOCK` only for one or more complete valid mismatches, and `INVALID` for every incomplete or unverifiable post-request case; a failure before a valid request exists uses the pre-request refusal below. The fixed byte maxima are 1,048,576 bytes apiece for fixture and prompt, 8,388,608 bytes (8 MiB) for events, 65,536 bytes apiece for verdict and completion, and 4,194,304 bytes for the manifest. A byte count equal to its maximum is admitted when every other check passes; maximum plus one is INVALID and MUST NOT be truncated into apparent evidence.

Every JSON document named below is DM-013-canonical UTF-8 followed by exactly one LF; its byte count and digest cover that LF. Every embedded object has exactly its listed keys, with no extension keys. The manifest subobjects have these exact key sets:

```text
candidate: schema, authorization_id, object_format, tree_oid, base_commit_oid,
           review_diff_sha256, review_diff_byte_count
projection: schema, excluded_pathspecs, tree_sha256
trigger: policy_sha, region_sha256, paths, matches
trigger.matches[]: control, pattern, path
suite: fixture_root_oid, fixture_root_sha256, inventory
suite.inventory[]: id, path, sha256, agent, expected_verdict, subject_sha256,
                   disposition
results[]: fixture_id, fixture_sha256, request_id, expected_verdict,
           actual_verdict, provider, model, effort, sandbox, argv_sha256,
           argv_byte_count, fixture_package_sha256, prompt_path, prompt_sha256,
           prompt_byte_count, events_path, events_sha256, events_byte_count,
           verdict_path, verdict_sha256, verdict_byte_count, completion_path,
           completion_sha256, completion_byte_count, started_at, completed_at,
           exit_status, timed_out, output_overflow
```

The manifest `schema` is exactly `forge-fresh-reviewer-evals/1`; `chain_id` equals its owning DM-012 chain; `request_id` is exactly 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters from the persisted 128-bit request; `requested_at` equals that request; `completed_at` is a later-or-equal timestamp; `results` is an array; and `outcome` is exactly `PASS`, `BLOCK`, or `INVALID` with the derivation above. The manifest and every cited child artifact MUST remain beneath that exact chain/request directory.

The `candidate` object has schema exactly `forge-commit-candidate/2` and otherwise inherits DM-012's v2 field grammars, including object-format-dependent full OIDs and the nullable unborn-base case; its authorization ID MUST recompute from its object format and full candidate `tree_oid`. The `projection` object has schema exactly `forge-fresh-reviewer-projection/1`; its `excluded_pathspecs` is exactly the ordered array `.forge/evals/tasks/**`, `.forge/evals/candidates/**`, `.forge/history/**`, `docs/**`, `skills/init/SKILL.md`, `system/seeds/eval-tasks/**`, and `tests/**`. Those namespaces contain the promoted/advisory fixture oracles, baselines and manifests, historical result disclosures, documentation mappings, init's seed-verdict mapping, seed copies, and test copies. The projection's `tree_sha256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of `b"forge-fresh-reviewer-projection-tree/1\0"` followed by DM-013 canonical JSON without a trailing LF for the UTF-8-path-byte-sorted included-entry array, whose objects have exactly `mode`, `object_type`, `oid`, and `path` copied from the full candidate tree. Thus the manifest retains full candidate identity and separately binds the exact known-oracle-free view. `trigger.policy_sha` is the authenticated full base commit OID, except for FR-083's explicitly fixed bootstrap identity; `region_sha256` and every field ending `_sha256` are 64 lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 values. `trigger.paths` is the nonempty, duplicate-free UTF-8-byte-sorted DM-012 path array. `trigger.matches` is duplicate-free and UTF-8-byte-sorted by `(control, pattern, path)`; each record names an exact admitted DM-003 row/pattern and a member of `paths`. `suite.fixture_root_oid` is the candidate-tree OID of `.forge/evals/tasks`, and `fixture_root_sha256` hashes the exact raw tree-object bytes. Its nonempty inventory is UTF-8-byte-sorted by `id`; each `id` matches `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}`, `path` is exactly `.forge/evals/tasks/<id>.md`, `sha256` hashes the complete fixture bytes, `subject_sha256` hashes the exact oracle-free subject, `expected_verdict` is `PASS`, `BLOCK`, or `FLAG`, and `disposition` is exactly `fresh-review` or `subject-specific-baseline-only`. A `fresh-review` member MUST expect `PASS` or `BLOCK`; a `FLAG` member can only be subject-specific baseline evidence.

The digest-chained prelaunch event's event-specific payload carries one `request` object with schema `forge-fresh-reviewer-eval-request/1` and exactly these keys:

```text
schema, chain_id, request_id, requested_at, iteration, candidate, paths,
policy_sha, trigger, suite, fixture_packages, artifact_prefix
```

Its `candidate` is the exact current nine-key DM-012 v2 candidate object; the manifest's seven-key `candidate` equals its projection after removing only compatibility alias `sha256` and timestamp `computed_at`. Its `trigger` and `suite` are exactly equal to the corresponding manifest objects; `paths` and `policy_sha` exactly equal their duplicate trigger members; `iteration` is an integer from 1 through 8 counted from the chain's effective inherited base plus one under FR-255 and DM-012, so an inherited budget leaves fewer rounds; and `artifact_prefix` is exactly `fresh-reviewer-evals/iteration-<NN>/<request-id>` with the two-digit iteration spelling. `fixture_packages` is UTF-8-byte-sorted by `fixture_id` and contains exactly one object with exact keys `fixture_id` and `sha256` for every `fresh-review` inventory member and none for a baseline-only member. Each package digest is the lowercase SHA-256 of the DM-013-canonical, no-trailing-LF object with exact keys `schema`, `candidate`, `request_id`, `fixture_id`, `fixture_sha256`, `subject_sha256`, and `route`; `schema` is `forge-fresh-reviewer-fixture-package/1`, its candidate is that seven-key projection, the fixture values equal this request, and `route` has exactly `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `sandbox`, `role_path`, `role_sha256`, and `config_sha256` as resolved from the candidate tree, with `provider` exactly `codex-cli` (a Gate-2-only spelling that readers map to `codex`) and `sandbox` exactly `read-only`. The coordinator MUST derive and persist that complete request, including the suite and packages, before the first child starts; collection and replay independently recompute every member and reject any difference.

Trigger derivation and complete request-plan construction are pre-request preflights, not schema variants. When the authenticated trigger region is missing, repeated, malformed, unreadable, unauthenticatable, or cannot be strictly derived, or when the complete fixture inventory, package plan, or candidate route cannot be constructed and verified, the coordinator MUST NOT invent missing evidence, persist a request ID or request object, append a prelaunch-request event, create a request artifact directory, launch a child, publish a manifest, or record a terminal suite step. It returns FR-220's exact INVALID exit-2 refusal and leaves the required gate pending. Because no suite request occurred, this refusal consumes no shared iteration; after the condition is repaired, the next attempt still receives the same next `N`. Only persisted suite requests enter FR-053's terminal recovery and `N+1` retry laws. Valid zero-match applicability remains unconfigured and records no fresh step.

Each result array is UTF-8-byte-sorted by `fixture_id` and contains exactly one record per `fresh-review` inventory member. Its identity, request, expected verdict, package digest, and route strings equal the request-derived values; `actual_verdict` is `PASS`, `BLOCK`, or null, where null makes the suite INVALID. Every path is the owner-derived repository-relative path to the named file beneath this request and fixture directory. Every byte count is a nonnegative JSON integer, never a Boolean, and equals the complete cited bytes within the fixed cap; every associated digest matches those bytes. `started_at`, `completed_at`, `requested_at`, and the manifest `completed_at` use exact UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, ordered request ≤ start ≤ completion ≤ manifest completion. `exit_status` is a JSON integer and `timed_out` and `output_overflow` are JSON Booleans. A valid judgment additionally requires exit status zero, both Booleans false, a complete valid events stream, a valid completion document, and the fixed native argv; otherwise `actual_verdict` is null and the suite is INVALID rather than BLOCK.

Each `completion.json` has schema `forge-fresh-reviewer-completion/1` and exactly these keys:

