```text
schema, authorization_id, request_id, fixture_package_sha256, argv,
argv_sha256, prompt_sha256, events_sha256, events_byte_count, verdict_sha256,
verdict_byte_count, started_at, completed_at, exit_status, timed_out,
output_overflow, error, reviewer_pid, process_group_id
```

Its duplicated bindings, digests, counts, timestamps, exit status, and Booleans exactly equal the result record and cited bytes. `argv` is the nonempty direct string array from FR-149; `argv_sha256` hashes its DM-013-canonical bytes without an LF and `argv_byte_count` in the result counts those same bytes. `error` is null or a nonempty string; reviewer PID and process-group ID are positive JSON integers or null. A valid judgment requires null `error`, both IDs greater than one, group ID equal to reviewer PID, and a process-group ID not reused by another result. `events.jsonl` is nonempty UTF-8 JSON-object lines, rejects duplicate object keys, NUL, CR, a missing final LF, and any non-object line. After UTF-8 decoding, the verdict grammar rejects NUL and CR, splits only on LF, strips surrounding Unicode whitespace, and ignores empty lines: the first remaining line is exactly `VERDICT: PASS` or `VERDICT: BLOCK`; the remaining lines contain exactly once each `authorization_id: <64-lowercase-hex>`, `request_id: <32-lowercase-hex>`, and `fixture_package: <64-lowercase-hex>` with the current expected values, in any order, and otherwise contain only zero or more lines matching `finding: (CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR) <nonempty-text>`. A PASS with a CRITICAL or MAJOR finding is INVALID; PASS with only MINOR findings and a finding-free BLOCK retain their literal verdicts.

For FR-255 selective reuse, `steps` MAY contain a new-candidate applicability record with the gate id, source candidate and source event/evidence digests, target authorization id and tree, both tree-delta digests and path endpoints, authenticated old/new policy and trigger digests, exact command and dependency-input identities, and the mechanically derived nonintersection/applicability result. It MUST reference an actual retained PASS and contain no substituted execution result. Replay, `verify`, review admission and finalize MUST independently validate that proof against the current candidate before treating the step as satisfied; absent, malformed or unverifiable proof makes the step incomplete. No proof satisfies Gate 1, a candidate-bound safety cell, binding review, approval or a fresh-reviewer manifest.

**DM-013**: `env_fingerprint` — the SHA-256 of a canonical sorted-key JSON record `{command_digest, cwd, platform, policy_digest, python_version, repo_head}` attached to every CLI-recorded gate and scan run. It is an identity-and-context record, NOT an independence proof: two back-to-back CLI runs on the same machine are not two independent environments, and no spec text may claim they are — the claim is "the CLI observed both runs". Gate 1 is recorded once per candidate; the fingerprint binds that single observation to its context and is never compared across candidates or stages. Every evidence record additionally carries the plain-field HEAD observed when it was written, and `commit_result` records head-at-commit, so a chain-evidence reader can see which base each gate ran against.

The canonical `env_fingerprint` preimage above remains unchanged. Every post-request `fresh-reviewer-evals` Gate-2 record names the one-time request ID and binds it to the current authorization ID, base commit, and review-diff digest/count. When a canonical manifest was safely published, the same record additionally names its DM-012 path, byte count, and digest; a post-request INVALID caused before safe publication retains the request/current-candidate binding and bounded diagnostic transcript but carries no manifest citation. A pre-request preflight refusal produces no Gate-2 verification, as DM-012 requires. Those fields establish request and candidate provenance, not a second environment or an independence claim; freshness comes from the persisted one-time request plus a newly launched reviewer session.

DM-013 normalization is exactly:

```text
canonical(x) =
  UTF-8(json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True,
                   separators=(",", ":"),
                   ensure_ascii=False))
```

`canonical(x)` has no trailing LF and no `sha256:` prefix. The two digests are:

```text
command_digest =
  lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(exact subprocess argv array))

preimage = {
  "command_digest": command_digest,
  "cwd": os.path.realpath(repository_root),
  "platform": sys.platform,
  "policy_digest": lowercase_hex_sha256(exact pinned policy bytes),
  "python_version": "<major>.<minor>.<micro>",
  "repo_head": <full current HEAD>
}

env_fingerprint = lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(preimage))
```

No environment mapping, locale, architecture value, or outer Forge invocation contributes. Gate 1 is recorded once per candidate: the newest current-candidate Gate-1 record is the sole Gate-1 evidence (a PASS, or the `docs-class candidate` skip), no fingerprint comparison between Gate-1 records exists, and no record is ever marked `pair_voided`. Historical chains written before Revision 17 may carry `pair_voided` markers and the `gate_1_pair_voided` event with reason `DM-013 env_fingerprint mismatch voided the Gate-1 pair`; readers keep accepting them as history and never emit them.

**DM-014**: Merge-chain state. A merge chain uses the DM-012 chain-ID namespace and storage family — `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.json`, `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.events.jsonl`, and `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/` artifacts — but has the distinct identity:

```text
schema: forge-merge-chain/1
kind: merge
```

Its top-level keys are exactly:

```text
schema, chain_id, kind, state, created_at, last_event_at,
inactive_after, owner, run, repository, worktree, branch, target,
policy_source, candidate, tier, steps, review, approval,
authorization, integration, cleanup
```

`chain_id` retains DM-012's `c-<UTC compact timestamp>-<4 hex>` grammar; `created_at`, `last_event_at`, and `inactive_after` are UTC ISO-8601 strings, with inactivity defaulting to 24 hours after `last_event_at`; `owner` is the initiating session identity; `run` is null on every new merge chain, because no admitted input sets it since Revision 22, and legacy informational data on older chains; it was an optional informational run ID or null and MUST NOT authorize or refuse a chain operation; `repository` is the main-checkout realpath; `worktree` has exactly `path`, `git_dir`, `common_dir`, and `claim`, where the first three are canonical/absolute identities and `claim` has exactly `status`, `path`, `inode`, and `digest` with status `unpublished`, `owned`, `releasing`, or `released`. `released` means no logical owner. A never-published release retains `inode: null` and the intended-record digest; its authoritative ownership intent plus release events prove that no claim was acquired. An acquired release retains its historical inode/digest. Once that chain's `ownership_released` event is authoritative, its terminal projection and status-idempotence never depend on the reusable pathname's later inode/content and a later claimant is never misclassified as divergence in the released predecessor. The deterministic path may be absent, may still name that chain's same recorded inode/content as a non-owning tombstone, or may name a later strict-valid claimant for the identical worktree. A current claimant is authoritative either when its `ownership_claimed` event matches the inode/content or, in the sole publish-before-event crash window, when the inode/content exactly matches its durable `ownership_intent` plus `unpublished` projection. Its immediate predecessor fields MUST name the most recently released claimant and exact `ownership_released` digest. Slot-lineage validation follows those immutable predecessor edges for at most the number of chain records, requires every referenced chain/digest/worktree identity to match, and rejects cycles, missing edges, or forks; such failure freezes the current claimant/admission but never retroactively corrupts an already released predecessor. `branch` is the full local branch ref; and `target` contains exact remote `origin`, destination `refs/heads/<configured-default-branch>`, and the manifest commit used to discover it. `policy_source` contains the committed policy OID and digest. Before the first successful fixed-tip fetch, `candidate` and `tier` are null and `steps`, `review`, `approval`, and `authorization` have their exact empty shapes; this is the sole pre-generation bootstrap state. The remaining objects hold only the state-specific evidence defined by FR-232..FR-237; absent state-specific values are null or empty arrays/objects, never omitted top-level keys.

Each candidate generation binds fixed values, never symbolic refs:

```text
remote = "origin"
destination_ref = "refs/heads/<configured-default-branch>"
remote_tip = <full fetched OID>
candidate_head = <full worktree HEAD OID>
diff_sha256 = SHA-256(exact bytes of git diff <remote-tip>...<candidate-head>)
policy_commit = <full candidate or integrated HEAD OID>
policy_digest = SHA-256(exact committed forge-project.md bytes)
worktree_identity = {path, git_dir, common_dir}
generation = <positive integer>
```

`generation_digest` is `lowercase_hex_sha256(canonical(<the exact nine-field mapping above>))`. The first successful fixed-tip fetch computes and materializes generation 1 as one result; the generation then increments exactly once whenever any of the eight identity fields preceding `generation` changes. Its newly assigned positive integer is included in the nine-field digest and does not recursively trigger another increment. Every gate, review, approval, rebase, push, recovery, and cleanup record carries the generation digest on which it acted. User-facing control approval names `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha>`; the stored approval additionally carries the chain ID and generation digest. When only `remote_tip` changes and FR-235's exact remote-only matrix row proves `candidate_head`, `diff_sha256`, `policy_commit`, `policy_digest`, `worktree_identity`, remote, and destination byte-identical, the implementation MAY retain Gate 3, Gate 4, and control approval only by appending an immutable `generation_carried_forward` event containing both generation digests and that equality proof; records are never rewritten or silently rebound. Any other generation change invalidates them according to FR-235.

The event log retains DM-012's append-only rule and adds, rather than inherits, digest chaining. Every merge event has exactly `schema`, `chain_id`, `sequence`, `at`, `event`, `generation_digest`, `previous_digest`, `payload`, and `digest`; `schema` is `forge-merge-event/1`, `sequence` starts at 1 and increments by one, the first `previous_digest` is 64 ASCII zeroes, every later `previous_digest` equals the immediately preceding event's `digest`, and `digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for the other eight fields. `event` is exactly one of `chain_started`, `ownership_intent`, `ownership_claimed`, `ownership_release_intent`, `ownership_released`, `gate_recorded`, `review_requested`, `review_attached`, `review_disposition`, `approval_recorded`, `generation_refreshed`, `generation_carried_forward`, `epoch_intent`, `fetch_intent`, `fetch_result`, `rebase_intent`, `rebase_conflict`, `rebase_result`, `reverification_result`, `push_intent`, `push_observed`, `cleanup_intent`, `cleanup_result`, `condition_recorded`, `lock_release_result`, `aborted`, or `closed`.

`generation_digest` is normally the current 64-lowercase-hex generation digest. It is null only while `candidate` is null, and in that bootstrap state only `chain_started`, `ownership_intent`, `ownership_claimed`, `fetch_intent`, a failed `fetch_result`, `condition_recorded`, `lock_release_result`, `ownership_release_intent`, `ownership_released`, or `aborted` is legal. The bootstrap `fetch_intent` payload fixes repository/worktree/branch/target, pre-fetch candidate HEAD, policy digest, operation nonce, and attempt number; its event `digest` is the FR-236 fence's `intent_digest`. A successful initial fetch/tip resolution computes the complete candidate tuple before append, and its `fetch_result` carries the new non-null generation-1 digest while atomically materializing that candidate. Every later event carries a non-null digest; a later `fetch_intent` carries the current generation and its successful `fetch_result` carries the retained or successor generation. A state-changing command MUST append and fsync its intent/result event, atomically replace and fsync the materialized state, and fsync the containing directory before reporting persistence; an event/state divergence replays from events unless FR-236 requires observation of Git or the remote.

The complete nine-field generation MUST exist before the successful `fetch_result` is constructed; its `diff_sha256` MUST equal FR-231/FR-236's authenticated `full_patch_output_digest`, and that event atomically materializes the already-complete immutable generation.

Every `epoch_intent.payload.delta.integration.epoch` has exactly `operation_nonce`, `generation_digest`, `intent_digest`, `started_at`, and `gate_plan`, and the materialized `integration.epoch` has that same exact member set. `gate_plan` has exactly `status`, `generation_digest`, `policy_digest`, `suite`, `suite_digest`, `cursor`, and `seal_event_digest`. For `status: "unsealed"`, the other six members are null. For `status: "sealed"`, the generation and policy digests are lowercase SHA-256 values, `suite` is the deterministic ordered array below and MAY be empty, `suite_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for `suite`, `cursor` is the zero-based next unrecorded position in `0..len(suite)`, and `seal_event_digest` is the authenticated event digest that sealed the plan. An empty sealed suite with cursor zero is complete and is distinct from an unsealed plan.

A normal `authorized → rebasing` `epoch_intent` MUST carry the complete unsealed object at that exact payload location; omission is invalid. Its hashed payload carries `integration.epoch.intent_digest: null`, and authenticated replay replaces only that slot with the outer event digest, so the event log rather than materialized state alone commits to the initial plan. The existing `reverification_failed → reverifying` retry transition MUST persist a fresh `epoch_intent` carrying a directly sealed full-suite plan before any child because that generation and policy are already known and no later event may seal that retry plan; its hashed `intent_digest` and `gate_plan.seal_event_digest` slots are null and replay fills both with that event's digest.

Exactly one matrix-selection event may change an epoch plan from unsealed to sealed. The closed sealer set is `fetch_result` when no integration result remains to be learned, including a pure-unchanged empty-suite row; `rebase_result` when it materializes the integrated generation and policy; and `generation_carried_forward` when that event itself materializes the final successor generation before reverification. The sealing event's `payload.delta.integration.epoch.gate_plan` carries the complete sealed object with `seal_event_digest: null`; authenticated replay substitutes only the outer event digest and requires every other payload and projected-state plan member byte-identical. It atomically binds its outer generation digest, the current candidate and committed-policy digests, `integration.epoch.generation_digest`, and the plan's generation and policy digests. No other event may seal or replace the plan, and the epoch's immutable identity remains `intent_digest`.

For a nonempty suite, each member has exactly `kind` and `id`. `kind` is `gate` and `id` is the applicable canonical FR-233 gate ID, or `kind` is `scoped-mutation` and `id` is the exact non-gate literal `scoped-mutation`. The order is Gate 1, scoped mutation, applicable stack gates in bytewise category order, applicable invariant gates in increasing numeric row order, then `assertion-sensor`. Before each child, the CLI re-derives that order from the plan's sealed generation and committed policy and requires byte equality plus the same digest. A gate child requires `status: sealed` and `cursor < len(suite)`; push intent requires `cursor == len(suite)`. Thus an empty sealed plan admits the existing no-suite push path but no gate child or `gate_recorded` event.

The cursor selects exactly one child identity. The corresponding `gate_recorded` payload's step delta adds exactly `gate_plan_position`, `gate_intent_digest`, and `inflight_digest`; `gate_plan_position` has exactly `seal_event_digest`, `suite_digest`, `cursor`, `kind`, and `id`. In that same fsynced event/state transition its integration delta advances cursor from `n` to `n + 1`; no skip, unrelated event, or replay may advance it, and a stale projection replays that event. Transporting a `scoped-mutation` position this way does not make mutation a gate or satisfy a gate criterion. A crash after child authorization but before `gate_recorded` retains one exact outstanding position. A legitimate successor generation or policy learned before sealing selects the successor plan; a gate or scoped mutation that changes the tuple after sealing takes FR-235's ordinary reclassification/parking path and clears the epoch. Neither is `frozen-chain`. A missing or replaced plan member, unauthorized sealer, bad digest/cursor, result for another position, or authenticated-event/materialized-state divergence is exact `frozen-chain`.

`integration` has the exact control keys `condition`, `primary_condition`, `epoch`, `remote_movement_count`, `intent`, `observed`, `pre_rebase`, `conflict`, and `push`; evidence payloads under the last five keys are the exact records required by FR-235/FR-236 and are generation-bound except for DM-014's explicit pre-generation fetch bootstrap. `push` is null before the first push intent and otherwise has exactly `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, `result`, `attempted_heads`, and `landed_head`; its current fields describe the latest attempt, `result` is null until classified and then has DM-017's exact result shape, `attempted_heads` is the nonempty append-only array of every full intended HEAD in `push_intent` event order, including a repeated OID for a deliberate later retry, and `landed_head` is null until observation scans that array in reverse and selects the latest member proved contained by the destination. New attempts replace only the current fields and append exactly one head; immutable events retain every historical intent/result. Containment of the current `intended_head` (the last attempted-head member) establishes completion of the current operation only while that OID also equals the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD. Containment of only an older member sets `landed_head` as partial historical truth that forbids abort or blind retry but does not establish `pushed` for the newer unlanded generation. When partial historical truth has already parked a chain, the later epoch's fresh-attempt identity is the digest of its newly appended `epoch_intent`: that event follows the most recent `push_intent`, is unique to the bounded epoch, and remains the current `integration.epoch` until the epoch appends its own push intent or parks. While that exact event ordering holds, the preserved vector is historical evidence but cannot re-park or complete the not-yet-recorded attempt before its push intent is appended; the final observation instead applies the ordinary unchanged/moved/unavailable pre-intent rows to the current generation. This exception is keyed to the durable epoch identity, not OID inequality, so a legitimate no-op integration that again yields the last attempted OID may append one deliberate repeated head for the new epoch. `observed` is null before remote observation and otherwise has exactly `exists`, `oid`, `contains_intended_head`, `attempted_head_containment`, `observed_at`, `inflight_digest`, and `output_digest`. `attempted_head_containment` has one member per `push.attempted_heads` entry in the same order, each with exactly `head` and `contained`. An authoritative present destination uses `exists: true`, a full `oid`, and boolean current plus per-attempt containment; an authoritative absent destination uses `exists: false`, `oid: null`, current containment false, and false for every attempt; failed/unavailable observation uses null for `exists`, `oid`, current containment, and every per-attempt `contained`. `condition` is exactly one of `none`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `remote-moved`, `remote-churn`, `push-failed`, `non-fast-forward`, `push-outcome-unknown`, `lock-release-failed`, or `foreign-git-state`. Its retained state and admitted response are closed:

| `integration.condition` | Retained state | Admitted response beyond `status` |
|---|---|---|
| `none` | the FR-232 state | only the ordinary FR-232 rows |
| `fetch-failed` | `classifying` when start/refresh fetch failed; `authorized` when finalize/recover/final-tip resolution failed | From `classifying`, `merge refresh`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort; from `authorized`, `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `rebase-failed` | `revising` | `merge refresh` below the cap, or safe abort |
| `remote-moved` | `authorized` | `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `remote-churn` | `awaiting_approval` | exact operator `merge approve`, or safe abort |
| `push-failed` | `pushing` | observation-first bare `merge recover`, or FR-232's exact fresh-noncontainment/no-live-process safe abort whose first step is that same authoritative observation |
| `non-fast-forward` | `authorized` | `merge finalize`, bare `merge recover`, or safe abort |
| `push-outcome-unknown` | `pushing` | observation-only bare `merge recover` until truth is known, or FR-232's exact fresh-noncontainment/no-live-process safe abort after that observation establishes not-landed truth |
| `lock-release-failed` | the durable primary state, including `closed` | bare `merge recover` may prove/retry release only; it never repeats the primary side effect |
| `foreign-git-state` | the state in which it was observed | bare `merge recover` may re-observe only; no mutation occurs until the state matches a different exact FR-236 predicate |

For `lock-release-failed`, `primary_condition` preserves the condition that existed before release failed; otherwise it is `none`. `cleanup.condition` is exactly `none` or `cleanup-failed`; the latter retains `cleanup_pending` and admits only `status` or `merge cleanup`. The v1-only `inactive-chain` and `frozen-chain` preconditions are never broadened for these merge conditions. The closed state enum remains the exact FR-232 enum. The chain file, append-only events, and evidence are retained under `.forge/chains/` after terminal cleanup.

**DM-015**: History-mutation activation adds exactly one anchored line to the committed plugin-schema `.forge-manifest`:

```text
history_mutation_mode: legacy-v1
```

or:

```text
history_mutation_mode: forge-verbs-v1
```

The line's exact anchor is immediately after the single `init_completed: true|false` line and before every `region:` line. On a plugin-schema manifest, no such line means `legacy-v1`; exactly one correctly anchored line with either value is valid; duplicate, empty, misplaced, malformed, or unknown values are invalid and fail closed for raw `git commit` and raw `git push`. Upstream-schema and non-Forge repositories remain under their existing inert/halt-only law. The parser reads strict UTF-8 only from `git show HEAD:.forge-manifest` in each matched Git segment's invoking repository context; working-tree edits and staged deletion cannot change the result. A fresh init writes `legacy-v1`; re-init inserts `legacy-v1` at the exact anchor when the field is absent and preserves either valid existing value byte-for-byte. Init/re-init MUST NOT silently change `legacy-v1` to `forge-verbs-v1`. Activation changes both raw-commit and raw-push treatment atomically and is itself a reviewed, operator-approved control change after the phase-3/4 checks exist.

An invalid mode admits exactly one history-producing repair path: candidate-bound `forge commit finalize` after the normal control review and approval, and only when the staged path set is exactly `.forge-manifest`. Its candidate bytes MUST equal this canonical transform of `HEAD:.forge-manifest`: preserve strict-UTF-8 LF-terminated bytes for every line except remove every line whose first colon-delimited field, after ASCII space/tab trimming, equals `history_mutation_mode`, then insert exactly one valid DM-015 line at the anchor; every other byte and line order is identical. `forge merge finalize`, history-producing `forge push`, raw Git, and any finalize with another staged path or byte change remain denied with FR-239's invalid-mode literal. A strict-valid standalone singleton at exactly `recovery_required` with `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, null `lock_release`, and no live or unresolved fence/process admits this repair-only finalize as the sole exception to FR-238's nonterminal-singleton interlock: finalize revalidates that exact tuple under the commit lock, changes no singleton byte, and commits only the approved canonical repair; every adjacent singleton tuple remains blocked.

The invalid-mode hook also admits `forge push` when and only when `.forge/tmp/push-state.json` is a strict-valid nonterminal singleton, solely in **invalid-mode recovery-only** mode. That mode may prove recorded process-group death, finish exact ordered lock release, perform identity-bound observation/classification of an already-durable fetch/rebase/abort/push/remote-observation intent or result, perform the authoritative remote observation (including its bounded exact-ref object fetch) required to settle an existing push intent, and abort only an exact owned rebase to prove restoration. It MUST NOT start an integration-target fetch, rebase, continue, new attempt, retry, push intent, or push, and MUST NOT replace the singleton; every permitted child is bound to the existing attempt nonce/rebase/push/fence identity. If recovery proves a valid-mode pre-operation HEAD restored and no admitted Git rebase or abort child run by that invocation returned non-PASS, it persists that result and stops with exit-0 `ok` partial recovery and exact next `forge push`. A non-PASS owned-abort child retains the ordinary FR-238 result precedence even when restoration is later proved: exact remaining owned conflict retains and emits `rebase-conflict`; proven restoration records `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt` and emits `rebase-failed`; unmatched attribution records its observation-only condition but likewise emits `rebase-failed` for the actual failure. Recovery-only then stops without starting new work. If an invalid current HEAD remains after all already-started process/result/remote/lock truth is settled, it parks at unwrapped `recovery_required/activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, preserves every prior push/landing fact, clears `attempt_nonce`, stops with FR-239's exact invalid-mode diagnostic and byte-exact `state-precondition`, and enables only the canonical repair finalize above. Existing current-head landed truth may close without another push when locked HEAD is that current intended head; every unresolved condition retains its ordinary exact recovery reason/action. An absent, `closed`, malformed, unreadable, symlinked, identity-mismatched, or side-effect-requesting tuple receives the ordinary invalid-mode denial or exact `frozen-chain` as applicable before mutation. This recovery-only admission produces no history; non-history Forge inspection and chain-preparation verbs remain available but cannot produce history.

Every existing phase-0 v1 fixture, result, evidence record, corpus, and manifest is immutable and MUST retain these recorded bytes:

Together their commands pin first/middle/last compound positions, global options before/between/after the verb, both singleton-option forms, env/quote/interpreter/script-path variants, all three sanctioned producers, all six HEAD activation contexts, ignored working-tree drift, quoted raw text, and appended raw Git. The referenced v1 cases retain their original four-field layout and expectations unchanged.

Generation 1 has `phase: "phase-3"` and `previous_manifest_sha256: null`; generation 2 has `phase: "phase-4"` and `previous_manifest_sha256` equal to the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete generation-1 manifest bytes. `artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted six-member array for the other six DM-016 v2 inventory files, each object having exactly `path` and lowercase-hex `sha256`; the manifest never hashes itself. `v1_artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted three-member array for the reason corpus, hook corpus, and phase-0 manifest named by the recorded hashes below, again with exactly `path` and `sha256`. `subjects` has exactly `production` and `tests`, each a nonempty, unique, path-bytewise-sorted array of repository-relative implementation or test paths.

`result_bindings` is the slot-bytewise-sorted eight-member array for exactly `activated-denial`, `cleanup`, `merge-last-line`, `merge-transition`, `push`, `push-last-line`, `re-verification-matrix`, and `recovery`. Each object has exactly `slot`, `status`, `result_id`, and `result_sha256`. `status` is `PASS` or the exact normative PENDING marker `pending-phase-4`. `PASS` requires a nonempty result ID and the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete mechanically resolved result evidence; the manifest validator MUST resolve that ID, verify those bytes and their PASS outcome, and verify that the result binds the manifested subject candidate. `pending-phase-4` requires both result fields to be null and is permitted in a generation-1 phase-3 manifest only for `push`, `activated-denial`, and `push-last-line`; every other generation-1 slot is a resolved `PASS`. Recording a fictional PASS is forbidden. A pending marker is not PASS evidence and cannot satisfy phase-4 activation or any FR-242 predicate.

No other post-phase-3 replacement is authorized, except that a generation-1 manifest whose subject bytes change is re-minted in place with its result bindings, result fixtures and byte pin, under binding review and operator approval, exactly as every landing since the phase-3 shipment has done; the re-mint keeps generation 1, its pending slots and its predecessor-free form, records no fictional PASS, and does not touch any other DM-016 artefact (Revision 22 records this standing practice). A generation/phase mismatch, bad predecessor digest, missing/extra/duplicate slot, unresolved or digest-mismatched PASS, pending status in another slot, or pending status surviving generation 2 fails closed and authorizes neither phase-3 shipment nor phase-4 activation.

**DM-017**: Standalone push chain state is the singleton `.forge/tmp/push-state.json`, schema `forge-push/1`; it is the standalone-push chain file for the byte-exact `frozen-chain` corruption precondition even though every envelope uses `chain_id: null`. Its top-level keys are exactly `schema`, `state`, `created_at`, `last_event_at`, `repository`, `remote`, `destination_ref`, `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip`, `rebased_head`, `push_intent`, `observed_remote`, and `recovery`. Its state is exactly one of `preparing`, `rebasing`, `pushing`, `closed`, or `recovery_required`. `repository` is the main-checkout realpath; remote is fixed to `origin`; destination is the committed manifest's full default-branch ref; OID fields are full OIDs when present or null only where their exact state-specific grammar permits, including authoritative destination absence.

`push_intent` is null before the first push and otherwise has exactly `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, `result`, `attempts`, and `landed_head`. The first five fields are the latest attempt. `result` is null until its push child is classified and otherwise has exactly `classification`, `exit`, `inflight_digest`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, and `recorded_at`; classification is exactly `success`, `non-fast-forward`, `known-failure`, or `outcome-unknown`, exit is an integer or null, digests are 64 lowercase hex, and the three flags are boolean. `attempts` is a nonempty append-only array in intent order; every member has exactly `attempt_nonce`, `expected_old_tip`, `intended_head`, `destination_ref`, `intended_at`, and `result`. Its nonce is 32 lowercase hex and names exactly one DM-017 ledger member; the other five fields equal the corresponding top-level fields for the last member byte-for-byte. A push-intent append copies the then-active recovery nonce into the new member, atomically clears the active field, sets state `pushing`, and normalizes `recovery.condition/next_action` to `none/none`; each ledger nonce names at most one member. Every push-child authorization, including same-epoch reuse of an existing unlaunched intent, requires a durable `pushing` plus `none/none` state replacement first. Duplicated intended-head OIDs are permitted only when a later bounded epoch deliberately retries that head, but their member nonces remain distinct. Result classification atomically updates both the top-level copy and last member and no later observation overwrites either. `landed_head` is null until observation scans the array in reverse and selects the latest attempted intended HEAD proved contained by the destination. It is completion truth only when it equals the current top-level `intended_head` and that OID is also the fresh attempt's locked `rebased_head`; an older-only value is retained partial-landed truth that forbids abort or blind retry while a new attempt reconciles the current checkout. `push_intent` remains present across `start-new-attempt`; a later push intent appends rather than erases history. During any active attempt, the non-null recovery `attempt_nonce`, its last ledger member, and later its rebase intent are the durable identity; a recovery wrapper may replace the immediate condition without changing that identity. Until the identity joins `attempts` or is definitively abandoned, the preserved attempted-head vector contains only prior attempts and cannot close or re-park it. The identity, rather than OID inequality or immediate source condition, controls the exception: successful no-op integration may produce the same OID as the preserved latest member and still append exactly one deliberate repeated member for this new bounded attempt. `observed_remote` is null before observation and otherwise has exactly `exists`, `oid`, `contains_intended_head`, `attempted_head_containment`, `observed_at`, `inflight_digest`, and `output_digest`. `attempted_head_containment` has one member for each distinct attempted intended HEAD in first-appearance order, with exactly `head` and `contained`. An authoritative present destination uses `exists: true`, a full `oid`, and boolean current plus per-attempt containment; a successful query proving the destination ref absent uses `exists: false`, `oid: null`, current containment false, and false for every attempted head; failed/unavailable observation uses null for those three scalar result fields and every per-attempt `contained`. Thus absence is distinguishable from observation failure, a later observation cannot erase any attempt/result, current-attempt containment establishes close truth, and older-only containment takes precedence over retry or abort without falsely completing the newer attempt.

`recovery` has exactly `condition`, `next_action`, `primary_state`, `primary_condition`, `primary_outcome`, `attempt_nonce`, `attempt_history`, `rebase`, `process`, and `lock_release`. `condition` is exactly one of `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-conflict`, `rebase-failed`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved`, `push-failed`, `push-observation-pending`, `push-old-tip-retry`, `non-fast-forward`, `remote-moved`, `push-outcome-unknown`, `lock-release-failed`, or `foreign-process-state`. `next_action` is exactly one of `none`, `start-new-attempt`, `observe-rebase`, `abort-owned-rebase`, `observe-remote`, `retry-push`, `recover-lock-release`, or `operator-quiescence`. The ordinary condition/action pairs are respectively `none/none`, `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, `fetch-failed/start-new-attempt`, `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase`, `push-failed/observe-remote`, `push-observation-pending/observe-remote`, `push-old-tip-retry/retry-push`, `non-fast-forward/observe-remote|start-new-attempt`, `remote-moved/start-new-attempt`, `push-outcome-unknown/observe-remote`, `lock-release-failed/recover-lock-release`, and `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence`; every other pair is invalid. `rebase-lock-unavailable` is an envelope-only acquisition refusal: FR-238 preserves an absent or prior singleton byte-for-byte, so neither that spelling nor `acquire-lock` is ever valid in persisted DM-017 state; either on disk makes the singleton schema-invalid exact `frozen-chain`. `activation-policy-invalid` is the no-history terminal-for-this-attempt condition selected when the final intended HEAD fails DM-015 immediately before push intent, or when invalid-mode recovery-only has settled every already-started process/result/remote/lock fact and an invalid current HEAD remains with no live or unresolved child. In the latter case every prior push intent/result and any partial/current `landed_head` remain immutable; the condition authorizes no retry or push. After an exact approved repair changes HEAD, the next invocation starts a wholly new attempt and never reuses the old rebase/push intent. `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase` is the exact recoverable standalone conflict waypoint: the next `forge push` may run only the owned abort, prove restoration, and park for a later fresh attempt; an abort failure outside that recoverable state uses `rebase-failed`. `push-observation-pending` is the exact post-intent state for a retained successful result or no durable result before authoritative remote observation; a retained known failure, non-fast-forward, or outcome-unknown result instead uses its same-named observation action. No refusal is emitted for an internal pending waypoint; authoritative observation first selects the next condition/output. `push-old-tip-retry` is legal after any retained push-result classification or no result when an authoritative reconciliation observation has just proven the exact old tip still current and intended HEAD absent; the result is preserved. “This epoch made no push attempt” means start-pipe proof establishes that no child received authorization for the current durable push intent. That just-completed observation is also the mandatory immediate pre-push recheck; the existing unlaunched current intent authorizes the epoch's sole push after identity revalidation, and no duplicate attempt member or second intent is appended. Once a push child received authorization, the epoch counts as attempted even if no result survived; it MUST release the lock and defer any retry to exact next `forge push`, whose newly acquired epoch appends the deliberate duplicate attempt. A retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery.

`attempt_history` is an append-only array for the lifetime of this singleton transaction and is empty before its first attempt. Every member has exactly `nonce`, `source_condition`, `base_condition`, `started_at`, `prior_push_attempt_count`, and `prior_intended_head`; `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex, `source_condition` is exactly `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, `fetch-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `push-old-tip-retry`, `non-fast-forward`, or `remote-moved`, `base_condition` is exactly `none`, `activation-policy-invalid`, or `remote-moved`, and `started_at` is UTC ISO-8601. `source_condition` is the condition immediately before attempt admission, with `none` used for an absent/closed singleton or `none/none` recovery waypoint. `base_condition` is the admitted lineage condition after that transition: it is `activation-policy-invalid` for the originating exact approved-repair admission and every exact no-side-effect retry descended from that active ledger identity, `remote-moved` for the originating exact partial-historical admission and every exact no-side-effect retry descended from that active ledger identity, and `none` for every other admission. Its exact current pair is respectively `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, `remote-moved/start-new-attempt`, or `none/none`; a descended retry copies the predecessor member's special `base_condition` and cannot invent or change that lineage. `prior_push_attempt_count` is a nonnegative integer equal to zero when `push_intent` is null and otherwise equal to the then-current `push_intent.attempts` length; `prior_intended_head` is null exactly when that count is zero and otherwise equals the then-current top-level intended HEAD. Immediately before atomically materializing `preparing`, every new attempt draws a nonce absent from every member of this transaction's ledger, appends/fsyncs its complete member, and sets `attempt_nonce` to that nonce with the same state replacement; a later closed-singleton replacement begins a new transaction and a new ledger. The exact approved-activation-repair and partial-historical lineages retain the pair derived from `base_condition` through `preparing`, `rebasing`, and every exact no-side-effect retry as an audit waypoint, while every other new-attempt admission atomically normalizes the current pair to `none/none`; all paths normalize to `none/none` before push authorization. A later observation-only wrapper may replace the pair, but the immutable base condition remains the sole value restored when that wrapper is exactly resolved. This occurs before fetch intent/fence publication, so even a no-side-effect release failure has a durable attempt identity and an exact current condition. A later-epoch old-tip retry that will append a deliberate duplicate push-intent member instead appends/fsyncs a fresh ledger member with `source_condition: push-old-tip-retry` and `base_condition: none` in the same transaction that sets it active and appends that member; the same-epoch retry of an already-recorded unlaunched intent creates neither ledger nor intent duplicate. Every `push_intent.attempts` member names its originating ledger nonce, each ledger nonce binds at most one such member, and its ledger `prior_push_attempt_count` equals that member's zero-based index. On successful fetch, `rebase.operation_nonce` MUST equal the active nonce and its intent digest binds it normally.

`attempt_nonce` is non-null from `preparing` admission until the attempt either appends its push intent or is definitively abandoned, and transiently during the atomic later-epoch retry append defined above. While non-null, the last ledger member MUST match it, its `prior_push_attempt_count` MUST equal the current attempts length (zero when `push_intent` is null), and no existing attempts member may name it. It remains unchanged through `preparing`, `rebasing`, exact owned conflict, and any `foreign-process-state`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved`, or lock-release waypoint still resolving that fetch/rebase/abort identity; once a rebase intent exists for that same ledger identity, its operation nonce MUST also match while the attempt is active. The atomic push-only retry transaction exposes no intermediate on-disk active-nonce state and reuses, without rewriting, the earlier integration's rebase record. This complete active-attempt predicate—not `source_condition`, current condition, or OID inequality—is the historical-vector exception: every existing attempt belongs to an earlier identity, so its containment can be retained but cannot classify, close, or re-park the active attempt. Atomic push-intent append copies the nonce into the new attempts member and clears the active field. Exact fetch failure, final-prepush movement/failure, activation-policy-invalid sink, successful/no-side-effect restoration, or another exact row that abandons the attempt also clears it; the no-side-effect retry row appends a new ledger member and replaces it with that fresh nonce, so the source-independent predicate survives every retry. The ledger itself is never cleared or rewritten before closed-singleton replacement. A missing, duplicate-within-ledger, replaced, prematurely cleared, already-member-bound, or mismatched nonce/ledger/rebase binding is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`; release recovery restores the same active nonce and never creates another attempt.

`primary_state`, `primary_condition`, and `primary_outcome` are null unless `condition == lock-release-failed`. Otherwise they are derived from already-durable ordinary-row or child-result evidence, never independently selected, and are exactly one of these triples: `(preparing, none, no-side-effect)`; `(preparing, activation-policy-invalid|remote-moved, no-side-effect)` only with the exact non-null fresh `attempt_nonce`; `(rebasing, none, none|no-side-effect|rebased)` while a durable fetch/rebase result still awaits ordinary classification; `(rebasing, activation-policy-invalid|remote-moved, none|no-side-effect|rebased)` only with that same fresh `attempt_nonce` and, once rebase intent exists, matching `rebase.operation_nonce`; `(pushing, none, none|push-not-attempted|pushed)` while a durable push result still awaits observation, after a successful pre-push observation whose intent has not executed, or after current containment with a refused close tuple; `(recovery_required, activation-policy-invalid, no-side-effect|rebased|push-not-attempted|push-not-landed|pushed)`; `(recovery_required, fetch-failed, no-side-effect|rebased)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-conflict, none)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-failed, no-side-effect)`; `(recovery_required, rebase-attribution-unresolved, none)`; `(recovery_required, none, no-side-effect|pushed)` after proved owned-abort restoration or current containment with a refused close tuple; `(recovery_required, push-failed|push-observation-pending|push-outcome-unknown|foreign-process-state, none)`; `(recovery_required, push-old-tip-retry, push-not-attempted|push-not-landed)`; `(recovery_required, non-fast-forward, none|push-not-landed)`; `(recovery_required, remote-moved, no-side-effect|rebased|push-not-attempted|push-not-landed)`; or `(closed, none, closed)`. The three push-derived `activation-policy-invalid` outcomes are legal only when invalid-mode recovery-only has classified a pre-existing durable push intent without starting a new integration, rebase, continue, retry, push-intent, or push child; the exact identity-bound remote-observation child permitted by DM-015 is part of that classification and is expressly allowed. `push-not-attempted` binds no-exec proof, `push-not-landed` binds the authoritative all-false attempted-head vector after an attempt, and `pushed` binds authoritative current-head containment retained because the locked HEAD/clean close tuple was not admitted. Here `none` means a durable child result awaits classification or only observation truth is known, `no-side-effect` is an exact no-effect/restoration predicate, `rebased` is the rewritten integrated result, `push-not-attempted` requires durable push intent plus no-exec proof, `push-not-landed` requires authoritative current-head noncontainment after an attempt, `pushed` requires authoritative current-head containment, and `closed` is the completed close row. `rebase-lock-unavailable` and every unlisted triple are invalid; an unlisted on-disk tuple makes the singleton schema-invalid exact `frozen-chain`. Release recovery restores the exact state/condition, `attempt_nonce`, and append-only attempt ledger, clears all three primary fields, and applies only its ordinary classification/observation transition without creating another attempt, regenerating its nonce, or replaying the recorded child or classified primary side effect.

`lock_release` is null unless `condition == lock-release-failed`; otherwise it has exactly `pending_layers`, `commit_lock`, `common_lock`, and `recorded_at`. `pending_layers` is the nonempty ordered array `["commit-lock", "common-lock"]` or `["common-lock"]`. `commit_lock` has exactly `path`, `device`, `inode`, `digest`, `owner_pid`, and `released`; `common_lock` has exactly `intent_path`, `device`, `inode`, `digest`, and `released`. Paths are canonical absolute paths, device/inode/owner values are nonnegative integers, digests are lowercase SHA-256, and each `released` is boolean. The commit-lock object preserves the last validated acquired identity even after its release; `pending_layers` equals exactly the objects whose `released` value is false and preserves inner-before-outer order. A standalone push defines the acquired FR-219 commit lock as the inner layer of its complete rebase transaction lock: failure to release either layer therefore satisfies the unchanged v2 precondition “Rebase lock could not be explicitly released after the primary outcome was recorded.” Recovery retains the common portable owner while it uses FR-219's serialized ownership/stale-owner protocol to prove and release the exact commit-lock layer first, updates and fsyncs `lock_release`, and only then releases the common layer. A foreign/live/replaced identity is never removed; the record and outer owner remain fail-closed for a later recovery. When both releases are proven, recovery clears `lock_release` with the primary fields and restores the ordinary primary tuple. Outside `lock-release-failed`, any non-null `lock_release` is schema-invalid.

`rebase` is null before rebase intent and otherwise has exactly `operation_nonce`, `action_prefix`, `intent_digest`, `branch_reflog_baseline`, and `head_reflog_baseline`. Each baseline has exactly `path`, `exists`, `size`, and `digest`: `path` is the canonical contained location resolved by `git rev-parse --git-path logs/<full-ref-or-HEAD>`; when `exists` is true it MUST be a nonsymlink regular file, `size` is its nonnegative exact byte length, and `digest` is lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes; when false, `lstat` at that contained path MUST return absence, `size` is 0, and `digest` is null. The operation nonce permanently equals the originating ledger member's nonce and, only while that attempt remains active, also equals the current already-fsynced `attempt_nonce`; clearing or replacing the active field never rewrites an extant rebase record. The action prefix is exactly `forge-push-rebase:<operation-nonce>:<pre-operation-head>:<fetched-tip>`, and the intent digest is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for the exact mapping `{repository, destination_ref, pre_operation_head, fetched_tip, operation_nonce, branch_reflog_baseline, head_reflog_baseline}`.

`process` is null before any fenced child, provisional stale-lock recovery, abort attempt, current attribution, or retained attribution history exists and otherwise has exactly `lock_owner_digest`, `lock_owner_host`, `lock_owner_pid`, `lock_owner_dead_at`, `inflight_digest`, `inflight_operation`, `inflight_intent_digest`, `host`, `pgid`, `group_dead_at`, `results`, `abort_attempts`, `attribution_history`, `attribution`, and `quarantine`. The four `lock_owner_*` fields are all null except during FR-235 provisional recovery, when they bind the strict-valid stale physical owner and its proved death. `inflight_digest`, `inflight_operation`, `inflight_intent_digest`, `host`, and `pgid` are all null until a fence is selected and otherwise bind that exact fence: the operation is exactly `fetch`, `tip-resolution`, `remote-observation`, `rebase`, `abort`, `push`, or `attribution-observation`, the two digests are 64 lowercase hex, and `group_dead_at` remains null until that selected group is proved dead. These are the complete standalone operation literals; `gate`, `continue`, `containment`, `worktree-remove`, `branch-delete`, or any other operation in a standalone process/fence/result is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`. After a parent crash without a durable result, the exclusive recovery owner MUST persist and fsync all six selected-fence identity/death fields before unlinking the fence; they cannot be cleared or replaced until the exact operation-and-intent-specific immutable result or recovery condition is durable. `results` is the empty array before the first completed/failed child observation and otherwise is append-only in fence-publication order. Every member has exactly `operation`, `intent_digest`, `inflight_digest`, `exit`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, and `recorded_at`; `operation` is one of that exact seven-member standalone set, both digests are 64 lowercase hex, `exit` is an integer or null, the three flags are boolean, and `recorded_at` is UTC ISO-8601. The result is appended and fsynced before its fence may be removed, is never overwritten by a later result/observation, and is selected for recovery only by its exact operation, intent digest, and fence digest. Push classification is additionally copied into the immutable `push_intent.result` member required above.

`abort_attempts` is empty before the first owned abort and otherwise is append-only in attempted order. Every member has exactly `abort_nonce`, `attempt_nonce`, `rebase_intent_digest`, `intent_digest`, `started_at`, and `no_exec_at`. Both nonces are 32 lowercase hex; `abort_nonce` is fresh from 128 operating-system-RNG bits and unique in this singleton transaction, `attempt_nonce` names the originating DM-017 attempt-history member, `rebase_intent_digest` copies that attempt's then-durable rebase intent, `started_at` is UTC ISO-8601, and `no_exec_at` is null unless start-pipe/fence proof establishes that no child was authorized, when it is the UTC proof time and never changes again. For a non-null active `attempt_nonce`, its current abort member is exactly the last array member whose `attempt_nonce` equals that active nonce, or is absent when no such member exists. Only that member may control current abort classification, attribution selection, envelope output, or admission of a later abort; every earlier member, whether for the same or another attempt, is immutable history and MUST NOT control those decisions. Before every owned abort fence, including a retry after an exact remaining conflict, the CLI appends and fsyncs a fresh member whose `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-push-abort-intent/1", repository, destination_ref, attempt_nonce, rebase_intent_digest, abort_nonce}`; that digest alone authorizes the fence/result and the append makes this member current. A crash after this append but before fence publication is settled before any later child: under both complete locks, the next invocation MUST verify the canonical fence is absent, no matching result, selected-fence/history, or quarantine member exists, the singleton's selected-fence fields do not name this intent, and replay contains no sanctioned fence removal. FR-236's fsynced-publication-before-authorization invariant then proves that no start byte could have been sent; the invocation atomically sets and fsyncs this current member's `no_exec_at` before Git/reflog classification. If any part is ambiguous, this settlement is not admitted and ordinary fence/foreign-state recovery remains fail-closed. Another abort member is forbidden until the current member is settled by a unique matching immutable result, a unique selected-fence attribution-history member after proved group death/quarantine, or non-null `no_exec_at`, and no live/unresolved fence remains. Exact conflict may then admit a fresh nonce; nonce reuse, a result/fence bound to the wrong member, multiple results for one member, out-of-order authorization, or an unproved unresulted predecessor is exact `frozen-chain`.

`attribution_history` is empty before the first attribution wrapper and otherwise is an append-only array whose members have exactly `attempt_nonce`, `operation_nonce`, `operation`, `source`, `intent_digest`, and `inflight_digest`. Both nonces are 32 lowercase hex and bind immutable provenance: for `rebase`, both equal the originating attempt-history nonce and the intent digest is the rebase-intent snapshot validated when the member is first appended; for `abort`, `attempt_nonce` names that originating ledger member, `operation_nonce` equals its unique `abort_attempts.abort_nonce`, and the intent digest equals that abort member. The retained member remains authoritative after the replaceable singular `rebase` field is cleared; validation thereafter uses its immutable nonce/ledger or abort-attempt binding, not equality to a later current rebase record. Operation is exactly `rebase` or `abort`; source is exactly `selected-fence`, `result`, or `pre-exec`; selected-fence/result copies the intent and inflight digests byte-for-byte from that exact evidence and requires a non-null 64-lowercase-hex inflight digest. `pre-exec` requires null inflight digest plus durable no-publication/no-authorization proof: for rebase it binds the then-current rebase-intent snapshot, while for abort it binds the exact abort member and requires that member's non-null `no_exec_at`. Materialization is deterministic for the exact nonce-bound operation identity: a unique matching immutable result MUST select `result`; only its absence permits the exact selected original-operation fence to select `selected-fence`; only absence of both permits byte-for-byte reuse of the unique matching history member; and only absence of all three plus the applicable durable no-exec proof permits `pre-exec`. Any ambiguity, precedence violation, duplicate member, or conflicting member for one nonce-bound operation identity is exact `frozen-chain`. A newly selected object is appended/fsynced exactly once in the same state replacement that first creates its wrapper; reuse never appends a duplicate, and history is never cleared before closed-singleton replacement.

`attribution` is null outside the enumerated wrapper tuples below and otherwise MUST be non-null and byte-for-byte equal to the deterministic six-key history member. The mandatory tuples are `condition == rebase-attribution-unresolved`; `condition == foreign-process-state` with selected `inflight_operation == attribution-observation` and exact matching observation-intent digest; and `condition == lock-release-failed` with `primary_condition == rebase-attribution-unresolved`, or with `primary_condition == foreign-process-state` plus that exact selected attribution-observation identity. Current attribution remains unchanged while that later observation fence runs, through its foreign-process or exact release wrapper, and until an operation-context row resolves the restored wrapper; clearing it never clears history, so a later unmatched state deterministically rematerializes the same source identity. A later remote- or attribution-observation result therefore cannot erase a nonzero/timeout/launch result for standalone rebase or abort, and FR-238's success/conflict/failure predicates always inspect the immutable result bound to the operation intent plus the exact attribution identity.

`quarantine` is the empty array before operator-reserved recovery and otherwise is a nonempty append-only array in completed recovery-stage order. Every member has exactly `kind`, `manifest_digest`, `artifacts`, and `quarantined_at`; `kind` is `portable-owner`, `inflight-fence`, or `recovery-reservation`, `quarantined_at` is UTC ISO-8601, and `artifacts` is a nonempty source-path-sorted array whose members have exactly `source_path`, `quarantine_path`, `file_type`, `device`, `inode`, `mode`, `size`, and `content_digest`. Each member is one top-level node actually renamed; nested directory entries are bound by its directory digest rather than duplicated as artifacts. The paths are absolute and contained in the recorded common directory; `file_type` is `regular`, `directory`, or `symlink`; and device/inode/mode/size are nonnegative integers. For a regular file or symlink, `content_digest` is lowercase SHA-256 of file bytes or symlink-target bytes. For a directory it is SHA-256 of the DM-013-canonical source-name-sorted nonrecursive entry array whose members have exactly `name`, `file_type`, `device`, `inode`, `mode`, `size`, and `content_digest`; entry `file_type` is `regular`, `directory`, `symlink`, or `other`, and its content digest is the regular-byte/symlink-target digest or null for directory/other. `manifest_digest` avoids a self-referential quarantine name: it is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical `{kind, artifacts}`, where each artifacts member is projected to the same exact fields except `quarantine_path`; each actual quarantine path is exactly its `source_path` with `.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` appended to that source basename in the same directory. Distinct sources therefore cannot collide. Under engaged halt the operator re-proves repository-wide quiescence and revalidates source identity plus destination absence immediately before each same-directory rename; an existing destination or any source change stops, so the protocol does not depend on a nonportable no-replace directory-rename primitive. Each successfully fsynced stage appends one member without overwriting earlier owner, reservation, or fence manifests, so a standalone recovery can bind sequential multi-kind quarantine. A persisted owner/group-death, quarantine, or child-result observation binds the exact physical-owner and in-flight identities and retains the timeout/output-limit/launch disposition. Before each fetch, rebase, push, or recovery side effect the CLI atomically writes and fsyncs the corresponding intent state and its directory. A nonterminal singleton is recovered by observation before a new operation can start; a prior `closed` record may be atomically replaced only after the next invocation acquires the complete common lock, re-reads the singleton, and passes preflight again. Malformed, unreadable, symlinked, or schema-invalid singleton state is exact corrupt-chain-file `frozen-chain`, not `state-precondition`, and is never overwritten automatically.

Revision-13 amendment to **DM-016**: hook-argv authority gains a third additive generation with an explicit, bounded supersession channel. `system/fr223/hook-argv-cases-v3.json` has exactly the top-level members `schema`, `v2`, `supersedes`, `case_count`, and `cases`. `schema` is `fr223-hook-argv/3`. `v2` has exactly `path`, `schema`, `sha256`, and `case_count` with values `system/fr223/hook-argv-cases-v2.json`, `fr223-hook-argv/2`, `310bfda5efdbfe3c99a1d189c8ff336782f90a79af741c4098ada4ae579bde27`, and `18`; the 18 v2 cases and, through v2, the 112 v1 cases are consumed by reference and MUST NOT be copied into `cases`. `supersedes` is an id-bytewise-sorted array whose members each have exactly `id`, `generation`, `expect`, and `reason`: `id` names exactly one referenced v1 or v2 case, `generation` names that case's schema, `expect` is the replacement expectation, and `reason` is the exact emitted denial literal when the replacement is a denial. A supersession changes only the named case's expectation; the referenced artifact bytes, the case's command, and every other referenced case stay byte-identical, and a later generation MAY supersede a supersession only by naming the same id again. Revision 13's sole member supersedes `no-match-bare-assignment-without-env` (`fr223-hook-argv/1`) to `deny-approve` with the exact `commit approve` operator-verb literal. The additive `case_count` is exactly `12`, the effective count is 142, and every additive case has exactly `id`, `command`, `activation`, `expect`, and `reason` with the v2 field grammar, `expect` drawn from the union of the v1 and v2 enums, and ids that collide with no referenced case. The twelve ids are `deny-<verb>-bare-<prefix>` for `<verb>` in `approve`, `merge-approve`, `skip` and `<prefix>` in `assignment-then-env-assignment`, `multiple-assignments`, `quoted-assignment-space`, `single-assignment`. The v3 eval fixture `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-hook-argv-matcher-v3.md` plants an `env`-only prefix skip and its `.result` baseline BLOCK MUST be recorded from a fresh `review-cheap` execution with the oracle section withheld; `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-hook-argv-v3.manifest.json` (`fr223-hook-argv-manifest/3`, members exactly `schema`, `artifacts`, `clauses`) pins the corpus, fixture, and result digests under one `hook-argv-supersession-v3` clause. Tests that replay referenced cases MUST apply the supersession map before asserting; the guard consumes v3 through its tests and evals only, since the supersession restores a code-level literal already pinned by FR-221 and introduces no runtime corpus read.

Revision-9 amendment to **DM-016**: Revision-9 reason-code authority is another additive, immutable corpus generation; it does not edit or remint either v1 or v2. Its exact inventory is:

```text
system/fr223/reason-codes-v3.json
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v3.md
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v3.result
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-archive-fidelity-v3.manifest.json
```

The corpus schema is exactly `fr223-reason-codes/3`. It contains the complete sorted 53-member `forge-cli/2` union: every 41-member v2 `{code, exit_class, precondition}` row byte-identical and the twelve §8 Revision-9 additions. Tests consume, rather than copy, v1 and v2, prove their existing artifacts and rows unchanged, and then prove the v3 union. The v3 `.manifest.json` is only an immutable eval-corpus inventory under the existing DM-016 discipline; it is not a run-close, archive-authentication, or legacy-recovery manifest. Fixture-family immutability applies once these v3 baselines land; a future change requires another additive generation.

Revision-10 amendment to **DM-016**: the new `run-scope-exceeded` reason requires this exact additive v4 inventory; it edits or remints no v1, v2, or v3 artifact:

```text
system/fr223/reason-codes-v4.json
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.md
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.result
.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v4.manifest.json
```

The corpus schema is exactly `fr223-reason-codes/4`, with exactly `schema` and `codes`; `codes` is the complete bytewise-code-sorted 54-member `forge-cli/2` union, every v3 row byte-identical plus only the §8 Revision-10 row. The manifest schema is exactly `fr223-reason-code-enum-manifest/4`, with exactly `schema`, `artifacts`, `predecessors`, and `result_bindings`. `artifacts` is the path-bytewise-sorted three-member array for the other three v4 files, each object having exactly `path` and lowercase-hex `sha256`; the manifest never hashes itself. `predecessors` is ordered by `reason_generation` 1, 2, 3, and each object has exactly `reason_generation`, `member_count`, `corpus_path`, `corpus_schema`, `corpus_sha256`, `manifest_path`, `manifest_schema`, and `manifest_sha256`. Generation 1 binds 25 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v1.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/1`, corpus hash `3646227d8437789e0407117dc09e00d6116edccb63e89354c746d4b9059c264b`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-phase0-v1.manifest.json`, `fr223-phase0-manifest/1`, and manifest hash `7741b877b1ed45047d680a077c5303b2314cd1f3ef0339821bd7105ac9acd5c9`. Generation 2 binds 41 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v2.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/2`, its hash from the generation-1 phase-3 manifest, that complete `.forge/evals/tasks/fr230-phase3-4-v2.manifest.json` generation-1 digest, and manifest schema `fr230-phase3-4-manifest/2`. Generation 3 binds 53 members, `system/fr223/reason-codes-v3.json`, `fr223-reason-codes/3`, corpus hash `13256ac9c9003205510866bc1fb295d11906dd6747b2ea2b94fb9733d6f5864e`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-archive-fidelity-v3.manifest.json`, `fr223-archive-fidelity-manifest/3`, and manifest hash `6a659fbfcc36480782da00fb1b479fd64be779f51f3945f1aed9309ef77e8b20`.

`result_bindings` is a one-member array whose sole object has exactly `slot`, `status`, `result_id`, and `result_sha256`, whose values are `reason-code-enum-v4`, `BLOCK`, `fr223-reason-code-enum-v4`, and `09a6c264d3c4abdcb411b1659573ef239dad5df6f71760e7f44d8b36d73bf387`, the digest of complete `BLOCK\n` result bytes. Validation resolves the matching prompt/result, requires its `expected_verdict: BLOCK`, and checks the manifested corpus. Phase-3 shipment conjunctively requires a valid generation-1 v2 manifest and this v4 companion whose generation-2 predecessor manifest digest equals those complete v2 bytes; v4 cannot fill or replace a v2 result slot. Phase 4 preserves all four v4 files byte-for-byte, and the generation-2 v2 manifest's `previous_manifest_sha256` MUST equal that same v4-recorded generation-1 digest while satisfying the ordinary no-PENDING proof.

Revision-18 amendment to **DM-016** (bead forge-plugin-diuy, Stage 1.6): the new `execution-result-pending` reason reserves the next additive reason-code generation after v4. Its exact inventory is `system/fr223/reason-codes-v5.json`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v5.md`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v5.result`, and `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v5.manifest.json`. The corpus schema is `fr223-reason-codes/5`; the manifest schema is `fr223-reason-code-enum-manifest/5`; and its predecessor array binds generations 1, 2, 3, and 4 in order under the same immutable predecessor discipline. Generation 4 is therefore a mandatory predecessor, so v5 cannot be minted until the complete v4 artifacts exist. Stage 1 mints neither v4 nor v5 and edits or remints no v1-v4 artifact.

Revision-21 amendment to **DM-016**: the new `lzma-unavailable` reason reserves the next additive reason-code generation after v5. Its exact inventory is `system/fr223/reason-codes-v6.json`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v6.md`, `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v6.result`, and `.forge/evals/tasks/fr223-reason-code-enum-v6.manifest.json`. The corpus schema is `fr223-reason-codes/6`; the manifest schema is `fr223-reason-code-enum-manifest/6`; its predecessor array binds generations 1 through 5 in order under the same immutable predecessor discipline; and no v1-v5 artifact or row is edited or reminted. (Revision 21 authority; deferred to task TA of run-20261004-backfill)

**DM-018**: Route and launch provenance. Every new execution record carries the route actually used for that execution: `role`, `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `sandbox`, `route_source`, and `route_sha256`. Route resolution uses FR-244..FR-246 when the execution is launched; no route is frozen by `run_started`, and a later execution MAY use a newly resolved route. `route_source` is exactly `local`, `committed-default`, or `plugin-default`; readers project historical missing route evidence as `unrecorded` without rewriting it. For one role, `route_sha256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the DM-013 canonical bytes, without a trailing LF, of exactly `{"schema":"forge-route/1","role":<role>,"provider":<provider>,"model":<model>,"effort":<effort>,"route_source":<route_source>}`. `sandbox` equals the actual FR-245 provider profile. A journal writer records these values as data and MUST NOT refuse an append because they differ from configuration or an earlier execution.

`run_started.orchestrator_model`, when recorded, is exactly `{observed: <nonempty-model-id>}` or `{observed: null, reason: <reason>}`, where the best-effort observer uses `var-unset`, `transcript-absent`, or `unreadable`. Valid-JSON non-object transcript records are skipped; a tail with no usable assistant model or an observed string outside the model-id grammar degrades to `{observed: null, reason: unreadable}`. This is transcript evidence only: no declaration is inferred, and absence, unreadability, or divergence never refuses run-open or any journal operation.

Every new-lane review request whose `lane` is `forge-review-lane/1` MUST carry `review.request.route` with exactly `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `route_source`, and `route_sha256` for its selected reviewer role. The request binds the route resolved for that review and the actual reviewer execution; legacy request shapes may omit it. No comparison with a run-open snapshot is permitted. Reviewer distinctness under FR-032 and provider-profile requirements under FR-245 remain binding at review launch and collection.

For a typed launch, wrapper and child identity remain only in the attempt-keyed `identity.json`, and `launch collect` binds that identity to the marker through `attempt`. The owner-only `forge-launch-marker/1` object has exactly `schema`, `run_id`, `task`, `agent`, `execution`, `attempt`, `role`, `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `sandbox`, `route_source`, `route_sha256`, `worktree`, `head`, `plugin_root`, `role_body_path`, `role_body_sha256`, `argv_digest`, `prompt_digest`, `launcher_argv_digest`, `timeout_seconds`, `environment_names`, `omitted_short`, `requested_at`, `collected_at`, and `collected_status`; `attempt` is `attempt-` plus sixteen lowercase hexadecimal characters, the two environment arrays contain sorted names and never values, `requested_at` is a parseable UTC-Z timestamp and never null, and `collected_at` and `collected_status` are initially null and thereafter are either both null or respectively a parseable UTC-Z timestamp and exactly `complete` or `failed`. The launcher MUST publish a marker at `<agent>/<execution>/launch.json` carrying the resolved route and detached launch identity before launch, and the collector MUST validate that marker and attempt identity before claiming successful execution. These launcher and collector checks are not journal-append preconditions; an execution record may cite the marker as an unchecked reference.

---

## 7. Functional Requirements

### Plugin packaging (FR-001..FR-006)

- **FR-001** (MUST): The plugin MUST ship `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` with `name: "forge"` and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` listing the plugin with `source: "./"`, so skills surface as `/forge:init`, `/forge:workflow`, `/forge:orchestrate`, `/forge:report`, `/forge:commit`, `/forge:worktree-merge`, and `/forge:drift`. The plugin MUST also ship `hooks/hooks.json` registering the PreToolUse commit guard, the PostToolUse invariant guard on Edit/Write, the Stop union of `scripts/forge/aggregate-telemetry.sh` plus `scripts/forge/drift-staleness.sh`, and SessionStart `scripts/forge/drift-staleness.sh`; a plugin-load smoke test MUST verify every registered hook is discovered.
- **FR-002** (MUST): The plugin MUST contain exactly seven skill directories under `skills/` (`init`, `workflow`, `orchestrate`, `report`, `commit`, `worktree-merge`, `drift`), each a `SKILL.md` with `name` and `description` frontmatter, and MUST NOT contain a `commands/` directory.
- **FR-003** (MUST): The vendored engine MUST live at upstream-identical relative paths: `scripts/codex_orch_tools.py`, `scripts/codex_orchestrator/{__init__,cli,journal,events,monitor}.py`, `tests/`, `docs/orchestration-contract.md`. Every deliberate in-file change to a vendored file MUST carry an inline `# forge: modified from upstream — <reason>` (Python/shell) or `<!-- forge: modified from upstream — <reason> -->` (markdown) marker.
- **FR-004** (MUST): An `UPSTREAM` file at the plugin root MUST record, for each upstream (nixlim/forge, alexzh3/codex-orchestrator): repository URL, vendored commit SHA, vendoring date, and a list of deliberate deviations (one line each). Every FR in this spec that deviates from an upstream behavior MUST have a corresponding deviation line.
- **FR-005** (MUST): All runtime code MUST run on Python ≥ 3.10 with the standard library only; shell scripts MUST run under bash on macOS (BSD userland) and Linux. `pyproject.toml` remains a dev-tooling-only file (ruff config, no build-system, no dependencies).
- **FR-006** (MUST): Every skill, hook, and agent file MUST reference plugin files only via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`; no absolute paths may appear in any shipped file (upstream forge-init's hard-coded `/Users/...` paths are not carried over).

### Vendored engine behavior (FR-010..FR-015)

- **FR-010** (MUST): The run journal MUST use the plain append-only record contract in FR-250..FR-252. The journal records executions and decisions and MUST NOT supply gate or lifecycle authority.
- **FR-011** (MUST): Journal readers MUST preserve unknown and legacy records as data under FR-252. `validate <run_dir>` MUST check structure only and MUST NOT inspect task completion, routes, scope, ownership, references, gate results, chain state, or close judgment.

- **FR-012** (MUST): The `state` and `monitor` subcommands MUST preserve upstream behavior: state statuses `idle|starting|active|complete|failed|unknown`, exit 2 on low parse confidence with the incompatibility message on stderr; monitor payload types `codex_agent_complete|codex_agent_failed|codex_agent_unknown|codex_agent_stale|monitor_error`, mtime-based staleness (default 600 s), selector exclusivity, and exit-code rules.
  Engine-launched Claude executions with a Claude stream-json events file MUST be summarized into the same monitor payload types under the same mtime-based staleness rule; Claude subagent records without an events file remain silently skipped.
- **FR-013** (MUST): The vendored unittest suite MUST pass via `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` from the plugin root. Doc-contract assertions MUST be migrated, not deleted: each of the 24 upstream assertions is either retained (engine prose unchanged), updated to the merged skill/doc text, or removed with a line in `UPSTREAM` naming the assertion and why.
FR-014 retired by Revision 22 (scope admission).
- **FR-015** (MUST): Run initialization MUST keep the upstream local-exclude protocol: append `/.codex-orchestrator/` to `git rev-parse --git-path info/exclude`, verify with `git check-ignore`, and never edit tracked `.gitignore` for the run root.
FR-016..FR-019 retired by Revision 22 (journal policy enforcement, activation and transactional binding).

### Run-close gate enforcement

FR-020..FR-025 retired by Revision 22 (run-close judgments and gated journal validation).

### Roles, routing, and Codex launches (FR-030..FR-039)

- **FR-030** (MUST): The Claude main session is the orchestrator/verifier and owns worktrees, the gate chain and all reintegration. Launched roles are `implementer`, `review-cheap`, `review-final` and `plan`; each launch resolves provider, model, effort and the per-`(provider, role)` sandbox through FR-244..FR-246 and records the actual values on its execution record. The committed Codex defaults are: implementer (`role: "implementer"`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `ultra`, sandbox `workspace-write`); first-pass reviewer (`role: "review-cheap"`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `high`, sandbox `read-only`); planner (`role: "plan"`, model `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `high`, sandbox `read-only`); Claude subagent `review-final` = binding final reviewer (`role: "review-final"`), and plan is read-only for both providers. Changing a committed model, effort, sandbox default or provider-profile value is a control-class change. A validated developer-local route selection is uncommitted data.
