- **FR-031** (MUST): Implementer executions MUST run in a dedicated git worktree; the implementer MAY commit only inside that worktree (its prompt template states: "You may commit inside this worktree. You must NEVER push, never touch any branch other than your own, and never run destructive git commands."). The orchestrator performs all reintegration.
- **FR-032** (MUST): Reviewer launches MUST use the provider's read-only or instruction-bounded profile under FR-245. The reviewer MUST be a distinct agent from the author. The binding `review-final` role MUST additionally resolve to a different provider or model than the implementer role's resolved route: `review request` for `review-final` refuses a route equal to the implementer route in both provider and model with exit 1, `state-precondition`, and exactly `forge: review request refused — review-final route equals the implementer route`. The committed defaults satisfy this (Codex implementer, Claude `review-final`); `review-cheap` needs only a distinct agent. Its prompt MUST contain the goal, acceptance criteria, constraints, and exactly one immutable target: either an exact full commit SHA for merge or post-commit review, verified as that commit, or a `forge-commit-candidate/2` staged-tree snapshot naming `base_commit_oid`, `tree_oid`, `authorization_id`, and `review_diff_sha256`. For a staged tree the reader MUST independently verify the tree object, authorization ID, and review-diff digest; no commit SHA is required. A fix-in-chain review additionally names the prior reviewed candidate and shows the exact candidate-to-candidate delta under FR-255. The prompt MUST NOT contain the implementer's handoff, claimed test results, earlier review verdicts, or the orchestrator's tentative conclusion.
- **FR-033** (MUST): Implementer sessions MUST NOT be resumed (`codex exec resume` is forbidden for `role: "implementer"`); every implementer task gets a fresh named agent and native session. The sole sanctioned resume is a reviewer confirmation round: same reviewer agent, next `execution-<NN>` directory, `resume` subcommand with the recorded `session_id`, and MUST NOT pass `-C` (the flag is rejected with `resume`; the working directory is inherited from the resumed session).
- **FR-034** (MUST): Every fresh Forge-launched role MUST use the exact provider-specific FR-245 argv template selected from its resolved route; the launcher supplies provider, model, effort, permission profile, sandbox, working directory and capture path. Launches MUST NOT use `--ephemeral` and MUST run detached in their own process group, never as a harness-managed background task. Immediately after launch and before arming the monitor, the launcher MUST write `<execution-dir>/pid` — three lines: PID, PGID, UTC ISO-8601 launch timestamp.

FR-210's subcommands gain the run-scoped, unserialized surfaces `launch --role implementer|plan --task <task-id> --worktree <abs> --brief <abs>`, `launch collect --execution <execution-NN>`, and `launch cancel --execution <execution-NN>`; all three require explicit global `--repo <abs>` and `--run-id <run>`, take the journal append lock only around log writes, and never take the worktree command lock. Before repository discovery, a missing global pair refuses exactly `forge: launch refused — explicit --repo and --run-id are required`, a chain identity refuses exactly `forge: launch refused — --chain-id is not admitted`, an incomplete or conflicting start tuple refuses exactly `forge: launch refused — --role, --task, --worktree and --brief are required, and --execution is not admitted`, and a collect or cancel tuple other than its sole two-digit execution id refuses exactly `forge: launch <collect|cancel> refused — exactly --execution execution-NN is required`.

Before creating any directory, file, or record, start performs in order the halt check; registered-worktree and dedicated linked-worktree check for an implementer; HEAD and committed `init_completed: true` check; canonical-realpath, owner-controlled, no-follow-leaf, regular UTF-8, NUL-free, at-most-1-MiB brief check; route resolution and sandbox selection; committed-prompt-input read; executable lookup; provider version-floor check; and same-worktree in-flight check. It then repeats the mutable in-flight check under the run's FR-251 single-writer append lock (the launch-lane lock), which also serializes execution and attempt id allocation and the `execution_started` append. The exact start-side refusals are `forge: launch refused — worktree is not a registered worktree of this repository: <path>`, `forge: launch refused — implementer worktree must be a dedicated linked worktree: <path>`, `forge: forge initialization incomplete — run /forge:init`, `forge: launch refused — brief path is not canonical; pass its absolute realpath`, `forge: launch refused — opened brief path could not be verified against the checked canonical path`, `forge: launch refused — brief must be a canonical absolute owner-owned regular UTF-8 file writable only by its owner or owner-private group, with no NUL byte and size at most 1 MiB`, `forge: <provider> launch refused — <provider> CLI could not be started`, and `forge: launch refused — execution <execution-NN> is still in flight in <worktree>; run launch collect or launch cancel`; a route-config refusal is carried verbatim.

For this check, the caller path is canonical only when it is absolute and equals its strict filesystem realpath, so any symlink component takes the canonical-path refusal. "Owner-controlled" means a regular file whose `st_uid == os.geteuid()` and whose world-write bit is unset. Group write is admitted only when the file GID is the owner's consistently enumerable primary group, every explicit member name resolves to that same UID, no different UID has that GID as its primary group, and no access ACL may widen writes; an incomplete or inconsistent identity or ACL lookup refuses. Forge opens the canonical leaf with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK`, obtains the opened descriptor's real path through `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` on Linux or `fcntl(F_GETPATH)` on macOS, and requires exact equality with the already-checked canonical caller path before it validates the descriptor with `fstat`, enforces the size bound, or reads bytes. A mismatch, unavailable descriptor-path lookup, unavailable Linux procfs or macOS `F_GETPATH`, or any other platform fails closed with the opened-brief-path refusal. This path binding does not claim an inode identity captured before `open`; same-path replacement remains subject to the opened descriptor's owner, mode, type, and size controls.

The launch version-floor refusal vocabulary is exactly `forge: launch refused — <provider> executable is unavailable: <executable>`, `forge: launch refused — <provider> version probe timed out after 10 s`, `forge: launch refused — <provider> version probe output exceeded 4096 bytes`, `forge: launch refused — <provider> version probe failed with exit <n>`, `forge: launch refused — <provider> version output is unparseable`, and `forge: launch refused — <provider> version <x.y.z> is below required <floor>`.

Under the launch-lane lock the launcher reuses the task's existing `<provider>-<role>-NN` agent or allocates the next free agent id, allocates the next `execution-NN`, and obtains an `attempt-[0-9a-f]{16}` id from the shared lane. It creates the agent directory when absent and the owner-only execution directory exclusively, writes owner-only `prompt.md`, creates an empty owner-only `events.jsonl`, opens the execution directory through its inherited owner-controlled descriptor, builds the wrapper configuration, atomically writes and fsyncs `launch.json`, and only then appends the `execution_started` log record; this is FR-036's required order. The execution record MUST record the selected route and cite the launch marker, prompt, events, and future `handoff.md`. The launch marker and completion artifacts, not journal state, determine launch idempotence and in-flight status. A prelaunch failure removes only artifacts created by that invocation; an ambiguous launch retains its artifacts for collection.

FR-037 prompt assembly is provider-specific but byte-stable. Codex stdin is the plugin-owned `system/codex/prompts/<role>.md`, then `\n--- committed agent-project-context ---\n` and the region read from `<head>:forge-project.md`, then `\n--- task assignment ---\n` and the brief. Claude stdin starts with the exact bytes `\n--- committed agent-project-context ---\n` and carries its plugin-owned role body through the FR-245 flag. A failure to read or parse any required committed or plugin-owned input refuses as `forge: launch refused — committed prompt inputs unavailable at <head>`. The FR-030 inline tuples remain the committed defaults; routing may select another validated provider tuple without rewriting those defaults.

The launch marker uses schema `forge-launch-marker/1` and exactly the keys `schema`, `run_id`, `task`, `agent`, `execution`, `attempt`, `role`, `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `sandbox`, `route_source`, `route_sha256`, `worktree`, `head`, `plugin_root`, `role_body_path`, `role_body_sha256`, `argv_digest`, `prompt_digest`, `launcher_argv_digest`, `timeout_seconds`, `environment_names`, `omitted_short`, `requested_at`, `collected_at`, and `collected_status`. After releasing the launch-lane lock, Forge starts the shared isolated wrapper with cwd exactly `<worktree>`, the FR-245 allowlisted environment after withholding values shorter than four bytes, and exactly `leaves: {prompt: prompt.md, events: events.jsonl, stderr: stderr.log, capture: handoff.md, staging: handoff.staging}`, `events_existing: true`, and `role_body_digest: null`. The wrapper configuration, including timeout and five-second grace, is bound by `launcher_argv_digest`; implementer timeout is 14400 seconds and plan timeout is 1200 seconds. Codex's `<handoff>` slot is the absolute owner-only staging capture; the wrapper redacts it exactly as the review lane redacts verdicts and publishes `handoff.md`, while Claude publishes the final already-redacted `result` text. The legacy `pid` sidecar is atomically written as wrapper PID, wrapper PGID, and UTC launch time on three lines. Launch completions reuse `forge-review-process/2` unchanged: `verdict_digest` and `verdict_size` bind the handoff, and process identity remains in `identity.json`. A successful start returns exact `next_required_step` `forge launch collect --repo <repo> --run-id <run> --execution <execution-NN>`. A spawn error after the record exclusively publishes either `launch-failed: errno <n>` or `launch-failed: spawn error` and clears through the ordinary collect mapping; if another completion won publication, that completion is validated and collected instead.

`launch collect` re-derives the marker path, binds every marker identity, route, worktree, HEAD, argv digest, and recomputed prompt digest to the execution, opens the attempt by descriptor, validates the exact shared completion schema and its binding to `identity.json`, and appends an `execution_finished` record after publishing the terminal completion. Success is `complete` only for return code zero, null error, no timeout, and a nonempty owner-only `handoff.md` whose size and digest match the completion; every other completion is `failed`, including both fixed FR-245 not-logged-in shapes. The summary is exactly `launch collect: <complete|failed>; returncode <n|none>; error <error|none>; timed_out <true|false>; handoff <n> bytes; observed_model <id|none>`. `files_changed` is the sorted union of tracked and untracked worktree paths since the recorded HEAD, and a plan with any such path gains caveat `plan execution changed files`. The two NUL-delimited Git path listings share one 1-MiB output budget. A path that does not decode as UTF-8 refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — worktree paths are not UTF-8 for <execution-NN>; restore changed tracked paths or rename/remove untracked paths, then retry launch collect`; a combined path list beyond that budget refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — worktree path list exceeds 1 MiB for <execution-NN>; reduce changed or untracked paths, then retry launch collect`. Collect atomically sets marker `collected_at` and `collected_status`; a repeat returns the marker's recorded status, and a crash recovery derives the same terminal facts from the completion artifact. Journal availability or contents MUST NOT decide whether collection or cancellation may proceed. A prose execution refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — execution <execution-NN> has no launch_marker; collect a prose launch by prose`; marker divergence refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — launch marker does not bind execution <execution-NN>: <field>`; an invalid attempt record refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — attempt record is invalid for <execution-NN>: <detail>`; completion divergence refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — completion does not bind execution <execution-NN>: <field>`; and unavailable worktree facts refuse exactly `forge: launch collect refused — worktree facts unavailable for <execution-NN>`.

Launch-lane recovery is exhaustive. `Clear` means, in order: exclusively publish the terminal completion only once the wrapper can no longer publish; append a failed `execution_finished` record; then set the marker's `collected_at` and `collected_status`. Before the 60-second identity deadline, no identity and no completion is `launching` and refuses with `forge: launch collect refused — execution <execution-NN> is still launching; retry after the identity deadline`; after it, collect may win the exclusive abandoned claim, publish `abandoned`, write a failed result, and mark collected. If the wrapper wins that identity-claim race, collect re-observes rather than guessing. `running` and `wrapper-alive / child-dead` refuse exactly `forge: launch collect refused — execution <execution-NN> is still running`. `wrapper-dead / child-alive` refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — wrapper-dead / child-alive for <execution-NN>; run launch cancel --repo <repo> --run-id <run> --execution <execution-NN>`. `group-empty`, `identity-mismatch`, changed boot id, or `wrapper-lost` exclusively publishes `wrapper-lost` and clears. Timeout, `events cap`, `stderr cap`, `bad line N`, `claude init mismatch`, `provider exit N`, `claude result error`, `redaction damaged <field>`, `wrapper failure`, `verdict missing`, `verdict empty`, `verdict cap`, `verdict invalid`, `not-logged-in`, `launch-failed: errno <n>`, `launch-failed: spawn error`, `cancelled`, `abandoned`, and `wrapper-lost` all map to a failed result. A retry is a new execution and attempt; the launch lane has no `stale` transition.

`launch cancel` admits only a launch-marked execution with no terminal launch result or completion and a known wrapper identity, and it holds the attempt publication lock from its identity re-read through exclusive completion publication. Its admission refusals are exactly `forge: launch cancel refused — execution <execution-NN> has no launch_marker`, `forge: launch cancel refused — execution <execution-NN> already has a completion or a terminal result; run launch collect`, and `forge: launch cancel refused — execution <execution-NN> has no wrapper identity yet; run launch collect`; failure to acquire the lock refuses exactly `forge: launch cancel refused — attempt publication lock was not acquired for <execution-NN>`. An abandonment-claim identity whose `wrapper_pid` is null calls the shared `claim_abandoned`: a winning claim publishes `abandoned`, while a wrapper that won the identity claim routes cancel to collect. A proven owned group is terminated TERM-then-KILL with the shared bounded grace and publishes `cancelled`; `group-empty`, `identity-mismatch`, changed boot id, or `wrapper-lost` publishes `wrapper-lost`; remaining group members refuse exactly `forge: launch cancel refused — kill-unconfirmed for <execution-NN>: <pids>` without clearing, and any other proof refuses exactly `forge: launch cancel refused — unexpected group proof <outcome> for <execution-NN>`. Immediately before publication cancel re-reads `identity.json`: `attempt`, `wrapper_pid`, `pgid`, and `wrapper_birth` MUST be unchanged, while a change to `reviewer_pid`, `reviewer_birth`, or `started_at` repeats ownership proof against the refreshed identity. If the wrapper publishes a different completion first, cancel validates it and routes to collect. Any of `identity-unproven`, `wrapper-identity-unproven`, or `recorded-identity-unproven` makes both collect and cancel signal nothing, publish nothing, and leave the execution, marker, and attempt byte-unchanged. Collect refuses exactly `forge: launch collect refused — identity-unproven for <execution-NN>; member PIDs <pids>; recorded PGID <pgid>; nothing was signalled`, and cancel refuses exactly `forge: launch cancel refused — identity-unproven for <execution-NN>; member PIDs <pids>; recorded PGID <pgid>; nothing was signalled`. Once those processes exit, a later collect may prove the group empty and clear as `wrapper-lost`. Each execution records the route actually used; no run-open snapshot constrains later executions.
- **FR-035** (MUST): Shell redirect targets in launch commands MUST be literal absolute paths (no `$VAR` in redirect position).
- **FR-036** (MUST): For every execution, the orchestrator MUST create the execution directory, write `prompt.md`, create an empty `events.jsonl`, attempt to append `execution_started`, report any I/O failure without blocking, and then launch. The events file always exists before a log record or monitor references it. Logging follows FR-251 and supplies no launch-admission policy.
- **FR-037** (MUST): Agent prompts MUST be assembled at launch from the plugin role template, the `agent-project-context` region read from `git show HEAD:forge-project.md`, and the task assignment, and saved verbatim to `prompt.md` before the `execution_started` record. They MUST NOT source project context from the working tree or a rendered copy. During first-install bootstrap, when the policy path is absent from HEAD, FR-083 self-review MUST use a fixed plugin-owned bootstrap context and omit the uncommitted region. Handoffs use the upstream six-heading contract unchanged.
- **FR-038** (MUST): The `.codex/` layer installed by init MUST contain: `config.toml` (root `approval_policy = "on-failure"`, `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"`, `[agents]` `max_threads = 6`, `max_depth = 1`, registering `implementer` and `review-cheap`), `agents/implementer.toml` and `agents/review-cheap.toml` (per FR-030 values), `rules/forge.rules`, and `hooks.json` with a Stop hook only (notification + `aggregate-telemetry.sh` to `.forge/tmp/`). The Codex layer MUST NOT register SessionStart; plugin-level Stop/SessionStart ownership is FR-001/FR-093, and the plugin-level PostToolUse invariant hook remains registered in `hooks/hooks.json` per FR-148.

Routing-chain-B amendment to **FR-038**: the installed `.codex/` layer additionally registers `plan` in `config.toml` and contains `agents/plan.toml` plus `prompts/plan.md` at FR-030's committed planner tuple; every original FR-038 value and prohibition remains unchanged.
- **FR-039** (MUST): `rules/forge.rules` MUST carry the four upstream `prefix_rule` deny entries verbatim (force push incl. `--force-with-lease`; `git reset --hard`; `git clean -fd`; `rm -rf` of `.`, `..`, `~`, `/`, `/*`) **plus a forge-added `prefix_rule` denying every `git push` invocation** — under D1 no Codex process ever pushes, so push capability is pure accident/attack surface (deviation from upstream forge recorded in `UPSTREAM`; the Codex sandbox's default network restrictions are defense-in-depth, not the control). Init MUST verify the file with `codex execpolicy check --rules .codex/rules/forge.rules` against both `git push --force` and `git push origin HEAD`, expecting decision `forbidden` for each. Init MUST surface the Codex trust caveat: until the operator trusts the repo in Codex, the entire `.codex/` layer is skipped by Codex.

### Monitoring hardening (FR-040..FR-043)

- **FR-040** (MUST): The orchestrate skill MUST instruct re-arming the monitor at most 60 minutes after its last arm/exit while any execution is in flight (the monitor marks stale/unknown targets done and stops watching them).
- **FR-041** (MUST): On a `codex_agent_stale` notification, the orchestrator MUST treat staleness as ambiguous and, before recording `execution_finished`: check the events file mtime, check process liveness (`ps` against the PID/PGID recorded in `<execution-dir>/pid`), and inspect the handoff and worktree. A conclusion of failure based on staleness alone is prohibited.
- **FR-042** (MUST): On `codex_agent_unknown` (low parse confidence), the orchestrator MUST run `state --dump-event-types`, MUST NOT infer agent status, and MUST surface the incompatibility to the user.
- **FR-043** (SHOULD): After a machine-sleep gap (wall-clock jump exceeding the stale threshold), the orchestrator SHOULD re-check all in-flight targets via `state` before trusting any stale notification emitted across the gap.

### Commit gate chain (FR-050..FR-057)

- **FR-050** (MUST): `/forge:commit` MUST run the 5-step chain in order — (1) classify changed files per the `file-categories` region plus the built-in `control` category and `review-final-floor` and derive the risk tier per FR-150..FR-151; (2) run the targeted `gate1-test-command`, the `stack-validations` commands for every touched category, the applicable invariant checks and assertion-quality sensor per FR-144 and FR-147, plus `run-evals.sh` when any `control` or `review-final-floor` file is touched; (3) apply the `changelog-policy` region; (4) stage the explicit target paths (never `git add .`/`-A`), create the DM-012 v2 snapshot, secret-scan its exact immutable review artifact, then adversarially review that same artifact, except that a mechanically eligible fast-tier candidate skips only the review under FR-152; (5) use exactly three distinct Bash tool calls in order: prepare authorization under the persistent commit lock, invoke one standalone safely quoted `git commit --cleanup=verbatim -m ...`, then perform unconditional cleanup and produced-commit verification. The sole exception is FR-083's first-policy bootstrap, which substitutes FR-149's fixed hard bootstrap checks for policy-dependent Steps 1–3 but retains the snapshot-derived secret scan, review-final, explicit approval, exact four-line v2 reviewed marker, and all three Step-5 boundaries. The chain is fail-closed: any non-skipped step failing before Git records success (including a required review agent being unavailable) means no commit, with the failure surfaced. A produced-identity mismatch after Git success preserves the untouched commit and marker, releases the lock, and freezes per FR-219; a later cleanup failure preserves and reports durable commit truth per FR-055 and never authorizes a retry. The commit lock is NOT held across the Step 4 review loop; its Step-5 epoch begins in the prepare call and ends only after the cleanup call attempts release.
- **FR-051** (MUST): The built-in `control` category is the complete non-narrowable path set in FR-253. Project `file-categories` MAY extend it and MUST NOT remove or narrow any built-in entry. Control-class candidates require `review-final` and explicit operator approval bound to the reviewed candidate after PASS. `scripts/**` and `tests/fixtures/**` are not built-in control paths; they retain suffix-based categories and the independent FR-254 review-final floor. A consumer's existing control row containing either remains an honored project extension until the consumer removes it. Advisory eval candidates under `.forge/evals/candidates/**` do not become control merely by existing; promotion into `.forge/evals/tasks/**` or creation/change of its baseline is control-class.
- **FR-052** (MUST): Except for a candidate mechanically classified `fast` under FR-150..FR-152, Step 4 MUST route `standard` non-control changes to a fresh `review-cheap` execution and `hard` changes, including every control change, `review-final-floor` match, and `trigger-paths` match, to `review-final`. The selected role's provider, model and effort come from its resolved route; the reviewer is always a distinct agent from the author, and `review-final` additionally satisfies FR-032's different-model rule. A fast candidate MUST still be staged explicitly and secret-scanned, but it skips the reviewer only as specified by FR-152. When review is required, the reviewer is always a distinct agent from the author. The review prompt MUST be the upstream mandated prompt with the constitution path re-rooted to `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/rules/review-constitution.md` and the `review-prompt-project-focus` + prose `project-triggers` + `completeness-project-items` region contents spliced in from the committed policy revision of `forge-project.md`; the review input is the exact bounded deterministic binary-capable patch rendered once from the recorded base tree to candidate tree, and the package separately names its `authorization_id`, object format, base commit/tree, candidate tree, review digest, and byte count. Before sending, that same artifact MUST be scanned for secrets; a finding BLOCKS the chain, the affected file is unstaged, and the user is informed. A file is never silently excluded from review while remaining in the candidate tree.

- **FR-053** (MUST): The review loop MUST re-verify affected validations after every fix and before re-review, using FR-255's deterministic invalidation and reuse rules. Its hard cap is eight review rounds for the same unresolved review, carried across candidate generations and never reset by aborting or starting another chain; FR-255 defines the inherited budget and its operator-only reset. Dispositioning any finding above MINOR requires operator approval. At the cap without every required PASS, record outstanding findings and residual risk and escalate; never commit. The fresh-suite accounting below remains part of this shared round budget.
- **FR-054** (MUST): After review PASS, and before creating any marker, the skill MUST validate the inherited DM-010 live session identity. It then uses the shared candidate helper to write the exact four-line DM-006 standard/hard marker at `.forge/tmp/authorized/<authorization-id>`, naming the object format and full candidate tree the reviewer saw through the immutable review artifact. When an eligible fast candidate completes Step 4 without review, the skill instead writes the exact six-line DM-006 form at that path, including `tier: fast` and `policy: <sha>` for the committed policy revision used to classify it. The explicit candidate marker MUST exist before Step 5's prepare call and remain through the standalone commit attempt so the guard can inspect it. Prepare revalidates the same live identity, parses the v2 marker, and re-observes the index tree before Git. Cleanup deletes only that explicit candidate marker after a verified matching commit or an ordinary Git failure that produced no commit; on any produced-object mismatch it releases the lock but retains the marker until expiry. The directory and every other candidate marker remain. Any re-staging after authorization changes the tree observation, requires a fresh snapshot generation, and reruns Step 4 even if it returns to an earlier tree OID. Each authorizer MUST write only its candidate marker; a fresh same-ID quarantine pair MUST refuse authorization, and other candidates' markers MUST remain untouched. A later authorizer for the same authorization ID MUST refuse rather than remove a fresh marker and `.quarantine` pair, and markers or quarantine latches for other candidates MUST NOT be modified. Stale markers, including strictly recognizable v1 forms, MAY be swept by age through the shared cleanup parser; a stale quarantined pair is removed marker-first, while the current candidate MUST be validated first so the unchanged stale denial remains observable.
- **FR-055** (MUST): Step 5 consists of exactly three distinct Bash tool calls. **Prepare** MUST first validate the inherited DM-010 live session PID, use previously observed literal repository/candidate values, run `check-halt.sh commit`, and stop on nonzero; then it acquires `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` via `acquire-commit-lock.sh` with the lock-path argument omitted. This no-argument invocation MUST preserve the existing behavior byte-for-byte and default to `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` (format `<PID> <TIMESTAMP>`, stale-PID takeover, 2 s poll, 300 s timeout, ownership via the stable `FORGE_SESSION_PID`). Under that persistent PID-owned lock it MUST validate the explicit DM-006 marker grammar and TTL through the shared candidate helper, re-observe the exact index tree/authorization ID, require current HEAD to equal the snapshot's recorded review base, and stop if any differs or fast eligibility no longer holds. A later same-tree HEAD is not a new expected parent. The prepare shell MUST arm failure-only release-and-candidate cleanup and disarm it only after all preparation checks succeed. **Commit** MUST set the tool working directory directly to the target repository; its command cell contains exactly one executable segment, `git commit --cleanup=verbatim -m <safely shell-quoted literal>`, with no preceding command, `cd &&`, variable-carried message, command or process substitution, or unsafe option. **Cleanup** MUST run after hook allow or denial and Git success or failure, inspect any produced HEAD through the shared bounded raw-object helper with replacement objects disabled, and require HEAD movement plus exactly the reviewed snapshot base as its one expected parent, exactly one expected tree, and the exact intended message-body digest before calling the commit successful. It always attempts no-argument lock release. It deletes the explicit marker only after a verified success or an ordinary failure with unchanged HEAD; a produced mismatch first atomically writes DM-006's exact sibling quarantine latch while the lock is held, then retains the marker and latch, emits no success event, exits 2, and reports FR-219's exact commit-left-untouched diagnostic. A successful verified commit followed by cleanup failure MUST be reported as commit success together with cleanup failure and MUST NOT be retried. Preparation, commit, and cleanup interruption paths retain the cleanup duty before a later attempt, except that a changed unverifiable HEAD is frozen and never retried. `/forge:worktree-merge` retains its one composite invocation and file-descriptor lock epoch; this three-call split MUST NOT be applied to it. FR-050's review loop remains outside the repo-wide commit lock. The persistent PID-owned lock epoch spans prepare, standalone commit, and cleanup under one unchanged live DM-010 identity; cleanup always ends the lock epoch, even when it retains the marker and latch on mismatch.
- **FR-056** (MUST): User skip directives MUST map exactly: "skip tests"/"skip validation" → Step 2; "skip changelog" → Step 3; "skip review" → Step 4; "just commit"/"skip everything" → Steps 2–4. Every skip is warned about in the reply. These mappings cannot skip mandatory control or review-final-floor review, control approval, or non-skippable evals; FR-217 governs the sole exact fresh-step exception. A skipped Step 4 writes no review-backed marker; the commit-guard hook (FR-090) still requires one, so the skill writes the exact five-line DM-006 form whose final line is `skip: user-directed`, records the skip durably in the chain evidence, and emits the `user_skip` decision event required by FR-157. Its candidate value is the authorization ID; the review-patch digest remains evidence rather than authorization.
- **FR-057** (MUST): Every gate result, review verdict, approval, skip and landing MUST remain in the owning chain's durable event log and evidence under `.forge/chains/`. Journal references are optional narrative data; no journal write, receipt, validation or run state is a chain precondition. Checkpoint commit after every verified task remains mandatory: the orchestrate skill invokes `/forge:commit` for the task's explicit candidate paths.

Step 2's `STRICT=1 run-evals.sh` operation is Recorded-baseline integrity and remains mandatory for every control-class or review-final-floor candidate. In Step 4, after explicit staging creates the DM-012 v2 snapshot and its exact immutable review artifact passes the secrets scan, the chain MUST derive trigger matches solely from that snapshot's exact paths and authenticated base-policy `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` region. A match requires the `fresh-reviewer-evals` Gate-2 step to PASS for that same candidate before the one binding candidate review begins. Neither fast classification nor any later review, approval, CI result, or baseline result skips or satisfies that fresh step.

FR-052 continues to select exactly one binding candidate reviewer for Step 4. The per-fixture reviewer sessions required by FR-103 are separate fresh Gate-2 executions, each distinct from the author, and are not the binding candidate review; their PASS cannot satisfy `review-final`, and a binding candidate PASS cannot satisfy any fixture result.

A fresh suite request is assigned the next shared candidate/review iteration number `N`. Suite PASS holds `N` open for that candidate generation's one binding review—the persisted completed-iteration counter remains `N-1` until Gate 3 uses `N`—so a fresh PASS at `N = 8` still admits that final binding review. Suite BLOCK or INVALID, including crash-orphan terminalization, closes and consumes `N` immediately. A pre-request trigger-derivation refusal is not a suite request, consumes no iteration, and retries at the still-next `N` only after its condition is repaired. A complete valid fresh-suite mismatch or binding-review BLOCK forbids another judgment on unchanged candidate bytes and requires a fix, affected deterministic validation, a new v2 snapshot and one-time request, the complete applicable fresh suite, and the binding review again. Because post-request INVALID contains no usable semantic judgment, an unchanged candidate MAY retry transport only at `N+1`, with a new request ID and an entirely new artifact directory; no artifact or child from the invalid request may be reused or mixed into the retry. Crash recovery first collects or classifies the already persisted request, MUST NOT relaunch an ambiguously started child under that request, and records incomplete recovery as terminal INVALID before any later retry. Iteration eight without every required PASS records residual risk and parks without commit; no ninth request or review is admitted.

No broad skip directive, including "skip tests", "skip validation", "just commit", or "skip everything", covers Recorded-baseline integrity for a control-class or review-final-floor candidate or a triggered `fresh-reviewer-evals` step. Recorded-baseline integrity is non-skippable. The sole fresh-step exception is FR-217's exact operator-issued `commit skip fresh-reviewer-evals --reason <text>`, durably bound to the current chain and candidate; it is never PASS and creates no manifest. A terminal fresh-evaluation result remains candidate-bound chain evidence under DM-012.

### Merge gate chain and reintegration (FR-060..FR-065)

- **FR-060** (MUST): `/forge:worktree-merge` MUST begin with two preconditions: (a) the worktree is clean — `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all` returns empty; a dirty worktree stops the merge (commit the work via the chain, or discard only with explicit user approval); (b) the full merge diff (`git diff origin/<default-branch>...HEAD`) is classified against `file-categories` plus the built-in `control` category and `review-final-floor`. Then Gates 1–4 in order: Gate 1 = the `gate1-test-command` region body; Gate 2 = the `stack-validations` commands for touched categories; Gate 3 = `review-final` over the merge diff with binding PASS/BLOCK; Gate 4 = diff summary — when the diff touches `control` paths, explicit user approval naming the candidate HEAD SHA is REQUIRED before reintegration; otherwise automatic proceed, including a floor-only match. Fail-closed: any gate not returning clean PASS means no merge, worktree left intact.

Gate 3 selects `review-final`; FR-246 supplies provider, model and effort from the resolved route and runs it through engine-owned `review request` plus `review collect`. A new merge review MUST NOT rely on an interactive subagent or `review attach`.
- **FR-061** (MUST): Before Gate 1, the skill MUST verify `git show HEAD:forge-project.md` succeeds and the committed `gate1-test-command`, `stack-validations`, and `file-categories` regions contain no `forge-init:` sentinel; the working-tree file MUST NOT satisfy this precondition. A missing committed file or empty/sentinel-bearing required region fails Gate 1 with the message `forge: <region> not configured — run /forge:init` and exit 1. A present, non-sentinel `stack-validations` body from which the shared policy parser derives no complete fenced `bash` or `sh` shell cell is a distinct malformed-policy case and MUST refuse with exactly ````forge: stack-validations region present but contains no fenced shell cell — write one fenced ```bash or ```sh cell per stack category (see /forge:init)````. The parser MUST retain the old not-configured literal byte-identically for its own empty/sentinel case. Commit, merge, and the defense-in-depth authenticated reparse performed during fresh-reviewer applicability MUST consume this same parser distinction; that fresh-reviewer reparse retains the exact parser literal as its `FreshEvalError.reason` rather than replacing it with the generic malformed-policy reason.
- **FR-062** (MUST): Reintegration MUST run under FR-235's mandatory Git-common-dir portable arbiter, acquired through the long-lived `common-lock hold --owner-kind push --operation push --ready-fd <fd>` wrapper and its readiness/release protocol below. The skill MUST run `check-halt.sh` before acquisition. Inside the lock it MUST fetch, run `git rebase origin/<default-branch>`, perform FR-063's required re-verification, and only then fast-forward `git push origin HEAD:<default-branch>`. Wrapper unavailability or refusal MUST fail loudly without a fallback lock. Merge commits, non-rebase pulls and integration branches are prohibited.
- **FR-063** (MUST): If the rebase incorporated commits beyond the worktree's own (the default branch advanced), Gates 1 and 2 MUST be re-run against the integrated tip **inside the lock, before the push**; if the rebase required conflict resolution, Gate 3 MUST also be re-run on the post-rebase diff (content changed). Any re-run failure aborts with the remote untouched, the lock released, and the worktree intact. A pure fast-forward needs no re-run. (Upstream forge re-verifies only after the push — a deliberate forge-plugin strengthening, recorded in `UPSTREAM`.)
- **FR-064** (MUST): Worktree cleanup MUST happen only after a successful push, using `git worktree remove` **without** `--force` (residual untracked files abort cleanup rather than being destroyed) and deleting the branch only after verifying its tip is contained in the pushed default branch (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`). No failed merge path may delete the worktree or branch.

After a successful push and immediately before cleanup, the workflow MUST fetch the remote default branch and prove the recorded pushed object is contained in it. It MUST freshly prove the named branch tip exists, the worktree remains attached to that branch, its HEAD equals the branch tip, its status is clean including untracked files, and its tip equals the recorded pushed object or is contained in the fetched remote default branch. After non-forced worktree removal and before branch deletion, it MUST re-read the branch tip, require it equal the pre-removal tip, re-prove containment, and delete exactly that object with `git update-ref -d <branch-ref> <verified-old-oid>`; it MUST NOT use `git branch -D`. A pre-removal proof failure preserves both worktree and branch; a later branch-ref proof failure preserves the branch and reports incomplete cleanup. Journal records and archive availability MUST NOT defer cleanup.

- **FR-065** (MUST): The orchestrator MUST re-run Gates 1 and 2 in its own environment (the integration target, not the agent's worktree) before any commit or merge that reintegrates agent work; agent-reported results are never accepted as gate evidence (record-authority rule: handoffs are claims).

An executable policy cell MAY fan its work out over internal parallel worker processes, provided every worker is a descendant of the cell's `bash -c` process (so the runner's isolated process group, kill-on-timeout, and fixed 1200-second bound cover them all), the cell itself fails closed on any worker failure or missing result, and the cell keeps its combined stdout plus stderr within the 65,536-byte cap by emitting bounded per-worker tails. The runner discipline is unchanged: one cell as one argv element, `forge` as `$0`, parameters through `"$@"`, no concatenation or interpolation. This repository's `gate1-test-command` cell applies it: full unittest discovery of `tests/test_*.py` partitioned round-robin over `min(4, cpu)` shards, refusing an empty module set, a nonzero shard, or a shard without a final unittest summary, and printing at most the last 8 KiB of each shard. FR-013's plain `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` remains the vendored-suite contract and must still pass; the sharded cell collects the same modules.

`/forge:worktree-merge` MUST acquire its reintegration lock as FR-235's mandatory Git-common-dir portable arbiter through the Forge CLI's long-lived wrapper `common-lock hold --owner-kind push --operation push --ready-fd <fd>`, invoked with `--json` from the merging worktree as a standalone default-branch push entrant (the legacy skill owns no CLI merge chain, so the `merge` owner kind is not available to it). The skill MUST open two dedicated pipes under `.forge/tmp`, pass the readiness pipe as an inherited descriptor numbered three or higher and the release pipe as the wrapper's stdin, and MUST close the shell's own read-write release-pipe descriptor in the wrapper's process (`9>&-`): a wrapper that inherits any other descriptor on its release pipe holds a writer on that pipe and never observes the end-of-file that completes a release, so the post-push wait and every earlier-exit release would hang with the lock held; because every child the lock-owning shell starts inherits both pipe descriptors, the skill MUST run every in-lock command with `8<&- 9>&-` appended and MUST NOT leave a backgrounded helper running past the release, and its post-push wait for the wrapper MUST be bounded (60 seconds), reporting `lock-release-failed` instead of hanging when the wrapper is still running. The skill MUST start the wrapper before any rebase step and treat only one complete canonical `forge-common-lock-ready/1` record on the readiness descriptor as lock acquisition, waiting in bounded slices that end as soon as the wrapper exits and no later than 330 seconds (the wrapper's 300-second shared deadline plus margin); a refusal, an exhausted deadline, or a holder that exits without a record stops the merge loudly with a holder hint naming `agent-rebase.lockdir` and the wrapper's outcome file, and never falls back to any skill-created lock. Release after the push is exactly the eight-byte stdin frame `release` followed by LF and end-of-file, after which the skill MUST require the wrapper's final `forge-cli/2` outcome to carry `reason_code: "ok"` and the exact message `forge: common rebase lock released`, reporting any other outcome as a failed merge step whose diagnostic carries the literal `lock-release-failed` (retained owner). Exiting the lock-owning shell closes the release pipe, and the wrapper releases and reports the missing frame as a refusal, so every earlier-exit path of FR-063 still releases the lock. A holder killed outright leaves a dead portable owner that later entrants refuse; in this phase no automated recovery surface is live for it, the skill MUST NOT delete owner artifacts, and clearing a dead owner is operator-reserved: the operator proves the recorded host and PID dead, removes exactly `agent-rebase.lockdir` and `agent-rebase.lock.intent`, and records the removal as a decision before any further reintegration. The skill's protocol MUST be exercised by an executable test that runs the skill's exact fenced bytes against the wrapper in a throwaway repository and proves readiness, release, a free namespace, and the earlier-exit release. The portable arbiter is acquired before the optional Python `fcntl.flock` layer and released after it; no skill-created lock namespace or `flock`-binary fallback is admitted. The skill holds the lock under the `push` owner's explicit transaction-record opt-out (`no_transaction_record`), which is accepted for the legacy skill because it lands no push singleton and records its landing as durable reintegration evidence; the CLI push verb MUST still replace that opt-out with its synchronous transaction recorder before its own activation, without changing CLI merge routing.

### Region file and rendering (FR-070..FR-073)

- **FR-070** (MUST): `forge-project.md` MUST follow DM-003: marker syntax, unfilled sentinels, exactly sixteen regions, and fail-closed defaults. The plugin ships the template at `system/template/forge-project.md`. The `@required` inventory in `scripts/forge/install.sh` and the `REGIONS` inventory plus exact-order contract in `tests/test_commit_and_region_template.py` MUST each enumerate exactly these sixteen names in DM-003 order: `project-overview`, `file-categories`, `stack-validations`, `gate1-test-command`, `changelog-policy`, `review-prompt-project-focus`, `project-triggers`, `completeness-project-items`, `agent-project-context`,
  `mutation-testing`, `invariants`, `risk-tiers`, `drift-config`, `trigger-paths`, `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers`, `guard-denied-commands`; no missing, duplicate, re-ordered, or unexpected region is accepted for the current format. The exact fourteen- and fifteen-region predecessor inventories remain readable only for deterministic append migration.
- **FR-071** (MUST): `/forge:init` MUST render the file into both harness surfaces per DM-004: CLAUDE.md `@forge-project.md` import line, and the AGENTS.md `<!-- FORGE:BEGIN/END -->` splice. Re-running init MUST refresh the spliced block from the current `forge-project.md` and never touch content outside the markers.
- **FR-072** (MUST): Re-init MUST preserve filled regions and refresh unfilled ones, using the upstream semantics: a region body containing `forge-init:` loses to the fresh template; a body without it is carried forward byte-identical, with exactly two plugin-owned exceptions. In a filled `risk-tiers` body containing exactly one correctly ordered `FORGE:DEPENDENCY-MANIFEST-PATHS` delimiter pair, init MUST preserve every byte outside that pair and replace the entire delimited block with the fresh template's block. A missing, duplicate, or misordered delimiter MUST stop before writing with exactly `forge: dependency-manifest block malformed — repair forge-project.md`; init MUST NOT guess an insertion point or carry forward an altered fixed block. The complete `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` body is the second exception: every current-format reinstall MUST retain an exact canonical match and replace any noncanonical body with the canonical current-template bytes, including a malformed body or a well-formed narrowing, while structural marker/inventory defects still stop before writing.
- **FR-073** (MUST): The gate skills (`commit`, `worktree-merge`, `workflow`) MUST read gate configuration exclusively from the committed-HEAD revision of `forge-project.md` via `git show HEAD:forge-project.md` (single source; the upstream triplication of `gate1-test-command` is eliminated). They MUST NOT read gate policy from the working-tree file or rendered `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`; the broader execution discipline is FR-149.

### Installer (FR-080..FR-085)

- **FR-080** (MUST): `/forge:init` MUST perform, in order: (0) preconditions — git repo root, prior `.forge-manifest` detection, project name + default branch confirmation (auto-detect via `origin/HEAD`, fall back to `main`), `command -v flock` check, and the provider route/version/model probes below; a rejected route stops init with the provider and model named; (1) mechanical install via `scripts/forge/install.sh` — write `forge-project.md` from template (region-merge on re-init), splice AGENTS.md, write CLAUDE.md import, install `.codex/` (preserving pre-existing non-forge `config.toml`/`hooks.json` as `<file>.forge-new`), append the gitignore block (guarded against double-append), create `.forge/evals/tasks/`, `.forge/history/drift/`, `.forge/history/migrations/`, `.forge/tmp/`, `.forge/tmp/authorized/`, `.forge/tmp/drift/`, and `.forge/tmp/decisions/`, then perform the dcg step in FR-085; (2) brownfield mining; (3) filling all sixteen regions, including mutation feasibility, executable invariants, risk tiers, drift configuration, mechanically validated trigger paths, the fixed reviewer-facing evaluation trigger table, and the project-specific guard denylist; (4) eval baselines; (5) self-review and manifest; (6) present for approval — never auto-commit.
  Before phase (4), and again during phase (5) before freezing the candidate, init MUST apply the same shared `parse_policy` / `_fenced_shell_cells` implementation used at gate time to the complete post-installer candidate bytes without executing any candidate cell. Any parser error stops init and is printed unchanged. In particular, a prose-only `stack-validations` body takes FR-061's exact fenced-cell refusal, while a conforming fenced body passes this self-check; init MUST NOT render a completed manifest or candidate while that error remains.

Once FR-244 and FR-245 are active, the Codex-only model probe above is superseded. Init resolves all four launched-role routes, enforces each selected CLI's FR-245 version floor from parsed version output rather than `--help`, and runs a bounded availability probe through the selected provider profile for every distinct resolved `(provider, model, effort)` tuple. An unavailable CLI, version below its floor, rejected model or effort, or not-logged-in result stops init with the fixed provider-specific FR-245 diagnostic; no route is silently substituted.

Phase 0 step 5 runs these four commands in order from the repository root: `git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --verify --quiet 'HEAD^{commit}' >/dev/null || { echo "forge: init stopped — the current branch has no commit yet; make a first commit, then re-run /forge:init" >&2; exit 1; }`; `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/route_config.py" init --repo "$REPO_ROOT"`; `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/route_floor.py" --repo "$REPO_ROOT"`; and `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/route_config.py" probe --repo "$REPO_ROOT"`. The first command stops an unborn branch before developer-local state is written, with exactly `forge: init stopped — the current branch has no commit yet; make a first commit, then re-run /forge:init`. On re-init, `forge: route init refused — routes file already exists` is the sole expected nonzero init result and processing continues; every other nonzero result stops init unchanged. The floor command checks each distinct provider and uses exactly the `route floor` forms `forge: route floor refused — <provider> executable is unavailable: <executable>`, `forge: route floor refused — <provider> version probe timed out after 10 s`, `forge: route floor refused — <provider> version probe output exceeded 4096 bytes`, `forge: route floor refused — <provider> version probe failed with exit <n>`, `forge: route floor refused — <provider> version output is unparseable`, and `forge: route floor refused — <provider> version <x.y.z> is below required <floor>`. These commands write only developer-local routes, exclude, and transient probe state and therefore do not move FR-080's first target-repository mutation. Rewriting this Phase 0 step does not activate the FR-083 headless-init amendment or the FR-183 init-review amendment: both remain deferred notwithstanding FR-246's earlier “until the chain that rewrites `skills/init/SKILL.md`” phrase.
- **FR-081** (MUST): Brownfield mining MUST follow the seed protocol: CI pipeline definitions are the source of truth for `stack-validations` and `gate1-test-command`; existing linters/formatters are adopted, never replaced; recurring fix/revert descriptions from `git log` feed the prose `project-triggers` review context.
  Only repository-relative paths that mining can mechanically validate as positive Git pathspec globs feed `trigger-paths`; prose rows MUST NOT be copied or mechanically interpreted. A repo whose history shows merge-commit workflow gets the conflict with the linear-history rule surfaced for user decision. The blast-radius suite in `gate1-test-command` MUST be confirmed with the user before the region is marked filled.
- **FR-082** (MUST): After filling regions, init MUST run the assembled Gate 1 and stack-validation commands once in an isolated clean checkout of the same committed HEAD from which `git show HEAD:forge-project.md` supplies them, and require them to pass (a gate failing on untouched committed code is miscalibrated — init stops and reports). On first install, this calibration MUST be deferred until the FR-083 bootstrap commit makes that policy the committed HEAD; no command from the uncommitted file may execute.
- **FR-083** (MUST): Init's own output is a control-class change. When HEAD already contains `forge-project.md`, init MUST write the proposed manifest, run FR-082 plus `STRICT=1 run-evals.sh`, spawn `review-final` over the full install/re-init diff including the manifest, verify `grep -rn "forge-init:" forge-project.md` returns nothing, and present the exact reviewed diff for explicit user approval—never auto-commit. On first install it MUST instead use a two-commit bootstrap: (a) write `.forge-manifest` with `init_completed: false`; run only the fixed bootstrap checks in FR-149, STRICT evals, and review-final over the exact full diff using FR-037's bootstrap context; and, after explicit approval, commit that unchanged reviewed diff through FR-050's bootstrap path; then (b) on the now-clean committed tree run FR-082, propose only the activation changes including `init_completed: true`, run the ordinary control chain and a fresh review-final, and require a second explicit approval before that commit. Workflow and agent launch MUST refuse while committed `init_completed` is not `true`, with `forge: forge initialization incomplete — run /forge:init`. A working-tree-only `true` never activates forge.

Candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation amendment to **FR-083**: an existing-policy install or re-init runs Recorded-baseline integrity and, after its v2 snapshot, runs `fresh-reviewer-evals` whenever the authenticated base-policy trigger matches. The first-policy bootstrap has no authenticated base trigger and therefore treats fresh evaluation as unconditionally applicable: bootstrap commit (a) MUST run both Recorded-baseline integrity and the complete supported fresh-review fixture set before its binding `review-final`. Its trigger decision, reviewer-launch wrapper, route floor, output grammar, and result verifier come only from fixed plugin-owned bootstrap code. Candidate-tree role, constitution, project-context, and fixture bytes may be supplied only as explicitly delimited untrusted subjects under test; they never become bootstrap authority or weaken the fixed controls. Activation commit (b) then follows the ordinary authenticated-base trigger rule.

Revision-15 headless-init amendment to **FR-083**: once FR-246's deferral marker is removed, each existing-policy, bootstrap, and activation binding `review-final` is an engine-launched detached request using the resolved route and completed only through `review collect`. Init MUST NOT spawn a new interactive review-final subagent or attach a verdict to a new request; the sole attach compatibility case is the pre-upgrade request shape admitted by FR-246.

Delivery boundary: this revision specifies the first-policy bootstrap fresh-review requirement but does not implement or authorize its fixed-authority coordinator. Until a separately authorized future revision supplies that coordinator, every bootstrap fresh-review request MUST fail closed before candidate control or reviewer launch with reason `fixed first-policy fresh-evaluation coordinator is unavailable` and, when rendered as gate output, diagnostic `forge: fresh reviewer eval evidence invalid: fixed first-policy fresh-evaluation coordinator is unavailable`; init stops before `review-final`, and no baseline, skip, or alternate launch path may substitute.
- **FR-084** (MUST): Init MUST be idempotent: re-running never overwrites filled-region bytes except for FR-072's delimited plugin-owned dependency block and complete canonical reviewer-trigger region, never overwrites existing eval fixtures or `.result` baselines, and re-splices AGENTS.md rather than duplicating the block.

Project content preservation under DM-003 and FR-038, FR-072, FR-080 and FR-084: `forge-project.md` has exactly one `<!-- FORGE:PROJECT-SPINE BEGIN -->` / `<!-- FORGE:PROJECT-SPINE END -->` pair under the fixed heading `### Project Spine Addenda` at the end of the DVRR spine before `## Plugin Skills`, outside every `FORGE:REGION`; it is not a region and does not change the sixteen-region inventory. Its plugin-owned bounding sentence permits project addenda to add or narrow rules but never to weaken a gate or expand authority. Re-init carries the complete block forward byte-for-byte and carries the prior header `Install date` forward. A missing, duplicate, reversed, or in-region project-spine marker refuses before mutation with exactly `forge: project spine addenda block malformed — repair forge-project.md`. For a legacy file with no project-spine pair, any text outside the regions that diverges from the fresh template is preserved byte-for-byte as `forge-project.md.forge-prev` and reported as a blocking init collision; no divergent legacy text is silently discarded, while a legacy file with no such divergence receives the fresh empty block. That legacy comparison first substitutes the prior `Install date` into the fresh render, then removes only the fresh contiguous addenda stanza from its fixed heading through the END-marker line and its single following separator blank line, and compares every remaining outside-region byte exactly. An absent `.forge-prev` may be created; an existing `.forge-prev` is accepted idempotently only when it is a regular non-symlink whose bytes equal the legacy file being preserved, and any differing or unsafe sibling refuses before mutation without changing either file. After read-only manifest classification and before the first target-repository mutation, init MUST preflight every Codex-layer template and every existing `.codex/hooks.json` or `.codex/config.toml` input, including unmarked and upstream-signature inputs. Each input MUST be a regular non-symlink strict UTF-8 file of at most 1 MiB; JSON is parsed with duplicate-key rejection, and TOML is parsed when `tomllib` is available. Malformed JSON, a duplicate JSON key, an oversized input, or a hooks-shape violation refuses before any mutation; malformed TOML does likewise when `tomllib` is available. A hooks document has a root object; its `hooks` member is an object whose event values are arrays of group objects; every group has a `hooks` array of handler objects; and every handler has a string `command`. Unknown members at every object level are permitted and preserved except that the complete object of a marker-owned handler is plugin-owned and replaced. In `.codex/hooks.json`, a handler is plugin-owned exactly when its command contains the literal marker `: 'forge-managed';`. Re-init removes all prior plugin-owned handlers. A prior group's residual is the same group after those handlers are removed; it is retained when at least one foreign handler remains or when the group has any member other than `hooks`, and only an empty hooks-only residual is dropped. Re-init emits the fresh template's events, groups, and handlers first in template order; for each event present in that template it then emits the retained residual groups from the prior same-named event in prior group order; it next emits prior events absent from the template, with their retained residual groups, in prior event and group order; and it finally emits prior foreign top-level members in prior order. This replaces all prior plugin-owned handlers with the fresh template's marker-bearing handlers while preserving every foreign handler, group member, event, and top-level member in a closed deterministic order. Every template handler MUST carry that marker; a foreign handler that copies it is intentionally treated as wholly plugin-owned and replaced. Thus Forge still installs only its own Stop handlers and MUST NOT itself register SessionStart, while a foreign project SessionStart handler is outside the Forge layer and is preserved. In `.codex/config.toml`, the plugin-owned namespace is the top-level `approval_policy` and `sandbox_mode` keys and the complete `agents` table tree; fresh template values win for its known owned members, but any content outside that namespace or any project-added member under `agents.*` causes init to keep the existing `.codex/config.toml` byte-for-byte and write the fresh template as `.codex/config.toml.forge-new`, a blocking collision. For TOML lexical ownership, whitespace, key and table order, and the spelling of owned values are plugin-owned and refresh from the template; outside TOML strings, a comment token is plugin-owned only when its comment bytes begin exactly `# forge-managed` or `# forge: modified from upstream —`, every other comment is foreign content and takes the same collision path, and a project comment that copies either prefix is intentionally treated as plugin-owned. Missing `tomllib` takes that same collision path; malformed TOML still refuses during preflight when `tomllib` is available. Valid unmarked non-Forge Codex files retain their existing collision behavior, and valid FR-184 upstream-signature inputs retain backup-and-replace behavior rather than merging. An absent `.forge-new` may be created; an existing `.forge-new` is accepted idempotently only when it is a regular non-symlink with bytes equal to the fresh template, and every differing or unsafe sibling refuses before mutation. Re-init preserves the project-spine bytes, install date, and foreign hooks, refreshes marker-owned hooks, and never silently drops foreign content.

- **FR-085** (MUST): During FR-080 phase (1), init MUST evaluate `command -v dcg`. If absent, init MUST continue successfully and record `forge: dcg not found — no project allowlist change`. If present, init MUST inspect with exactly `dcg allowlist list`, restrict inspection to the project-scoped entries, and apply a fixed-string match for `core.git:branch-force-delete`. Scope selection and the fixed-string match MUST be stable under formatting changes limited to whitespace, so fake-dcg tests are deterministic. When the match is absent init MUST invoke exactly `dcg allow core.git:branch-force-delete --project --reason "forge worktree-merge deletes branches only after merge-base containment proof"` once and record `forge: dcg allowlisted core.git:branch-force-delete for this project`; when the match is present init MUST NOT invoke `dcg allow` and MUST record `forge: dcg allowlist already contains core.git:branch-force-delete for this project`. An allowlist inspection or update failure when dcg is present MUST be non-fatal: init MUST record `forge: dcg allowlist update failed` and continue. Re-init MUST preserve this idempotence.

### Kill-switch and enforcement hooks (FR-090..FR-095)

- **FR-090** (MUST): The plugin's PreToolUse hook on Bash MUST match any command segment (split on `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, and newlines) that invokes git — the token `git`, a path ending in `/git`, or `env` with assignments followed by `git` — followed, after any global options (including `-C <path>`, `-c <k=v>`, `--git-dir=...`), by the subcommand token `commit` or `push`. Shell aliases, functions, and other wrappers are out of scope (see Threat model). The guard blocks (permission decision deny, with the reason in the message) when: (a) `check-halt.sh` (global `AGENT_HALT` or scoped `AGENT_HALT_commit` sentinel) reports a halt; or (b) for `git commit`, the guard is armed because either `HEAD:.forge-manifest` contains a line matching anchored `^plugin_ref: ` (the DM-005 line rule, never a bare substring), or a worktree `.forge-manifest` exists and does not parse as upstream schema (an `upstream_commit:` line or any `region: <name> (<file>)` line identifies upstream schema); while armed, the guard uses the shared stdlib candidate helper in its invoking Git context to resolve and sanitize the pinned repository/index, compute the DM-012 index-tree observation and authorization ID, and resolve `.forge/tmp/authorized/<authorization-id>` at the common main-checkout root, then denies if that marker is missing, malformed per DM-006, names a different format/tree/ID, or is older than 30 minutes; or (c) a fast marker fails the independent policy-continuity or eligibility recomputation in FR-154. No legacy staged-diff digest is an authorization fallback. A worktree manifest matching neither schema is malformed and therefore arms the guard. Deleting or staging deletion of a committed plugin manifest does not lift the guard because the HEAD disjunct remains true; stripping `plugin_ref` from a worktree-only bootstrap manifest does not lift it because the worktree disjunct is a schema test, not a `plugin_ref` test. A repository whose manifest is upstream schema and whose HEAD manifest has no anchored `plugin_ref: ` line remains inert except for the halt check. A stale-marker sweep MUST exclude the current candidate until after its validation so a present stale current marker still emits the unchanged `(marker stale)` denial (this ordering preserves the stale denial during an age sweep); every existing denial reason string—`(marker missing)`, `(marker malformed)`, `(marker stale)`, `(marker hash mismatch)`, `(fast-path policy drift)`, and `(fast-path eligibility drift)`—MUST remain byte-identical. For `/forge:commit` Step 5, the command-shape rejection bucket comprises exactly the shapes the shipped stability predicate rejects: a `git commit` preceded by another command in the same invocation; command or process substitution anywhere in the invocation; a `--` pathspec delimiter; and the unsafe flags and valued options (`-a`/`--all`, `-i`/`--include`, `-o`/`--only`, `--amend`, `--allow-empty`, `--allow-empty-message`, `-p`/`--patch`, `--interactive`, `-F`/`--file`, `-C`/`--reuse-message`, `-c`/`--reedit-message`, the equals-attached forms `--fixup=<commit>` and `--squash=<commit>`, `--pathspec-from-file`, `--pathspec-file-nul`); the space-separated forms `--fixup <commit>` and `--squash <commit>` remain admitted. A bare `git commit`, or one carrying only `--cleanup=verbatim`, the benign flags (`-q`/`--quiet`, `-v`/`--verbose`, `-n`/`--no-verify`, `-s`/`--signoff`, `--no-edit`, `-S`/`--gpg-sign`), `-m`/`--message`, and the other admitted valued message options, remains admitted against a valid marker exactly as the committed guard tests pin; the bucket's public denial remains exactly `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker hash mismatch)`; an internal non-user-visible `unsafe-command-shape` code MAY distinguish the cause, but stderr and hook reason bytes MUST NOT change. Forge installs no repository-native Git hook; existing user/repository hooks may still run during the ordinary commit and any index mutation they make is contained by the post-Git produced-identity check.
- **FR-091** (MUST): `check-halt.sh` MUST implement the upstream contract: global + scoped sentinels at the main-checkout root (resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, worktree-transparent), append-only audit line to `.forge/tmp/halt-audit.log` (`<UTC ISO-8601> halt detected (pid <pid>, cwd <cwd>, sentinel <name>)`), exit 0 clear / 1 halted, and exit 0 with a warning outside a git repo. Agents MUST NOT create, delete, or bypass sentinels without explicit user direction.
- **FR-092** (MUST): The halt MUST be checked at: commit Step 5.0, worktree-merge before rebase, workflow before launching each new execution, and orchestrate between monitor cycles. When halted: no new work, no reintegration, report and wait.
- **FR-093** (MUST): Plugin `hooks/hooks.json` MUST register a Stop union that invokes both `aggregate-telemetry.sh .forge/tmp/decisions --append-csv .forge/tmp/telemetry.csv --session "<session-id>"` and `drift-staleness.sh`, and a SessionStart entry that invokes only `drift-staleness.sh`. The telemetry writer MUST append under `.forge/tmp/telemetry.lock` and include the session ID on every appended row; it MUST NOT truncate or replace telemetry written by another session. Each command MUST independently detect an initialized forge repository by `.forge-manifest`; when it is absent, each MUST be silent, write no telemetry or nudge state, and exit 0, so Stop and SessionStart are inert in non-forge repositories. Failure of one Stop member MUST NOT suppress invocation of the other; both are advisory hook work and MUST NOT launch semantic drift review.
- **FR-094** (SHOULD): The commit guard SHOULD log every block to `.forge/tmp/halt-audit.log` as: UTC timestamp, executable, deny reason code, and a command excerpt truncated to 200 characters with common secret patterns (API/bearer tokens, `password=`, PEM blocks) redacted — never the raw full command line. The log file SHOULD be created with mode 600.
- **FR-095** (MUST): The Bash PreToolUse guard MUST implement the optional additive `guard-denied-commands` region from DM-003 as cooperative mistake prevention, not tamper-proof command mediation. A missing region is the legacy empty policy and MUST leave every pre-existing hook decision and output byte-identical. A present region is valid only when its stripped body is exactly `No additional denied commands configured.` or is a Markdown table whose header is exactly `| pattern | reason |`, whose separator is exactly `|---|---|`, and which has at least one data row with exactly two nonempty single-line cells. Each pattern cell MUST parse under POSIX `shlex` rules to one through 64 nonempty tokens; pattern and reason cells are each bounded to 4096 UTF-8 bytes, the table is bounded to 256 rows, and duplicate parsed token tuples, malformed quoting, extra cells or prose, NUL, CR, or a multiline reason make the region malformed. Rows retain operator order, and the first matching row supplies the reason. A pattern is data and MUST never be passed to `bash -c`, interpolated into shell text, sourced, `eval`uated, glob-expanded, or executed.

  Matching is case-sensitive equality over a prefix of the guard's parsed direct-invocation argv, never a raw-command substring. The parser MUST first apply FR-090's existing leading-assignment and complete supported `env`-prefix resolution. It MUST canonicalize a directly invoked `git` or path ending in `/git` to the token `git` and, when the existing Git parser recognizes the action, remove Git global options so the compared argv is `git`, the parsed subcommand, then its subcommand arguments. Thus the pattern `git push --force` denies `git push --force origin main` but does not deny `git push`; executable substitutions and direct commands in parsed compound segments remain inspectable. Literal here-document body text is data and MUST be excluded, but command and legacy-backtick substitutions in an unquoted-delimiter body remain executable and MUST be inspected; quoting any part of the delimiter suppresses that body expansion. After here-document masking and before prefix comparison, the direct-invocation lexer MUST derive the argv Bash executes: it removes active backslash-newline pairs that are outside single quotes and whose backslash is not itself escaped; recognizes attached and fd-prefixed Bash redirection operators (including `>`, `<`, `>>`, `2>`, `&>`, `>&`, and `<<<`) as syntax rather than command-word bytes without splitting quoted `>` or `<`; and decodes `$'…'` ANSI-C quoted fragments to their literal word value. `$"…"` locale quoting MAY be treated as ordinary double quoting. An otherwise-inspected shape that cannot be normalized unambiguously, including a locale- or Bash-version-dependent ANSI-C escape with no single portable UTF-8 argv value, MUST fail closed with the existing malformed-policy diagnostic rather than being omitted. Aliases, functions, `sudo`, `command`, `bash -c`, and other wrappers remain deliberately out of scope. This is honest direct-invocation coverage for cooperative agents; an agent able to choose an unparsed wrapper can evade it by design.

  The guard MUST resolve one full `head_policy_sha` for the invoking repository and read the region only from that immutable revision with the FR-149 `git --no-replace-objects show <policy-sha>:forge-project.md` path; staged and working-tree bytes are never policy. Repository-context resolution for this control is tri-state. Classification probes MUST run with `LC_ALL=C` and `LANG=C`; only a completed Git probe whose stderr begins with the exact `fatal: not a git repository` prefix establishes confirmed absence. A Git launch failure, any other nonzero or unclassifiable response (including a configuration-read failure), or a missing, unreadable, or unresolvable returned path is operational failure and MUST deny with the same malformed-policy diagnostic. This distinction applies to every context lookup feeding `guard-denied-commands` and MUST NOT alter the pre-existing raw-commit/raw-push controls' context handling. A present malformed region MUST deny every otherwise-inspected Bash invocation with exactly `forge: guard-denied-commands policy malformed — repair committed forge-project.md`; absence of a committed HEAD in an unborn repository, absence of `forge-project.md`, or absence of the region remains the compatibility no-op, while operational HEAD or policy-read failure fails closed with that malformed-policy diagnostic. Operator halt remains first. Malformed-region refusal follows halt and precedes every other denial; the existing fixed operator-verb denials then retain their literals, and a valid configured match precedes activation/raw-Git, index-owner, and marker checks. A valid match MUST deny with exactly `forge: operator-denied command — <reason>`, where `<reason>` is the selected stripped reason cell, and use only a stable internal reason code such as `configured-command-denied` for audit and telemetry. The new diagnostics are hook-only: they add no FR-220 reason-code member and do not alter any FR-221 denial literal or immutable corpus row. Fast-marker policy-continuity comparison MUST include the complete `guard-denied-commands` region, treating simultaneous absence in current and historical policies as equal legacy absence.

### Eval harness (FR-100..FR-103)

- **FR-100** (MUST): `run-evals.sh` is the model-free Recorded-baseline integrity runner over `.forge/evals/tasks/`. It MUST preserve the upstream fixture and public output contract: required frontmatter keys `id category agent expected_verdict`; verdicts `PASS|BLOCK|FLAG`; review agents cannot expect FLAG; exit 0 = no recorded mismatch (and, outside strict mode, may include PENDING), 1 = recorded mismatch or `STRICT=1` with PENDING, and 2 = malformed fixture or empty suite (`NO TASKS FOUND — gate vacuously satisfied`). It compares committed fixture declarations with adjacent recorded `.result` values and launches no agent; its PASS is never evidence that any reviewer produced that verdict on the current candidate.

Candidate-tree execution clarification to **FR-100**: when a commit chain records this result against a v2 candidate, it MUST run the trusted fixed runner with its working directory set to a disposable exact materialization of that candidate tree, verify those bytes again after the process, and re-observe the live index before recording the step. A source-working-tree run, even with identical output, cannot satisfy that candidate-bound step.

Revision-20 candidate-checkout clarification to **FR-100** (GH#41): Since 0.6.11, the exact candidate checkout has refused, anywhere in the whole tree, absolute symlink targets, symlink targets that escape the checkout root, and Git gitlinks.

- **FR-101** (MUST): Init MUST first import any migration fixtures and their committed `.result` baselines under FR-182. It MUST then add only gaps from the three seed templates (review-catches-planted-bug/BLOCK, review-passes-clean-change/PASS, injection-is-flagged/BLOCK) concretized against the target repo, establish baselines by running the named agent and writing `tasks/<id>.result` only for newly seeded fixtures without an imported baseline, and require a clean `run-evals.sh` exit 0 before Phase 5. An imported baseline MUST NEVER be re-recorded, re-minted, or overwritten. Baseline establishment is a one-time immutable oracle-creation operation, not a candidate-bound fresh result, and neither that launch nor its `.result` may satisfy FR-103 for a later candidate.
- **FR-102** (MUST): Eval documentation MUST describe gate-output and verdict-derived fixtures: the exact prompt supplies fixture Input, the expected verdict supplies the immutable oracle, and chain ID, candidate and evidence references supply provenance. Fixtures and baselines MUST NOT be overwritten; a `.result` MUST NOT be edited to make a gate pass. A legitimately changed fixture is promoted only as a new version with a newly established immutable baseline. Fresh-reviewer results remain transient request-bound chain artifacts and MUST NOT be copied into `.result`.
- **FR-103** (MUST): Evaluation is two distinct fail-closed layers. Every control-class or review-final-floor candidate MUST satisfy Recorded-baseline integrity over the complete committed `.forge/evals/tasks/` suite by a current execution or a valid FR-255 applicability proof; that layer proves only fixture/result presence, grammar, immutability, and equality. When the exact DM-012 staged path set matches any positive pathspec in the `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` region loaded from the candidate's authenticated base-policy SHA, unless FR-217's exact durable fresh-step operator skip applies, the same chain MUST additionally obtain fresh, independent judgments for every supported review-role fixture from the candidate tree's fixed known-oracle-free projection, under the candidate route and FR-149 bounds. The fresh manifest MUST bind the v2 authorization ID, object format/tree, base commit, review-diff digest/count, chain ID, one-time request ID, trigger evidence, projection definition and digest, complete fixture inventory, route, process completion, and verdict artifacts; any absence, malformation, stale/foreign binding, unavailable reviewer, or expected/actual mismatch blocks the candidate.
