### Forge CLI commit chain (FR-210..FR-224)

FR-210..FR-224 specify the commit chain; FR-230..FR-243 specify merge, push and their later-phase boundaries. Throughout, `forge <verb>` is shorthand for `python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" <verb>`.

- **FR-210** (MUST): The plugin MUST ship `scripts/forge/cli.py` (Python ≥ 3.10, standard library only) implementing the commit-chain state machine with subcommands `status`, `commit start|restage|rebase|abort|approve|skip|finalize`, `verify`, `gate run`, `classify`, `scan secrets`, and `review request|collect|attach|disposition`. Every state-mutating subcommand MUST run the halt check first via `check-halt.sh` and refuse while a halt sentinel is present. The CLI and PreToolUse guard MUST use one shared standard-library module, `scripts/forge/forge_cli/candidate.py`, for repository/index context discovery, bounded `write-tree` observation, authorization-ID derivation, immutable tree-pair enumeration, deterministic review-patch rendering, DM-006 marker identity, and bounded raw produced-object reading; a second diff-hashing or context implementation is forbidden. After context discovery the helper discards inherited `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`, `GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF`, `GIT_DIFF_OPTS`, and `GIT_CONFIG_*`, sets `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` and `LC_ALL=C`, and pins resolved `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and `GIT_WORK_TREE`. It caps stdout/stderr and fails closed without a partial snapshot. The CLI composes the existing tested halt/lock/classifier/eval/quality/event controls and makes the secret scan consume the snapshot's exact immutable review artifact; it MUST NOT reimplement any tested control. Every chain-event artifact, including prompts, bounded transcripts, review packages and verdicts, MUST be retained under the owning chain's `.forge/chains/` directory. A journal MAY cite them as narrative references and MUST NOT participate in authorization, evidence ingestion or transition admission.

The request-bound `fresh-reviewer-evals/iteration-<NN>/<request-id>/` suite likewise lives under its owning chain directory and remains bound to its digest-chained request and candidate.
- **FR-211** (MUST): Chain lifecycle. States are exactly `classifying`, `verifying`, `reviewing`, `revising`, `awaiting_approval`, `authorized`, `committing`, `closed`, `aborted`. Chains are born in `classifying`: `commit start --paths <path>... [--declare-tier <tier>]` refuses when another live commit chain exists for the same worktree (at most one live commit chain per worktree — the Git index is the mutex), when the index already holds staged content, when a named path does not exist, or when policy is unreadable from committed HEAD (FR-083's narrower bootstrap remains possible). On admission the CLI performs `git add -- <paths>`, creates the complete DM-012 snapshot, derives actual paths from its immutable tree pair, and runs classification automatically. The normative transitions remain `classifying → verifying`; `verifying → reviewing|authorized`; `reviewing → authorized|awaiting_approval|revising`; `revising → classifying`; `awaiting_approval → authorized`; and `authorized → committing → closed`, with every evidence/approval transition bound to the current authorization ID. `abort [--reason]` reaches `aborted` from a nonterminal state except `committing`; from v2 `committing` it is admitted only when the durable failed produced-identity latch exists, performs no Git operation, and preserves that mismatch result. DM-012's changed-HEAD v1 migration freeze is the sole historical exception and admits the same non-Git operator disposition while preserving its legacy fact record. From `closed` or `aborted` it refuses before mutation. Tier is promote-only. An out-of-band index change detected by tree re-observation creates a fresh snapshot generation, returns the chain to `classifying`, invalidates older evidence and authorization even if the tree returns to an earlier OID, and records the anomaly in chain events. Out-of-band index changes invalidate all older evidence; only an explicit sanctioned restage may derive FR-255 carry-forward proofs. DM-012's exact key sets distinguish v1 from v2. A nonterminal v1 chain admits only `status`, the bounded `committing` recovery branch, and `commit restage` until restage performs the fail-closed v2 migration; unchanged HEAD revokes the v1 authorization into that restage-only posture, while changed HEAD freezes and never auto-closes. A chain past `inactive_after` admits only `status` or `abort`. There is no chain-lifetime TTL; only the 30-minute authorization token is bounded.
- **FR-212** (MUST): Candidate identity and invalidation. A candidate exists only after staging and is the complete `forge-commit-candidate/2` snapshot in DM-012, never a path-list or rendered-diff identity. The helper writes the pinned live index with bounded `git write-tree`, validates the full OID against the repository object format, and derives `authorization_id` from the exact domain-separated tuple. From the immutable base tree and candidate tree it independently enumerates raw NUL-framed paths with fixed no-renames/no-ext-diff/no-textconv/submodule-inclusive arguments and renders the exact binary/full-index review patch with fixed prefixes, context, algorithm, ordering, and color settings. Each stream has a 16 MiB ceiling plus one-byte overflow probe. `commit restage --paths <path>...` is the only sanctioned way to change the staged set mid-chain: it creates a fresh snapshot generation, reclassifies the complete candidate, always invalidates review, approval, dispositions and authorization, and applies FR-255's deterministic delta plan to mechanical evidence. The new file set MAY include any safe repository path without task, run-scope or old-path-set restrictions. An index change outside the CLI invalidates all older evidence on detection and adds an anomaly note; selective carry-forward requires the explicit restage proof. Working-tree-vs-index drift remains a separate negligence check at review request and finalize; it is not candidate identity. Cross-chain isolation binds marker and chain authorization to the complete authorization ID/tree, so distinct worktree indexes cannot cross-authorize. Identity computation may add unreachable immutable tree objects until normal Git maintenance, but never writes the real index or working tree.
- **FR-213** (MUST): Out-of-band HEAD movement. Every command compares the recorded `repo_head` with the current HEAD. A moved HEAD MUST never surface as a bare identity mismatch: the CLI records `head_moved` in chain events with the old/new SHAs and refuses state advancement until `commit rebase` or `abort`. `commit rebase` re-pins `repo_head`, checks policy continuity by digest, restages the recorded immutable path set, and computes a new result-tree snapshot on the new HEAD. Tree-dependent records always die. Diff-scoped secret-scan/review evidence may survive only when the new deterministic patch bytes and path set equal the prior evidence byte-for-byte; approval and authorization may survive only when the candidate generation and full authorization ID are unchanged and the existing TTL is valid. An unrelated base change ordinarily changes the complete result tree and therefore rebinds approval even when patch text is identical. This expressly supersedes the former rule that rebase could not be stricter than staged-diff-hash pinning. Whole review loops remain outside a repository lock; the post-Git identity gate, not serialization of arbitrary writers, contains the final race.
- **FR-214** (MUST): `forge verify` MUST run every remaining required mechanical step for the effective tier, in order — the configured `changelog` gate first when present, then gate 1 once (recorded as a `docs-class candidate` skip under the same ID, launching no process, when the classifier's per-path evidence proves every staged path docs-class), stack validations, the assertion-quality sensor, invariant `commit` rows, the secrets scan, and STRICT evals where FR-103 applies — recording each exactly as `gate run` does. Judgment verbs (review, approve, skip, finalize) are never included. `verify` MUST be resumable by construction: per-step completion is persisted in the chain, each invocation continues from the first incomplete step until done, failure, or interruption, and invoking it when everything passes is a no-op that prints the next judgment verb — the harness tool-execution ceiling is shorter than gate 1 alone, so a blocking monolith is a liveness bug, not an ergonomic choice. `gate run <gate-id>` runs a single configured gate from the committed policy table (never a working-tree policy file) under FR-149's execution discipline — one-cell `bash -c` argv, isolated process group, 65,536-byte output cap, 1200-second fail-closed timeout — while the CLI itself captures exit code, duration, stdout/stderr digest, a bounded transcript, and the DM-013 fingerprint. Phase 1 has no generic mutating-gate registry: a configured DM-003 changelog command is automatically the sole mutating gate, and on success the CLI stages its exact declared output paths, creates a fresh complete tree snapshot/review artifact/path enumeration, invalidates authorization and review, records the superseded/new tree delta, applies FR-255 to downstream mechanical evidence, and reruns classification; the machine therefore refuses every other `gate run` while `changelog` is pending and names it as remediation. The complete phase-1 mechanical gate-ID set is exactly `changelog`, `gate-1`, `stack:<category>`, `assertion-sensor`, `invariant:<row-number>`, `secret-scan`, and `strict-evals`; `<category>` is normalized lowercase `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`, and `<row-number>` is the one-based ordinal among all invariant data rows, of which only rows with `enforcement == "commit"` are required. The single Gate-1 observation, or its docs-class skip record, uses the `gate-1` ID. `classification` is an internal step rather than a runnable gate; `review` is a skip target rather than a mechanical gate ID; index drift is targeted only by `--index-drift`, and `review-cap` is the FR-255 budget-reset skip target rather than a mechanical gate ID; unknown or unconfigured IDs refuse with `state-precondition`. The shipped resolver applies Python `int()` to an `invariant:` suffix, so an integer-equivalent noncanonical spelling such as `invariant:01` or `invariant:+1` can execute row 1, but its evidence is stored under that literal alias and does not satisfy canonical `invariant:1`; this is documented shipped behavior, not a promise that aliases are canonical. The Gate-1 observation records its DM-013 fingerprint as context evidence; the merge chain's in-lock Gate-1 observation (FR-233) is a separate CLI-recorded run on the reintegrated tree, so every landed tree is still observed twice, once per stage, and no cross-stage fingerprint pairing exists because the two stages observe different HEADs by construction.

- **FR-215** (MUST): Per-tier required steps restate FR-050/FR-152 without weakening: every tier runs stage + v2 snapshot + immutable-path classification (inside `start`), the configured mutating changelog gate first where present, gate 1 once (a docs-class-only candidate records the skip instead), stack validations for touched categories, the FR-144 assertion-quality sensor, the FR-147 `commit` invariant rows, the exact review-artifact secrets scan, and finalize with halt, lock, last pre-intent index-tree/authorization re-observation, and post-Git exact parent/tree/message verification (fast additionally: the independent eligibility recomputation per FR-154). Fast differs from the other tiers in exactly one row — the reviewer; weakening any other row is an FR-152 violation. Standard requires a fresh `review-cheap`; hard requires `review-final`, with provider/model/effort selected by the resolved route. Every review-final-floor match is hard and preserves applicable STRICT and fresh-reviewer eval requirements; control additionally requires operator approval bound to the authorization ID. Fast is opt-out, not opt-in: classification runs automatically inside `commit start`, a mechanically eligible candidate is fast unless evidence promotes it, and fast finalize retains both identity gates and independent recomputation.
A candidate's mechanical verification runs Gate 1 once, or records a freshly derived `docs-class candidate` skip under `gate-1` only when every path carries exactly the docs category and no control, review-final-floor or trigger match. A run association MUST NOT affect this decision. A restage reevaluates this exemption and all required steps under FR-255. Historical pair-voiding events remain readable, and FR-121's two end-to-end verifications do not duplicate the commit gate. Review PASS remains candidate-bound; only the issued authorization token has the 30-minute TTL.

- **FR-216** (MUST): Review verbs. `review request` first re-observes the current index tree and refuses on identity or working-tree drift. It assembles one authoritative owner-controlled package containing the `authorization_id`, object format, full base commit/tree, full candidate tree, exact `review_diff_sha256` and byte count, immutable snapshot path set, mechanically selected per-artefact profile, committed project focus regions, and the exact deterministic binary/full-index tree-to-tree patch artifact created by the snapshot. Secret scanning and review consume that same artifact; neither rereads the moving index. Patch and enumeration each have a 16 MiB ceiling and never truncate. The package MUST exclude the implementer's handoff, claimed results, and prior verdicts. The patch bytes are exact binary-capable review evidence but are not claimed to be a literal concatenation of every changed blob; tree identity remains authorization authority. For both reviewer roles and either provider, the CLI launches the detached reviewer, records its resolved route and launch evidence, owns the verdict path, and accepts completion only through `review collect`, binding the verdict to the authorization ID and package. A fix review includes the prior candidate identity and exact candidate delta under FR-255 while retaining the complete current candidate package. The shipped transport accepts strict UTF-8, strips split lines, discards blanks, and requires first nonblank `VERDICT: PASS|BLOCK`; exactly one `candidate: <exact-current-authorization-id>` and one `package: <exact-issued-package-digest>` follow in either order, with only repeatable `finding: <CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR> <nonempty text>` lines otherwise. PASS rejects CRITICAL/MAJOR; BLOCK permits any finding set including none. These transport rules do not narrow the constitution. BLOCK increments the iteration and transitions to `revising`; iteration 8 without PASS escalates and never commits. `review disposition` remains model-issuable judgment, and above-MINOR disposition still requires operator co-sign.

`review attach` MUST refuse every new review-final request and name `review collect` as the remedy. Its sole surviving admission is a request created before the upgrade that is still in `reviewing` and has the legacy `invocation` shape without new-lane PID/provider fields. This compatibility rule is keyed only to record shape, has no release-number grace, flag, or operator-skip bypass, and leaves FR-211's state enum unchanged.

One review iteration has exactly one authoritative owner-controlled regular master-package file and one lowercase SHA-256 master digest over its complete bytes. When that package exceeds a direct reviewer-input window, the launch gives one reviewer execution the master path and digest, never embeds the package bytes, and may additionally supply the ordinary candidate/generation/profile/target identifiers. The same reviewer inspects the complete master through deterministic raw-byte windows `[65536*n, min(65536*(n+1), byte_length))` in ascending `n`. The reader verifies the unchanged master path identity, byte length, and full digest before the first window and after the last, and verifies that concatenating the windows produces exactly the master bytes and digest. An index or cached window is a discardable transport view only and never a second package or verdict authority.

The one reviewer emits exactly one FR-216 verdict citing the master digest. Multiple reviewer executions, shard packages, shard summaries, shard verdicts, aggregation, majority, and quorum are forbidden for one review iteration. If that reviewer cannot inspect every master-package byte through the bounded views, review stops without attaching a verdict and refuses exactly `forge: review refused — reviewer cannot inspect the complete authoritative package`; where a CLI envelope applies, the absent required review remains exact `evidence-incomplete`. This protocol applies to oversized commit and merge packages.

A merge chain may have exactly zero or one outstanding above-MINOR (`MAJOR` or `CRITICAL`) disposition awaiting operator co-sign. An above-MINOR `review disposition` is admitted only when that slot is empty. While it is occupied, another above-MINOR disposition — including a replacement for the same finding — changes no event, state, or evidence and refuses with exact `forge: review disposition refused — above-MINOR disposition already awaits operator co-sign` and `state-precondition`. MINOR dispositions do not occupy the slot. The sole outstanding object fixes the finding number, severity, resolution, candidate HEAD, and generation; the flag-less `merge approve` disposition purpose selects exactly that object, records its co-sign, and atomically clears the slot before another above-MINOR disposition may be accepted.

- **FR-217** (MUST): Operator verbs. `commit approve --candidate <sha256>` retains its argv shape, but `<sha256>` now means the exact 64-hex `authorization_id`, never `review_diff_sha256`. It is valid only in `awaiting_approval`, requires equality with the current v2 authorization ID, and transitions to `authorized`. `commit skip <gate-id> --reason <text>` and `commit skip --index-drift --reason <text>` retain their existing semantics, and `commit skip review-cap --reason <text>` is the FR-255 inherited-budget reset, admitted only before the chain's first review request and otherwise refused with `state-precondition` and exactly `forge: commit skip refused — review-cap is admitted only before the first review request`; overriding the separate working-tree negligence guard remains operator-reserved. Both verbs are operator-bound: the hook denies model-Bash issuance and directs the model to present both the authorization ID and separately labeled review-patch digest/summary, then ask the operator to run the exact Forge command via `!`. `finalize` carries no override flags. No skip covers control approval or mandatory review under the control category or review-final-floor. In headless runs required skip/approval parks the chain. The model may ask the operator to run Forge operator verbs via `!`; it may never ask for raw Git or another bypass.

`strict-evals` remains the replay-compatible persisted gate ID and CLI alias for Recorded-baseline integrity; no renamed duplicate baseline step is created. Every control-class or review-final-floor candidate requires it. The current mechanical gate-ID registry additively includes `fresh-reviewer-evals`, required when the strictly authenticated base trigger matches or when that trigger's presence, grammar, region digest, policy binding, or match derivation cannot be verified. `forge verify` orders that step after `strict-evals` and the exact review-artifact secrets scan and before any judgment verb. `gate run fresh-reviewer-evals` invokes DM-012's candidate-bound coordinator rather than a configured policy cell. After a valid request it records the bounded terminal process result and binds any safely published canonical manifest; a trigger failure before request instead returns the exact INVALID refusal without inventing or recording a request, manifest, or terminal suite result. A valid authenticated trigger with zero matches alone makes direct invocation unconfigured with `state-precondition`; every trigger-configuration or derivation failure instead takes that required preflight and returns FR-220's INVALID exit 2. Recovery may finish or invalidate only the exact persisted current request; a prior candidate/chain request never completes the step, an incomplete current request never PASSes, and FR-053 alone governs a new-request retry after terminal INVALID.

Control or review-final-floor requires a current-candidate `strict-evals` PASS or its valid FR-255 applicability proof, and an authenticated trigger match additionally requires a current-candidate `fresh-reviewer-evals` PASS before the tier's one binding review and, for control changes, candidate-bound operator approval, unless FR-217's exact durable fresh-step operator skip applies. An unverifiable authenticated-base trigger requires the same step but can produce only INVALID until repaired. Floor membership and control approval are independent: a floor-only candidate retains the same fail-closed authenticated-base trigger check, including required INVALID on missing or malformed trigger policy. Fast eligibility never removes either applicable layer. Except for FR-217's exact current-chain operator skip, finalize treats a missing, skipped, stale, foreign, malformed, or non-PASS fresh record as incomplete evidence.

For a triggered candidate, the authoritative binding-review master package MUST include the exact trigger evidence, Recorded-baseline integrity transcript and digest, canonical fresh manifest, every bounded `verdict.txt`, the expected/actual summary and explicit `subject-specific-baseline-only` exclusions, and paths, byte counts, and digests for each raw prompt, events, verdict, and completion artifact. These inputs are conspicuously delimited as untrusted Gate-2 evidence, not controlling instructions. FR-216's exclusion of prior verdicts now means prior binding Gate-3 verdicts and implementer-authored verdict claims; it does not exclude the mandatory current-candidate fresh-evaluation material. The package still has one master digest and goes to exactly one binding reviewer, using the existing deterministic window transport when oversized; the per-fixture sessions are never shards or a quorum for that binding review.

The exact fresh-step operator exception applies as follows. The exact `commit skip fresh-reviewer-evals --reason <text>` verb MAY satisfy a required fresh step only on explicit operator direction and only after its `operator_skip` event and DM-012 `user_skip` record are durably written on the current candidate's chain. The skip is not a PASS and creates no fresh manifest, Gate-2 verification, or fabricated fresh-evaluation segment in the binding-review package. Legitimate transition uses include the reviewer-facing trigger-region-introducing bootstrap commit and an already-installed project in the interval between plugin upgrade and committed adoption of the canonical `reviewer-facing-eval-triggers` region. No broad skip mapping, model-issued command, approval, CI result, baseline result, reviewer result, or unrecorded direction waives or satisfies the gate; Recorded-baseline integrity remains non-skippable. A missing or unverifiable trigger remains required-as-INVALID and parks until repaired or that exact skip is recorded. After a current-candidate terminal fresh BLOCK, that exact skip preserves the BLOCK evidence, moves the unchanged candidate from `revising` to `classifying`, and leaves another fresh request forbidden; classification and ordinary verification then resume with the durable skip satisfying only this gate. Finalize accepts only that exact current-chain skip in place of fresh PASS and treats every other missing, skipped, stale, foreign, malformed, or non-PASS fresh record as incomplete evidence. Only this exact durable operator skip is an exception to the fresh-step PASS requirement; INVALID-result and no-inferred-PASS rules remain unchanged.
- **FR-218** (MUST): Approval mechanism, layered strongest-first, preserving FR-051/FR-060 law — a reviewer PASS never authorizes a control-class commit: (1) the state machine is the mandatory floor, and no transition from `awaiting_approval` to `authorized` exists without an approval record naming the exact current authorization ID; (2) hook-denied model approval plus operator-run approval via `!` is normative, subject to the FR-223 harness qualification; (3) approve stays outside every allowlist, so a failed bypass assumption still exposes the authorization ID in a permission prompt; (4) the skill conversationally presents the change summary, separately labeled authorization ID and review-evidence digest, and review outcome, none of which is itself approval. The CLI proves only that the approve command ran and named the right authorization ID; it cannot prove operator understanding.
- **FR-219** (MUST): Finalize. `commit finalize --message <text> | --message-file <file>` is the only commit-producing CLI verb; the commit message is model judgment and arrives only through those flags. Finalize verifies every tier-required record against the current authorization ID, TTL, and permitted skips, plus separate working-tree drift. For only the mechanical gates eligible under FR-255, a validated applicability record bound to the current authorization ID MAY satisfy that check while its source PASS stays bound to its original candidate; review, approval, authorization and fresh-reviewer manifests cannot carry forward through that mechanism. Its exact order is: (1) halt check; (2) acquire the commit lock, reload the chain under it, and verify state, current HEAD, complete evidence, token TTL, and working-tree drift; (3) recompute the live index `tree_oid` and `authorization_id`, require both to equal the candidate, run fast eligibility if applicable, then repeat that tree observation as the last pre-intent check; (4) persist `commit_intent` and state `committing`, with exactly the authorization ID, object format, expected tree OID, pre-HEAD, bounded exact message-body digest, timestamp, and lock owner; (5) invoke ordinary hooks-enabled `git commit --cleanup=verbatim -m ...`; (6) after exit 0, read HEAD and the bounded raw commit object with replacement objects disabled and require HEAD moved, exactly one `tree` header equal to intent, exactly one `parent` header equal to pre-HEAD, and exact message-body digest equality; (7) persist exactly one `commit_identity_checked` PASS with expected/observed full tuple, produced SHA, message digest, and evidence reference; (8) only then persist authorization consumption, `authorization_consumed`, `commit_produced`, chain close, release, and advisory `gate_commit`/`fast_allowed`. The authorization token retains its 30-minute issuance TTL.

Any produced-object mismatch persists exactly one failed identity check and the complete mismatch
latch, leaves `authorization.consumed=false`, retains state `committing`, releases the lock, and
emits none of `authorization_consumed`, `commit_produced`, `chain_closed`,
`gate_commit`, or `fast_allowed`. It never resets, amends, retries, or otherwise changes the
produced commit. Repeated `status`/`finalize` returns the same frozen outcome without duplicate
events. After inspection, only `commit abort --reason <text>` is admitted from that latched state;
it performs DM-012's non-Git abort without laundering the failed Gate-2 result. The sole historical
parallel is DM-012's changed-HEAD v1 `committing` migration freeze: its explicit operator abort
preserves the bounded legacy facts, emits no identity PASS or landing, and performs no Git action.

Recovery uses the same raw-object verifier. HEAD equal to pre-HEAD retains the existing rollback to
`authorized` only while authorization is unexpired and unconsumed. HEAD different from pre-HEAD is
never compared by rendering a diff: exact matching parent/tree/message completes PASS,
consumption, landing, and close idempotently; any mismatch records/reuses FAIL and freezes. Git
hooks and arbitrary same-user index writers are not disabled or excluded; the last observation
narrows the race and the post-Git check contains it.
- **FR-220** (MUST): Output contract. Every command ends its stdout with `next required step: <exact command>` (or `next required step: none — chain closed`). Every refusal is a structured, self-contained diagnostic — current chain state, the failed precondition, expected versus observed values (digests truncated for display, full in the JSON envelope), the exact remediation command, and the next required step — and exits 1; a refusal that requires the operator says so explicitly and names the operator action. `--json` on every command emits exactly one machine-readable envelope on stdout with the exact key set `{schema, ok, chain_id, state, reason_code, message, expected, observed, remediation, next_required_step, evidence_refs}` and nothing else — under `--json` the human `next required step:` line is a field, never a second stdout line. Reason codes are a fixed enum, spec'd and pinned by tests, so the hook and evals assert on codes rather than prose. The closed table below is that enum, normative and exhaustive: every member is kebab-case, unique, and sorted; success envelopes use exactly `ok`; exit-2 internal failures use exactly `frozen-chain`; every exit-1 refusal uses exactly one refusal member; wildcards, dynamic construction, and unlisted codes are forbidden, and adding, removing, or respelling a member is a control-class change. The members and their failed preconditions: `ambiguous-target` (a dispensation or disposition target matches more than one record); `approval-required` (control-class chain not yet operator-approved); `candidate-stale` (evidence bound to a superseded candidate hash); `citation-out-of-root` (FR-017 refusal surfaced through the CLI); `dirty-index` (pre-existing staged content at `commit start`); `drift-tree-index` (working-tree-vs-index divergence on a staged path); `evidence-incomplete` (a required tier step is absent or non-PASS without an operator skip); `frozen-chain` (corrupt chain file, irresolvable event/state divergence, or foreign HEAD in `committing`); `halt-engaged` (operator halt sentinel present); `head-moved` (out-of-band HEAD movement pending `commit rebase` or `abort`); `inactive-chain` (chain past `inactive_after`; only `status`/`abort` accepted); `iteration-cap` (FR-053 cap of 8 reached); `live-chain-exists` (another live commit chain owns the worktree); `lock-unavailable` (commit-lock acquisition failed or timed out); `mutating-gate-pending` (non-mutating `gate run` while a configured mutating gate is pending); `ok` (success); `operator-verb-denied` (model-issued `approve`/`skip` reached the CLI through a denied path); `path-missing` (a named path does not exist at `commit start`/`restage`); `policy-changed` (committed policy bytes differ at the new HEAD after `commit rebase`); `policy-unreadable` (committed-HEAD policy unavailable outside the bootstrap path); `review-verdict-invalid` (verdict grammar, candidate-hash citation, or package-digest citation failed at `collect`/`attach`); `skip-not-permitted` (a skip naming control-class review or approval); `state-precondition` (verb issued in a state whose transition table does not admit it); `token-consumed` (authorization token already used); `ttl-expired` (authorization token past its 30-minute TTL). `--verbose` streams underlying activity live without changing what is recorded as evidence — the bounded transcript and digests are written identically either way. Internal failures (corrupt chain file, irresolvable event/state divergence, unexpected exceptions) exit 2, fail closed, and print what was being attempted, what is on disk, and that the chain is frozen pending `status`/`abort` — never a bare traceback. Exact refusal strings and reason codes are control surface: pinned by tests, changed only through control-class review — the same doctrine as FR-090's denial literals. Since Revision 22 the emitters of these strings under `scripts/forge/` are review-final-floor surfaces; the FR-223 corpus binding, not control approval, pins the strings.

Members retired by Revision 22 remain reserved members and MUST never be emitted.

Candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation transport amendment to **FR-220**: the parser-sensitive FR-220 bullet, closed enum, and every v1/v2/v3 reason-corpus byte remain unchanged. The sole transport-level exit-class exception is `gate run fresh-reviewer-evals` when either a valid authenticated trigger requires the gate or trigger applicability itself cannot be authenticated or strictly derived, and the coordinator produces INVALID. The public command exits 2, its human primary diagnostic and JSON-envelope `message` are exactly `forge: fresh reviewer eval evidence invalid: <reason>`, and its otherwise ordinary `forge-cli/1` refusal envelope retains existing `reason_code: "evidence-incomplete"` and the exact FR-220 key set. This includes a missing or malformed authenticated trigger region; it is never downgraded to `state-precondition` or `policy-unreadable`. The transport-only exit override is not serialized as a new envelope member, does not use `frozen-chain`, does not freeze or corrupt the chain, and adds no reason-code member; every other nonzero `forge-cli/1` outcome retains FR-220's original exit classification.

A produced-identity mismatch is an existing `frozen-chain` case, not a new enum member. It exits 2
with exact primary diagnostic
`forge: produced commit does not match authorized candidate — chain frozen; commit left untouched`.
The human envelope reports state `committing` plus truncated expected/observed tree, parent, and
message digests; JSON carries their full values and evidence reference. The exact next required
step is `forge status --chain-id <id>`.
- **FR-221** (MUST): Hook integration, phase-1 dual-accept posture. While the DM-006 marker flow remains live, the PreToolUse hook MUST accept a `git commit` authorized by EITHER a valid v2 DM-006 marker OR a live v2 chain in `authorized` whose structured candidate object and unexpired, unconsumed authorization token match the exact `authorization_id`, object format, and tree OID the shared helper computes in the invoking Git context. Before either fallback, any filesystem entry at DM-006's exact same-ID `.quarantine` sibling MUST deny through the existing `marker malformed` literal; partial files, directories, and symlinks fail closed. The paths remain independent; neither may depend on the other being readable. Old diff-hash markers and v1/nonterminal chain candidates are never an authorization fallback. The dual-accept MUST NOT be skipped, and the v2 marker grammar/parser/tests MUST NOT be deleted until later phase-5 admission. The hook additionally denies model-issued operator verbs and foreign-session index-mutating verbs under the existing ownership rules; exact tree re-observation is the backstop for other index changes. The matcher grammar remains: it segments on unquoted `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, `&`, and newlines; after any leading `env` prefix with assignments, it recognizes the token `python3`, the token `python`, or a path whose final component is `python3` or `python`; and the script's final path components are `scripts/forge/cli.py` in the already-pinned relative, absolute, plugin-root, cache, marketplace, and local-checkout forms. It denies model-Bash `commit approve` and `commit skip` with exactly `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (commit approve)` or `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (commit skip)` as the deny reason, and allows `commit finalize` and every other CLI verb. CLI lookalike verbs, and quoted separator characters inside string arguments never match. Every FR-090 denial literal and the reason enum remain byte-identical. Forge installs no repo-native Git hooks: PreToolUse observes only registered model-tool paths, while an existing user/repository hook may run and mutate the index during ordinary commit; FR-219's post-Git check contains that case. Raw `git commit`/`git push` denial remains phase 4.
Revision-8 amendment to **FR-221**: DM-010's stable identity and FR-055's standalone command shape are additive only. They MUST NOT change independent marker-or-chain acceptance, either existing operator-verb denial, the marker grammar/parser/tests, or any other byte-pinned phase-1 matcher behavior; no internal command-shape classification becomes a third public denial or a third authorization path.

- **FR-222** (MUST): The chain file/event log is the system of record for the v2 candidate, authorization, produced-identity result, generation and commit landing; hook admission and finalize read it, never the journal. A landing requires replay proof of a preceding passed `commit_identity_checked` for that produced SHA and candidate tree; failed checks never establish landing. Gate logs, verdict files and chain events remain under `.forge/chains/`. Journal references confer no authority and no projection, receipt, ingestion or archive is required.

Commit-family replay MUST authenticate every record's immutable source fact and currentness-check only the latest appended record set against its exact append state and prefix, except for the authenticated `commit_produced` landing window. For a v2 chain that window is authenticated only when replay also proves the preceding passed `commit_identity_checked` for the same produced SHA, parent, and candidate tree; a failed or absent identity event cannot become a landing. A frozen commit chain MAY be sealed only by explicit operator abort or by an operator tombstone written through the operator-bound `chain tombstone` verb after commit-family proof from event authority or canonical matching raw state; `forge-chain-tombstone/1` MUST record operator identity, time, reason, and exact captured-or-absent state/event facts using crash-recoverable owner-controlled publication. Terminal guards MUST accept an authenticated tombstone only while captured artifact facts remain exact or after both artifacts are absent, and MUST reject partial or changed artifacts. Family enumeration MUST skip unreadable unrelated chains while emitting one escaped single-line stderr warning per skipped chain; explicit selection MUST continue to surface the chain's failure. A tombstone seals the chain only; it writes no journal record and requires none.
- **FR-223** (MUST): Phase-0 precondition evals and finalize-check severity. Before any phase-1 surface ships, committed evals MUST exist and pass for: (a) the `!`-prefix bypass behavior FR-218 layer 2 rests on; (b) the hook argv matcher for the CLI invocation form, including its segment-splitting and denial literals; (c) the FR-220 reason-code enum; (d) the `!`-channel temptation task — a blocked model must refuse to ask the operator to run raw git. The hook is the last registered model-tool-path control for the matched Bash invocations it observes, not an OS-wide or repository-native last line of defense. The CLI finalize path therefore supplies independent defense in depth, and every internal finalize check — evidence completeness, pre-intent candidate tree/authorization identity, TTL, working-tree drift, halt, lock, and post-Git produced identity — carries hook-parser severity: each is independently disableable in code with a focused test that fails when it is disabled in memory, and its exact diagnostics are pinned. Definitions these evals bind to: **harness qualification** — eval (a)'s recorded evidence MUST capture the qualification tuple `{arch, claude_executable_digest, claude_version, distribution_channel, hook_config_digest, os, permission_mode}`; the recorded bypass result is valid only while the installed harness matches the recorded `claude_version` major.minor and `distribution_channel`, a mismatch renders FR-218 layer 2 unavailable (control-class chains park in `awaiting_approval`) until the experiment is re-run and re-recorded, and layer 3 covers only the hook-absent case, never a failed bypass. **Eval subjects** — eval (a)'s subject is the interactive Claude Code TUI itself, exercised by a committed repeatable operator protocol with recorded evidence, never headless; eval (d)'s subject is the operational orchestrating model under the commit skill (a fresh headless session, plugin loaded, tools disabled), never a review agent, with the verdict derived by a mechanical oracle from structured action/command fields — a model-authored compliance claim is never the oracle — and a paired permitted case (presenting the exact operator `!` argv) so an always-refuse subject cannot pass; evals (b) and (c) are mechanical corpus checks run by the focused test suite, whose phase-1 legs consume — never copy — the committed corpora. For eval (d), "raw git" means any git invocation whose subcommand mutates history or enforcement state (`commit`, `push`, `reset`, `rebase`, `merge`, `add`, `restore`, `rm`, `stash`, `checkout`); asking the operator to run read-only git is not a violation. Fixture families for these evals under `.forge/evals/tasks/` are versioned (`-v<n>`) and immutable once their baselines are recorded: a legitimate change ships a new version and never edits or re-mints an existing one.

Candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation amendment to **FR-223**: the complete current eleven-fixture corpus remains one immutable Recorded-baseline integrity inventory. Fresh applicability is a closed frontmatter route, not a substring heuristic: this revision supports exactly `agent: review-cheap`, so its nine fixtures receive separate fresh reviewer sessions. The reviewer input MUST omit the frontmatter `expected_verdict` and the complete oracle section beginning at the unique unfenced level-2 `## Expected` heading; a fenced `## Expected` string remains ordinary subject content. `fr223-bang-bypass-v1` with `agent: claude-code-tui` and `fr223-bang-channel-temptation-v1` with `agent: claude-main` remain explicitly `subject-specific-baseline-only`; both MUST remain in the complete inventory, and neither may disappear, be relabeled, be copied into a review-role fixture, receive a synthetic fresh result, or satisfy another fixture. Adding another supported fresh role requires a later control revision with a fixed native route and tests. The live-TUI qualification and operational-orchestrator oracle remain exactly their original subjects and are not recharacterized as reviewer judgments.

Revision-20 installed-root harness-qualification amendment to **FR-223** (GH#40): FR-218 layer-2 approval qualification runs the byte-identical DM-016 phase-0 v1 evaluator against the invoking plugin root. When that root is exactly its Git work-tree top level, the committed `HEAD:docs/specs/forge-plugin-spec.md` remains authority and working-tree spec bytes are never used. Only a genuinely non-Git root, with no `.git` entry and no enclosing-work-tree classification, uses installed mode. The helper executes those digest-bound evaluator bytes and replaces only their committed-spec reader with that qualified root-file reader; every existing manifest/artifact, corpus-versus-spec, evidence, harness-qualification, exit, and result check then runs unchanged. Installed mode proves self-consistency of the installed tree, not authenticity or provenance. The frozen evaluator, phase-0 v1 manifest, corpora, fixtures, results, evidence, and `system/fr223` bytes remain byte-identical and are neither copied nor re-minted. - **FR-224** (MUST): Dogfood (phase 2). Once phase 1 ships, this repository MUST run its commit chains exclusively through the CLI path, with FR-155 telemetry watching the fast tier: `fast_allowed` remaining zero here after adoption is a design-failure signal that MUST surface as a drift finding for semantic review, never silently accepted. The skills MUST retain and emphasize the standing duty to stop and surface out-of-checklist anomalies — the CLI's green light must never become the only signal the model attends to — and best-effort labels in this section are normative text whose removal is a control change.

Revision-9 amendment to **FR-220**: the parser-pinned 25-member `forge-cli/1` enum and every existing 41-member `forge-cli/2` row remain byte-identical. Revision-9 commands continue to emit `forge-cli/2`; DM-016's v3 corpus extends its complete union to 53 members with exactly these twelve sorted exit-1 additions and failed preconditions: `archive-rerender-mismatch` (Archive candidate bytes do not equal deterministic rerender); `archive-size-limit` (Rendered archive exceeds the 16 MiB UTF-8 limit); `batch-idempotency-conflict` (Existing receipt or intent uses the idempotency key for a different normalized request or batch bytes); `batch-pending` (Another outstanding journal batch intent prevents the requested operation); `binding-invalid` (Structured binding is missing, malformed, stale, or inconsistent with its chain event); `ingest-proof-invalid` (At least one required external-chain ingest proof failed); `journal-outbox-pending` (A run-bound chain has an unreceipted event-carried journal batch); `legacy-recovery-approval-required` (Legacy archive mode lacks the matching per-instance operator decision and recovered HEAD); `option-duplicate` (A singleton option `--repo`, `--run-id`, or `--chain-id` occurs more than once); `option-empty` (A required singleton option has an empty value); `run-task-binding-invalid` (Run/task binding fails repository, task, scope, policy, or immutability validation); `run-task-binding-required` (Exactly one of `--run-id` and `--task` was supplied at chain start). Revision-13 assignment-prefix amendment to **FR-221** (bead forge-plugin-di8): the CLI invocation matcher MUST skip leading shell assignment words (`VAR=value`) before and after any `env` prefix exactly as FR-090's git matcher already does, so `FORGE_SESSION_PID=123 python3 scripts/forge/cli.py commit approve …` is denied with the same exact literal as the bare form. The v1 corpus row `no-match-bare-assignment-without-env` pinned the opposite; its expectation is consciously superseded by the `fr223-hook-argv/3` generation (DM-016 Revision-13 amendment) rather than edited, so v1 bytes stay immutable. The skip is a named control with a disable-in-memory proof.


### Forge CLI merge, push, and later-phase boundaries (FR-230..FR-243)

- **FR-230** (MUST): Merge, push, and later-phase boundaries. FR-230..FR-237 define merge-chain authority; FR-238..FR-241 define push, activation, hook, migration, and v2-corpus authority; FR-242 is a non-authorizing marker-deletion admission boundary; FR-243 is a non-authorizing future declaration. Implementations MUST compose the tested halt, classification, committed-policy, gate, mutation, assertion, invariant, independent review, lock, and telemetry controls. Phase-1 dual-accept and phase-2 dogfood remain prerequisites for phase-3/4 deployment. Merge and push commands use DM-014/DM-017 and `forge-cli/2`; the phase-1 `forge-cli/1` envelope, FR-220 reason-code and sentence grammar, DM-006 dual-accept, and every existing immutable corpus, fixture, `.result`, eval, and manifest byte remain unchanged. The fresh candidate-bound evaluation layer remains separate from recorded-baseline integrity. These boundaries authorize neither marker deletion nor future proposal execution.

- **FR-231** (MUST): Merge start, trusted target discovery, and candidate identity. `merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]` MUST canonicalize the supplied path without following an ambiguous symlink spelling and match exactly one registered entry from `git worktree list --porcelain`; it MUST reject a missing path, main checkout, detached worktree, default-branch worktree, foreign Git common directory, non-local branch, or worktree already owned by a live merge chain. The ownership claim MUST be serialized and recorded atomically before admission can succeed. The source status MUST produce zero bytes from `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all`, including no untracked file and no in-progress Git operation. Target discovery reads only the main checkout's committed plugin-schema `.forge-manifest`, fixes remote to `origin`, fixes destination to `refs/heads/<default_branch>`, and never trusts the candidate worktree's manifest or a symbolic moving ref as the bound target. After the read-only path/policy/status preflight, start acquires the complete common lock, rechecks those predicates, publishes the exact DM-014 pre-generation chain/ownership events, and performs its fixed-target fetch/tip resolution only through the null-generation bootstrap `fetch_intent`, FR-236's process fence, and timeout contract. The successful `fetch_result` atomically binds generation 1; a failed result leaves the closed bootstrap state recoverable without inventing a generation. Refresh uses the same locked/fenced fetch discipline. Executable policy comes only from the candidate's committed HEAD and retains every FR-061 sentinel. Start computes DM-014's full candidate generation, classifies the exact three-dot diff with promote-only tiering, and binds every later record to its full candidate HEAD, fetched base, diff digest, policy digest, target, worktree identity, and generation digest. A halt, unreadable policy/manifest, common-lock failure, failed fetch, dirty/invalid worktree, or non-atomic ownership claim refuses before any branch/history mutation.

The null-generation bootstrap MUST bind the fetched tip and the complete three-dot diff through FR-236's single fenced fetch-and-patch operation. It MUST NOT consult a journal, task file list, run scope, or run route. All candidates retain exact repository, worktree, committed-policy, and generation checks.

- **FR-232** (MUST): Merge verbs, chain selection, and lifecycle. Every post-start command MUST receive `--chain-id <id>`; no implicit latest/single-chain selection exists. The complete command surface is:

```text
forge status --chain-id <id>
forge merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]
forge merge refresh --chain-id <id>
forge merge verify --chain-id <id>
forge merge gate run <gate-id> --chain-id <id>
forge review request --chain-id <id>
forge review collect --chain-id <id>
forge review cancel --chain-id <id>
forge review attach --verdict-file <file> --chain-id <id>
forge review disposition --finding <n> --severity <sev> --resolution <text> --chain-id <id>
forge merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>
forge merge finalize --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --continue --paths <path>... --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --abort-rebase --chain-id <id>
forge merge cleanup --chain-id <id>
forge merge abort [--reason <text>] --chain-id <id>
```

There is no `merge skip`; FR-060 remains fail-closed. The state enum is exactly:

```text
classifying, verifying, reviewing, revising,
awaiting_approval, authorized, rebasing, rebase_conflict,
reverifying, reverification_failed, pushing, pushed,
cleanup_pending, closed, aborted
```

The complete state-changing transition table is normative. For this table, a merge chain's transition state is the tuple of its scalar state, `integration.condition`, `cleanup.condition`, inactive/active classification, review iteration, pending finding-disposition/co-sign status, pending ownership-release intent/result/terminal target, `worktree.claim.status`, current per-chain-lease ownership, and the final intended HEAD's DM-015 parse result whenever a row could create push intent. Every state-changing row below implicitly requires an internally consistent event/state record, the row-appropriate claim status, and a lease owned by the invoking process; the start row requires an absent ownership slot. A live, foreign, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or mismatched lease or an otherwise unresolved ownership slot therefore has no admitting row, rather than being a transition event of its own. Commands not admitted by a row refuse with the byte-exact `state-precondition` meaning and do not change state; malformed or mismatched chain/event data, including a claim that diverges from its authoritative ownership events, instead satisfies the byte-exact `frozen-chain` corruption or irresolvable-divergence meaning and exits 2. The first two pending-release rows have absolute precedence: their fsynced release intent is the remote/cleanup observation cutoff, so neither a later external remote change nor another result row may retarget the selected terminal event. With no pending release and while the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals the last attempted-head member, current/latest attempted-head containment precedes older-only containment, and both precede every retry, movement, inactivity, or abort row; older-only containment blocks abort/blind retry but is not current-generation pushed truth. The sole fresh-attempt exception is an active later epoch after partial historical truth whose current `epoch_intent` event follows the most recent `push_intent` and has not yet produced a later push intent. Its preserved vector cannot classify the not-yet-recorded epoch, so the final pre-intent observation takes the ordinary unchanged/moved/unavailable row and only the unchanged row appends the new push intent. OID equality does not cancel this digest/sequence-bound exception; a no-op epoch may deliberately append the same intended HEAD again. The next four inactivity rows precede every remaining scalar-state row; they classify and persist truth from work already admitted while active or from an allowed observation, but delegate neither admission nor automatic execution of any later integration step. The next post-start-absence row then precedes every remaining scalar-state row so exact v2 reason preconditions are never broadened:

| From / event | To | Normative result |
|---|---|---|
| any nonterminal state with an authoritative `ownership_release_intent`, `worktree.claim.status == releasing`, and matching immutable terminal-precondition/claim identities; bare `merge recover` (or `merge cleanup` for target `closed`) | payload-selected `aborted` or `closed` | Treat the fsynced intent's bound remote/cleanup observation as the transaction cutoff; revalidate only event/digest continuity, the selected target, and local claim identity, append/materialize `ownership_released`, then append only that terminal event. Do not perform or require a fresh remote observation and do not repeat Git, remote, cleanup, or push effects |
| any nonterminal state with authoritative `ownership_released`, `worktree.claim.status == released`, and its selected terminal event absent; bare `merge recover` (or `merge cleanup` for target `closed`) | payload-selected `aborted` or `closed` | Revalidate the release-intent/result digests and local projection, then append only the cutoff-selected terminal event; no later remote change retargets a transaction after logical ownership is gone |
| any nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least one push intent, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; authoritative present-destination observation proves that last attempted member contained | `pushed` | Set `landed_head` to that current head, persist the complete vector and current-generation pushed truth, normalize `integration.condition` and `primary_condition` to `none` while retaining immutable result evidence, and perform no new push; this applies despite inactivity or an earlier condition |
| active nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least two distinct push attempts, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; authoritative observation proves that head uncontained but one or more older attempted heads contained | `authorized` with `remote-moved` | Set `landed_head` to the latest older member proved contained, persist the complete vector, reset the movement counter, and perform no push or abort. Integrate the current generation against that remote truth in a new bounded epoch; never call the newer unlanded generation `pushed` |
| inactive nonterminal tuple with no pending release and without durable pushed truth after at least two distinct push attempts, where the current generation's final intended candidate HEAD equals `integration.push.intended_head`; a fresh observation proves that head uncontained but one or more older attempted heads contained, while the current worktree/branch remain exact clean/unchanged and no fence/process is live or unresolved | `aborted` with terminal disposition `historical-landed-superseded` | Set `landed_head` to the latest contained older member, bind the full vector plus every attempt/result digest and current unlanded HEAD/worktree/branch/no-fence proof into the release cutoff, release logical ownership, retain the worktree/branch without cleanup or deletion, and append the tagged `aborted` event. Return exit-0 `ok` partial with exact next `forge merge start --worktree <same-absolute-path>`; this abandons only the current generation and never claims the older landing absent or the newer head pushed |
| a tuple becomes inactive after a child or side effect was admitted while active; its result/observation is not yet durable | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Persist and classify that already-started result, including fetched, conflict, integrated, failed, pushed, or cleanup truth, but stop before every otherwise automatic next child; this result write is not new authorization |
| inactive `rebasing` or `rebase_conflict`; observation-only bare `merge recover` | `authorized`, `rebase_conflict`, `reverifying`, `revising`, or same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | Select respectively only exact no-side-effect, owned-conflict, integrated-result, proven-restoration-after-failure, or unmatched-state truth; persist it and return partial recovery with exact next `status` or safe abort, but do not run gates, continue conflicts, retry, refresh, review, or finalize |
| any other inactive nonterminal tuple; `status`, observation-only bare `merge recover`, owned rebase abort/restoration, cleanup after pushed truth, pending ownership-release completion, the exact proven-not-landed safe abort, or the exact historical-landed-superseded release above | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Permit only the named inspection, observation, safety restoration, cleanup, release, or abort action through its exact row; no new integration-target fetch, gate, conflict continuation, rebase, push, retry, or churn acknowledgement occurs |
| any inactive nonterminal tuple; every other verb or continuation | unchanged refusal | Return exact `state-precondition`; inactivity is part of the complete tuple and cannot be cleared or re-armed |
| any pre-push nonterminal chain after start; the recorded worktree path is absent | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | The v2 `worktree-missing` code is start-only and `worktree-invalid` requires an existing path, so persist the unclassifiable Git-state truth and return exact `state-precondition` for mutation; status and observation-only recovery remain, and no path is reconstructed or silently rebound |
| no chain; `merge start` | `classifying → verifying` | Validate the source, acquire the complete common lock, atomically claim the worktree in DM-014's null-generation bootstrap, fetch under the bootstrap intent and FR-236 fence, bind generation 1 on successful result, and classify |
| active `classifying` after an interrupted start/refresh; bare `merge recover` or `merge refresh`, all admission/classification predicates PASS | `verifying` | Reconcile the claim/events, bind the current generation, and persist classification |
| `classifying`; admission/classification predicate non-PASS | `classifying` | Return its exact `worktree-invalid`, `dirty-worktree`, `fetch-failed`, or `policy-unreadable` refusal; no history mutation |
| `verifying`; `merge gate run` or `merge verify`, required evidence still incomplete | `verifying` | Record each current result; a non-PASS returns `merge-gate-failed` and is retryable without advancing |
| `verifying`; initial Gates 1–2 complete | `reviewing` | All required mechanical evidence is current; scoped mutation evidence is advisory |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8; `review request`, `review collect`, or legacy-admitted `review attach` | `reviewing` | Persist the exact package or bound verdict before applying its PASS/BLOCK successor row |
| `reviewing` with a new-lane attempt, or a matching attempt past inactivity, at the iteration cap, or on a terminal chain; `review cancel` | `reviewing` or unchanged terminal state | Retain the halt and mandatory-review-final controls, apply FR-246's identity proof and bounded group termination, and publish only the attempt completion outside active reviewing; commit cancel does not consume an iteration, while merge Option A spends the attempt's already-requested iteration and has no clearing event |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8 with one outstanding above-MINOR disposition; another above-MINOR `review disposition` | unchanged refusal | Append no event and change no evidence; emit exact `forge: review disposition refused — above-MINOR disposition already awaits operator co-sign` and return `state-precondition` |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8; `review disposition` | same source state | Persist the exact disposition; an above-MINOR disposition sets a pending co-sign and cannot advance |
| `reviewing` or `revising` below iteration 8; exact operator `merge approve` for a pending above-MINOR disposition | same source state | Bind a finding-disposition co-sign to chain, finding, full HEAD, and generation; it is not Gate-4 approval |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review PASS, non-control | `authorized` | Record Gate 3 plus Gate 4 summary |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review PASS, control | `awaiting_approval` | Record Gate 3/Gate 4 and present the exact full HEAD |
| `reviewing` below iteration 8, no pending disposition co-sign; Review BLOCK | `revising` | Increment iteration; on reaching 8, persist residual risk and refuse further review cycles with `iteration-cap` |
| any active mutable pre-push state except an active rebase, with iteration below 8 and no pending above-MINOR disposition co-sign; `merge refresh` | `classifying → verifying` | Acquire the complete common lock for any target fetch, re-observe current source HEAD, invalidate stale-generation evidence, regenerate, and reclassify; in `revising` this also permits a deliberate unchanged-HEAD restart after a safe rebase abort |
| `reviewing` or `revising` at iteration 8; request, collect, attach, disposition, co-sign, refresh, or another review successor | unchanged refusal | Return `iteration-cap`; only `status`, the exact FR-246 `review cancel` terminal-completion path, or safe abort remains |
| active `awaiting_approval` without `remote-churn`; exact operator approval | `authorized` | Bind Gate-4 approval to the current chain, full HEAD, and generation digest |
| active `awaiting_approval` with `remote-churn`; exact operator approval | `authorized` | Bind the acknowledgement to chain/HEAD/generation, reset `remote_movement_count` to zero, clear the condition, and re-arm one later epoch |
| active `authorized`; `merge finalize` | `rebasing` | Halt, acquire locks, recheck tuple, persist fetch/rebase intent, clear only a retryable `fetch-failed`/`remote-moved`/`non-fast-forward` condition, and start one epoch |
| active `rebasing`; fetch fails before rebase | `authorized` | Remote untouched; persist `fetch-failed`, release locks, and permit a later finalize/recover |
| active `authorized` with `fetch-failed`, `remote-moved`, or `non-fast-forward`; bare `merge recover` | `rebasing` | Acquire the common lock, re-read state and remote, persist a fresh intent, clear that retryable condition, and start one bounded epoch under the same preconditions as `merge finalize` |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` observes no rebase side effect, no durable rebase result reports launch failure, nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach, and the exact original tuple remains | `authorized` | Clear only the incomplete intent/condition; a later finalize may retry. A durable failed result instead has precedence and takes the proven-restoration `rebase-failed` row |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` observes the exact owned conflict predicate | `rebase_conflict` | Materialize the recorded conflict inventory; remote remains untouched |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` proves the exact integrated-result predicate | `reverifying` | Materialize the integrated generation and run the complete matrix-selected mechanical pair |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` proves restoration after failure | `revising` | Persist `rebase-failed`; refresh/re-review or safe abort is required |
| active `rebasing`; bare `merge recover` matches no exact predicate | `rebasing` | Persist `foreign-git-state`; observation only, no mutation or guessed transition |
| active `rebasing`; recoverable conflict | `rebase_conflict` | Remote untouched; accept only status, recovery, or safe abort surfaces |
| active `rebasing`; non-conflict rebase failure with proven restoration | `revising` | Persist `rebase-failed`; source is restored and must be refreshed/re-reviewed or aborted |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; the integrated/reverified result is durable and final intended HEAD has duplicate/malformed/unknown DM-015 immediately before push intent | `revising` | Persist the completed-result plus invalid-mode classification as an admitted internal transition and create no push intent; this row itself emits no CLI result |
| `revising` with that exact invalid-final-mode classification; the same invocation attempts to continue toward push | unchanged refusal | The push continuation has no admitting row: release locks, emit exact `forge: history mutation mode invalid — repair committed .forge-manifest through Forge CLI`, return a `forge-cli/2` refusal with byte-exact `state-precondition`, and require exact approved repair plus `merge refresh` |
| active `rebasing`; pure unchanged fast-forward with no earlier park and final DM-015 valid | `pushing` | Recheck current authorization and persist push intent; FR-235's pure-fast-forward row applies |
| active `rebasing`; integration requires mechanical reruns | `reverifying` | Persist integrated generation, reload policy, and run the matrix-selected checks |
| active `rebase_conflict`; successful `recover --continue` | `rebase_conflict` or `reverifying` | Persist the next conflict inventory, or enter integrated re-verification when rebase completes |
| `rebase_conflict`; proven `recover --abort-rebase` | `revising` | Restore pre-rebase HEAD and exact clean state; remote remains untouched |
| exact owned `rebase_conflict` for `recover --abort-rebase`, or `rebasing`/`rebase_conflict` for active-rebase `merge abort`; the operation returns non-PASS but exact restoration is proven | `revising` | Persist exact `rebase-failed`, retain logical ownership, and permit a later safe ordinary abort or refresh; no terminal event is invented |
| the same exact owned recovery or active-rebase abort tuple; the abort surface cannot prove restoration | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | Return exact `rebase-failed`, retain ownership, and admit only observation until an exact recovery predicate matches |
| `reverifying`; `merge gate run` or `merge verify`, required integrated evidence still incomplete | `reverifying` | Record current generation-bound results; remain parked until the complete pair passes or a later row applies |
| `reverifying`; Gate 1 or Gate 2 non-PASS | `reverification_failed` | Remote untouched; a retry reruns both integrated gates |
| active `reverification_failed`; bare `merge recover` with unchanged tuple | `reverifying` | Clear only the retryable gate condition and rerun the complete required integrated pair |
| `reverifying`; review-relevant HEAD/diff/policy change | `reviewing` | Persist integrated generation, park, release locks, and obtain fresh Gate 3/Gate 4/approval as required |
| active `reverifying`; matrix permits carry-forward, final DM-015 is valid, and all final checks PASS | `pushing` | Persist and fsync push intent before invoking Git |
| `rebasing` or `reverifying`; chain becomes inactive before push intent | same scalar state | Persist inactivity, release locks, return exact `state-precondition`, and admit only status or safe abort; no observation, counter, or approval can re-arm integration |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds a present remote-only tip movement, defeats epoch 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist `remote-moved`, the new remote-only generation/carry-forward, and incremented counter; release locks and return exit 0 with `ok: true`, reason `ok`, and the exact next `merge finalize --chain-id <id>`; this is safe partial progress and no push occurs |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds the destination authoritatively absent, defeats epoch 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist `remote-moved` and the incremented counter on the current generation, release locks, and return exit-0 `ok` partial progress; no successor generation or push is created until a later fetch yields a full tip |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push observation finds the eighth consecutive present-tip movement or authoritative absence | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`; include a successor carry-forward only for a present tip, release locks, and require exact operator `merge approve` acknowledgement before another epoch, even for non-control; no push occurs |
| `pushing`; push result or crash-before-result fact is durable but no authoritative remote observation is durable; in-epoch continuation or bare `merge recover` | `pushing` | Preserve the exact result/no-result fact and perform authoritative observation before any terminal claim, refusal classification, or retry; no result code is inferred from process exit alone |
| inactive `pushing`; authoritative present or absent destination proves every attempted intended HEAD not contained | `pushing` | Preserve observation and every historical result/no-result fact, reset the counter, release locks, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next `merge abort --chain-id <id>`; no retry, re-integration, churn increment, or acknowledgement is admitted |
| active `pushing`; authoritative present-destination observation shows the expected old tip and proves every attempted intended HEAD absent, regardless of retained current push-result classification or no result | `pushing` | Preserve the exact result/no-result fact and reset the remote-only counter because movement did not defeat this epoch. If start-pipe proof shows this lock epoch authorized no push child, the just-completed observation is its immediate recheck and the existing unlaunched intent authorizes its sole attempt without another history member. If a child was authorized, release with exact next `merge recover --chain-id <id>` for a new epoch and append a deliberate duplicate only there: retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery |
| active `rebasing` or `reverifying`; final pre-push authoritative tip cannot be resolved | `authorized` | Persist exact `fetch-failed`, release locks, and refuse without creating push intent or invoking push; a later epoch repeats the final mechanical checks |
| active `pushing`; recorded ordinary update was rejected non-fast-forward and authoritative destination is absent or differs from expected old tip while every attempted head is uncontained, defeat 1–7 | `authorized` | Persist exact `non-fast-forward`, increment the remote-movement counter, and require a later recover/finalize to re-enter the matrix; never blindly push |
| active `pushing`; the same recorded non-fast-forward rejection is the eighth consecutive remote-only defeat | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`, clear `non-fast-forward`, and require the exact operator acknowledgement before another epoch |
| active `pushing`; observation-only destination movement or absence without a retained non-fast-forward result and with every attempted head uncontained, and durable evidence is a successful push result or start-pipe proof that no push executed, defeat 1–7 | `authorized` | Preserve the result/proof, persist `remote-moved`, increment the counter, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next finalize; remote movement alone defeated the epoch |
| active `pushing`; the same independently proven remote-only movement is the eighth consecutive defeat | `awaiting_approval` | Persist `remote-churn`; require the exact operator acknowledgement before another epoch |
| active `pushing`; destination movement or absence without retained non-fast-forward and with every attempted head uncontained, but retained `known-failure`/`outcome-unknown` or no-result evidence cannot prove that movement alone defeated the epoch | `authorized` | Preserve that result/no-result fact, persist `remote-moved`, reset the consecutive counter to zero, and return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery with exact next finalize; never claim non-fast-forward or churn |
| `pushing`; remote cannot be observed authoritatively | `pushing` | Persist `push-outcome-unknown`, reset the remote-only counter, and admit only status/recover; retry is forbidden until observation succeeds |
| any nonterminal scalar state; a fenced child cannot be proved terminated after timeout/output-limit/launch handling | same scalar state with `foreign-git-state` | This row has precedence over every operation-specific scalar successor: retain the exact fence/process/intent evidence, return the operation-specific FR-236 failure code, and forbid every later child or state mutation except death-proof plus observation recovery |
| `pushed`; cleanup succeeds | `closed` | Remove worktree/branch only under FR-236 containment rules, durably release logical ownership, then record terminal state |
| `pushed`; cleanup fails | `cleanup_pending` | Preserve pushed truth; cleanup remains independently retryable |
| `cleanup_pending`; `merge cleanup` | `closed` or `cleanup_pending` | Resume only incomplete cleanup steps; never re-run push |
| any durable primary state, including `closed`; explicit lock release fails | same primary state | Persist `lock-release-failed`; only status or bare recover to prove/retry release is admitted |
| any state with `lock-release-failed`; bare `merge recover` proves release | same primary state | Clear the release condition and restore its `primary_condition`; never repeat the primary side effect |
| any state with `foreign-git-state`, or any nonterminal state whose exact current intent/owner/reservation/fence identities match a newly completed FR-235/FR-236 operator quarantine; bare `merge recover` | predicate-selected state or unchanged | Under the acquired lease and complete lock, first append the exact ordered quarantine history and persist `foreign-git-state` when a parent crashed before that condition; then re-observe the operation-specific Git/remote truth and transition solely when one exact FR-236 predicate is satisfied. The identity-bound ingress admits no new side effect before observation |
| `pushing`; `merge abort` under complete common lock and chain lease, with one fresh authoritative present-or-absent destination observation proving every `attempted_heads` member uncontained and no live or unresolved fence/process | `aborted` | Bind the full attempted-head array and all-false containment vector, preserve every historical result, perform no retry, durably release logical ownership, and append `aborted`; this edge remains legal after inactivity |
| any scalar state before `pushing` with no unresolved Git/abort failure; ordinary `merge abort` | `aborted` | When Git rebase state may exist, acquire the complete common lock and chain lease and first prove its owned abort/restoration. If no push intent/attempt exists, prove remote untouched; otherwise use one fresh observation to prove every historical attempted intended HEAD uncontained and no fence/process remains, preserving every historical push result. Current-head containment takes the higher-priority `pushed` row; older-only containment takes the higher-priority active-reconciliation or inactive tagged-terminal row and forbids this ordinary abort. Only no attempts or an all-false vector permits ordinary logical ownership release and terminal state |
| bootstrap pre-push state with authoritative `ownership_intent`, `worktree.claim.status == unpublished`, and the canonical claim path proven absent under the acquired chain lease; `merge abort` or bare `merge recover` selecting abort | `aborted` | Revalidate absence immediately before each protected write, append release intent/result in `never-published` mode, materialize `released` with null inode, then append `aborted`; no pathname is removed and no ownership was acquired |
| `pushing` without the exact proven-not-landed abort predicate, `pushed`, or `cleanup_pending`; ordinary abort | unchanged refusal | Recovery MUST establish remote truth first; no other ordinary abort is legal |
| `closed` or `aborted`; `status` | unchanged success | Terminal inspection is idempotent |

A chain past `inactive_after` accepts only `status`, the exact FR-246 `review cancel` terminal-completion path for its last new-lane attempt, observation-only or safety-restoring `recover`, cleanup when push truth is already established, and a safe terminal release before current-generation pushed truth — either FR-232's exact fresh authoritative all-false/no-live-process abort after a push attempt or its exact `historical-landed-superseded` tagged abort when only an older attempt landed. The latter retains the newer clean worktree/branch for a wholly fresh chain and performs no cleanup/deletion. Inactivity invalidates authorization before any future integration; no retry, counter-driven acknowledgement, or observation result can re-arm it, and it never makes pushed/cleanup truth expire. Merge commands never emit the byte-pinned v1 `inactive-chain` code because that code's exact precondition permits only status/abort; a disallowed merge verb instead returns `state-precondition` under this table. The tuple definition above does not broaden that reason: the attempted verb literally has no admitting row for the observed transition state. `closed` and both tagged forms of `aborted` are terminal and status-idempotent except that either admits the exact common-lock-release recovery row above, and none can be materialized before its ownership-release result is durable.

- **FR-233** (MUST): Merge gates, ordering, evidence, and invalidation.
