Merge mechanical gate IDs are exactly `gate-1`, `stack:<category>`, `assertion-sensor`, and `invariant:<row-number>`; the invariant number is its one-based ordinal among all committed invariant data rows and only `enforcement == "merge"` rows participate. `merge verify` and `merge gate run` MUST preserve this initial order: (1) Gate 1 once; (2) scoped mutation immediately after Gate 1, always advisory and never a gate ID; (3) stack validations for touched categories, merge invariants, and the assertion sensor as Gate 2; (4) unconditional fresh `review-final` Gate 3, including fast constituent commits; (5) Gate 4 summary and exact candidate-HEAD approval for control. Every executable uses committed policy, one complete shell cell as one `bash -c` argv element, an isolated process group, a 65,536-byte combined-output cap, FR-149's 1200-second fail-closed gate timeout or the narrower configured control timeout, and CLI-captured exit, duration, digest, bounded transcript, and generation. After every executable, the CLI MUST recompute full HEAD, exact three-dot diff digest, policy digest, target tuple, and exact clean status. Any unexpected mutation or tuple change invalidates downstream evidence and parks through reclassification; a scoped mutation runner may not silently change candidate bytes. Merge Gate-1/Gate-2 failures use `merge-gate-failed` and leave the remote untouched. Merge approval imports no DM-006 30-minute TTL: it remains current until generation invalidation or chain inactivity.

- **FR-234** (MUST): Merge review, revision, approval, and no-skip law. `review request` packages the exact current three-dot diff, full candidate HEAD, generation digest, committed policy focus/profile, and target; it excludes handoffs, claimed results, and earlier verdicts. For merge review, FR-216's `candidate:` value is the full current candidate HEAD and `package:` is the exact issued package digest; `review attach` applies FR-216's shipped transport grammar to a verdict in a regular owner-controlled file no larger than 65,536 bytes. `review disposition` is instead the exact flag-only FR-232 command, validates the named finding/severity against the attached verdict, and stores its nonempty resolution. An above-MINOR disposition remains in its source `reviewing` or `revising` state until the operator runs the same exact `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>` surface; that record is explicitly `purpose: finding-disposition`, binds the finding number/resolution/HEAD/generation, and cannot satisfy later Gate-4 approval. Review BLOCK enters `revising`, fixes require `merge refresh` plus affected re-verification, and iteration 8 records residual risk and stops; at the cap `merge refresh` cannot reopen the loop. Every HEAD rewrite, changed diff, changed policy, or conflict resolution requires fresh Gate 3 and Gate 4; control requires renewed approval naming the integrated full HEAD. The sole retention exception is DM-014's immutable remote-only carry-forward record under FR-235. Gate 4 records `{candidate_head, generation_digest, diff_summary, control_paths, review_verdict, recorded_at}`; for control, `merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha>` is operator-bound, valid only for the exact current tuple, and stores chain ID plus generation digest with `purpose: gate-4`. The PreToolUse hook denies model-issued merge approval with exactly `forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (merge approve)`. No skip covers merge review, Gate 4, or approval; no approval TTL is imported from the commit chain.

Revision-10 clarification to **FR-234**: the disposition-purpose `merge approve` is unambiguous only because FR-216 permits one outstanding above-MINOR object. It co-signs and clears that sole object atomically. A persisted set with more than one outstanding object is schema/event divergence and exact `frozen-chain`; a second command can never create that state. The complete master-package and single-reviewer rules in FR-216 apply to every oversized merge package, including the exact whole three-dot diff.

Revision-15 headless-lane amendment to **FR-234**: once FR-246's deferral marker is removed, the merge-review `review attach` sentence above is superseded for every new request. Merge Gate 3 launches through the same detached lane and reaches a verdict only through `review collect`; `review attach` remains admitted solely for FR-246's pre-upgrade legacy request shape. Every later FR-234 reference to an “attached verdict” means the current generation's bound verdict: collected for a new-lane request or attached only for that legacy shape.

- **FR-235** (MUST): Bounded locked finalize and re-verification. Every sanctioned common-lock entrant, including `/forge:worktree-merge`, CLI merge/cleanup/abort, standalone push, recovery, and the phase-5 scanner, MUST acquire the same mandatory Git-common-dir portable arbiter. No process selects an independent backend by host capability.

The mandatory arbiter is the pair `agent-rebase.lock.intent` / `agent-rebase.lockdir` on macOS and Linux. Its crash-safe acquisition is the following exact protocol rather than an unowned bare `mkdir`: create and fsync a mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file containing one DM-013-canonical record with exactly `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `host`, `pid`, `nonce`, `operation`, and `started_at`; `schema` is `forge-rebase-lock/1`, `owner_kind` is `merge`, `push`, or `phase5`, `chain_id` is the selected chain ID for merge and null otherwise, `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 operating-system-RNG bits, and `operation` is exactly `start`, `refresh`, `finalize`, `recover`, `cleanup`, `abort`, `push`, or `phase5-scan`. Publish that complete inode by a no-replace hard link at `agent-rebase.lock.intent`, fsync the common directory, unlink the temporary name, create mode-0700 `agent-rebase.lockdir`, hard-link the same inode at `agent-rebase.lockdir/owner.json`, and fsync both directories. Only that matching intent owner may create/materialize the directory, and portable acquisition succeeds only when intent and owner names are nonsymlink regular files with the recorded identical inode and digest.

Where `flock` is available, the portable-arbiter owner then opens the owner-controlled regular file `agent-rebase.lock`, polls for an exclusive kernel lock within the same single 300-second deadline, and after acquisition rewrites/fsyncs that file with the same canonical owner record; the complete common lock requires both layers. Where `flock` is unavailable, the portable arbiter alone is complete. The order is always portable arbiter then optional `flock`, and release is optional `flock` then portable arbiter; no entrant holds `flock` while waiting for the portable namespace or ever treats `flock` alone as complete. Thus a Linux entrant and macOS entrant contend on the same no-replace inode namespace, while `flock` remains a secondary compatibility layer rather than a separate lock backend. A non-crash failure before complete acquisition releases only optional kernel ownership and portable names proven to be that invocation's inode, fsyncs each affected directory, and retries within the one deadline; an unmatched name is never removed.

After every fence/result is resolved, complete-lock release explicitly unlocks/closes the optional `flock` layer while retaining the portable owner; unlock failure retains that owner and records `lock-release-failed`. Portable release then unlinks the inner owner, fsyncs and removes the lock directory, fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the outer intent, and fsyncs the common directory again. The parent fsync between `rmdir` and outer-intent unlink is mandatory: a crash cannot durably remove the identifying intent while leaving an unattributable empty directory. A crash at any portable acquisition or release boundary therefore leaves either no published owner or the complete outer-intent inode that identifies the attempt. The only automatically recoverable published topologies are: (a) outer intent plus lockdir/inner owner all strict-valid and the two names share that inode/digest; (b) a strict-valid outer intent with the lockdir absent; or (c) a strict-valid outer intent plus an owner-controlled nonsymlink mode-0700 lockdir containing no entries. Topologies (b) and (c) are the exact before-mkdir/owner-link acquisition or after-inner-unlink/rmdir release windows; any other missing, extra, symlinked, mismatched, or nonempty topology is unprovable. Reclaim is allowed only when the outer record is strict-valid, its host is local, its PID is proven dead, and any FR-236 in-flight record is absent or separately proven dead. A contender never runs release directly: it MUST first win the recovery reservation below in `fallback-owner` or `fallback-owner-and-fence` mode. A live, foreign-host, PID-reused, malformed, mismatched, or otherwise unprovable owner is never stolen.

All stale-portable-owner and dead-fence recovery uses the separate immutable reservation `<git-common-dir>/agent-rebase.recover`: a proven-dead portable owner with no fence, a proven-dead portable owner plus dead-but-published FR-236 fence, and a current mandatory-portable owner that also holds exclusive Linux `flock` but finds a legacy/anomalous dead fence preventing complete admission. After read-only proof of the applicable same-host PID/PGID absence, a contender creates a complete mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file containing one DM-013-canonical record with exactly `schema`, `recovery_kind`, `host`, `pid`, `nonce`, `started_at`, `stale_owner_inode`, `stale_owner_digest`, `stale_owner_host`, `stale_owner_pid`, `stale_owner_kind`, `stale_owner_chain_id`, `inflight_inode`, `inflight_digest`, `inflight_host`, `inflight_pgid`, `inflight_owner_kind`, `inflight_chain_id`, `owner_dead_at`, and `group_dead_at`. It writes and fsyncs the file, publishes its inode by a no-replace hard link at the reservation path, fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the temporary name, and fsyncs again. `schema` is `forge-rebase-recovery/1`, `recovery_kind` is exactly `fallback-owner`, `fallback-owner-and-fence`, or `flock-held-dead-fence` (the labels remain schema literals although the portable owner is now universal), and the nonce has the same 128-bit grammar. In either `fallback-*` mode `stale_owner_inode`, digest, host, PID, kind, and `owner_dead_at` are non-null; `stale_owner_chain_id` is non-null exactly when that kind is `merge` and is null for `push` or `phase5`. In `flock-held-dead-fence` mode every stale-owner field plus `owner_dead_at` is null because the contender owns the current portable and kernel layers. Every in-flight field plus `group_dead_at` is null in `fallback-owner` mode. In either fence mode `inflight_inode`, digest, host, PGID, kind, and `group_dead_at` are non-null; `inflight_chain_id` is non-null exactly for kind `merge` and null for kind `push`.

The no-replace winner is the sole provisional common-lock recovery owner and every ordinary entrant treats the reservation as unavailable. It may acquire the named chain lease or exclusively re-read/CAS the push singleton, persist every recorded owner/fence identity and death proof in a merge `condition_recorded` event or DM-017 `process` when a named transaction record exists, and clear only pathnames whose inode/digest it revalidates immediately before unlink. In either fallback mode it first revalidates the outer intent against the reservation's stale inode/digest. For topology (b) it creates the mode-0700 lockdir; for (c) it revalidates that exact empty directory; it then hard-links the validated outer inode at the inner owner name and fsyncs both directories. Topology (a) is already complete. Only after the complete matching pair is revalidated does it run the ordinary release sequence, checking the recorded inode/digest immediately before every unlink; any mismatch or failed completion abandons automatic removal. It then releases the chain lease before waiting for another lock form. Either fallback-mode winner competes through ordinary fallback acquisition next; a flock-held winner retains the physical lock it already owns. Once the physical lock is held and any recorded fence is absent with proofs durable, the winner immediately verifies that the canonical reservation still names its own inode/digest, unlinks it, fsyncs the common directory, and only then reacquires the chain lease/re-reads state for ordinary observation recovery. It never holds the chain lease while acquiring the physical lock.

If a reservation claimant crashes, automation never unlinks, replaces, or auto-reclaims the canonical reservation, even after proving its PID dead: portable pathname APIs provide no atomic compare-and-unlink, so recursive automatic takeover would admit an ABA race. Every contender preserves it, exhausts the shared deadline, and returns `rebase-lock-unavailable` with reservation path/inode/digest/owner plus every recorded stale-owner/fence identity. Quarantine is an operator-reserved recovery only after the operator proves repository-wide process quiescence and the claimant/recorded owner/recorded PGID deaths, engages `AGENT_HALT`, and reconciles every named chain/singleton with Git and remote observations. Under that halt the operator constructs DM-017's exact `recovery-reservation` manifest entry for the canonical reservation as its sole artifact, revalidates the reservation inode/content and absent destination immediately before moving exactly that inode to same-directory `agent-rebase.recover.quarantine-<manifest-digest>`, and fsyncs the common directory; a mismatch aborts without removal. The operator then re-proves quiescence, deaths, and state reconciliation, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may a new contender publish a fresh no-replace reservation. Before any new side effect, the first later complete-lock holder appends that entry to the affected singleton's DM-017 quarantine history or to a merge `condition_recorded` payload with the same exact ordered-history shape, then performs operation-specific observation; a later owner/fence quarantine appends rather than replaces it. The CLI never performs or recommends an unconditional move. Thus concurrent automated successors cannot ABA-remove one another's reservation and at most one provisional recovery writer exists. Linux dead-fence recovery and every fallback recovery require the next ordinary holder to observation-reconcile any named merge chain or push singleton before a new side effect; recovery never treats operation outcome as known. The single 300-second budget covers physical acquisition, reservation publication, stale-owner proof, the in-flight check when present, and FR-236's exact pre-intent admission recheck; timeout, an unprovable owner, a stale reservation, or absence of either portable mechanism returns `rebase-lock-unavailable` with path/inode/digest/owner evidence. Commands needing both locks otherwise acquire the complete common rebase lock before the per-chain lease and re-read state after both; this reservation is the sole bounded exception and counts as the common-lock predecessor only for proof persistence and stale-name clearing, never for a new Git/remote side effect. One lock epoch performs at most one integration-target fetch that selects `fetched_tip`, one rebase/integration attempt, one matrix-selected mechanical suite, and one push attempt. In addition it may perform at most one separately fenced pre-attempt authoritative observation — ordinary final-pre-push observation or recovery reconciliation of a prior intent — and, if it makes its sole push, one separately fenced post-attempt outcome observation. An old-tip reconciliation in a no-push-yet recovery epoch doubles as the immediate pre-push check; it is never followed by a redundant third observation before push. Either bounded observation may fetch the exact observed ref object needed for identity/ancestry proof but cannot start another integration, suite, or push. A present movement found by the pre-attempt observation may materialize only the corresponding remote-only successor generation from that observation. The epoch never loops indefinitely or holds the lock across external review/approval.

A live, foreign-host, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or unprovable portable owner/topology is permanently unavailable to automation; the CLI neither steals it nor prints an immediately executable removal. Only this operator-reserved recovery is admitted: the operator proves repository-wide and all-relevant-host quiescence/death of every possible owner, engages `AGENT_HALT`, proves no recovery reservation exists, and reconciles every nonterminal merge chain and push singleton against Git and the remote. The operator records and then immediately revalidates one DM-017 `portable-owner` manifest entry whose source-sorted artifacts are the extant canonical outer intent and lockdir top-level nodes without following a symlink; the lockdir directory digest binds its inner owner and every other entry. Still under the halt, each exact artifact, lockdir first and outer intent second, is renamed within the common directory to its distinct nonexisting `<source-basename>.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` destination and the common directory is fsynced after each rename; any identity, content, entry-set, or target-existence mismatch stops without another move. This is quarantine, never deletion. The operator then re-proves quiescence and reconciles every affected transaction and lock-release fact, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may an ordinary entrant acquire fresh names. Before a new side effect, that first complete-lock holder appends the one manifest entry to the exact ordered DM-017 `quarantine` history in every affected push singleton or persists a merge `condition_recorded` payload with exactly `condition`, `quarantine`, and `observation_digest`, where `quarantine` has that same ordered-history shape; it then performs the operation-specific observation. Earlier reservation/owner/fence entries are never overwritten. A topology that cannot be completely manifested/revalidated remains permanently blocked.

If rebase changes the candidate and external review/approval is required, the epoch runs integrated Gate 1, scoped mutation, and Gate 2; persists the integrated generation; parks; and releases the lock. Gate 3, Gate 4, and approval occur outside the lock. A later finalize reacquires, proves tuple identity and the expected remote tip, and reruns Gate 1, scoped mutation, and Gate 2 in that final epoch before push. Immediately before push it performs a fresh authoritative remote-tip read. A change detected by that read parks without pushing. A present-tip movement after the successful read is left to ordinary non-fast-forward rejection; a deletion in that unavoidable final window can instead let the ordinary non-force refspec recreate the destination, and that exact limitation is classified only from push result plus post-attempt observation under FR-236. Rows are applied from most specific to least specific — conflict resolution, then policy/diff/HEAD change, then remote-only movement, then the unchanged row — using this normative matrix:

| Observation under lock | Required before push |
|---|---|
| Bound candidate HEAD changed | Invalidate hash-bound evidence and return `candidate-stale`; reclassify only through `merge refresh` |
| Recorded pre-push worktree path is absent after start | Persist `foreign-git-state` and return exact `state-precondition`; neither start-only `worktree-missing` nor existing-path `worktree-invalid` is broadened |
| Existing worktree identity becomes invalid, or checkout/remote/branch/destination is no longer the fixed target | Return the byte-exact `worktree-invalid` or `push-target-invalid` condition respectively; never silently retarget |
| Final intended HEAD's committed DM-015 mode is duplicate, malformed, or unknown | First persist the completed-result/invalid-mode classification and enter `revising` as an admitted internal transition without an envelope; create no push intent. The attempted push continuation from that parked tuple is not admitted, changes no further state, emits the exact invalid-mode diagnostic, and returns byte-exact `state-precondition`; approved repair plus refresh is required |
| Remote unchanged; HEAD/diff/policy unchanged; no earlier park | Recheck authorization and push; current pure-fast-forward treatment retains initial gates |
| Remote advanced but final HEAD, diff, and policy remain identical | Reload/reclassify; rerun Gate 1, mutation, and Gate 2; retain Gate 3/Gate 4/approval only through DM-014's explicit carry-forward record |
| Candidate HEAD rewritten, even with identical diff | Rerun Gate 1/mutation/Gate 2, then fresh Gate 3 and Gate 4; renew control approval |
| Diff digest changed | Reclassify and repeat Gates 1–4; renew control approval |
| Policy digest changed | Reclassify and repeat Gates 1–4 because commands and review context changed |
| Conflicts were resolved | Repeat Gates 1–4 unconditionally; approval names integrated HEAD |
| Any in-lock gate is non-PASS and the tuple is unchanged | Remote untouched; enter `reverification_failed` |
| Any in-lock gate or scoped mutation changes HEAD, diff, or policy | Remote untouched; persist the new generation, release the lock, enter `classifying → verifying`, and then apply the specific HEAD/diff/policy row before judgment |
| Tuple changes after re-verification | Refuse before push and park through the applicable row |
| Final authoritative remote-tip resolution fails before push intent | Persist `fetch-failed`, return to `authorized`, release the lock, and perform no push; the next epoch reruns the final mechanical checks |
| Final authoritative query succeeds but proves the destination ref absent | Persist `remote-moved` on the current generation, increment the remote-only counter, release the lock, and perform no push or ref creation; no successor generation exists until a later query yields a full tip |
| Remote changes during the final pre-push window | A detected pre-invocation change or absence parks; a later present-tip race is ordinary non-fast-forward, while a deletion after the read may let the ordinary non-force refspec recreate the destination; persist push intent/result and classify only by post-attempt observation |
| Push succeeds | Record and fsync `pushed` before attempting cleanup |

The consecutive-remote-movement counter is persisted in `integration`, increments only when remote movement or authoritative destination absence alone defeats an epoch, resets after an epoch ends for any other reason or after push, and reaches `remote-churn` on the eighth such defeat. After push intent, “alone” requires either the retained actual non-fast-forward rejection, a successful push result followed by an authoritative later movement/absence, or start-pipe proof that no push executed; a known independent failure, unknown outcome, or ambiguous missing result never increments the counter. Defeats one through seven park in `authorized`; the eighth parks in `awaiting_approval`, and FR-232 defines both transitions and the sole resume verbs. Another epoch then requires the operator-bound acknowledgement in FR-232. Liveness is claimed only under eventual remote quiescence. Explicit lock release occurs after the primary state/outcome is durable; failure preserves that primary truth, records `lock-release-failed`, and admits only status/recovery until release can be proved.

FR-235's preliminary read-only identity/death proof may be used solely to construct the immutable recovery reservation and authorizes neither lifecycle classification nor clearing; after reservation publication, a surviving physical fence may be inspected, classified, or cleared only while holding that reservation, and common-lock acquisition MUST NOT clear it before reservation-held lifecycle recovery has classified it.

- **FR-236** (MUST): Rebase, push, cleanup, and crash recovery. Before fetch/rebase, conflict continue/abort, push, worktree removal, or branch deletion, the CLI MUST persist and fsync the applicable merge event or standalone singleton intent before the side effect and MUST forbid it if intent persistence fails. Before a merge chain has generation 1, that merge event is exactly DM-014's `fetch_intent` with `generation_digest: null`; its event digest is the in-flight fence's `intent_digest`, and only a successful `fetch_result` may bind the first non-null generation. Every later merge side-effect intent is generation-bound normally. Recovery observes actual Git metadata, HEAD, the worktree/index, and a freshly fetched/queried remote; event replay or a stale tracking ref alone never proves an external side effect.

The sole pre-side-effect-intent subprocess exception is the repeated admission recheck and deterministic reflog-path resolution after physical common-lock ownership but before the corresponding merge chain, push singleton, or DM-017 rebase intent can legally contain that result. Its closed direct-argv set is `git worktree list --porcelain`, `git rev-parse --git-dir --git-common-dir --show-toplevel HEAD`, `git rev-parse --git-path logs/HEAD`, `git rev-parse --git-path logs/<fixed-full-branch-ref>`, `git symbolic-ref -q HEAD`, `git cat-file blob <fixed-commit>:<fixed-manifest-or-policy-path>`, and `git -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.untrackedCache=false --no-optional-locks status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all --ignore-submodules=none`, with fixed `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`. The implementation MUST first qualify that its Git honors `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`; otherwise this mechanism is unavailable and fails closed. These commands read only object/ref/worktree/reflog-path identity, disable optional index refresh, configured fsmonitor/untracked-cache helpers, and promisor-object lazy fetch, invoke no hook, filter, pager, textconv, credential, network, or user shell, and cannot mutate Git or repository bytes. Reflog bytes are then `lstat`-validated and hashed in-process, not through another command. Every exception result is revalidated under the physical lock immediately before its first durable state use. The commands still use a direct argv, isolated process group, 65,536-byte combined cap, TERM/0.25-second/KILL escalation, and the one remaining 300-second common-lock acquisition deadline. A syntactically complete observation maps to the ordinary dirty/identity/policy or reflog-baseline result; launch, cap, timeout, unsupported no-lazy-fetch semantics, or unproved completion is retried only within that deadline and then returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable` because the complete common lock was never admitted. No state or history side effect follows such failure. An unkillable member of this exact no-write set cannot race a later mutation, and this exception MUST NOT admit any other Git verb, option, config key, helper, or subprocess.

Every other subprocess executed while the common lock is held — matrix gates, fetch/tip resolution, authoritative remote or attribution observation, rebase/continue/abort, push, containment checks, worktree removal, and branch deletion — MUST have a durable intent, use FR-236's in-flight fence, and run as a direct argv in an isolated process group with a 65,536-byte combined stdout/stderr cap. Git/remote/cleanup operations have a fixed 1200-second timeout; matrix gates retain FR-233's configured narrower timeout under the same 1200-second outer maximum. Timeout or output-cap breach sends SIGTERM to the group, waits 0.25 seconds, then sends SIGKILL; launch failure follows the same fail-closed outcome, and an unkillable survivor or unprovable group exit sets merge `foreign-git-state` or standalone-push `recovery_required` before any later side effect. After any termination, observation precedes retry. Matrix-gate failures use `merge-gate-failed`; fetch/tip failures use `fetch-failed`; an exact owned recoverable conflict has precedence and uses `rebase-conflict`, while every rebase/continue/abort failure outside that predicate uses `rebase-failed`; a timed-out or output-capped push remains outcome-unknown until observation and uses `push-outcome-unknown`; attribution-observation failure preserves its observation-only wrapper, after which the same invocation's attempted classification continuation has no admitting row and returns exact `state-precondition`; and containment/removal/deletion failures use `cleanup-failed`. Bounded output is preserved in evidence with its digest and launch/timeout/output-limit flags.

The null-generation bootstrap uses one fence and one isolated process group for a composite child that performs exactly the epoch's sole integration-target fetch followed by one read-only full-patch subprocess over `<full-remote-tip>...<full-candidate-head>`. Only PASS from both constituents may produce successful fetch evidence. The patch subprocess's exact direct argv is `git --no-pager --no-replace-objects -c core.quotePath=false -c color.ui=false -c diff.renames=copies -c diff.renameLimit=0 -c diff.algorithm=myers -C <canonical-worktree> diff --patch --no-color -O/dev/null --find-renames=50% --find-copies=50% --find-copies-harder -l0 --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --ignore-submodules=none --diff-filter=ACDMRTUXB <full-remote-tip>...<full-candidate-head> --`. Before launch it unsets every inherited variable whose name begins `GIT_CONFIG_` and exact `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_NAMESPACE`, `GIT_PREFIX`, `GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF`, `GIT_DIFF_OPTS`, `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE`, and `GIT_GRAFT_FILE`, then overlays exactly `LC_ALL=C`, `LANG=C`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, `GIT_PAGER=cat`, and `PAGER=cat`. The implementation MUST qualify that Git honors `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH`; inability to establish that property fails closed. The fixed-object patch read disables replacements, optional locks, lazy fetch, external diff, textconv, paging, system/global config, and inherited repository/config injection, and invokes no hook, filter helper, credential helper, network, user shell, index refresh, ref update, worktree write, or object write. The isolated process group, 1200-second fail-closed timeout, TERM/0.25-second/KILL escalation, and complete-result proof remain mandatory. Patch stdout alone is streamed unbounded into SHA-256, never buffered whole, stored, parsed, or echoed; its digest is its only residue, and every other output retains the 65,536-byte combined cap.

Bootstrap evidence keeps its existing immutable owner-controlled sidecar at `.forge/chains/<chain-id>/scope-fetch-<fetch-intent-digest>-<inflight-digest>.json` with schema `forge-run-scope-fetch-binding/2` and exactly sixteen members: `schema`, `chain_id`, `fetch_intent_digest`, `scope_request_digest`, `candidate_head`, `remote_tip`, `command_template_digest`, `command_digest`, `full_patch_command_digest`, `full_patch_output_digest`, `environment_digest`, `publication`, `retained_inflight`, `child_result`, `recorded_at`, and `digest`; `digest` hashes the DM-013 canonical mapping of the preceding fifteen members. From Revision 22 every bootstrap is the former unbound form: the three scope-specific members `scope_request_digest`, `command_template_digest` and `command_digest` are null, no name-status or other scope constituent is launched, and `child_result.output_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the exact zero-byte name-status-output slot, distinct from `full_patch_output_digest`. The schema name, path, member count and digest arithmetic are deliberately unchanged by the operator ruling of 2026-10-06 recorded in §5A so the engine's publication and recovery code and every existing sidecar keep working; a reader never treats the three scope members as authority, and a sidecar written before Revision 22 with populated scope members remains readable. `remote_tip` is the full OID returned by the successful fixed-target fetch; no later tracking ref or remote query may supply it. `full_patch_command_digest` hashes the exact argv above, `full_patch_output_digest` hashes its complete stdout and MUST equal the materialized DM-014 `diff_sha256`, and `environment_digest` binds the exact unset/overlay contract above. `fetch_intent` payloads carry no scope request; a successful bootstrap `fetch_result` copies the non-null binding with `scope_proof: null`, and a failure before valid publication records both scope members null and retains `classifying` with the existing `fetch-failed` outcome.

`publication` has exactly `canonical_path`, `temporary_path`, `device`, and `inode`; the canonical path is the repository-relative sidecar path above, the temporary path is exactly that path plus `.tmp-<retained-inflight-nonce>`, and nonnegative device/inode values come from `fstat` immediately after exclusive temporary-file creation. `retained_inflight` has exactly `path`, `device`, `inode`, `inflight_digest`, `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `operation`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `started_at`, `intent_digest`, and `nonce`; it copies the strict-valid `forge-rebase-inflight/1` path, identity, digest, and record, with owner kind `merge`, matching chain IDs, intent digest equal to `fetch_intent_digest`, and operation `fetch` or `tip-resolution`. `child_result` has exactly `operation`, `intent_digest`, `inflight_digest`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `exit`, `output_digest`, `launch_failed`, `timed_out`, `output_limit_exceeded`, `group_dead_at`, `resolved_tip`, and `recorded_at`. It is the result for the whole composite child; its operation, intent, fence, host, PID, and PGID match `retained_inflight`, exit is zero, all three failure flags are false, group death is proved, and `resolved_tip` equals `remote_tip`. `output_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the empty auxiliary-output slot; it is distinct from `full_patch_output_digest` and cannot replace it.

After the composite child succeeds and group death is proved, the complete common lock and chain lease remain held and the original fence remains present. The parent requires the canonical and temporary sidecar paths absent, creates the temporary as a mode-0600 `O_EXCL`/no-follow regular file, obtains its descriptor identity, writes and fsyncs the complete DM-013 canonical sidecar with no trailing LF, and revalidates the fence path/device/inode/bytes. It publishes the same inode at the canonical name by no-replace hard link, fsyncs the directory, revalidates both names as that same recorded inode/content with link count two, unlinks only the recorded temporary name after an immediate no-follow identity/content check, fsyncs, then reopens the canonical name under a 16,384-byte cap and requires the recorded inode/content with link count one and temporary name absent. Exact mode, bytes, self-digest, publication, intent, fence, environment, candidate, command, and child-result bindings are mandatory.

Recovery classifies the two deterministic names under both locks. Both absent means no valid publication; canonical absent plus a strict-valid one-link recorded temporary inode resumes only publication; both names as the strict-valid recorded same inode/content with link count two resumes only temporary unlink/fsync and final validation; canonical present as the strict-valid recorded one-link inode with temporary absent is complete. A crash after unlink but before directory fsync resolves to one of the latter two states and is idempotent. Every other topology, link count, identity, path, mode, content, missing retained fence before `fetch_result`, wrong intent, or conflict is exact `frozen-chain`; no unrelated name or inode is removed. A completed canonical sidecar is reusable only when byte-identical. Recovery after valid publication copies that evidence into successful `fetch_result`, binds generation 1 and enters `verifying`, and reruns neither fetch nor patch. Failure or crash without complete valid evidence cannot invent a tip or diff: after the normal process-death proof it records failed `fetch_result` with null evidence, retains `classifying`, and uses `fetch-failed`. The result and evidence are durable before the original fence is removed. The one-fetch epoch and single-fence laws remain unchanged, and evidence is never overwritten or unlinked by automation. Existing sidecars and event copies remain readable under their recorded schema without rewrite; their scope fields are historical data only, and they cannot authorize scope admission or manufacture a missing fetch or patch proof.

The durable intent for operation `gate` is the current sealed DM-014 gate-plan position. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-merge-gate-intent/1", chain_id, epoch_intent_digest, seal_event_digest, generation_digest, policy_digest, suite_digest, cursor, kind, id, authorizing_event_digest}`. `epoch_intent_digest` is the current `integration.epoch.intent_digest`; the next five identity members are the exact current sealed-plan values; `kind` and `id` are its cursor-selected suite member; and `authorizing_event_digest` equals `seal_event_digest` at cursor zero or the immediately preceding `gate_recorded` event digest that durably advanced to a later cursor. If a retry `epoch_intent` directly sealed the plan, the epoch and seal digests are equal. Those values MUST be re-derived from the authenticated event tail and materialized state before fence publication and again before the start byte. A `gate_recorded` result copies that intent digest and fence digest, carries DM-014's exact position members, and advances the cursor as DM-014 requires.

If the parent crashes after a gate start byte and before `gate_recorded`, the surviving fence plus unchanged sealed cursor identify the exact suite position; a live/unprovable child blocks all later positions, and proven death permits only result recovery or a rerun of that same position under a fresh fence after tuple observation. Recovery never advances from absence of a result. An unsealed or empty-complete plan cannot authorize a gate. A mismatched seal, plan, authorizing event, generation, policy, cursor, operation, intent digest, or result is exact `frozen-chain`, not an unattributed retry or generic gate failure; an authenticated matrix successor or gate-caused tuple change instead takes DM-014's ordinary seal or park law.

The `remote-observation` operation has two exact intent phases so recovery never conflates a failed final read with an unknown push outcome. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-remote-observation-intent/1", transaction, chain_id, attempt_identity, phase, push_intent_digest}`. `transaction` is `merge` or `push`; `chain_id` is the selected merge chain or null for standalone; and `phase` is exactly `final-prepush` or `post-push`. Merge `attempt_identity` is the current `epoch_intent` event digest. Standalone `attempt_identity` is the active recovery `attempt_nonce` for `final-prepush` and the current last `push_intent.attempts` member's nonce for `post-push`; thus a later push-only retry is distinct even though it reuses an earlier integration's immutable rebase record. For merge, `push_intent_digest` is null in `final-prepush` and is the current `push_intent` event digest in `post-push`. For standalone, it is null in `final-prepush` and in `post-push` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{attempt_number, attempt_nonce, expected_old_tip, intended_head, destination_ref, intended_at}`, using the current one-based `push_intent.attempts` index and its immutable intent fields. Historical push intents may remain present during a later `final-prepush` phase; null means only that this exact active ledger identity has no push intent yet. The persisted chain/singleton tuple supplies every mapping member before the fence is published. A missing, wrong-phase, wrong-attempt, or wrong-push-intent binding is event/state divergence and returns exact `frozen-chain`. Death/quarantine recovery of `final-prepush` re-enters only the ordinary final-tip observation rows and uses `fetch-failed` if authoritative truth remains unavailable; recovery of `post-push` uses only the push-outcome observation rows and `push-outcome-unknown` when truth remains unavailable.

`attribution-observation` is a standalone-only, read-only operation admitted only by strict-valid `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase` with a non-null DM-017 `process.attribution`. Its fence `intent_digest` is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical bytes for exactly `{schema: "forge-attribution-observation-intent/1", repository, destination_ref, attempt_nonce, attribution}`, where repository and destination ref are the current singleton values, `attempt_nonce` equals both the current active nonce and the attribution object's originating attempt nonce, and `attribution` is its exact six-key object byte-for-byte. Its direct argv may perform only FR-238's Git/reflog identity and predicate reads; it cannot rebase, continue, abort, create a push intent, or push. A missing/mismatched active nonce, changed attribution, wrong digest, or use with merge ownership is exact event/state divergence and `frozen-chain`. Its durable result is appended normally but never replaces the original attribution. If its group becomes foreign/unprovable, `foreign-process-state` retains both the original attribution and this observation fence identity; after exact death or quarantine recovery, the transaction restores the same attribution wrapper without classifying or replaying the original rebase/abort, and a later invocation may repeat only the read-only observation. A failed or completed-but-unmatched observation leaves the wrapper unchanged; the same invocation's attempted classification continuation is then unadmitted and returns its existing observation-only `state-precondition`.

The common rebase lock includes a repository-wide subprocess fence at `<git-common-dir>/agent-rebase.inflight`; possession of the mandatory portable arbiter and optional secondary `flock` without resolving that path and any recovery reservation is not possession of the complete common lock. After the operation intent is durable and before any child can execute, the parent forks a child that establishes its isolated process group, acknowledges that PID/PGID to the parent, and blocks on an owner-controlled start pipe; pipe EOF before an explicit start byte makes the child exit without `exec`. The parent writes and fsyncs a complete mode-0600 same-directory temporary regular file and publishes it by no-replace hard link at the fence path. Its DM-013-canonical record has exactly `schema`, `owner_kind`, `chain_id`, `operation`, `host`, `pid`, `pgid`, `started_at`, `intent_digest`, and `nonce`; `schema` is `forge-rebase-inflight/1`, `owner_kind` is `merge` or `push`, `chain_id` is the selected chain or null, `operation` is exactly `gate`, `fetch`, `tip-resolution`, `remote-observation`, `attribution-observation`, `rebase`, `continue`, `abort`, `push`, `containment`, `worktree-remove`, or `branch-delete`, `intent_digest` is the SHA-256 of the durable event/singleton intent authorizing that child, and `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 operating-system-RNG bits. `attribution-observation` requires `owner_kind: push` and null `chain_id`; any other owner pairing is invalid. The parent fsyncs the common directory, unlinks the temporary name, fsyncs again, and only then sends the start byte. A publication attempt that does not complete closes the pipe so no command executes, removes only its own proven temporary/inode names, and retries acquisition/publication within the operation's one fresh 300-second publication budget after revalidation; an existing or unresolved fence is never overwritten. Only deadline exhaustion or proof that no required portable no-replace-link/fsync mechanism exists returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable`; one failed publication attempt never emits that code immediately.

Each fenced operation receives one fresh bounded 300-second publication budget; a retry occurs only after the required lock and publication-state revalidation and consumes without resetting that same budget; deadline exhaustion still returns exact `rebase-lock-unavailable`; the acquisition deadline never refuses a first publication attempt.

The fence remains while the process group may exist. On ordinary completion, the parent durably records the exit/result tied to the fence digest before it verifies and unlinks that inode/content and fsyncs the common directory; a standalone result is appended to DM-017 `process.results`, and any later observation subprocess receives a new fence and appends a distinct result rather than overwriting it. After a parent crash, a physical-lock holder or FR-235 provisional recovery owner validates the fence. When a same-host process group yields `ESRCH` from `kill(-pgid, 0)`, that exclusive owner first persists the exact group-death proof together with the fence's operation, intent digest, fence digest, host, and PGID in the selected chain's `condition_recorded` event or DM-017 `process`, then may unlink and fsync the fence; the transaction remains nonterminal, and actual Git/remote classification runs next under a newly published observation fence before any new mutating operation is admitted. For standalone rebase or abort, selection of `rebase-attribution-unresolved` atomically copies that persisted operation/intent/fence identity into DM-017 `process.attribution` before any later observation fence may replace the selected-fence fields; merge uses its immutable event payload equivalently. A crash between those steps is safe because group death and the complete operation identity are durable, the physical lock or recovery reservation excludes a concurrent sanctioned mutator, and the nonterminal chain/singleton still requires identity-bound observation. A live group, foreign host, reused/unprovable PGID, or malformed/mismatched fence is never cleared automatically and consumes the same 300-second common-lock budget before `rebase-lock-unavailable`. A timed-out child whose group is not proven gone therefore leaves both the chain/singleton fail-closed condition and this global fence, so no other merge chain or standalone push can mutate the repository concurrently with a survivor. Fence unlink/fsync failure after a primary result is durable is failure to release the complete common rebase lock and uses only `lock-release-failed`.

For a foreign-host, reused/unprovable-PGID, malformed, or mismatched fence, DM-017's `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence` and merge `foreign-git-state` name an operator-reserved path, not an automated transition. The operator MUST establish repository-wide and all-relevant-host quiescence/death, engage `AGENT_HALT`, reconcile every nonterminal chain/singleton plus Git and remote truth, and resolve or quarantine any FR-235 owner/reservation first. The operator then constructs one exact DM-017 `inflight-fence` manifest entry and immediately revalidates the fence's exact `lstat` identity and SHA-256 of its regular bytes or symlink target without following it; a directory or other malformed type additionally requires the same sorted nonrecursive entry manifest as FR-235. Under the halt, the exact unchanged fence is renamed within the common directory to the nonexisting `agent-rebase.inflight.quarantine-<manifest-digest>` destination and the common directory is fsynced; any identity/content/entry/target mismatch stops. The operator re-proves quiescence and reconciliation, retains the quarantine artifact and manifest, and explicitly clears the halt. The first subsequent complete-lock holder MUST acquire the selected chain lease or exclusively CAS the singleton, append that entry after every earlier recovery-stage entry in the exact quarantine history, persist the operation-specific Git/remote observation, and only then transition from `foreign-git-state` or `foreign-process-state`; it cannot launch a new side effect first. For a merge, the operator-quarantine `condition_recorded` payload has exactly `condition`, `quarantine`, and `observation_digest`, where condition is `foreign-git-state`, quarantine has DM-017's exact ordered-history shape, and observation digest is lowercase SHA-256 of the applicable canonical Git/remote observation; for standalone push it is stored in `recovery.process.quarantine`. If complete quiescence, manifest revalidation, or transaction reconciliation cannot be proved, the fence and `operator-quiescence` state are permanently blocked.

Each rebase intent records a unique 32-lowercase-hex `operation_nonce` drawn from 128 bits of the operating-system cryptographic RNG, pre-rebase HEAD, fetched tip, full branch ref, generation digest, and the exact action prefix `forge-merge-rebase:<chain-id>:<generation-digest>:<operation-nonce>`. The CLI sets that value as `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` for the rebase and every continuation. A crash-after-rebase integrated result is proven only when all of these hold: no durable result reports launch failure, non-conflict nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach; the recorded branch remains checked out in the recorded worktree; no Git operation metadata remains; status is exactly clean; the recorded fetched tip is an ancestor of observed HEAD; and the contiguous newest branch-reflog segment starts with old OID equal to recorded pre-rebase HEAD, ends with new OID equal to observed HEAD, contains no later entry, and every entry's action begins with that exact unguessable prefix. An exact owned conflict instead follows the conflict row despite Git's nonzero result. Any other failed leg sets `foreign-git-state`; no integrated generation is invented.

For `rebase_conflict`, `--continue` and `--abort-rebase` are mutually exclusive. Recovery MUST prove the rebase metadata belongs to the selected worktree, chain, nonce, and pre-rebase generation. The authorized set is the nonempty, duplicate-free repository-relative path set from exact NUL-delimited `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U -z --`; when each conflict is first observed, the CLI also records SHA-256 digests of exact NUL-delimited `git ls-files --stage -z` and `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all` records after excluding that authorized set. Every `--paths` value is normalized repository-relatively, rejects absolute/empty/`.`/`..` traversal, NUL, duplicate, and pathspec-magic input, and MUST equal the authorized set. Before staging, every non-conflict index/status record MUST remain byte-identical to its recorded baseline; this admits Git's legitimate rebase-staged state but rejects unrelated staged, tracked, or untracked contamination. The CLI stages only with `git --literal-pathspecs add -- <normalized-paths>` and continues noninteractively under the recorded action prefix using recorded commit messages. Further conflicts replace the authorized set and both baselines and remain `rebase_conflict`. If continuation completed before a crash, only the exact integrated-result predicate above advances to `reverifying`; otherwise `foreign-git-state` remains observation-only. `--abort-rebase` MUST prove restoration of the recorded pre-rebase HEAD, absence of Git operation metadata, exact clean state, and unchanged branch/worktree identity before entering `revising`. A Git state matching neither recorded pre-rebase, exact owned conflict, nor proven integrated state sets `foreign-git-state` and returns `state-precondition` for every mutating verb.

An explicit `merge recover --continue` or `merge recover --abort-rebase` outside the selected chain's exact owned `rebase_conflict` tuple MUST return exit 1 `state-precondition`, emit a diagnostic naming the actual scalar state, append no event, make no state change, and MUST NOT be routed as bare `merge recover`; active-rebase `merge abort` retains its existing behavior, and explicit flags are never silently ignored.

Before each push, `pushing` records expected old remote tip, intended full HEAD, destination ref, and the append-only attempted-head history. It also proves the current intended HEAD descends from that expected old tip and invokes exactly the ordinary non-force refspec through direct argv `git push --porcelain origin <intended-head>:<destination-ref>` with `LC_ALL=C`: no leading `+`, `--force`, `--force-with-lease`, deletion refspec, or other force/CAS option is permitted, and `--porcelain` changes only stable result reporting. An actual non-fast-forward classification requires a completed nonzero child plus exactly one target status record whose tab-delimited flag is `!`, whose ref field's destination is the exact recorded ref, and whose summary is exactly either `[rejected] (non-fast-forward)` or `[rejected] (fetch first)`; these are Git's two stable ordinary stale-destination rejection summaries. Malformed, missing, duplicate-target, truncated, or every other rejection output never selects that classification. Launch/timeout/cap/survivor ambiguity remains outcome-unknown, while another completed nonzero result is known failure. An authoritative observation is a successful fresh remote ref query/fetch followed, when the ref exists, by ancestry checks for the current intended HEAD and every distinct historical attempted head; a successful query proving the ref absent is authoritative absence, not `fetch-failed` or unobservable. Absence observed before invocation always parks without a push. The ordinary protocol has one explicit limitation: deletion after the successful final read but before the server applies the refspec can make that same non-force invocation recreate the destination instead of rejecting non-fast-forward. Only that final-window creation is permitted because preserving the adopted ordinary-fast-forward-only contract and also promising atomic expected-old no-create semantics would require a force/CAS option; result plus the mandatory post-attempt observation therefore decides truth without claiming that every deletion race rejects.

Containment of the current intended HEAD has precedence over every result, old-tip, movement, inactivity, retry, or abort classification only while that last attempted member is also the current merge generation's final intended candidate HEAD or standalone fresh attempt's locked `rebased_head`: recovery then sets `landed_head` to that member and transitions to `pushed`/`closed`, even if an earlier result was ambiguous or the remote later advanced. If that bound current head is uncontained but an older attempted head is contained, recovery records the latest-contained older member as partial `landed_head`, forbids abort and blind retry, and parks the active merge at `authorized/remote-moved` (or standalone at `recovery_required/remote-moved`) so the current checkout is integrated in a new bounded attempt; it never calls the newer unlanded generation pushed or closed. In that later attempt, the exact fresh-attempt identity — a post-park merge `epoch_intent` digest or standalone rebase nonce/intent digest retained under `remote-moved/start-new-attempt` — has not yet joined the attempted-head history. Prior containment remains evidence but cannot classify that identity, and the final authoritative observation either appends its intent on unchanged tip or takes the ordinary moved/unavailable pre-intent row. This rule applies even when no-op integration produces the same OID as the latest historical member; the new intent then appends one deliberate repeated OID and ends the exception. An inactive merge instead takes only FR-232's exact `historical-landed-superseded` terminal release: retain the clean newer worktree/branch, perform no cleanup/deletion, release ownership, and require a fresh chain. Only an all-false authoritative vector for the currently bound attempted head may proceed to ordinary old-tip, movement, rejection, or not-landed abort handling. If the present remote still equals the current expected old tip with that all-false vector, one ordinary retry is safe regardless of whether the retained current result is success, non-fast-forward, known failure, outcome-unknown, or absent after a crash-before-result; the exact result and all history are preserved. If start-pipe proof shows the complete-lock epoch authorized no push child for its current durable intent, that just-completed reconciliation observation is the immediate pre-push recheck and the existing unlaunched intent authorizes its sole attempt without appending a duplicate history member. Once a push child was authorized, even when no result survived, the epoch instead parks so a newly acquired epoch performs that recheck/retry and appends the deliberate duplicate; retained `known-failure` returns exact exit-1 `push-failed`, while every other retained result/no-result returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery. When the destination is authoritatively absent or its OID differs from expected old tip with all attempted heads uncontained, `non-fast-forward` is used only if the retained update result actually rejected that ordinary push as non-fast-forward; observation without such a rejection instead records condition `remote-moved`, returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and re-enters the integration matrix without claiming a rejected update. Only independently proven remote-only defeats increment the FR-235 counter; ambiguous or independently failed attempts reset it. If the destination cannot be observed authoritatively, the chain stays `pushing`, emits `push-outcome-unknown`, and MUST NOT retry blindly. A known push failure other than non-fast-forward stays `pushing` only while observation proves the old tip unchanged; otherwise observation decides.

Cleanup is admitted only after current intended-head containment established scalar `pushed`; it MUST freshly prove that exact current `landed_head` is contained in the remote default branch and that the recorded local branch has not moved. An older-only partial `landed_head` never admits cleanup, and cleanup never substitutes another historical attempt or the current worktree HEAD. It then runs `git worktree remove` without `--force` and deletes the branch only with non-force deletion after containment. An already removed worktree or absent branch is idempotent success for that step. Branch movement, observation failure, residual worktree content, or any partial removal yields `cleanup_pending`/`cleanup-failed`, never rewrites the push as failed, never deletes by force, and never re-runs push. Crash recovery handles each intent window independently: no observed side effect retries the step; observed rebase state routes to its owned recovery; current-head containment routes to `pushed`, older-only containment routes to partial-landed reconciliation, observed removed worktree/branch advances only already-admitted cleanup, and an unclassifiable observation sets `foreign-git-state`. A failed explicit lock release similarly preserves the already-recorded primary state and is retried without replaying the primary side effect.

- **FR-237** (MUST): System of record and concurrency. The DM-014 chain file and append-only event log are the system of record for merge authorization, integration, recovery, push, and cleanup. Terminal chain state, complete events, gate logs, review/approval artifacts, and cleanup truth remain under `.forge/chains/`. Optional journal references are narrative data, never merge authority or a prerequisite to any transition.

An `ownership_intent` payload has exactly `worktree_digest`, `claim_path`, `intended_claim_digest`, `predecessor_chain_id`, and `predecessor_release_digest`. The predecessor fields are both null when the deterministic path has no prior terminal owner; after tombstone collection they are the terminal predecessor's chain ID and exact `ownership_released` event digest. The subsequent `ownership_claimed` payload has exactly `ownership_intent_digest`, `claim_inode`, `claim_digest`, `predecessor_chain_id`, and `predecessor_release_digest`; its first field is the exact intent-event digest, inode/digest are the observed published claim and MUST match the intent's intended record, and predecessor fields repeat the intent byte-for-byte. Those fields replay an event durable before materialized `owned` state and create the successor's causal authorization edge without relying on wall-clock ordering.

Atomic ownership is an owner-controlled regular file at `.forge/chains/owners/<lowercase-hex-sha256-of-DM-013-canonical-worktree-identity>.claim` and is only a materialized projection of the chain's ownership events. Before publishing it, `merge start` persists the initial chain plus `ownership_intent` event and materializes `worktree.claim` as `{status: unpublished, path: <absolute-claim-path>, inode: null, digest: <intended-record-digest>}`; it then creates a mode-0600 temporary regular file in the same directory, writes and fsyncs one DM-013-canonical JSON record with exactly `chain_id`, `host`, `pid`, `session`, `started_at`, and `worktree_digest`, and atomically publishes the complete inode with a no-replace hard link to the claim path. It fsyncs the parent, unlinks the temporary name, appends `ownership_claimed`, materializes `worktree.claim.status` as `owned` plus the observed inode/digest, and only then reports admission. A crash before the link leaves no claim and bare recover may retry the same publication or abort the unpublished chain; a crash after the link but before the event/state update is completed only by matching the claim inode/content to the persisted intent. Thus the protocol never exposes a mkdir-without-owner window. A pre-existing claim naming a nonterminal, internally consistent chain returns `live-merge-chain-exists`; a terminal consistent claim is a released tombstone and is collected under that chain's lease before start retries. A malformed or unmatched claim cannot be a protocol partial write and is an irresolvable ownership-event/materialized-state divergence, so it returns exit 2 `frozen-chain`, reports path/inode/digest, and is never stolen automatically.

Claim quarantine is permitted only when repository-wide quiescence/owner-death proof, engaged `AGENT_HALT`, and complete chain/event reconciliation prove that the revalidated inode/content is garbage not named by any authoritative `owned` or `releasing` projection or `ownership_claimed` event. Only then does the CLI print the shell-quoted operator-reserved `mv <absolute-claim-path> <absolute-claim-path>.quarantine-<sha256>`; after the operator moves that exact inode, quiescence/reconciliation are re-proved and the halt is explicitly cleared before `merge start`. If any authoritative acquired owner names the path/inode/digest, even when the on-disk bytes are missing or divergent, logical ownership remains and no quarantine/retry recommendation is emitted: Automation MUST NOT abandon or reconstruct an acquired claim; a separately reviewed operator recovery is required. A live or unprovable possible owner likewise receives no quarantine recommendation.

Normal acquired-ownership completion appends and fsyncs `ownership_release_intent`, materializes `worktree.claim.status` as `releasing`, and verifies the claim path still names the recorded inode/content. That event's payload has exactly `target_terminal`, `terminal_disposition`, `source_state`, `terminal_preconditions_digest`, and `release_mode`; `release_mode` is exactly `acquired` or `never-published`, and `terminal_disposition` is exactly `ordinary` or `historical-landed-superseded`. `target_terminal` is `aborted` with disposition `ordinary` only after owned-Git restoration plus either (a) no push intent/attempt and durable remote-untouched proof or (b) a prior push intent/attempt, one fresh authoritative observation whose full attempted-head containment vector is all false, and proof no live/unresolved fence/process remains; case (b) preserves all historical push results and does not claim the remote was never transiently changed. Current containment instead selects pushed truth. Older-only containment selects active partial-landed reconciliation, except that an inactive chain MAY select target `aborted` with disposition `historical-landed-superseded` only under FR-232's fresh full-vector, clean/unchanged current worktree/branch, and no-live-fence predicate; that disposition means the current generation was abandoned while the recorded older `landed_head` remains true, performs no cleanup/deletion, and preserves the worktree for a fresh chain. `target_terminal` is `closed` only with disposition `ordinary` after fresh containment of the exact current `landed_head` and every cleanup result are durable. For ordinary aborted-after-attempt, `terminal_preconditions_digest` binds the complete ordered attempted-head array, the complete all-false observation vector and observation digest, every historical push-intent/result event digest, and the no-live-fence/process proof. For historical-landed-superseded it binds the full vector, latest-contained older head, current uncontained intended head, every attempt/result digest, exact clean/unchanged worktree/branch identities, no-live-fence/process proof, and the literal disposition; for closed it binds the current `landed_head`, its containment observation, and all cleanup results. Fsync of this intent is the transaction's observation cutoff: later external remote changes do not supersede or retarget its immutable terminal selection. The subsequent `ownership_released` payload has exactly `release_intent_digest`, `release_mode`, `terminal_disposition`, `claim_inode`, `claim_digest`, and `claim_observation_digest`; its first field is the exact intent-event digest, its disposition repeats the intent byte-for-byte, and its last field is the lowercase SHA-256 of DM-013 canonical `{claim_path, exists, inode, digest}` observed immediately before the result. Acquired mode requires `exists: true` and the matching canonical claim inode/digest throughout release. It appends `ownership_released`, materializes status `released`, and only afterward appends the payload-selected `closed` or `aborted`; those authoritative events, not pathname absence, release logical ownership. The historical-landed-superseded `aborted` payload has exactly `terminal_disposition`, `landed_head`, `superseded_head`, and `observation_digest`; its literal disposition and OIDs repeat the release-intent-bound facts. A crash before the release result resumes solely from the exact intent after verifying immutable event/digest continuity and the local claim identity; it MUST NOT perform or require a fresh remote/cleanup observation. A crash after the result verifies only the release/precondition digests and completes the selected terminal event. Both pending stages are excluded from every new containment-priority row, including if the remote changes after the cutoff. Status `released` permits the old matching claim inode to remain only as a non-owning tombstone. After terminal truth is durable, the invocation best-effort revalidates that exact inode/content immediately before unlink and parent fsync; failure to collect it cannot revoke terminal truth, and a later start collects it under the terminal chain's lease before publishing a new claim.

The sole missing-claim exception is a chain whose authoritative `ownership_intent` and materialized projection remain `unpublished` with `inode: null`. Under its acquired per-chain lease, abort/recovery MUST prove the canonical claim path absent and revalidate that absence immediately before each protected append or state replacement. It appends an intent/result with `release_mode: never-published` and `terminal_disposition: ordinary`; the result uses `claim_inode: null`, the unchanged intended-record digest as `claim_digest`, and `claim_observation_digest` over the exact mapping with `exists: false`, `inode: null`, and `digest: null`. It then materializes `released` with null inode and that intended-record digest and appends `aborted`; it never unlinks a claim pathname. A crash at any boundary resumes from those events under the same absence-and-lease proof. Any missing or mismatched claim after ownership was acquired, or any claim appearing during a never-published release, is instead an irresolvable ownership-event/materialized-state divergence and returns `frozen-chain`. Thus abort and close have no ownership-release refusal that could broaden `state-precondition`, `cleanup-failed`, or common-lock-only `lock-release-failed`. Because the claim belongs to the chain rather than the start process, terminal proof permits logical release regardless of whether the recorded session process remains live.

Per-chain serialization uses the regular file `.forge/chains/<chain-id>.lock`, published atomically from a complete, fsynced mode-0600 same-directory temporary inode through the same no-replace hard-link protocol. Its canonical record has exactly `chain_id`, `host`, `nonce`, `pid`, `session`, and `started_at`; `nonce` is 32 lowercase hex from 128 OS-RNG bits. Acquisition waits at most 300 seconds. The owning invocation MUST verify that the canonical lease path still names its recorded inode/digest immediately before every protected event append, state replacement, or lease unlink; loss or replacement of the name removes its admitting transition and it performs no write. It unlinks and parent-fsyncs only after the protected update; a crash therefore leaves a complete record, never an empty partial.

Bare recover may reclaim a strict-valid lease only after same-host PID death plus state/event reconciliation and only while holding either the complete common lock or FR-235's sole provisional recovery reservation bound to this chain. That repository-wide exclusion serializes reclaimers. The winner retains the observed stale inode/digest, revalidates the canonical pathname against both immediately before unlink, unlinks and parent-fsyncs it, and publishes its own complete lease through the ordinary no-replace protocol before releasing the exclusion. A mismatch restarts inspection without removal. No contender that did not hold that exclusion may remove a lease, and every later protected write still performs the owner-path revalidation above; therefore two same-host death-proof contenders cannot ABA-remove one another's winning lease. A proven-live record is never quarantined: timeout returns `state-precondition` with exact next step `forge status --chain-id <id>`, and the holder must finish or be safely stopped. A foreign-host, PID-reused/unprovable, malformed, or mismatched record is likewise never stolen automatically. Operator quarantine is permitted only after the operator establishes repository-wide quiescence/owner death, engages `AGENT_HALT`, and reconciles state/events; only then may the reported fixed-path lease be moved to its same-directory `.quarantine-<sha256>` name. The operator then re-proves quiescence and reconciliation, explicitly clears the halt, and only afterward may bare recovery compete for a new lease; the CLI never performs or immediately recommends that move for a live or unprovable possible owner. Commands needing both locks acquire the common rebase lock first, then this lease, re-read state after both, and release the lease before the common lock; code holding the lease MUST NOT wait for the common lock. Commands needing no Git mutation hold only the per-chain lease for their bounded state update, so disjoint worktrees may classify/review concurrently. Merge start/refresh/finalize/recover/cleanup, merge abort when owned Git state may require abort/reconciliation, and standalone `forge push` serialize every fetch/rebase/push/cleanup/Git-abort mutation under the complete common rebase lock, including FR-236's fence. Inactive pre-push chains lose authorization but retain safe recovery/abort; pushed truth never expires. Malformed chain state, ownership-event/materialized-state divergence, event divergence not safely replayable, or foreign Git state sets the exact DM-014 fail-closed condition; automation never deletes terminal chain/event evidence.

- **FR-238** (MUST): Standalone `forge push`. The phase-4 command MUST be available while DM-015 is still `legacy-v1`, so activation never removes the only legal default-branch push path. It runs only in the main checkout, on the current branch exactly equal to the committed manifest's configured default branch, with remote exactly `origin`, destination fixed to that full branch ref, exact empty `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all`, and no in-progress Git operation. It performs the halt and read-only preflight, acquires the complete common rebase lock, and then acquires the existing FR-219 `.forge/tmp/commit-lock` before creating or changing DM-017. The order is always common lock then commit lock; push releases commit lock before common lock, and code holding commit lock MUST NOT wait for common lock. `forge commit finalize` continues to take only the commit lock and therefore forms no cycle, but the phase-4 implementation extends its complete transition tuple: after acquiring that lock and before candidate re-verification or `committing` intent, it `lstat`s and strict-parses `.forge/tmp/push-state.json`. Absence or schema-valid `closed` admits ordinary history mutation. The sole nonterminal exception is a schema-valid singleton at exactly `recovery_required` with `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`, null `lock_release`, and no live or unresolved fence/process: that tuple admits only DM-015's already-reviewed, approved, candidate-bound canonical `.forge-manifest`-only repair finalize, preserves every singleton byte, and requires the next `forge push` to bind the repaired HEAD as a new attempt. Every other schema-valid nonterminal singleton leaves the commit chain unchanged in `authorized`, makes finalize unadmitted with byte-exact `state-precondition`, and names exact next `forge push`; a malformed/unreadable/symlinked singleton is the exact DM-017 corrupt chain file and returns `frozen-chain`. Because push cannot hold or change the singleton without this same commit lock, the check is stable and a crashed push transaction durably interlocks later sanctioned commits without making finalize acquire the common lock.

Failure or timeout of push's second acquisition returns byte-exact `lock-unavailable`, releases the common lock, and leaves an absent or prior singleton byte-identical. Once acquired for push, the commit lock is the inner layer of that invocation's complete rebase transaction lock. Its release is attempted before common-lock release; failure of either layer persists DM-017's exact `lock_release` identities and primary tuple, retains the portable common owner, returns exact `lock-release-failed`, and admits only recovery. Recovery proves/releases the commit layer through FR-219's existing serialized ownership/stale-owner mechanism before the common layer and never repeats a primary child or side effect. Thus the unchanged `lock-release-failed` precondition still means the complete rebase lock could not be explicitly released after primary truth was durable; `lock-unavailable` remains acquisition-only. DM-015's invalid-mode recovery-only admission covers every strict-valid nonterminal primary or wrapper whose recorded intent/result may have produced the invalid current HEAD: it permits only ordered release, death proof, identity-bound observation/classification, authoritative observation of an existing push intent, or exact owned abort/restoration; it never replays a child or starts integration/push work and stops at restored valid HEAD, current landed truth, or the repairable activation tuple. The push invocation otherwise holds both locks through singleton re-read/recovery, rebase, final HEAD/clean revalidation, push-intent/result persistence, authoritative outcome observation, and the final HEAD/clean revalidation before close. Under both locks it re-reads the singleton: any nonterminal record is observation-recovered first, while only absent/`closed` may be replaced with a new `preparing` record after preflight is repeated. Common-lock failure likewise leaves an absent or prior singleton byte-identical. The serialized invocation then persists fetch intent, fetches the fixed remote tip, rebases the default branch onto that exact tip, proves intended HEAD descends from the fetched expected-old tip, and uses only FR-236's exact ordinary non-force refspec and its explicit final-window deletion limitation. A sanctioned commit cannot move HEAD/index during that interval; an unsanctioned movement makes the complete transition tuple fail its exact pre-push or pre-close HEAD/clean predicate, so the attempted mutation/close has no admitting row and returns byte-exact `state-precondition` without falsely completing an older pushed OID.

Before rebase, the command resolves and validates both DM-017 reflog paths, records their exact existence/size/digest baselines with the nonce, action prefix, and intent digest, fsyncs that state, then sets the exact action prefix as `GIT_REFLOG_ACTION` for rebase and any owned abort. A reflog is **baseline-unchanged** only when its current existence, size, and exact-byte digest equal the recorded object. It has an **owned appended suffix** only when the current regular file retains an exact byte-for-byte prefix of the recorded size/digest (or begins at byte zero for a recorded-absent file), every appended byte parses as complete Git reflog records, the suffix is nonempty, and every appended action begins with the exact unguessable prefix. Truncation, replacement, symlink, malformed or partial records, a non-prefix appended record, or bytes after the owned suffix satisfies neither predicate.

Normal completion and crash recovery use the same closed predicate set. Successful-no-op, no-side-effect, and rewritten-result predicates each require the recorded repository still be the main checkout on `destination_ref`, no Git-operation metadata, exact clean status, and no durable result reporting launch failure, nonzero exit, timeout, or output-limit breach. **Successful no-op integration** additionally requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` be an ancestor of that HEAD, and both branch and HEAD reflogs be either baseline-unchanged or contain only an owned appended suffix ending at that same HEAD; because the recorded HEAD already contains the fetched tip, this predicate is safe when command execution is crash-ambiguous but not when a failed result is durable, and it sets `rebased_head = pre_operation_head`. **No side effect requiring retry** instead requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` not be its ancestor, and both reflogs be strictly baseline-unchanged; an absent result or proof that the start-pipe child exited before authorization may select this row. **Rewritten integrated result** requires current HEAD differ from `pre_operation_head`, `fetched_tip` be its ancestor, owned appended suffixes for both reflogs, and a branch suffix whose first old OID is `pre_operation_head` and last new OID is current HEAD.
