Only successful no-op integration or rewritten integrated result may set `rebased_head` and create push intent. **Owned conflict** instead requires the rebase metadata live in the recorded main checkout, name `destination_ref` as head-name, record `pre_operation_head` as orig-head and `fetched_tip` as onto, require the branch reflog remain strictly baseline-unchanged, and require an owned HEAD-reflog suffix beginning at `pre_operation_head` whose last new OID equals current HEAD. Only that predicate may invoke abort, and it selects exact `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, never `rebase-failed`. **Proven restoration** requires HEAD equal to `pre_operation_head`, no Git-operation metadata, exact clean status, unchanged main-checkout/default-branch identity, and reflog bytes consisting only of the baseline plus owned prefix-bearing suffixes ending at the restored HEAD. A durable non-conflict failed result with exact proven restoration or exact clean no-side-effect facts records `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt`; it cannot select the owned-conflict or either integrated-success predicate. A durable actual rebase/abort failure with integrated-looking, contaminated, dirty, or otherwise unmatched state instead preserves the immutable failure result and records `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase`, but its current invocation still emits exact `rebase-failed` because the admitted Git operation actually failed. More generally, an external same-OID rewrite, a contaminated/later reflog entry, mismatched metadata, or any observation satisfying none of these exact predicates without a durable actual Git failure records the same attribution-unresolved recovery condition and returns exact `state-precondition`; that complete transition state admits observation only, never guesses a successful result, and never pushes.

An exact owned conflict therefore causes no push, persists `recovery_required` with `rebase-conflict/abort-owned-rebase`, and returns exact `rebase-conflict`; the next `forge push` may only abort that owned rebase. Every such abort first appends/fsyncs DM-017's fresh nonce-bound abort-attempt intent; a retained exact conflict permits another attempt only after the predecessor is settled and with a new abort nonce, so no abort reuses the originating rebase intent as its operation identity. Before any new abort child, the invocation observation-classifies a settled predecessor: restoration is parked without replay, exact conflict alone admits the fresh attempt, and unmatched PASS/no-exec/historical-result truth enters the nonce-bound attribution wrapper and returns observation-only `state-precondition`. Successful proven restoration clears the conflict to a no-side-effect recovery waypoint and returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery. A non-PASS abort uses `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt` when restoration is nevertheless exact; if the owned conflict remains exact it retains/returns `rebase-conflict`; and an unmatched state records `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase` but returns exact `rebase-failed` only for an actual abort failure in that invocation. A durable non-conflict failure with exact no-side-effect/restoration proof causes no push and returns `rebase-failed`; a dirty, integrated-looking-after-failure, or otherwise unprovable state likewise returns `rebase-failed` while retaining attribution-unresolved observation-only recovery. Exact `state-precondition` is reserved for a later ambiguous observation with no actual failed Git invocation in that command. Even after an integrated predicate matches, FR-239's intended-HEAD DM-015 check precedes `rebased_head`/push-intent persistence and internally parks invalid mode as `activation-policy-invalid`; only the subsequent unadmitted push continuation returns `state-precondition`. The next `forge push` invocation performs these observation predicates first while holding both locks. Push intent/outcome follows FR-236: authoritative current-head landed observation closes success; older-only containment starts safe reconciliation without close; present old-tip observation permits one locked retry; an actual ordinary-update rejection followed by moved-or-absent observation uses `non-fast-forward`; unrelated observation-only movement or authoritative absence records `remote-moved`, returns exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and starts a new attempt on the next invocation; and unavailable observation uses `push-outcome-unknown` with no blind retry. Immediately before push-intent persistence and again after outcome observation before `closed`, HEAD MUST equal the recorded intended HEAD and status MUST be exact clean under the commit lock. This verb deliberately performs no ancestry provenance or chain-produced-SHA audit and MAY push commits no closed chain produced. Its `forge-cli/2` envelope always has `chain_id: null`.

- **FR-239** (MUST): Phase-4 activation and raw denial. The hook reads DM-015 only from committed HEAD in each matched Git context and applies this truth table: missing or `legacy-v1` preserves phase-1 dual-accept and raw-push behavior byte-for-byte; `forge-verbs-v1` atomically denies raw `git commit` and raw `git push`; duplicate/malformed/unknown mode denies either raw verb as invalid. Halt denial has precedence over all mutation handling; operator-verb denial applies to a matched Forge operator verb; invalid mode then precedes activated raw denial; in legacy mode existing marker/chain denial remains unchanged. Under either valid mode the sanctioned Forge CLI producer paths are exactly `forge commit finalize`, `forge merge finalize`, and `forge push`; legacy-v1's separately admitted raw behavior remains byte-identical, while `forge-verbs-v1` leaves only those three sanctioned Forge CLI producers and no raw-commit/raw-push admission. That is not an exhaustive claim about other Git porcelain or plumbing. Under an invalid mode only the DM-015 byte-exact repair form of candidate-bound `forge commit finalize` may produce history, while merge finalize and history-producing push receive the invalid-mode denial. The hook admits `forge push` only when a strict-valid nonterminal singleton exists and marks that invocation recovery-only before CLI execution; the CLI then enforces DM-015's closed no-new-work set. An absent/closed or corrupt singleton never uses this exception, an allowed recovery-only segment never blesses an appended raw segment, and the invocation cannot create history even if its observation discovers work to do. Existing FR-221 quote-aware segment splitting, leading-`env` handling, interpreter/path-final-component grammar, decoy rejection, and quoted-separator handling remain normative. Each segment is classified independently: an allowed Forge CLI segment never blesses an appended raw Git segment.

Because a rebase can change the intended HEAD after the outer hook classified its segment, merge finalize and standalone push MUST strict-parse DM-015 again from the exact intended HEAD immediately before push-intent persistence, under their complete locks and through FR-236's qualified no-write committed-blob read. Missing/`legacy-v1`/`forge-verbs-v1` is valid according to DM-015; duplicate, malformed, misplaced, or unknown is not. Invalid final mode creates no push intent. The already-admitted integration-result classification first durably parks merge in `revising` or standalone in `recovery_required` with internal DM-017 `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`; that classification step emits no CLI envelope. The same invocation's attempted continuation from that now-invalid complete transition tuple has no admitting push row, changes no further state, emits the exact invalid-mode line below, and returns byte-exact `state-precondition`. After approved repair, merge requires refresh and standalone begins a new operation rather than reusing the old intent. `activation-policy-invalid` is therefore a persisted standalone internal condition and an optional hook audit label, not a CLI reason. The exact new hook/CLI diagnostic literals are:

```text
forge: operator verb denied — present the candidate and ask the operator to run this via ! (merge approve)
forge: raw git commit denied — use Forge CLI commit finalize
forge: raw git push denied — use Forge CLI merge finalize or Forge CLI push
forge: history mutation mode invalid — repair committed .forge-manifest through Forge CLI
```

Hook audit labels `activation-policy-invalid`, `raw-git-commit-denied`, and `raw-git-push-denied` remain outside the CLI reason enum because the hook emits deny JSON rather than an envelope; the same spelling `activation-policy-invalid` also names DM-017's explicitly separate persisted internal condition. The security claim is narrowly denial of raw `git commit`/`git push` under the PreToolUse threat model; phase 4 MUST NOT claim exhaustive prevention of every Git porcelain/plumbing mutation or require chain provenance for `forge push`.

- **FR-240** (MUST): Phase-4 migration, close protocol, and marker retention. Init/re-init writes or preserves DM-015 as specified and never silently activates an existing repository. Activation occurs only in a distinct reviewed, operator-approved control candidate after phase-3 merge, phase-4 push, hook, recovery, v2 corpus, and disable-in-memory checks pass. The same phase-4 implementation packet MUST update the source/template/installed hook registrations, CLI help and routing inventories, init/re-init manifest rendering, `/forge:worktree-merge`, Beads integration in rendered `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, and synchronization/conformance tests. The exact close-protocol replacement block is:

```bash
bd dolt push
python3 "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/forge/cli.py" push
git status
```

Those lines are executed sequentially with each exit checked: a nonzero `bd dolt push` prevents Forge push from starting, a nonzero Forge push prevents `git status` from masking it as success, and `bd dolt push` itself is unchanged. Phase 4 MUST retain DM-006, the marker grammar/parser, every marker test, the phase-1 dual-accept, and every immutable v1 artifact even when activated raw denial makes marker admission dormant. No installed skill or template may continue to instruct a raw commit/push close path after activation.

- **FR-241** (MUST): V2 corpora, qualification, and last-line controls. Before a phase-3/4 implementation surface ships, every exact DM-016 artifact MUST exist and the v2 manifest MUST bind its digests, schema versions, the complete sorted §8 reason union, the hook-vector corpus, and the implementation/test subjects. Existing v1 artifacts are consumed, never copied or re-minted, and each v1 row is compared byte-for-byte. The v2 matcher corpus covers all three sanctioned producer paths, the merge-approval denial, legacy/enabled/invalid activation, compound commands with an allowed CLI segment plus appended raw Git, quoting/path/interpreter variants, and exact denial literals. The phase-3/4 manifest also requires deterministic transition, re-verification-matrix, recovery, push, cleanup, activated-denial, and last-line test results even though DM-016 names no additional eval prompt/result files. FR-223's harness-qualification tuple and current-major.minor/distribution-channel law continue unchanged for any operator-`!` premise. Every merge-finalize and push last-line check — target identity, clean tuple, evidence, review/approval, halt, lock, rebase result, final intended-HEAD DM-015 validity, final remote observation, push intent, containment, and cleanup safety — MUST be independently disableable in memory with a focused test that fails when that check is disabled. STRICT evals are fail-closed.

Revision-10 amendment to **FR-241**: the DM-016 generation-1 phase-3 manifest MAY carry `pending-phase-4` only in the `push`, `activated-denial`, and `push-last-line` result bindings. The pending token is explicit non-evidence: it authorizes phase 3 only, never phase 4, activation, or marker deletion. Phase-4 activation MUST land the generation-2 predecessor-bound manifest with those exact three slots changed to mechanically resolved PASS bindings and no other v2 artifact changed. A fabricated, unresolved, mismatched, or merely asserted PASS fails closed. Recording a fictional PASS is forbidden.

Candidate-bound fresh reviewer evaluation amendment to **FR-241**: FR-241's final sentence continues to make Recorded-baseline integrity fail closed; it does not characterize that model-free result as a fresh judgment. Every existing v1, v2, and v3 fixture, `.result`, corpus, eval, and manifest byte retains its immutable subject and frontmatter. The fresh layer inventories the complete current fixture suite but launches only the closed supported review-role disposition in FR-223; the subject-specific live-TUI and operational-orchestrator fixtures remain baseline-only and MUST NOT be relabeled, copied, or represented by synthetic reviewer results.

- **FR-242** (MUST): Phase-5 marker-deletion admission is a conjunction, not authority to delete. A later control revision MAY propose deletion only when one mechanically recorded candidate proves all eleven predicates: (1) release/manifests and executable compatibility tests prove phase-1 dual-accept shipped before phase 3 and was never bypassed in the supported upgrade lineage; (2) committed DM-015 is `forge-verbs-v1`; (3) the recorded DM-016 hashes prove every v1 corpus/eval/manifest byte unchanged; (4) v2 reason, matcher, merge-transition, matrix, recovery, push, cleanup, and activated-denial eval/test results all PASS; (5) every merge-finalize and push last-line check has a focused disable-in-memory test that fails on its mutant; (6) `STRICT=1` evals PASS; (7) full unittest discovery PASSes twice consecutively and serially after the candidate's last fix, with each suite's own tail retained; (8) a closed inventory scan of installed skills, templates, hooks, CLI help, rendered close protocol, and routing proves none produces raw commit/push or marker authorization; (9) an atomic scan under the applicable locks proves no nonterminal legacy chain and no unexpired DM-006 marker remains consumable; (10) marker grammar, parser, and tests remained present through the complete phase-4 candidate; and (11) the exact deletion candidate receives binding independent PASS and explicit operator approval. Missing, unavailable, stale, or non-PASS evidence makes the predicate false. Revision 7 does not authorize deletion, and a phase-5 implementation MUST preserve compatibility for every supported repository that has not yet proven activation rather than globally removing its only admission path.

For this predicate, the **supported upgrade lineage** is a closed ordered list of full released plugin commit OIDs declared by the phase-5 candidate, beginning with the first phase-1 dual-accept release and ending with the complete phase-4 release; every adjacent pair must have an immutable executable upgrade replay proving the marker and chain paths were simultaneously accepted. A missing release, unresolved OID, or release in that list whose committed hook lacks either path makes predicate (1) false. Each predicate record names the exact deletion-candidate full commit OID and tree OID, policy and artifact digests, command argv, append-only evidence sequence, start/end timestamps, exit, and bounded suite tail. “After the last fix” means the candidate commit/tree was fixed before the first run, both distinct records bind those same exact OIDs, their evidence sequence proves they ran serially in order with no intervening candidate mutation, and any later path change necessarily creates a new candidate commit/tree and invalidates both records; blob OIDs are never ordered by recency.

The predicate-(8) closed scan roots are the source and rendered/installed copies of `skills/`, `system/`, `hooks/`, `agents/`, `rules/`, CLI help/routing, `.forge-manifest` rendering, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `forge-project.md`; an unreadable or uninventoried consumer is failure. For predicate (9), every DM-012 `forge-chain/1` record with `kind: commit` is a legacy-admission chain regardless of DM-015 mode; “nonterminal” means any state other than `closed` or `aborted`, plus any terminal record with an unconsumed authorization or unresolved `committing` intent. “Consumable marker” is conservatively any owner-controlled regular file in `.forge/tmp/authorized/` whose 64-lowercase-hex name and content parse as any DM-006 form and whose recorded timestamp is no more than 30 minutes old under the same UTC clock law; malformed, symlinked, unreadable, or clock-ambiguous entries make the scan fail rather than count as absent.

The scan window begins only after the operator has engaged global `AGENT_HALT`; it then acquires the common rebase lock, the commit lock, and every existing merge-chain lease in that order, verifies no new chain/marker directory entry appeared between two complete sorted snapshots, and retains those locks through evidence persistence. No code holding a later lock may wait for an earlier one. The halt is operator-created and operator-cleared; phase-5 automation neither creates nor clears it. These definitions make predicate (9) independent of any history-mode field in DM-012 and make missing/malformed state fail closed.

- **FR-243** (MUST): Phase-6 declaration and deferral. The future names are exactly:

```text
forge plan seal --file <plan>
forge proposal unseal
```

They are reserved for a best-effort ordering/audit control only. It provides no confidentiality, does not make proposal bytes unreadable to a same-user process, and MUST NOT be described as an information-flow guarantee. Neither name has an authorized parser branch, state/schema key, storage, skill routing, reason code, permission, or success authority. A later control revision MUST define canonical schema/digest, transition and resealing/overwrite policy, proposal identity, path/symlink containment, concurrency/inactivity/crash recovery, chain evidence, output codes, tests, and operator authority before implementation.

### Per-developer routing and provider interchange (FR-244..FR-246)

- **FR-244** (MUST): Routes file. Forge MUST read at most one developer-owned routing file per clone, at `<common-root>/.forge/local/routes.toml`; it is never committed, and a copy at any linked-worktree root is misplaced and refuses launch. The `scripts/forge/route_config.py` CLI MUST expose `init | show | check | resolve | probe`; its `init` verb MUST create the fully commented-role seed once at mode `0600` and idempotently append `/.forge/local/` to the common checkout's Git `info/exclude`; it MUST NOT overwrite an existing file. “Fully commented-role” means every optional role table and assignment is commented while the required `schema = "forge-routes/1"` line remains the seed's sole active assignment, so the untouched seed is valid and selects defaults. The active file permits only the optional tables `[implementer]`, `[review-cheap]`, `[review-final]`, and `[plan]`.

  Revision-16 route-probe amendment to **FR-244**: `probe` performs one bounded launch per distinct resolved `(provider, model, effort)` tuple, merging its roles in first-seen order. It uses the FR-245 `plan` profile: Codex argv is exactly `codex exec --json --output-last-message <scratch>/last-message.txt -s read-only -c approval_policy=never -c model=<m> -c model_reasoning_effort=<e> -C <scratch> -`; Claude argv is exactly `claude -p --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <m> --effort <e> --system-prompt-file <plugin>/system/claude/prompts/plan.md --tools "Read,Grep,Glob" --permission-prompts none`; both receive exactly the one-line brief `Confirm that this model route is available and reply briefly.\n` on stdin. Each child receives only the FR-245 allowlisted environment, runs with cwd a fresh owner-only directory under `<common-root>/.forge/tmp/route-probe/` rather than a system temporary directory, and starts with `start_new_session`; the probe concurrently drains stdout and stderr, fixes its probe timeout at 120 seconds independently of FR-245's launch timeouts, caps stdout at 16 MiB and stderr at 1 MiB, and on an early authentication refusal, timeout, or cap breach sends the process group SIGTERM, waits at most 5 seconds, then sends SIGKILL if it survives. Codex succeeds only on exit 0 with a nonempty last-message capture; Claude succeeds only on exit 0 with a nonmalformed object-per-line stream whose final `result` has `is_error: false`. The not-logged-in refusals are exactly `forge: codex launch refused — codex CLI is not logged in; run codex login manually and retry` and `forge: claude launch refused — claude CLI is not logged in; run interactive /login manually and retry`. Its other refusal templates are exactly `forge: <provider> launch refused — route probe timed out`, `forge: <provider> launch refused — route probe <stdout|stderr> exceeded limit`, `forge: <provider> launch refused — route probe failed`, `forge: <provider> launch refused — <provider> CLI could not be started`, and `forge: route probe refused — unsafe scratch directory`, with `<provider>` replaced by `codex` or `claude` and `<stdout|stderr>` by the breached stream. It writes one sorted-key JSON report line per tuple, with exactly the fields `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `roles`, `ok`, `returncode`, `timed_out`, `output_limit`, `observed_model`, `permission_denials`, and `environment_names`, and exits 1 when any tuple fails. Its only writes are the owner-controlled local `.forge/tmp/route-probe/` directory and transient per-launch scratch and Codex capture beneath it; it removes each per-launch scratch after use and never writes repository or working-tree content.

  Parsing MUST use a fixed, line-oriented regular-expression grammar, never `tomllib` (the Python 3.10 FR-005 floor). After rejecting NUL and CR, every physical line MUST full-match exactly one of these forms; string escapes and multiline values are not admitted:

  ```text
  ^[ \t]*$
  ^[ \t]*#[^\r\n]*$
  ^[ \t]*schema[ \t]*=[ \t]*"forge-routes/1"[ \t]*(?:#[^\r\n]*)?$
  ^[ \t]*\[(implementer|review-cheap|review-final|plan)\][ \t]*(?:#[^\r\n]*)?$
  ^[ \t]*(provider|model|effort)[ \t]*=[ \t]*"([^"\\\r\n]*)"[ \t]*(?:#[^\r\n]*)?$
  ```

  There MUST be exactly one schema assignment before any active table, no duplicate table, no duplicate key within one table, and no assignment outside its table. Every table is optional, but a present table has exactly all three keys: `provider`, `model`, and `effort`; a partial table is malformed and never inherits the missing key. `provider` is exactly `codex` or `claude`. `model` MUST match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:/@+-]{0,127}(\[1m\])?$`, and the otherwise matching value `inherit` is refused. Codex effort is exactly `minimal | low | medium | high | ultra`; Claude effort is exactly `low | medium | high | xhigh | max` (`xhigh` added by operator ruling of 2026-09-27, amended in place, no revision change). A value outside the selected provider's committed profile set is malformed.

  An absent role table resolves to the committed default at the launch HEAD: `.codex/agents/<role>.toml` when present, otherwise `system/codex/agents/<role>.toml`; when neither committed path supplies that role, including in an uninstalled repository, resolution falls through to the plugin default. Precedence is exactly local, then committed default, then plugin default, with no environment layer. Every resolution records exactly one `route_source` from `local | committed-default | plugin-default | unrecorded`; `unrecorded` is compatibility evidence, never a selectable precedence layer.

  Revision-16 review-final default amendment to **FR-244**: for `review-final` the committed default is `.codex/agents/review-final.toml` when present at the launch HEAD, otherwise `system/codex/agents/review-final.toml`, otherwise the `model:` and `effort:` frontmatter of `agents/review-final.md` read as provider `claude` (FR-111's committed-default compatibility metadata), otherwise the plugin default; every step in that chain reports `route_source: committed-default` except the plugin default.

  Before parsing, Forge MUST open with `O_NOFOLLOW`, require a nonsymlink regular file (`S_ISREG`), require `st_uid == geteuid()`, require `st_mode & 0o022 == 0`, cap the file at 16 KiB, prove it is untracked, and prove Git ignores it. A missing local file is ordinary default resolution. A present file that is malformed, foreign-owned, symlinked, nonregular, group/other-writable, oversized, tracked, unignored, unreadable, or misplaced refuses the launch; none of those conditions is reported or persisted as a route mismatch.

- **FR-245** (MUST): Provider profiles. Forge MUST commit one argv template, permission profile, and recorded `sandbox` value for each of the eight `(provider, role)` cells below; the committed defaults and sandbox table in `scripts/forge/route_config.py` are control-class under FR-253, while the argv templates under `scripts/forge/forge_cli/engine/` are review-final-floor surfaces whose changes also fire the reviewer-routing fresh evaluations. Chains B and E supply the role bodies and executable review cells; chain I supplies the deferred implementer and plan cells. `<m>` and `<e>` are the resolved model and effort, `<worktree>` is the selected execution worktree, the brief is stdin, and the named prompt/body is plugin-owned committed input.

  The committed role-body inputs are `system/codex/prompts/plan.md`, `system/claude/prompts/implementer.md`, `system/claude/prompts/review-cheap.md`, and `system/claude/prompts/plan.md`; `agents/review-final.md` remains the committed Claude final-review body.

  | role | `codex` profile | `claude` profile |
  |---|---|---|
  | `implementer` | `codex exec --json --output-last-message <handoff> -s workspace-write -c approval_policy=never -c model=<m> -c model_reasoning_effort=<e> -C <worktree> -`; sandbox `workspace-write` | `claude -p --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <m> --effort <e> --append-system-prompt-file <plugin>/system/claude/prompts/implementer.md --tools "Read,Write,Edit,Bash,Grep,Glob" --permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools "Bash" --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence`, launched with cwd exactly `<worktree>`; Bash granted; sandbox `instruction-bounded` |
  | `review-cheap` | `codex exec --json --output-last-message <verdict> -s read-only -c approval_policy=never -c model=<m> -c model_reasoning_effort=<e> -C <worktree> -`, with the review-cheap prompt on stdin; sandbox `read-only` | `claude -p --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <m> --effort <e> --system-prompt-file <plugin>/system/claude/prompts/review-cheap.md --tools "Read,Grep,Glob,Bash" --permission-prompts none --dangerously-skip-permissions --no-session-persistence`; Bash granted; sandbox `instruction-bounded` |
  | `review-final` | `codex exec --json --output-last-message <verdict> -s read-only -c approval_policy=never -c model=<m> -c model_reasoning_effort=<e> -C <worktree> -`, with the pinned review-final body on stdin; sandbox `read-only` | `claude -p --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <m> --effort <e> --system-prompt-file <derived-review-final-body> --tools "Read,Bash,Glob,Grep" --permission-prompts none --dangerously-skip-permissions --no-session-persistence`; `<derived-review-final-body>` is the owner-only launcher materialization of `<plugin>/agents/review-final.md` after frontmatter stripping and plugin-root-reference expansion, and its exact bytes are bound into the package digest; Bash granted; sandbox `instruction-bounded` |
  | `plan` | `codex exec --json --output-last-message <handoff> -s read-only -c approval_policy=never -c model=<m> -c model_reasoning_effort=<e> -C <worktree> -`, with `<plugin>/system/codex/prompts/plan.md` plus the brief on stdin; sandbox `read-only` | `claude -p --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <m> --effort <e> --system-prompt-file <plugin>/system/claude/prompts/plan.md --tools "Read,Grep,Glob" --permission-prompts none --no-session-persistence`; no Bash or write tool; sandbox `read-only` |

  The Claude reviewer cells run with permission checks bypassed via `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so Bash executes without prompts; the no-write boundary stays instruction-bounded, as FR-111 states; and `--no-session-persistence` means the session is not persisted.

  The Claude implementer and plan cells also run with `--no-session-persistence`; the implementer retains `--permission-mode acceptEdits` and `--allowedTools "Bash"`, while the plan cell exposes only `Read,Grep,Glob` with no Bash, write tool, or permission bypass.

  Codex handoff/verdict capture MUST use `--output-last-message`; Claude capture MUST extract the final `result` event from its line-JSON stream. Codex success requires exit zero and a valid nonempty capture. Claude success requires exit zero and `is_error: false` in the final result; `subtype` is never a success signal. The Codex not-logged-in shape refuses with exactly `forge: codex launch refused — codex CLI is not logged in; run codex login manually and retry`. The Claude shape refuses with exactly `forge: claude launch refused — claude CLI is not logged in; run interactive /login manually and retry`. Forge never manages or refreshes credentials.

  A child receives an allowlisted environment, never the inherited environment wholesale. The base names are `HOME`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `USER`, `TMPDIR`, and `TERM`; Codex additionally admits only `CODEX_HOME`; Claude additionally admits `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX`, `AWS_*`, `GOOGLE_*`, and `CLOUD_ML_REGION`. Forge MUST NOT pass `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, any `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_*`, `FORGE_SESSION_PID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_PID`, any other hook-originated `CLAUDE_*`, or any other `CODEX_*`. Attempt and completion records carry only the sorted admitted variable names, never their values. Persisted provider output leaves passed values of `USER`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, and `TERM` unchanged and redacts every other passed allowlisted value as FR-246 specifies.

  Feature support MUST be detected by parsed CLI version, never by `--help` text: the measured floors are Claude Code `>= 2.1.283` and codex-cli `>= 0.155.0`. Claude effort is not observable in the stream—the `init` event exposes only `per_turn_effort_active`—and the CLI does not reject an unknown `--effort` value; FR-244's route grammar therefore closes the accepted effort set at resolution time. The lack of post-launch effort attestation is an accepted out-of-scope tradeoff. Fixed profile timeouts are review 2400 seconds, implementer 14400 seconds, and plan 1200 seconds. Candidate-bound Gate 2 remains on its committed Codex route; provider interchange does not silently reroute it.

- **FR-246** (MUST): Headless review-final lane. `review-final` MUST be an engine-launched detached process on the same generalized lane as `review-cheap`; tier still selects the reviewer role, while the resolved route selects provider, model, and effort. The lane retains argv/prompt digest recomputation, owner-controlled no-follow regular-file descriptors, the 65,536-byte verdict cap, and tmp-then-`os.replace` completion publication. For an engine-launched reviewer, Forge extracts the shared review-verdict transport from the byte-identical retained raw final message as its single trailing verdict block—the last stripped line exactly `VERDICT: PASS` or `VERDICT: BLOCK` followed only by transport-grammar lines—and fails closed on zero or several exact verdict lines (including an otherwise quoted exact verdict line) or any other trailing line. Any stripped line beginning with `VERDICT:` that is not exactly `VERDICT: PASS` or exactly `VERDICT: BLOCK`, anywhere in the message, also fails closed. The engine prompt derives its Gate 1 scope from the chain's current-candidate record: a newest current-candidate passed run states that full discovery passed on that exact candidate, cites its recorded evidence, prohibits re-running full discovery or the Gate 1 cell, and directs focused modules plus in-memory disable checks; an operator or docs-class skip states the recorded reason and leaves test selection to the reviewer within the fixed review timeout; any other state fails closed.

  The wrapper MUST concurrently drain both child streams. Stdout is the line-JSON `events.jsonl` stream and is capped at 16 MiB; stderr is written to `stderr.log` and capped at 1 MiB. Either cap breach kills the group and records `error: "events cap"` or `error: "stderr cap"`. Every stdout line from both Codex `--json` and Claude `stream-json` MUST decode as one JSON object; an empty, malformed, truncated, or non-object line fails closed with the first bad line number and is never silently skipped or treated as a verdict.

  `review attach` is retired for every new review-final request in commit, merge, and init. The sole admission is a request created before the lane upgrade that is still in `reviewing` and has the legacy `invocation` shape with no new-lane PID/provider fields. That exception is selected by shape, not release number, and has no flag, operator skip, or time-based grace. Every new request MUST complete through `review collect`; FR-211's lifecycle states remain unchanged.

  The detached wrapper is launched as `[sys.executable, "-I", "-c", <exact-wrapper-source>, ...]` with `start_new_session=True`, and it MUST first prove `getpid() == getpgid(0) == getsid(0)` so it is the sole session and process-group leader; it starts the reviewer without a new session, and the wrapper, reviewer, and nondetaching descendants therefore retain that PGID. The wrapper source bytes, isolated `-I`, profile timeout, and five-second grace are all bound by `launcher_argv_digest`. Before starting the reviewer, the wrapper exclusively claims and publishes owner-only `identity.json` with exact schema `forge-review-identity/1` and the exact keys `schema`, `attempt`, `wrapper_pid`, `pgid`, `wrapper_birth`, `reviewer_pid`, `reviewer_birth`, and `started_at`; after the reviewer starts it atomically replaces that record with the recorded reviewer identity. Linux birth identity is `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22 parsed after the final `)` together with `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`; state `Z` is gone. macOS birth identity is `kp_proc.p_starttime` at microsecond resolution read with `ctypes` `sysctl(CTL_KERN, KERN_PROC, KERN_PROC_PID, pid)`, never `ps -o lstart=`. An unreadable birth identity reports `identity-unproven` and never authorizes an automated kill. The wrapper's identity publish and collect's abandonment use one exclusive-claim rule: each writes complete bytes to an owner-only temporary regular file, fsyncs it, then `os.link`s it to `identity.json` through the attempt-directory descriptor; `EEXIST` means the other side won. An abandonment claim uses the same exact identity key set with all process-identity fields null, after which collect exclusively publishes the abandoned completion; a wrapper that loses the claim starts no reviewer, writes nothing else, and exits. After winning, only the wrapper may update identity through tmp-then-`os.replace` plus directory fsync.

  Every new request is attempt-keyed and records `lane: "forge-review-lane/1"`, a random `attempt-<hex>` directory, the five-field `route`, top-level `provider` and `sandbox`, and owner-relative `events_path`, `stderr_path`, `identity_path`, `completion_path`, `verdict_path`, and `prompt_path`. The commit lane resolves at `candidate.base_commit_oid`; the merge lane resolves at the candidate's `remote_tip`. The execution records the actual resolved route; no run-opening route is consulted or compared. An unknown request lane or identity schema refuses without guessing as exactly `forge: review request shape newer than this plugin — finish or abort the chain on the requesting version`. The resolved route selects either provider for both review roles under FR-052, FR-153, and FR-215. The FR-083 installer and FR-183 init-review activation remain deferred until the chain that rewrites `skills/init/SKILL.md`; init therefore keeps its pre-lane surface in this revision. The FR-060 headless-review lane is likewise deferred for the legacy `/forge:worktree-merge` skill, which owns no Forge CLI merge chain and keeps its interactive review-final until it does; FR-234's CLI merge lane is unaffected.

  The wrapper MUST enforce the fixed FR-245 profile timeout. The wrapper receives exactly the FR-245 allowlisted environment after withholding every admitted value shorter than four bytes; it passes that unchanged environment to the reviewer and records only sorted `environment_names` and sorted `omitted_short`. Thus values such as `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX=1`, or `LANG=C` are withheld and reported, not passed. The redaction-name set is exactly `environment_names` minus `USER`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, and `TERM`. Thus Codex redacts passed `HOME`, `PATH`, `TMPDIR`, and `CODEX_HOME`; Claude redacts passed `HOME`, `PATH`, `TMPDIR`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX`, every admitted `AWS_*` and `GOOGLE_*` name, and `CLOUD_ML_REGION`. Passed values of the four excluded names remain unchanged, unless one equals a redacted-name value, in which case the redacted-name pattern still redacts it. Before any persisted byte, both stream pumps redact the redaction-name values longest-first in their raw and JSON-escaped byte forms, with a carry buffer one byte shorter than the longest redacted raw or JSON-escaped pattern so every split offset is covered. Stdout is parsed one complete line at a time, strings including keys are redacted and re-serialized, and a non-JSON line is raw-redacted before it records `bad line N`; redaction damage fails closed only when it changes an extractor field (`type`, `subtype`, `is_error`, `result`, `init.model`, `init.permissionMode`, `init.tools`, `message.model`, or a `modelUsage` key). The exemption covers only the first, validated Claude `init` event: a value-only redaction match within that event's `init.permissionMode` or `init.tools` remains redacted in persistence but is not damage because the raw value already matched digest-bound committed profile constants. Any later event with subtype `init` retains the damage rule. The first event's keys and every other extractor field retain the damage rule, and every other redaction is unchanged. Stderr is redacted before persistence. A redacted value occurring in a `candidate:` or `package:` citation invalidates the verdict after redaction. Codex writes only to an owner-only staging file, which the wrapper reopens with `O_NOFOLLOW`, owner and regular-file checks even after provider rename, redacts, publishes as `verdict.txt` by tmp-then-`os.replace`, digests, and unlinks; Claude's final `result` text is extracted from the already-redacted stream. Transformed values such as base64, hex, split strings, or other re-encodings are outside redaction coverage, and a descendant that deliberately calls `setsid` or `setpgid` is outside the process-group boundary; these are accepted out-of-scope tradeoffs, not claimed protections.

  Only after `_launch_inputs` has verified `argv_digest`, and only for provider `claude`, the wrapper derives the expected initialization from that digest-bound argv. Exactly one `--tools` and at most one `--permission-mode` may occur, each followed by a nonempty value token that does not begin with `-`; `--dangerously-skip-permissions` may occur at most once and MUST NOT coexist with `--permission-mode`. The `--tools` value MUST contain only nonempty, pairwise-distinct comma-separated entries. Any violation is an ambiguous or incomplete launch configuration and fails closed as `wrapper failure` before the reviewer starts. For a valid profile, `--dangerously-skip-permissions` means `permissionMode: "bypassPermissions"`; otherwise `--permission-mode X` means `permissionMode: "X"`; otherwise the expected value is `default`. The expected tools are the comma-separated entries of the sole `--tools` value, sorted only for comparison. Before redaction, the first stdout event MUST be `{"type":"system","subtype":"init"}`, its `permissionMode` MUST equal the expected value, and its `tools` MUST be a list of unique strings whose set exactly equals the expected tools; reordering is accepted, while a missing, extra, or duplicate tool is rejected. A malformed first line remains `bad line 1`; EOF before any event, a valid non-init first event, a missing required init field, or a mode/tools mismatch stops the child through the existing early-stop path and completes exactly with `claude init mismatch`.

  The wrapper drains both pipes concurrently, validates every provider stdout line as a JSON object, caps durable `events.jsonl` at 16 MiB and `stderr.log` at 1 MiB, and uses only this closed spaced error vocabulary for non-success completion classes: `events cap`, `stderr cap`, `bad line N`, `claude init mismatch`, `not-logged-in`, `provider exit N`, `claude result error`, `verdict missing`, `verdict empty`, `verdict cap`, `verdict invalid`, `redaction damaged <field>`, `wrapper failure`, `abandoned`, `wrapper-lost`, `cancelled`, and `launch-failed: <cause>`. Timeout is represented by `timed_out: true`; `identity-unproven` is a refusal, never a completion value, and `cancelled` is reserved for the proven-group cancellation path below. Codex success requires exit zero and a nonempty staged capture; Claude success requires exit zero and final `is_error: false`, never `subtype`. The wrapper installs a Python-level SIGTERM handler and never uses `SIG_IGN`. Before self-escalation on timeout, cap, bad line, or `claude init mismatch`, that handler stops persistence, closes both logs, measures durable `events_bytes`, and publishes completion with null `returncode`; it then sends TERM to its whole group, waits at most five seconds, and sends KILL. External TERM from cancel stops persistence and exits without publishing, leaving sole terminal authority to the canceller. After normal completion publication, the wrapper similarly sweeps any remaining member of its group TERM-then-KILL so no nondetached descendant outlives the attempt. In every path, bytes stop changing before the completion that measures them is published.

  The exact `forge-review-process/2` key set is `argv_digest`, `completed_at`, `error`, `prompt_digest`, `returncode`, `reviewer_pid`, `schema`, `started_at`, `verdict_digest`, `verdict_size`, `wrapper_pid`, `provider`, `route_source`, `route_sha256`, `sandbox`, `observed_model`, `timed_out`, `events_bytes`, `pgid`, `attempt`, `environment_names`, and `omitted_short`. Claude's observed model comes only from its captured stream; Codex records no invented observed model. Wrapper completion remains tmp-then-`os.replace` plus fsync. Verb-side `abandoned`, `wrapper-lost`, `cancelled`, and post-event `launch-failed` completions reuse this exact key set with null process fields where none existed and are published only once by linking an fsynced temporary file to `completion.json`; `EEXIST` requires re-evaluation and never overwrites a wrapper completion. A completion is accepted only for the current attempt; provider, route source, route digest, sandbox, attempt, argv digest, and prompt digest must match the request, process identities must match `identity.json`, and null `returncode` is admitted only with `timed_out: true` or a non-null error. A completion found in a noncurrent attempt directory is ignored as verdict evidence and receives an owner-only, exclusive, idempotent `stale.json` marker.

  Until 60 seconds after the durable owner record, an attempt with neither identity nor completion is `launching`; after that deadline collect may win the exclusive identity claim, publish `abandoned`, and clear it. With identity present, every probe re-reads birth identities and enumerates nonzombie group members (`/proc/<pid>/stat` field 5 on Linux, `pgrep -g` on macOS). A live recorded wrapper proves the group is owned and reports running or `wrapper-alive / child-dead`; both wait for completion. If the wrapper is gone but its recorded reviewer is alive with matching birth identity and PGID, `wrapper-dead / child-alive` refuses and names `review cancel`. If no completion exists and the recorded group is empty, a recorded PID has `identity-mismatch`, or the boot id changed, collect publishes `wrapper-lost` and clears. Members with the recorded PGID but no matching recorded process are `identity-unproven`: collect names their PIDs and refuses; cancel likewise refuses with the exact diagnostic below, signals nothing, publishes no completion, and leaves the attempt and request unchanged. Own child zombies are reaped with `waitpid(WNOHANG)` before classification.

  FR-210's shared review verbs include `cancel`. `review cancel` acts only on a new-lane attempt with a known identity schema and refuses legacy or still-`launching` requests by shape. Its ownership proof is either a live wrapper with matching birth identity, or—after the wrapper is gone—a live recorded reviewer with matching birth identity and PGID, which proves the group never emptied. Only after ownership is proven does cancel enter the TERM-then-KILL procedure for the whole group, wait at most five seconds after KILL to prove nonzombie membership empty, and exclusively publish `cancelled` and clear the request; remaining members refuse as exactly `forge: review cancel refused — kill-unconfirmed: <pids>`; the request is not cleared. `identity-unproven` refuses as exactly `forge: review cancel refused — identity-unproven; member PIDs <pids>; recorded PGID <pgid>; nothing was signalled`; it publishes no completion and leaves the attempt and request unchanged. `identity-mismatch`, changed boot id, or `group-empty` becomes `wrapper-lost`. Commit-chain cancel is admitted in `reviewing`, past `inactive_after`, at the iteration cap, and on a terminal chain whose last new-lane attempt lacks completion; outside active `reviewing` it writes only completion, no chain event, and ignores HEAD/index drift while retaining halt. Merge cancel retains the mandatory-review-final control and halt checks and the same terminal-only admission.

  Recovery is exhaustive and the attempt completion is sole terminal authority. Route, version, executable, and prompt-write failures occur before any event. A post-event spawn error exclusively publishes `launch-failed: <cause>` and the same verb clears the commit request; merge records the attempt terminal and follows its lane rule. `launching`, `abandoned`, `wrapper-lost`, `cancelled`, and authentication failure admit retry; stale attempts never transition. Timeout, either cap, a bad line, `claude init mismatch`, non-auth nonzero exit, Claude `is_error: true`, missing/empty/oversize verdict, verdict grammar or citation failure, extractor-field redaction damage, and `wrapper failure` all produce one synthetic BLOCK whose finding starts `finding: MAJOR no reviewer verdict — <error>` and names the completion path. For this error the synthetic finding is exactly `finding: MAJOR no reviewer verdict — claude init mismatch; completion <path>`. That BLOCK follows the ordinary review-verdict path and consumes the iteration. On the commit lane, cancelled, abandoned, wrapper-lost, launch-failed, and not-logged-in do not consume an iteration; after its completion, a matching terminal marker is recorded by a same-detail `review_requested` event and the next request is admitted. Under merge Option A, those five non-verdict terminals spend the already-requested one of eight iterations, have no clearing event or marker, and a later `review request` supersedes the request at the next iteration; if the eighth attempt ends this way, only safe `merge abort` remains. `review attach` refuses any request carrying `provider`, `pid`, or `attempt` and names `review collect`; it admits only a pre-upgrade request still in `reviewing` with legacy `invocation` and no `pid` or `provider`. Real-process-group tests MUST independently disable the isolated `-I` launch, identity-before-reviewer, exclusive claim, birth checks, bounded post-KILL wait, redaction boundary, completion binding, and survivor controls in memory.

FR-247 retired by Revision 22 (task-completion provenance).

FR-248..FR-249 retired by Revision 22 (run-close projection and result-before-gate journal admission).

---

## 8. API / Schema Contracts

### Journal writer and structural reader

One run directory contains one append-only `journal.jsonl`. Every new record is one JSON object with `kind` and `run_id`; record fields and tolerant legacy reading follow FR-250..FR-252. A writer serializes complete line appends under the single-writer append lock and returns after writing. It does not consult session ownership, another run, a scope registry, route snapshots, chain state, references, task provenance, or prior close outcome.

| Writer surface | Plain record produced |
|---|---|
| `run-open` | `run_started` with run id, repository, intent text, and actor |
| `journal task-start` or `journal task-finish` | `task` with task id, title, and free-text scope; completion may be described as text and imposes no state transition |
| `journal execution-start` | `execution_started` with execution/task ids, actual role/provider/model/effort, worktree, and start time |
| `journal execution-result` | `execution_finished` with execution/task ids, actual route/worktree, start/end time, exit status, output artifact path, and launcher-reported tokens when available |
| `journal decision-add` | `decision` with free text, actor, and optional references |
| `run-close` | `run_closed` with free-text outcome and no judgment field |

The surfaces accept the data needed to form their record and inject the selected run id and record kind; a raw-record append admits any JSON object containing those fields. Chain ids and verdict paths may be references on any record without chain lookup or reference validation. Execution pairing, task status, repeated run opening or closing, and referenced-path existence never make an append a policy refusal. I/O failure emits exactly `forge: journal append failed: I/O error`; an input that is not a JSON object or lacks `kind` or `run_id` emits exactly `forge: journal append refused: record must be a JSON object with kind and run_id`; a `run_id` outside the FR-250 grammar emits exactly `forge: <operation> refused — invalid run id` before any directory or file is created, and the value is never echoed. These are the only writer refusal classes: I/O failure and a malformed envelope.

`validate <run_dir>` is structural only. Its sorted-key JSON output is `{ok: bool, issues: [str]}`, with exit 0 iff `issues` is empty; a malformed JSON-object line, or an object with neither a current `kind`/`run_id` envelope nor a legacy `type` envelope (legacy records carry `run_id` only on `run_started`, so `run_id` is not required of them), contributes exactly `forge: journal validate failed: malformed JSON object at line <n>`. Known, unknown, and every legacy kind remain readable data and never require semantic replay, a chain receipt, or gate evidence. A reader carries unknown and legacy objects through without interpretation. A validation result has no authority over a writer, chain, approval, merge, or run operation. The committed legacy fixtures under `tests/replay/` validate with no issue.

### PreToolUse commit guard — hook contract

Input: Claude Code PreToolUse hook JSON on stdin (`tool_name`, `tool_input.command`). Output: on block, JSON `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "permissionDecision": "deny", "permissionDecisionReason": "<reason>"}}`; on allow, exit 0 with no decision. Reasons: `forge: operator halt engaged (<sentinel>)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker missing)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker malformed)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker stale)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (marker hash mismatch)`; `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (fast-path policy drift)`; and `forge: commit not authorized — run /forge:commit (fast-path eligibility drift)`.

### PostToolUse invariant guard — hook contract

Input: Claude Code PostToolUse hook JSON on stdin for Edit/Write. Output: advisory text beginning `forge: invariant advisory — ` on a hook-row non-pass; always exit 0 and never emit a deny decision. Outside an initialized forge repository: no output, exit 0.

### `run-evals.sh [STRICT=1]`

Recorded-baseline integrity only; this model-free command launches no reviewer and makes no current-candidate judgment claim. Stdout lines `PASS <id>` / `FAIL <id> (expected X, got Y)` / `PENDING <id>` plus summary. Exit: 0 ok, 1 recorded mismatch (or STRICT+PENDING), 2 malformed/empty.

### `check-halt.sh [<scope>]`

Exit 0 clear, 1 halted (message names the sentinel); outside git repo: warning + exit 0.

### Gate-pass marker (DM-006)

Written only by `/forge:commit` after Step 4's reviewed PASS, mechanically eligible fast completion, or FR-056 skip path at `.forge/tmp/authorized/<authorization-id>`. The shared helper renders the exact four-, five-, or six-line LF-terminated DM-006 bytes. The guard recomputes the pinned live index-tree observation in its invoking Git context, resolves the authorization-ID path at the common root, validates filename/content/tree agreement and TTL through the same marker grammar, and independently recomputes fast policy/eligibility for the fast form. Step 5 deletes only that marker after verified success or no-produced-commit failure; a produced mismatch retains it until expiry.

### `aggregate-telemetry.sh` — CSV and decision-event contract

The script accepts the `.forge/tmp/decisions` directory, continues to parse its existing `*.md` fenced `telemetry` blocks, and additionally parses FR-157's `events.jsonl`. Its standalone CSV CLI is `aggregate-telemetry.sh <decisions-dir> --csv <path> [--since <UTC-ISO-8601> --until <UTC-ISO-8601>]`; its concurrent Stop mode is `--append-csv <path> --session <session-id>`. The two window flags MUST appear together, define a half-open `[since, until)` filter over parsed event `at` values, and fail with exit 2 when malformed or reversed. Without the flags, the deterministic window is 00:00:00Z on the first day of the current UTC calendar quarter through the invocation instant. The Stop invocation in FR-093 uses that default and appends a `session` column value without replacing prior rows. Separately, `drift-check.sh` MUST apply the same paired `--since`/`--until` bounds—FR-155's `window_start` and `window_end`—to its own direct FR-157 event aggregation when constructing the JSON `telemetry` object; the drift path MUST NOT invoke a CSV-writing mode or request, write, retain, or read any CSV artifact. In a Forge-initialized repository, whenever `<decisions-dir>/events.jsonl` exists, standalone mode MUST emit the requested CSV with the exact header and exactly one `__decision_totals__` row, even when there are no per-unit `*.md` files or no fenced telemetry blocks; per-unit Markdown availability MUST NOT gate event parsing or CSV creation. If no valid in-window event remains, the row's thirteen counters are all zero. Its standalone CSV header has exactly 22 columns:

```csv
unit,feature,model,elapsed_s,critical_path_s,tokens,cost_usd,review_iterations,rework_s,eligible_commits,fast_allowed,fast_denied_policy,fast_denied_eligibility,user_skips,review_blocks,halt_events,guard_denies,assertion_blocking,assertion_advisory,assertion_waived,review_cheap_findings,review_final_findings
```

Existing per-unit rows retain their first nine fields and use empty counter fields. The script appends exactly one row whose `unit` field has the literal value `__decision_totals__`, whose other eight legacy fields are empty, and whose thirteen appended fields are nonnegative base-10 integers for the selected window. `eligible_commits` counts `gate_commit`; `guard_denies` counts `fast_denied_policy`, `fast_denied_eligibility`, and `guard_deny`; the other five new counters map to their same-named singular event literals, with `review_cheap_finding` and `review_final_finding` aggregated into plural columns. Aggregation deduplicates `gate_commit`, `fast_allowed`, `fast_denied_policy`, `fast_denied_eligibility`, `user_skip`, `review_block`, and `guard_deny` by `(event, candidate)` whenever `candidate` is nonempty. For those seven gate-outcome event types, records with `candidate == ""` count as separate occurrences. `halt_event` and the five measurement event types remain occurrence-counted. Thus FR-155's `fast_allowed` numerator and `eligible_commits` denominator use the same candidate rule. CSV quoting follows RFC 4180. The prior-quarter comparison reads only committed drift reports. The current report's window and counters are sourced only from the Drift summary JSON's `telemetry` object; neither a standalone CSV nor the append-only `.forge/tmp/telemetry.csv` is an input to `/forge:drift`, `drift-check.sh`, or the committed drift report.

Append mode has exactly 23 columns: the literal leading `session` column followed by the standalone 22-column header above. A nonempty `session-id` is required and is RFC-4180 encoded in every row emitted by that invocation. Under `.forge/tmp/telemetry.lock`, the writer MUST create an absent or zero-byte target by writing that exact 23-column header once followed by the invocation's ordinary per-unit rows and exactly one `__decision_totals__` row, each prefixed by the same session value. For a nonempty target it MUST first parse the first record and require byte-for-byte equality with the exact 23-column header; a missing, duplicate, or different header is malformed and MUST cause exit 2 without changing the file. It then MUST append only complete LF-terminated records at EOF, MUST NOT repeat the header, and MUST preserve every existing byte. An invocation with no valid telemetry source follows §9's missing-input exit-0 contract and appends no rows. A read, validation, locking, or append failure exits 2 and leaves the pre-invocation file bytes unchanged; implementations MUST stage the invocation's complete encoded record block before one locked append so another session cannot interleave records.

### Drift summary schema v1

Every drift summary is a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly the top-level keys `checks`, `findings`, `generated_at`, `policy_sha`, `schema_version`, `status`, and `telemetry`; canonical output sorts every object key lexicographically, renders one line followed by one LF, and sets the integer `schema_version` to `1`. A reader of a committed legacy drift report accepts and ignores its `journal_patterns` key; `schema_version` stays `1` because the reader rule above makes legacy and new reports interchangeable for every consumer. `generated_at` is a UTC ISO-8601 string. `policy_sha` is a full commit SHA, or the empty string only when resolving HEAD is the represented failure.

Each `checks` element has exactly `check` (stable check identifier string), `duration_ms` (nonnegative integer), `outcome` (`passed`, `finding`, `failed`, or `skipped`), and `summary` (non-secret string). `telemetry` always has exactly 17 direct members: `available` (boolean), the thirteen nonnegative integer counters from the window-bounded aggregation, `event_prune`, and `window_start`/`window_end` (UTC ISO-8601 strings when available, otherwise empty strings). `event_prune` has exactly `entries_removed` (nonnegative integer), `failure` (a stable non-secret failure-code string, or `""` when pruning succeeded or did not run), and `new_oldest_at` (the oldest retained event's UTC ISO-8601 `at`, or `""` when no entry remains, pruning did not run, or pruning failed). A prune failure leaves aggregation telemetry available, sets `entries_removed` to `0` and `new_oldest_at` to `""`, and sets `failure` to the applicable nonempty code. When `available` is false because telemetry aggregation and pruning did not run, every one of the thirteen counters and `entries_removed` is `0`, `failure` and `new_oldest_at` are `""`, and both window strings are empty.

`status` is always an object: state `ok` and `findings` have only `state`; state `failed` also has a stable `failure` string and has `dirty_paths` only for `dirty-worktree`, where it is a bytewise-sorted array of unique repository-relative path strings. Each finding has exactly `check`, `code`, `evidence`, `severity`, and `summary`; `check` and `code` are stable nonempty strings, `evidence` is an array of non-secret strings, `severity` is exactly `CRITICAL`, `MAJOR`, or `MINOR`, and `summary` is nonempty. This is the single finding-object shape consumed by FR-162.

The four outcome shapes below are literal schema examples; concrete arrays may contain more objects but may not change their element shapes.

Exit 0:

