### `cli.py` — Forge CLI output envelope (FR-220)

Without `--json`, stdout is human-oriented and ends with exactly one `next required step: <exact command>` line (or `next required step: none — chain closed`). With `--json`, stdout is exactly one UTF-8 sorted-key JSON object and nothing else: `{"chain_id": str|null, "evidence_refs": [str], "expected": str|null, "message": str, "next_required_step": str, "observed": str|null, "ok": bool, "reason_code": str, "remediation": str|null, "schema": "forge-cli/1", "state": str|null}`. Exit 0 success; exit 1 refusal with `reason_code` drawn from the fixed spec'd enum; exit 2 internal failure/frozen chain. Refusal strings and reason codes are pinned control surface per FR-220. Its separately labeled fresh-evaluation transport amendment supplies the sole non-frozen exit-2 exception without changing these envelope bytes: a required-or-unverifiable INVALID `fresh-reviewer-evals` result retains `evidence-incomplete`.

### Phase-1.1 CLI grammars — shipped contract

The complete phase-1 mechanical gate-ID registry is:

```text
changelog
gate-1
stack:<category>
assertion-sensor
invariant:<row-number>
secret-scan
strict-evals
fresh-reviewer-evals
```

`<category>` is normalized lowercase `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`. `<row-number>` is the one-based invariant data-row ordinal and only a row whose enforcement is `commit` is required. Gate 1 appears once under the one ID; a docs-class-only candidate records a `docs-class candidate` skip under that same ID. `classification` is internal; `review` and `review-cap` are only skip targets; index drift uses `--index-drift`. Unknown/unconfigured IDs return `state-precondition`. The shipped `int()` resolver can execute `invariant:01` or `invariant:+1`, but evidence remains keyed by that literal and cannot satisfy canonical `invariant:1`.

`strict-evals` is the replay-compatible alias for Recorded-baseline integrity and remains required for every control or review-final-floor candidate; only a valid FR-255 new-candidate applicability proof may carry an unaffected prior PASS. `fresh-reviewer-evals` is coordinator-owned and candidate-bound; it is required when the authenticated-base trigger matches and is conservatively required-as-INVALID when trigger applicability cannot be authenticated or strictly derived. It follows `strict-evals` and the review-artifact secrets scan and precedes every judgment verb. Only a valid authenticated trigger with an empty derived match array makes direct invocation unconfigured and returns `state-precondition` rather than a skipped or vacuous PASS.

There is no generic mutating-gate grammar. `changelog` is the sole mutating gate. Its no-gate body is a case-insensitive, dot-all full match of:

```regex
No changelog gate (?:is configured|applies)(?: for| to)?(?: this)? .*?repository\.
```

Otherwise DM-003's exact one-cell/output grammar applies. Only the first matching prose `Output path(s):` declaration contributes, every qualifying Markdown row contributes, prose tokens precede Markdown tokens, and later prose declarations are ignored; the two parser channels retain DM-003's distinct backtick normalization and shared first-occurrence deduplication. Success extends the explicit path set, stages those outputs, recomputes candidate identity, applies FR-255's delta and evidence-invalidation rules, and reclassifies.

The shared review-verdict transport is:

```text
VERDICT: PASS|BLOCK
candidate: <exact-current-candidate>
package: <exact-issued-package-digest>
finding: <CRITICAL|MAJOR|MINOR> <nonempty text>
```

Input is strict UTF-8; each split line is stripped and blanks discarded. The verdict is first. Candidate and package each occur exactly once later in either order. For a v2 commit, `<exact-current-candidate>` is the authorization ID and the package separately carries `review_diff_sha256`; for a merge it remains the full candidate HEAD. Findings are optional/repeatable and every other line is invalid. PASS permits MINOR only; BLOCK may contain no finding. `OBSERVATION` is not a transport severity. This is transport validation, not the complete substantive review constitution.

DM-013 canonicalization is compact sorted-key UTF-8 JSON with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, no trailing LF, and no prefix. `command_digest` hashes the exact subprocess argv array. `env_fingerprint` hashes exactly `{command_digest, cwd, platform, policy_digest, python_version, repo_head}`, with `cwd = os.path.realpath(repository_root)`, `platform = sys.platform`, Python `major.minor.micro`, the exact pinned-policy digest, and full live HEAD. Nothing else contributes. Revision 17 performs no fingerprint comparison between Gate-1 records: the newest current-candidate record alone completes or reopens Gate 1, and the retired `pair_voided` marker and `gate_1_pair_voided` event are read as history only.

### `forge-merge-chain/1` — merge argv and state contract

The persisted top-level key set is exactly:

```text
schema, chain_id, kind, state, created_at, last_event_at,
inactive_after, owner, run, repository, worktree, branch, target,
policy_source, candidate, tier, steps, review, approval,
authorization, integration, cleanup
```

`schema` is `forge-merge-chain/1`, `kind` is `merge`, and DM-014 defines candidate canonicalization and persistence. Its orthogonal condition enum is exactly:

```text
integration.condition = none | fetch-failed | rebase-failed |
  remote-moved | remote-churn | push-failed | non-fast-forward |
  push-outcome-unknown | lock-release-failed | foreign-git-state
cleanup.condition = none | cleanup-failed
```

DM-014's condition table is part of the state contract: a condition cannot invent another state, reason-code meaning, or admitted verb.

The accepted argv forms are exactly:

```text
forge status --chain-id <id>
forge merge start --worktree <path> [--declare-tier <tier>]
forge merge refresh --chain-id <id>
forge merge verify --chain-id <id>
forge merge gate run <gate-id> --chain-id <id>
forge review request --chain-id <id>
forge review collect --chain-id <id>
forge review cancel --chain-id <id>
forge review attach --verdict-file <file> --chain-id <id>
forge review disposition --finding <n> --severity <sev> --resolution <text> --chain-id <id>
forge merge approve --candidate <full-head-sha> --chain-id <id>
forge merge finalize --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --continue --paths <path>... --chain-id <id>
forge merge recover --abort-rebase --chain-id <id>
forge merge cleanup --chain-id <id>
forge merge abort [--reason <text>] --chain-id <id>
```

The state value is exactly one of:

```text
classifying, verifying, reviewing, revising,
awaiting_approval, authorized, rebasing, rebase_conflict,
reverifying, reverification_failed, pushing, pushed,
cleanup_pending, closed, aborted
```

Every state-changing event is append-only and generation-bound except DM-014's closed null-generation bootstrap set before the first successful `fetch_result`. FR-232 is the transition table, FR-235 is the matrix, and FR-236 is the observation-based recovery law. Every locked Git/remote subprocess uses FR-236's exact 1200-second, isolated-process-group, 65,536-byte-output, termination, and post-termination observation contract. Files, verdicts, state, and events MUST be owner-controlled regular files; hostile symlinks, oversized verdicts, malformed UTF-8/JSON, pathspec magic, unknown keys/states, and ambiguous chain selection fail closed.

### `cli.py` — Forge CLI v2 output envelope and closed reason enum

Existing phase-1 commands continue to emit `forge-cli/1`. Every new merge/push command emits `forge-cli/2` with the same exact key set and types: `{"chain_id": str|null, "evidence_refs": [str], "expected": str|null, "message": str, "next_required_step": str, "observed": str|null, "ok": bool, "reason_code": str, "remediation": str|null, "schema": "forge-cli/2", "state": str|null}`. Human stdout still ends with exactly one `next required step: <exact command>` or `next required step: none — chain closed`; `--json` emits exactly one sorted-key object and no other stdout. Exit classes and control-surface stability retain FR-220. The complete sorted 41-member enum is normative; all 25 v1 rows below are byte-identical to `fr223-reason-codes/1`, and all 16 additions are exit 1 with the exact failed-precondition text shown:

| Code | Exit class | Exact failed precondition |
|---|---:|---|
| `ambiguous-target` | 1 | a dispensation or disposition target matches more than one record |
| `approval-required` | 1 | control-class chain not yet operator-approved |
| `candidate-stale` | 1 | evidence bound to a superseded candidate hash |
| `citation-out-of-root` | 1 | FR-017 refusal surfaced through the CLI |
| `cleanup-failed` | 1 | Pushed candidate containment or non-force worktree/branch cleanup cannot complete |
| `dirty-index` | 1 | pre-existing staged content at `commit start` |
| `dirty-worktree` | 1 | Merge source or default-branch push checkout fails the exact clean predicate |
| `drift-tree-index` | 1 | working-tree-vs-index divergence on a staged path |
| `evidence-incomplete` | 1 | a required tier step is absent or non-PASS without an operator skip |
| `fetch-failed` | 1 | Required fixed-target fetch/tip resolution failed |
| `frozen-chain` | 2 | corrupt chain file, irresolvable event/state divergence, or foreign HEAD in `committing` |
| `halt-engaged` | 1 | operator halt sentinel present |
| `head-moved` | 1 | out-of-band HEAD movement pending `commit rebase` or `abort` |
| `inactive-chain` | 1 | chain past `inactive_after`; only `status`/`abort` accepted |
| `iteration-cap` | 1 | FR-053 cap of 8 reached |
| `live-chain-exists` | 1 | another live commit chain owns the worktree |
| `live-merge-chain-exists` | 1 | Another live merge chain owns the selected worktree |
| `lock-release-failed` | 1 | Rebase lock could not be explicitly released after the primary outcome was recorded |
| `lock-unavailable` | 1 | commit-lock acquisition failed or timed out |
| `merge-gate-failed` | 1 | Required initial or integrated merge gate is unavailable or non-PASS |
| `mutating-gate-pending` | 1 | non-mutating `gate run` while a configured mutating gate is pending |
| `non-fast-forward` | 1 | Ordinary destination update was rejected as non-fast-forward |
| `ok` | 0 | success |
| `operator-verb-denied` | 1 | model-issued `approve`/`skip` reached the CLI through a denied path |
| `path-missing` | 1 | a named path does not exist at `commit start`/`restage` |
| `policy-changed` | 1 | committed policy bytes differ at the new HEAD after `commit rebase` |
| `policy-unreadable` | 1 | committed-HEAD policy unavailable outside the bootstrap path |
| `push-failed` | 1 | Push returned a known failure other than non-fast-forward |
| `push-outcome-unknown` | 1 | Remote outcome cannot be authoritatively observed |
| `push-target-invalid` | 1 | Checkout, remote, branch, or destination ref is not the fixed sanctioned target |
| `rebase-conflict` | 1 | CLI-owned rebase awaits the specified conflict-recovery verb |
| `rebase-failed` | 1 | Rebase/continue/abort failed outside the recoverable conflict state |
| `rebase-lock-unavailable` | 1 | Common rebase lock timed out after 300 seconds or no portable mechanism exists |
| `remote-churn` | 1 | Eight consecutive integration epochs were defeated by remote movement |
| `review-verdict-invalid` | 1 | verdict grammar, candidate-hash citation, or package-digest citation failed at `collect`/`attach` |
| `skip-not-permitted` | 1 | a skip naming control-class review or approval |
| `state-precondition` | 1 | verb issued in a state whose transition table does not admit it |
| `token-consumed` | 1 | authorization token already used |
| `ttl-expired` | 1 | authorization token past its 30-minute TTL |
| `worktree-invalid` | 1 | Existing path is not a valid registered non-main local-branch worktree |
| `worktree-missing` | 1 | `merge start --worktree` path does not exist |

Hook audit labels `activation-policy-invalid`, `raw-git-commit-denied`, and `raw-git-push-denied` are deliberately not CLI members; DM-017 separately reuses the first spelling as an exact persisted internal condition. The 25 v1 members retain their exact phase-1 preconditions when included in the v2 union; inclusion does not broaden them. In particular, merge emits `candidate-stale` only for evidence bound to a superseded candidate HEAD/hash, never for worktree/policy/target drift; merge uses `worktree-invalid` only when the path still exists but is not a valid registered non-main local-branch worktree, uses `push-target-invalid` for its exact fixed-target condition, and treats post-start worktree absence as `foreign-git-state` plus exact `state-precondition`; and merge emits `approval-required` only for a control-class Gate-4 approval. The FR-232 table's complete validated transition tuple is the state tested by the exact `state-precondition`: inactivity, pending disposition co-sign, pending ownership release, non-control churn acknowledgement, a live/foreign/unprovable/malformed lease, and foreign-state restrictions use that code only because the attempted verb has no admitting tuple row. Final-mode classification is an admitted evidence transition, but the later push continuation from its parked invalid tuple is not admitted and alone emits `state-precondition`. A malformed/unmatched ownership claim uses `frozen-chain` only because FR-237 defines it as an irresolvable ownership-event/materialized-state divergence; a malformed DM-017 singleton uses it only because DM-017 expressly is the standalone-push chain file. Both are exactly within the v1 precondition. The v1-only `inactive-chain`, `head-moved`, `policy-changed`, `live-chain-exists`, `path-missing`, `token-consumed`, and `ttl-expired` remain unused by merge/push unless their byte-exact precondition itself holds. `lock-unavailable` is used by standalone push only for its exact FR-219 commit-lock acquisition failure/timeout precondition; `frozen-chain` is likewise unavailable for any condition outside its byte-exact chain-corruption/event-state-divergence/foreign-committing-HEAD precondition.

Revision 22 reserves the journal-only member `citation-out-of-root`; it MUST NOT be emitted. `ambiguous-target` remains live for review-disposition targets. Retained chain-artifact checks still fail closed: the chain-storage artifact-root check and the engine's artifact-path identity check use `evidence-incomplete` when a cited artifact is missing, unreadable or outside the chain root, and `state-precondition` when an input's grammar is invalid, including an invalid `--run-id` given to `launch`. A floor-only attempt to skip mandatory final review is an unadmitted transition and uses `state-precondition`, without changing the immutable `skip-not-permitted` corpus precondition.

### Revision-9 `forge-cli/2` reason-union extension

The immutable v2 corpus remains the complete 41-member phase-3/4 union. The additive DM-016 v3 corpus is the complete sorted 53-member union for all `forge-cli/2` commands: those 41 rows byte-identical plus exactly these twelve rows. They all exit 1.

| Code | Exit class | Exact failed precondition |
|---|---:|---|
| `archive-rerender-mismatch` | 1 | Archive candidate bytes do not equal deterministic rerender |
| `archive-size-limit` | 1 | Rendered archive exceeds the 16 MiB UTF-8 limit |
| `batch-idempotency-conflict` | 1 | Existing receipt or intent uses the idempotency key for a different normalized request or batch bytes |
| `batch-pending` | 1 | Another outstanding journal batch intent prevents the requested operation |
| `binding-invalid` | 1 | Structured binding is missing, malformed, stale, or inconsistent with its chain event |
| `ingest-proof-invalid` | 1 | At least one required external-chain ingest proof failed |
| `journal-outbox-pending` | 1 | A run-bound chain has an unreceipted event-carried journal batch |
| `legacy-recovery-approval-required` | 1 | Legacy archive mode lacks the matching per-instance operator decision and recovered HEAD |
| `option-duplicate` | 1 | A singleton option `--repo`, `--run-id`, or `--chain-id` occurs more than once |
| `option-empty` | 1 | A required singleton option has an empty value |
| `run-task-binding-invalid` | 1 | Run/task binding fails repository, task, scope, policy, or immutability validation |
| `run-task-binding-required` | 1 | Exactly one of `--run-id` and `--task` was supplied at chain start |

The two option messages are exactly `forge: CLI option refused — duplicate <option>` and `forge: CLI option refused — empty <option>`. Journal-engine-only transaction refusals remain the exact FR-019 diagnostics and do not acquire invented CLI reason codes. Any suffix/inode divergence presented through a CLI envelope uses the existing byte-exact `frozen-chain` row.

Revision 22 reserves the archive, batch, binding, ingest, journal-outbox, legacy-recovery, and run-task-binding members above; they remain immutable corpus entries and MUST NOT be emitted. The preceding paragraph is corpus history retained verbatim; since Revision 22 the FR-019 transaction diagnostics it names are retired and never emitted.

### Revision-10 `forge-cli/2` reason-union extension

The v1, 41-member v2, and 53-member v3 corpora and their rows remain immutable. Revision 10 adds one `forge-cli/2` member, so the next reason corpus generation MUST be additive schema `fr223-reason-codes/4` with the complete sorted 54-member union: every v3 row byte-identical plus exactly this row. The matching v4 enum fixture, result, and manifest are a new additive generation under DM-016; this row MUST NOT be inserted into or used to remint v1, v2, or v3.

| Code | Exit class | Exact failed precondition |
|---|---:|---|
| `run-scope-exceeded` | 1 | Post-fetch three-dot changed paths exceed the bound task's admitted scope |

Its human diagnostic is exactly `forge: merge start refused — changed paths exceed bound task scope`. It is emitted only after the ordinary acquired-ownership abort release is durable; an unbound merge cannot emit it.

Revision 22 reserves `run-scope-exceeded`; it remains an immutable corpus entry and MUST NOT be emitted.

### Revision-18 `forge-cli/2` reason-union extension

The v1, 41-member v2, 53-member v3, and Revision-10 54-member `fr223-reason-codes/4` generations stay byte-identical. The next generation after v4 MUST be additive schema `fr223-reason-codes/5` with the complete sorted 55-member `forge-cli/2` union: every v4 row byte-identical plus exactly this row. This row MUST NOT be inserted into or used to remint v1, v2, v3, or v4; Stage 1 mints neither v4 nor v5.

The Revision-21 renderer refusal in §9 uses this reason code when surfaced through `forge-cli/2`. (Revision 21 authority; deferred to task TA of run-20261004-backfill)

### `forge-push/1` — singleton transaction contract

`.forge/tmp/push-state.json` has DM-017's exact key set and one of five states. In this table, a matching push observation condition is exactly `push-observation-pending`, `push-failed`, `non-fast-forward`, or `push-outcome-unknown`, each with `next_action: observe-remote`; a matching push retry condition is exactly `push-old-tip-retry/retry-push`, whose mandatory locked re-observation selects the same outcome rows before any retry. After the first push intent, every authoritative observation evaluates the complete `attempts`-derived distinct-head vector first: current/latest containment may close, older-only containment must reconcile a new attempt, and only an all-false vector may take an old-tip/rejection/movement route. The exact active-attempt predicate in DM-017 takes precedence over that preserved prior-attempt vector regardless of the active ledger member's source condition, current recovery condition, or whether integration repeats an earlier OID. It remains controlling through every `preparing`/`rebasing`, `foreign-process-state`, `rebase-attribution-unresolved`, and `lock-release-failed` waypoint until its nonce joins `push_intent.attempts` or is definitively abandoned; prior containment can be retained but cannot close or re-park an attempt that has not yet joined that history. The invalid-mode recovery-only row below has precedence over every ordinary non-lock row and cannot fall through after partial recovery. Both common and commit locks are implicit preconditions for every non-lock row. Every row that would persist push intent, retry push, begin a new attempt from the current checkout, or close additionally requires locked HEAD equal to its row's bound current HEAD and exact clean status; mismatch leaves the attempted continuation unadmitted and returns `state-precondition` after preserving already-observed truth. Its legal state transitions are:

| From / event | To | Result |
|---|---|---|
| absent or `closed`; complete common lock unavailable | same absent or `closed` | Return `rebase-lock-unavailable`; singleton bytes remain unchanged and no fetch/rebase/push occurs |
| any nonterminal state; recovery common lock unavailable | same state | Return `rebase-lock-unavailable`; preserve the prior singleton byte-for-byte |
| any state after complete common-lock acquisition; FR-219 commit lock is unavailable or times out | same state or same absent singleton | Return exact `lock-unavailable`, release the common lock, preserve singleton bytes, and perform no fetch/rebase/push or recovery write |
| any strict-valid nonterminal state or exact release wrapper; invoking committed mode is invalid and the hook marked this `forge push` recovery-only | predicate-selected state, `closed`, or unchanged | Acquire only the locks needed by the recorded transaction; prove death/release, observe/classify only existing identity-bound intent/result truth, observe an existing push intent, or run exact owned abort/restoration. Never start integration-target fetch, rebase, continue, attempt, retry, push intent, or push, and never fall through to another row. Stop at valid restored HEAD with exit-0 `ok` partial recovery only when no admitted Git child run by this invocation returned non-PASS. A non-PASS owned abort uses the ordinary disjoint result: exact remaining conflict retains/returns `rebase-conflict`; proven restoration persists/returns `rebase-failed/start-new-attempt`; unmatched attribution persists its observation condition but returns `rebase-failed`. Current-head landed truth may select `closed`; every other unresolved condition keeps its ordinary exact result. Once all prior truth is settled but invalid HEAD remains, preserve it and every push/landing fact, clear `attempt_nonce`, park unwrapped `activation-policy-invalid`, and emit the exact diagnostic plus `state-precondition` |
| any nonterminal state; a fenced child cannot be proved terminated after timeout/output-limit/launch handling | `recovery_required` | Record `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence`, retain exact fence/process/intent evidence and any original `process.attribution`, and return `fetch-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `push-outcome-unknown`, or observation-only `state-precondition` by the exact seven-member standalone operation set and observation phase: `final-prepush` uses `fetch-failed`, `post-push` uses `push-outcome-unknown`, and after `attribution-observation` preserves its wrapper the same invocation's classification continuation is unadmitted and uses `state-precondition`; launch no later child. Any other standalone operation is exact `frozen-chain` |
| absent or `closed`; new `forge push` after complete-lock acquisition, singleton re-read, and repeated preflight | `preparing` | Bind repository, fixed remote/ref, and pre-operation HEAD; append/fsync the first `attempt_history` member and atomically set its nonce as `attempt_nonce` with the state, then fsync fetch intent |
| `preparing`; fixed fetch/tip resolution fails, including a successful initial query proving the required destination ref absent | `recovery_required` | Record `fetch-failed`, clear only the active `attempt_nonce`, and retain its ledger member; no full `fetched_tip` exists, no rebase/push occurred, and observation precedes a later new attempt |
| `preparing`; fixed tip fetched | `rebasing` | Record `fetched_tip`; copy `attempt_nonce` to `rebase.operation_nonce`, derive the action/intent digest, and fsync rebase intent |
| `rebasing`; successful-no-op or rewritten-integrated predicate matches but final intended HEAD has invalid DM-015 | `recovery_required` | Persist the completed-result classification plus `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt` as an admitted internal transition, clear `attempt_nonce`, and create no `rebased_head` or push intent; this row emits no CLI result |
| `recovery_required` with that exact `activation-policy-invalid/start-new-attempt`; the same invocation attempts to continue toward push before approved repair | unchanged refusal | The continuation has no admitting row: emit the exact invalid-mode diagnostic and return byte-exact `state-precondition` without another state change |
| `rebasing`; successful-no-op or rewritten-integrated predicate and final intended-HEAD DM-015 valid, but the final pre-push tip read before push intent is unavailable | `recovery_required` | Preserve the integrated-result evidence, record `fetch-failed/start-new-attempt`, clear the active nonce while retaining its ledger member, return exact `fetch-failed`, and perform no push; this is distinct from unavailable post-intent outcome observation |

| any nonterminal state after at least one push intent and without an active attempt; authoritative present destination contains the current top-level `intended_head`, and locked HEAD plus the latest member's intended head and fresh `rebased_head` still equal that head with exact clean status | `closed` | This row has precedence over every non-release outcome row: set `push_intent.landed_head` to the current last attempted member, record the complete vector and success, normalize `recovery.condition/next_action` to `none/none` while retaining immutable result evidence, and close without another push |
| any nonterminal state after at least two distinct push attempts and without an active attempt, where the latest member's bound intended HEAD equals the current top-level `intended_head`; authoritative observation proves that head uncontained but one or more older attempted heads contained | `recovery_required` | Set `landed_head` to the latest contained older member, preserve the complete vector/results, record `remote-moved/start-new-attempt`, return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and integrate the still-current newer checkout in a fresh attempt; never close or abort merely because the older head landed |
| any post-intent state without an active attempt; authoritative current-head containment is durable but locked HEAD no longer equals that intended head or status is not exact clean | unchanged refusal | Normalize `recovery.condition/next_action` to `none/none`, preserve remote/result truth, do not close, and return byte-exact `state-precondition` because the close transition tuple is not admitted; no older pushed OID is reported as completion of the moved checkout |
| `pushing`, or `recovery_required` with a matching push observation/retry condition; authoritative present destination equals old tip and every attempted intended HEAD is uncontained under any retained result classification or no result | `pushing` then ordinary push outcome | Preserve the result/history and record `push-old-tip-retry/retry-push`. If start-pipe proof shows this epoch authorized no push child, the observation is its immediate recheck; atomically set state `pushing` and consume the retry condition to `none/none`, then the existing unlaunched intent authorizes its sole attempt without appending a duplicate. Otherwise release for exact next `forge push`; its newly acquired epoch appends/fsyncs a fresh ledger member with `source_condition: push-old-tip-retry`, atomically appends the deliberate duplicate push-intent member bearing that nonce, sets state `pushing`, clears the active field, consumes the condition to `none/none`, and then makes its sole push attempt. | `pushing`, or `recovery_required` with a matching push observation condition; observation-only unrelated destination movement or authoritative absence without a retained non-fast-forward result and with every attempted intended HEAD uncontained | `recovery_required` | Preserve every other result/history fact, record `remote-moved`, return exit-0 `ok` partial recovery, and start a new attempt next invocation; absence observed before invocation never starts a push, while FR-236 governs the explicit deletion-after-read limitation |

| `recovery_required/lock-release-failed`; release recovery proves the pending inner then outer layers released | recorded primary state/condition/outcome | Clear `lock_release` and the three primary fields and restore DM-017's exact primary tuple, active nonce, and attempt ledger without repeating it. If the restored primary tuple is under invalid committed mode, the hook admits only the applicable recovery-only classification or release completion and the invocation stops with that row's exact result; otherwise apply the ordinary classification/observation row |
| `recovery_required` with `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence`; next `forge push` under the physical lock or FR-235 reservation proves the recorded group dead | `recovery_required` | Persist group-death proof plus the exact operation/intent/fence identity before clearing the fence. `fetch`/`tip-resolution` selects `fetch-failed/start-new-attempt`; `rebase`/`abort` first matches the identity to its unique attempt-history or abort-attempt nonce, then applies DM-017's result-over-selected-fence precedence, atomically appends the selected attribution if new, makes it current, and selects `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase`; `push` selects `push-outcome-unknown/observe-remote`; `remote-observation` matches its persisted intent digest against the unique current attempt/phase/push-intent binding, with exact `final-prepush` re-entering the final-tip rows and selecting `fetch-failed/start-new-attempt` only if the repeated authoritative read remains unavailable, while exact `post-push` selects `push-outcome-unknown/observe-remote`; exact `attribution-observation` MUST match the retained attribution-derived intent, preserves that original object byte-for-byte, and restores `rebase-attribution-unresolved/observe-rebase` for a later read-only observation without replay. Any other standalone operation is exact `frozen-chain`. No mutation or later child precedes the selected observation |
| any nonterminal state whose exact current owner/reservation/fence/intent identities match newly completed FR-235/FR-236 operator quarantine, including `recovery_required/foreign-process-state`; next `forge push` owns both locks | `recovery_required` | Exclusively revalidate the singleton, append every newly completed stage to its exact ordered quarantine history without overwriting earlier entries, first persist `foreign-process-state/operator-quiescence` when a parent crashed before that condition, and then persist operation-specific observation. Select the same operation-and-phase-mapped condition/action as the proven-dead row; a `remote-observation` fence without the exact attempt/phase/push-intent binding is `frozen-chain`, and the identity-bound ingress admits no new side effect before observation |
